What was left is not a defect anyone can fix by writing code: **it is a question only the capability can answer** — *what does a functional-testing policy review refuse on?* **A defect with no author sits; a decision with an owner can be scheduled**, and the correction changes which of those this is. The original finding follows and its (i) and (iii) stand. **HIGHEST SEVERITY — THE GOVERNANCE TRIAD CANNOT DECLINE, AND THE DEFECT IS IN THE FRAMEWORK, NOT IN ONE RUNTIME.** The triad exists so that review is not performed by the reviewed: R-12.2 makes stages 4–6 mandatory and R-12.1 forbids bypassing a stage. **All three are implemented. None can refuse.** *(i) In the canonical runtime* — `canonical-runner-capability.ts:141–161` implements each review as an existence check: `policy-review` emits `approved: true` when `story !== undefined`, `guardrail-review` when `testDesign !== undefined`, `architecture-review` when `architectureComponents.length > 0`. The negative branch is `emit.notApplicable`, **not a refusal**, and the runner's own sequencing makes each artefact present by the time its review runs — so the negative branch is unreachable and would not refuse if reached. *(ii) In the framework, which is the real finding* — `StageEmitter` (`capability-framework/src/stages.ts:90–94`) offers exactly **two** constructors, `ok` and `notApplicable`. A stage can say *"I did work"* or *"I did no work"*. **It cannot say "I did work and the answer is no."** And `certify()` (`certification.ts`) maps an applicable result to `certified: true` **without ever inspecting the value the stage produced** — `value` is `unknown` and is never read. So a triad stage that correctly computed `approved: false` would still certify. Measured: triad completes with 1 agent per stage, `certified: true`, `firstRefusal: null`. Meanwhile the **20 agents that declare a triad stage** (2 at architecture/policy-review, 18 at guardrail-review) are not involved at all. | **EVERY CERTIFICATION THIS PLATFORM HAS PRODUCED WAS REVIEWED BY THREE STAGES THAT COULD NOT REFUSE.** Not one runtime: `certify()` and `StageEmitter` are `@dbiz/capability-framework`, used by **all five implemented capabilities** — functional-testing, dev-change, discovery-flow, penetration-testing and performance. The framework fact alone establishes this independently of what any capability's triad computes, because the value is never read. **Refusal is expressible only as absence:** the sole route to `certified: false` from a stage that ran is `notApplicable`, which asserts *"there was nothing to review"* — so *the review failed* and *there was nothing to review* are the same signal, and a capability wanting to refuse must lie about having worked. **A reviewer that always approves is worse than an absent one**, because the run carries the verdict into evidence: `certification.verdicts` reports `stage "policy-review" completed with 1 agent(s)`, which reads as review having occurred. A reader has no signal that the approval was a `!== undefined` test. This is D-012's unconditional `certified: true` one level up, in the mechanism that exists to catch exactly that. | **Platform — `@dbiz/capability-framework`** (the primitive) · Functional Testing capability (the triad's agents) | **NOT repaired in Section C, and NOT by making the existence checks stricter** — a tighter existence check is still an existence check, and the defect is that review is not performed rather than that it is performed leniently. **Two separable changes, and the order is load-bearing.** **(1) The framework gains a refusal primitive** — a third `StageEmitter` constructor carrying a stated reason, a `StageResult` field for it, and a `certify()` branch mapping it to `certified: false` with a reason distinct from not-applicable. | **D-003** | **No shared nonce store implementation.** Replay protection is injectable and proven to work across instances when a shared store is supplied (R-11), but the default is per process. | Document 17 declares the Intelligence Plane *"multiple per region, horizontally scaled"*. Running the default there means a nonce refused by one instance is **accepted by another** — no restart needed, a load balancer is sufficient. That is a replay window against C-08.22. | Platform runtime | Supply a shared implementation before any multi-instance deployment. The platform reports `replayProtectionIsSingleProcessOnly` so the condition is visible, and readiness reports it as `degraded` — but nothing can *enforce* it, because the platform cannot detect its own topology. Where the store lives is a deployment decision and belongs in an ADR against document 17. |

| **D-007** | **DECLARATION-VERSUS-IMPLEMENTATION DRIFT — a debt class, not a single defect.** No gate in this platform compares what a declaration *says* against what the implementation *does*. Every gate verifies implementation against implementation (imports, exports, invocation, census) or declaration against declaration (ADR completeness, closure baselines). The seam between the two is unmeasured, and three independent instances have now been found — each by reading, none by a gate. **(i) F-4** — [`conformance.test.ts`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/test/conformance.test.ts) lines 61–66 assert *"this engine does not"* call `publishTests`; [`domains/synchronisation.ts`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/synchronisation.ts) line 128 calls it unconditionally, wired at `canonical-capability.ts` line 136. The comment (`bff18a0`, 2026-08-02) post-dates the call (`35eb5c7`, 2026-07-29) by four days. **(ii) `TestDesignSyncAdapter`** — ADR-0061 was accepted 2026-07-30 naming the agent-catalogue runtime a retirement target; Phase 6.9 then added 13 high-fidelity operations to that runtime alone on 2026-08-02, invalidating the accepted decision's premise without amending it. **(iii) Healing ownership** — the canonical workflow declares FT-026/027/028 at stage 8, plane EP→IP; all thirteen `healing.*` agents are implemented at plane IP, stage reflection. **(iv) `route()` ↔ mounted NestJS controller** — found independently on 2026-08-04 by another writer in this repository, and the strongest evidence that this is a class rather than a coincidence: `route()` had served `GET /api/application-templates` since the wizard stopped bundling the application list, but **no controller mapped it**, so the deployed server returned Nest's own 404 while every `route()`-level test passed. Its own fix records it as *"the third occurrence of that defect class (publish-update, branding, and now this one)"* — three live failures from one unmeasured seam. The guard in `controller-coverage.test.ts` could not see it because it inspected only tenant sub-resource paths, not top-level ones; it now covers both shapes and additionally asserts that a controller which exists is actually **mounted**. **(v) Package delivery direction — the load-bearing instance, and the reason the Intelligence Plane knows the Execution Plane's address at all.** [ADR-0037](../docs/adr/ADR-0037-execution-target-simplification.md) §116 declares that the Execution Plane *"pull[s] its signed package from the IP (the 'only egress', R-05.1)"*, and R-5.1 mandates that every cross-plane exchange is EP-initiated. The implementation does the opposite: `launcher/generator/generateBindings.mjs:37` emits `send: (pkg, signature, attempt) => epSend(executionPlaneEndpoint, pkg, signature, attempt)` — an Intelligence Plane that **pushes** the sealed package to an address it holds. Declared model and implemented model disagree in the direction that defines the sovereignty split, and no gate compared them; the contradiction survived ADR-0037's acceptance, the CROSSPLANE-001 certification and the CLAES wiring audit, all of which describe the pull model in prose while the emitted bindings push. **Ruled 2026-08-04: EP pulls — enforcement of ADR-0037 and R-5.1, not an amendment to either.** Eight further Intelligence-Plane references (`bootstrapContext.mjs:8`, `configurationService.mjs:5`, `configurationValidator.mjs:8`, `executionPlaneValidator.mjs:7,13`, `bindingsService.mjs:26`, `devBootstrap.mjs:19,21,40`, `docker-compose.dev.yml:30`) exist only to hold, validate and thread the address this instance requires; each has a plausible local justification while the whole contradicts R-5.1. Recorded by [ADR-0069](../docs/adr/ADR-0069-capability-one-connector-realisation.md) §2.4, §2.5. | **The pattern is more serious than any instance.** A declaration that has silently stopped being true is worse than an absent one: it is load-bearing for review, and a reviewer who reads it is misled with no signal that anything is wrong — the *declared-but-unbuilt* failure class (§3) inverted into *declared-but-no-longer-true*. Instance (iii) is the costliest today: there is **no Execution-Plane mechanical healing anywhere in the platform**, so healing cannot operate with the Intelligence Plane unreachable, which R-12.4 and INV-7 require of stages 8 and 9 — the degraded-mode guarantee is unmet for healing specifically. `verify-functional-workflow-substructure` passes throughout, because it checks sub-phases, reviewers, gates and metrics *under* the 37 steps and never compares a step's declared `plane`/`stage` against what the agent registry reports. | Platform governance (the class) · Functional Testing capability · Execution Plane (instance iii) | **Close the class first, then the instances — detection is cheaper than repair and does not depend on the Execution Plane existing.** **Instance (iv) is now CLOSED at the class level:** `governance/verification/verify-http-surface-parity.js` compares route()'s dispatch table against the mounted NestJS controllers in **both** directions (HS-1 unmapped action · HS-2 dead mapping · HS-3 unmapped top-level path · HS-4 dead prefix · HS-5 declared-but-not-mounted), is self-proving positive and negative (R-13.4), carries two recorded fault proofs — one per shape the defect actually took — and is registered in `run-all.js` (68 gates). **It was validated retrospectively against the parent tree of all three historical fixes, and failed each one, naming the exact route:** `de83eba` → *branding, check-compatibility, installed, publish-update, rollback, sync-config, update-history* (HS-1); `669980c` → *branding* (HS-1); `87e103a` → *application-templates* (HS-3). The first of those would have caught two of the three live incidents in a single run, months before the second was independently discovered. **Still open:** extend `verify-functional-workflow-substructure` to assert every canonical step's declared `plane` and `stage` against `ALL_AGENTS` (WP5a) — the same comparison applied to the workflow declaration. Instances (i) and (ii) are addressed by ADR-0069 P-69.6 and P-69.2/P-69.8. Instance (iii)'s repair is Execution-Plane work, **blocked on the DBiz-owned Execution Plane product repository** (`PROJECT_STATE.md` §9.1): proactive and reactive healing must execute EP-side to satisfy R-12.4/INV-7, while IP-side healing *judgment* correctly remains at stage 10 (producer-never-judges). |
| **D-008** | **A published measurement can be taken across a concurrently-changing tree, and nothing records that it was.** Observed 2026-08-04: `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine` reported **310** tests, then **313**, during one governance session. The cause was **not** a build defect — it was a concurrent edit. The working tree was verified clean at 05:44:27; `application-template.controller.ts`, `app.module.ts` and `controller-coverage.test.ts` were written by another writer at 05:47:01, 05:47:13 and 05:48:36; the next measurement read 313. `controller-coverage.test.ts` derives its test population from `readdirSync` over `*.controller.ts`, so it **correctly** grew when a controller was added — the guard worked exactly as designed. | The measurement was right both times; what is missing is that **neither reading records the tree state it was taken against**. Under R-13.2 every measurement must carry an evidence envelope — source, time, method, confidence, traceability — and a commit-ish is precisely the traceability a test count needs. Without it, two honest measurements of the same repository disagree and there is no way to tell whether the difference is a regression, a fix, or a concurrent edit. The first diagnosis attempted here was *wrong* for exactly that reason: a clean rebuild appeared to "confirm" a build-staleness cause, because by then the concurrent edit was already on disk. **A confounded measurement is more dangerous than a missing one — it produces a confident, false root cause.** | Governance evidence | Stamp every published test and gate measurement with the `git rev-parse HEAD` and working-tree-dirty state it was taken against, and refuse to publish a measurement taken against a dirty tree without saying so. Cheap, and it converts this class of confusion into a recorded fact. |

| **D-009** | **A gate suite cannot fault-prove its own red portion — the unproved set is exactly the failing set.** Measured 2026-08-04: `record-fault-proofs.js` recorded 149 proofs and reported **8 NOT PROVED**, which map one-for-one onto the **7 failing gates** (`verify-operational-readiness` carries two probes): ai-vendor-neutrality, change-control-completeness, governance-self-validation, operational-readiness ×2, functional-completeness, repository-hygiene, intent-conservation. The mechanism is structural, not a defect: a fault proof is a *clean → faulted → clean* triple, and a gate already failing on the clean tree cannot establish the clean leg, so its proof cannot be recorded no matter how well the probe is written. | **The red portion of the suite is precisely the portion whose detection is unproven** — the gates most in need of evidence are the ones that cannot supply it, and confidence is therefore weakest exactly where the platform already knows it has problems. `verify-governance-self-validation` being one of the seven sharpens it: **the gate that audits governance is inside the unproven set**, so the suite's self-assessment has a blind spot it cannot see from the inside (R-13.5, GCI). This is not a reason to distrust the 149 recorded proofs, which are unaffected; it is a reason never to read "149 proofs recorded" as "the suite is proven". | Platform governance | No repair is available while a gate is red — the dependency runs the right way round, and inverting it (recording a proof against a failing gate) would be manufacturing the confidence R-13.4 exists to measure. **What is available now and is the actual ask:** report the unproved set as a first-class number beside the proof count, so `149 proofs` is never published without `8 unproved, all in the failing set`. Each failing gate's own repair closes its proof as a side effect; the count is the tracking mechanism until then. |

| **D-010** | **An Intelligence-Plane script opens an outbound connection into the customer tenancy.** `packages/functional-testing-engine/launcher/generator/devBootstrap.mjs:27` calls `fetch(new URL('/health', url).href, …)` to poll the **Execution Plane** for readiness before handing off to the launcher. Its own header declares it *"(Intelligence-Plane side)"*. It is tracked, unignored, referenced by `docker-compose.dev.yml` through `FTE_EXECUTION_PLANE_ENDPOINT`, and recorded as **IMPLEMENTED** in `CROSSPLANE-001` WS4 — live, certified code, not a stray artefact. Found by `verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js` on its first run against the tree. | Under a literal reading this is three rules at once: **R-3.2** (the IP SHALL NOT open connections to customer systems), **R-5.1** (all cross-plane communication is *initiated by the Execution Plane*) and **R-5.2** (no callback, queue, webhook or socket from the IP into the customer tenancy). The direction is the whole point of Rule 5 — the EP pulls, the IP never dials — and a readiness probe inverts it. **Mitigating context, stated fairly:** this is Developer Mode container-startup orchestration, where both planes run on the developer's own machine, so the "customer tenancy" it reaches is a local container. It is not on the shipped runtime path. **Immediate cost regardless:** the gate fails its clean run, so under D-009 its two fault proofs cannot be recorded — the R-3.2 mechanism the platform just gained is registered but unproven until this is settled. | Platform / Developer Experience (CROSSPLANE-001 WS4) | **Not resolved unilaterally — this is a constitutional question, and ADR-0069 is frozen.** Three options, in the order I would take them: **(a)** delete the probe and let the container runtime express the dependency natively (`depends_on: condition: service_healthy`), which removes the IP→EP call entirely and is almost certainly less code than it replaces; **(b)** invert it so the EP signals readiness to the IP, preserving R-5.1's direction; **(c)** allowlist it with a recorded rationale bounding it to the dev topology — available, but it sets the precedent that IP→EP dialling is acceptable, which is the exact thing R-5.1 exists to forbid, so it should be the last choice rather than the easy one. |

| **D-011** | **Nothing measures what is reachable ONLY from something about to be deleted — and the first instrument built to measure it cannot yet do so.** *(Corrected 2026-08-04. This entry first asserted that the canonical runtime could not seal an ExecutionPackage and that retirement would delete the assembly machinery. **That assertion was WRONG and is withdrawn** — see below. The correction is kept in place of a silent edit because the way it was wrong is the actual debt.)* **What was wrong:** the inventory placed `authoring-bridge.mjs` in the RETIRING set. ADR-0061 §6 step 6 says *"re-point `authoring-bridge.mjs`"* and §8 names only *"legacy usage in"* it — **the bridge survives**. The FT-M6 canonicalization report measured parity against *"Canonical (`canonical-capability` + bridge)"*, so the bridge was always part of the certified definition. One misclassified entry point orphaned everything the bridge reaches and manufactured a false crisis. The measurement was arithmetically correct; the premise was false. **What survives the correction, narrowly:** `canonical-capability.ts` and `canonical-runner-capability.ts` **alone** hold zero references to `ExecutionPackage` — verified across all 15 modules; their only seal is `sealPlatformEvent`, which seals an audit event. Package assembly lives on the bridge. That is a fact about composition, not a capability gap, and **ADR-0061's parity claim stands**. | **The real debt is that the question cannot currently be answered.** With the bridge correctly classified, the tool reports 0 orphans — but that result is **degenerate, not reassuring**: `authoring-bridge.mjs:37` imports the package **barrel** (`./dist/src/index.js`), which re-exports everything, so the surviving closure is all 52 modules, exactly the package size. Any module is reachable from a barrel, so import reachability can never find an orphan while one survives. **This platform's gates verify what is PRESENT; none verifies what is REACHABLE ONLY FROM SOMETHING ABOUT TO BE DELETED.** That blind spot is now twice-instanced — `observation-interpretation` and the design-synchronisation adapter were both found by hand, not by a gate — and the instrument built to close it is not yet valid. | Platform governance · ADR-0061 migration | **Make the measurement valid before trusting either answer.** Barrel re-exports must resolve to the module that DEFINES each named import rather than counting as a dependency on everything the barrel exports — the same distinction already drawn in `verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js`, where `export … from` is a pass-through and not a consumer. Until that lands, `retirement-inventory.mjs` reports a number it cannot support, and **no retirement decision should rest on it in either direction**. Once valid, the tool is a candidate for `run-all.js` as a standing gate rather than a one-off script: the class of defect it targets recurs at every deletion, not only this one. ADR-0069 P-69.2 already blocks retirement until every inventory entry is resolved, which is the correct shape regardless of what the corrected inventory reports. |

| **D-012** | **THE CANONICAL RUNTIME REPORTS A CERTIFICATION VERDICT IT DID NOT COMPUTE.** `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/executive-reporting.ts:169` emits `{ label: 'verdict', value: model.certificationSummary.verdict }` — the verdict is read straight from the supplied `ReportingModel`, under a comment reading *"canonical ReportingModel sections — preserved by identity."* **Measured, not inferred**, by mutation: running the canonical capability twice with every decision-shaped input flipped and diffing all 896 output leaves, 50 moved and exactly **one followed its input verbatim** — set `certificationSummary.verdict = 'CERTIFIED'` and the capability reports `CERTIFIED`. Reproducible via `governance/capability/measure-copy-through.mjs`. The adjacent leaf `certificationSummary[1].value` did **not** move, so this is targeted pass-through, not object splatting. **Two further facts complete it:** no canonical domain computes a certification verdict anywhere — `executive-reporting.ts:169` is the only occurrence in all thirteen — and **no domain ever returns `certified: false`**; all thirteen hardcode `certified: true` unconditionally. | **This is the July board finding's class — a system reporting a certification status it did not produce — now in the runtime that SURVIVES retirement.** R-12.5 makes stage 11 Certification the Intelligence Plane's stage to *perform*; a capability whose verdict is whatever was fed in has not certified anything, and one whose per-domain `certified` flag is a literal cannot fail. The 49 other moved leaves were all genuine derivations — `coverageSummary.coverageScore` returned **0.5** where the input supplied **1**, recomputed across two acceptance criteria — which is what makes the single echo diagnostic rather than noise. **A field-shape guard would have flagged that 0.5 as a supplied coverage verdict and cleared nothing; the behavioural test caught the real defect and produced no false positive on the same run.** | Functional Testing capability — canonical runtime | **Do not fix inside WP4.** A live-code change in a canonical domain is a different risk class from a scenario, and the honest fix depends on an unsettled architecture question: **does d13 compute a verdict, or cite one computed upstream?** The trace answers half of it — there is no upstream verdict to cite, because nothing in the sequence computes one — so the fix is a **gap in the canonical sequence**, not a local repair to d13. Pass B's copy-through guard is built to FAIL on this entry and is deliberately not weakened: a red guard naming a known defect is a better state than a green one accommodating it. The unconditional `certified: true` is recorded here as adjacent and unexamined; it deserves its own measurement. |

**D-012 is a MISSING CANONICAL STEP, not a defect in `executive-reporting`.** FT step 11 is Certification and **R-12.5** makes it the Intelligence Plane's stage to *perform*. The canonical sequence does not perform it: searching all thirteen domains for `verdict|CERTIFIED|certify(`, `executive-reporting.ts:169` is the **only** certification verdict anywhere, and it is read from the input. There is nothing upstream for d13 to cite, so repairing d13 in isolation would replace an echo with a fabrication. The repair is a certification step the sequence does not have.

**The same gap measured from a second angle — the `certified` flag is decoration.** Three bounded questions, answered 2026-08-04:

| Question | Result |
|---|---|
| Can any canonical domain return `certified: false`? | **No — 13/13.** All hardcode `certified: true`. The only `certified: false` in the package is in `capability.ts` and `orchestrators.ts`, both **agent-path retirement targets** |
| Does the canonical composition **consume** the flag? | **No — zero reads.** `canonical-capability.ts` and `canonical-runner-capability.ts` never read `.certified` from any domain output |
| Does any canonical domain have a failure path? | **No — 13/13** return `failure: null` and never a non-null failure |

So on the surviving runtime a domain **cannot fail, cannot report itself uncertified, and its certification flag is read by nobody**. The flag is produced thirteen times and consumed zero times. This is not a second defect but the same one seen from the production side: even once something computes a verdict, the sequence has no mechanism by which a domain could contribute a negative outcome to it. `verify-functional-completeness` C-6 is written to fail on this and is deliberately not softened — a red property naming a real absence.

| **D-013** | **DECLARED CAPABILITY THAT IS STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE — a field whose type promises discrimination its construction cannot deliver.** Two instances, both found 2026-08-04 while designing failure conditions, and both of which would have supported a plausible-looking condition that could never fire. **(i) `synchronisation.publicationStatus`** is typed `'published' \| 'partial' \| 'failed'` and derived three ways at `domains/synchronisation.ts:165–168` — but every `PublishedObject` in the domain is constructed with `status: 'published'` as a **literal** (lines 130, 136, 143, 150, 161), so nothing can produce `'failed'` and the `'partial'`/`'failed'` branches are unreachable. **(i-b) — MEASURED 2026-08-04, and this half is REACHABLE AND FIRES TODAY.** The same expression opens with `publishedObjects.length === 0 ? 'published'`, so **a run that published nothing reports `published`**. On the `no-criteria` input the canonical run produces `publishedObjects=0, publicationStatus='published'` — measured, not argued. It is the vacuous-green class (D-015) in the runtime, third instance after *coverage of zero requirements is total coverage* and `verify-customer-readiness`, and it is the one with the furthest reach: `publicationStatus` is what `executiveReporting.executiveSummary` reports and what `runtime-entry-point-bridge` hands its caller as `executionPlanning.publicationStatus`. **An empty publication and a complete one are indistinguishable at every consumer.** Whether the correct value is `'failed'`, a fourth state, or a negative finding is a semantics decision and belongs with the publication rework, not to a local patch. **(ii) `defect-management` `eligible: true`** at `domains/defect-management.ts:138` is hardcoded on every defect, so `eligibleCount === defects.length` always and the field discriminates nothing. **(iii) The `'unknown'` failure-classification path is unreachable end to end** — proven 2026-08-04 by variant F rather than argued. `execution.ts:142` emits exactly one failure string for every failed component, `'component did not settle'`; `healing.classify` matches it against `/settle|synchron/i` and returns `'synchronisation'`; and healing maps **every** failed component, so `defect-management`'s `?? 'unknown'` fallback never fires either. Variant F drove 4 of 4 components to failure and produced 4 defects, **0** classified `'unknown'`. Three separate constructs — `classify`'s `'unknown'` return, the `?? 'unknown'` fallback, and `SEVERITY_MODEL[...] ?? { low, p3 }` — all exist to handle a case the pipeline cannot generate. | **The harm is FALSE CONFIDENCE IN COVERAGE, not dead code.** Dead code is inert; a three-state status field is *read* as evidence that partial and failed publication are handled, and a boolean named `eligible` is read as evidence that eligibility is assessed. A reviewer, a certification report, or the next author designing a failure condition all take the declaration at face value — and in this case one nearly did: the obvious Section B condition for synchronisation was `publicationStatus !== 'published'`, which would have been a failure branch that can never be taken, shipped as coverage. This is the same shape as the fabricated healing condition that was rejected, one level down and harder to see, because the unreachability lives in the *constructors* rather than the field. | Functional Testing capability — canonical domains | **Not repaired in Section B**, deliberately: reworking `publicationStatus` changes the domain's own publication semantics, and publication is reworked anyway when the design-synchronisation capability is ported to the canonical runtime — **deferred to Section C**, where the change is coherent rather than incidental. The rejection reasoning is recorded in `synchronisation.ts` at the failure-decision site so the next author does not re-reach for the same unfireable condition. Section B instead reports the detectable property: the connector acknowledging fewer test cases than were submitted, where the `?? testCase.id` fallback currently makes a partial acknowledgement indistinguishable from a complete one. `eligible` has no Section B consumer and is recorded here pending the same rework. |

| **D-014** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-04: NEITHER DEFECTIVE; THE EXPECTATION WAS WRONG.** **The canonical runtime leaves a requirement uncovered on its own blessed reference fixture, and nobody has ever asked whether the fixture or the domain is wrong.** Measured 2026-08-04, the first time the runtime was capable of saying so: `test-management-intelligence` reports `[coverage] 1 of 2 requirement(s) left uncovered by the organised suite` when run on `referenceInput('A')` — the same input the conformance suite certifies against and the copy-through measurement uses. **This is not a defect introduced by the Section B failure branches.** It is a fact those branches made visible: `requirementCoverage.uncovered` has been computed as non-zero on this input all along, and until a domain could report a negative finding, nothing read it. | **The first negative finding this runtime has ever produced appeared on the first run, on the fixture the platform treats as clean.** That is either (a) the reference input legitimately under-specifying — `REQ-1` carries two acceptance criteria and the organised suite covers one — in which case the fixture should say so explicitly rather than presenting as a clean baseline; or (b) `test-management-intelligence` genuinely failing to organise a suite over both criteria, in which case a real capability gap has been sitting behind a green conformance suite. **The two readings have opposite remedies and nobody has distinguished them**, because until now the question could not be asked: the conformance suite asserts structural properties of the result, not that coverage is complete. | Functional Testing capability — canonical runtime | Distinguish (a) from (b) before Section B's step (b) computes a verdict, because a verdict over run state will inherit whichever answer is true. **RESOLVED — the discriminator was run and the answer is neither.** `test-management-intelligence` lines 157–162 do not compute coverage: they **relay** `repository.coverageSummary` and `repository.missingAssets`. The fixture's `REQ-1` carries two acceptance criteria while `coverage`, `similarity` and `traceability` reference `ac:0` only, so a repository covering one of two is the modelled state and "1 uncovered" is the correct finding over it. Confirmed by construction: a coverage-complete variant reaches `total=2 covered=2 uncovered=0` with **zero** negative findings, so the domain can reach clean and was never wrong. **What was wrong was an unstated assumption — that a REFERENCE input implies a CLEAN verdict.** It never did; the conformance suite asserts structural properties of the result, so the question could not previously arise, and the first negative finding this runtime was ever capable of producing was mistaken for a defect. **Actions taken:** variant `'A'` is annotated at the fixture as deliberately modelling an incomplete repository, so it reads as intent; variant `'C'` is added as a coverage-complete input. **Prohibition retained: do not "fix" this by relaxing the condition** — the condition is the only reason the question became visible, and relaxing it would restore the silence rather than the correctness. **Consequence for Section B step (b):** a verdict over run state must be measured over BOTH inputs. Without a clean run, *"the verdict correctly reports a gap"* and *"the verdict always reports a gap"* are indistinguishable — the same trap as a certification step that can only say one thing. |

**D-012 — CLOSED 2026-08-04 under DECLARED CITATION, measured. `C-5 PASS — 1 reproduced leaf(s), 1 declared and verified against the named source: executiveReporting.certificationSummary[0].value; every other moved leaf was transformed.`**

The closing move was not to the runtime but to the measurement, because the measurement was the thing that could not tell two situations apart. A citation and an echo are **behaviourally identical** — both reproduce a supplied value unchanged — so no value-flow test can separate them, and C-5 was flagging a correct citation exactly as loudly as an undeclared echo. The runtime now DECLARES which outputs quote which inputs (`src/canonical-citations.ts`, one entry), and C-5 **verifies the declaration against the named source rather than trusting it**. The property gained more failure modes than it gave up:

| Situation | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Copy-through with no declaration | FAIL | FAIL |
| Declared citation whose named source **does** carry the value, in both runs | FAIL | PASS |
| Declared citation whose named source does **not** carry the value | *(inexpressible)* | **FAIL** |
| Declaration naming an output that reproduces nothing | *(inexpressible)* | **FAIL** |

**Proved by fault injection before the pass was accepted, four probes against the built artefact:** clean → PASS; declaration removed while the echo remains → `UNDECLARED COPY-THROUGH`; source repointed to `describedBy` → `FALSE CITATION`; a holding declaration on a non-reproduced leaf → `STALE CITATION`. **The third probe did not isolate what it was meant to** — moving the `outputPath` both orphaned the real echo and broke the declaration, so it tripped the other two branches and the stale branch never ran. A fourth probe was built to isolate it. Recorded because a probe that fires the *wrong* branch reports as a successful proof, which is the vacuous-green class (D-015) wearing fault-injection clothing.

**The declaration is verified in BOTH runs deliberately.** A wrong source path that coincidentally matches one run will not match the other, so the check is provenance rather than coincidence — the same reasoning that restricted C-5 to distinctive strings.

**What this does not close.** `verify-functional-completeness` remains red on C-3 and C-4, so under D-009 these four proofs **cannot be recorded machine-readably** — a failing gate cannot establish the clean leg of a *clean → faulted → clean* triple. They are reproducible from `governance/capability/` and are recorded here in the meantime, which is weaker than a recorded proof and is stated as such.

---

**D-012 instance CLOSED — `runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:117`, and nothing was looking for it.**

```js
const certified = canonical.executiveReporting.certificationSummary
  .some((e) => e.label === 'verdict' && /^certified/i.test(e.value));
```

**An entry point published `certified` because its INPUT said so.** This is D-012's sentence — *a system reporting a certification status it did not produce* — not as a latent property of a domain but as the value a caller actually receives from `RuntimeExecutionOutcome.certification.certified`. It now reads `certificationVerdict.verdict`.

**It surfaced only as a side effect of the relabel.** Renaming the summary entry to `priorVerdict` would have left this predicate as a `.some()` that can never match — a silent, permanent `certified: false` — so the rename forced the read. Nothing in the platform was looking for it: 1,410 tests and 69 gates passed over it throughout, the copy-through harness measures the canonical *result* and not what the bridge derives from it, and D-012's original entry recorded the echo at the domain without tracing who consumed it. **The lesson is narrow and worth keeping: the echo was recorded as a fact about a field, and the damage was in a reader of that field two modules away.** A defect entry that names a value should name its consumers, or it under-reports its own severity.

---

**Two ABSENCES found while measuring step (c)'s blast radius. Neither is a defect in something that ran; both are places where nothing runs.**

**(i) No test asserts any ExecutionPackage gate expression.** `canonical-authoring-composer.ts:62` builds the EP's gates as `entry.label + '==' + entry.value` over `certificationSummary`, so relabelling changed every canonical run's package content from `verdict==NOT CERTIFIED` to `priorVerdict==NOT CERTIFIED`. Nothing failed, because nothing asserts what the composer emits. The gates are carried to the Execution Plane and evaluated there (R-20.7), so this is IP-authored content whose correctness is checked in neither plane — the IP does not test it and the EP is not permitted to question it.

**(ii) `verify-customer-readiness.js:157` cannot fail on the thing it appears to check.**

```js
manifest !== null && ev.packageContentHash !== null
  && manifest.contentHash === ev.packageContentHash
```

It reads as an integrity check binding a manifest to a package hash. Both operands are fields of the **same emitted evidence artefact**, written by the same generator in the same run, so they agree by construction and the comparison can only fail if the generator contradicts itself between two adjacent lines. It recomputes nothing from source. **A green here means "the file is internally consistent", and is read as "the package is what the evidence says it is".** This is the vacuous-green class (D-015) in a *gate* rather than a scenario property — the third instance, and the most consequential, because it is registered in `run-all.js` and counted among the 62 passing. Its repair is to recompose the package from source and compare, which is the only version of this check that can fail.

**Both are the same shape as D-011's wider limit: the suite verifies what is present, and neither of these is absent — they are unexamined.**

---

**D-012 update (2026-08-04) — the half with teeth was closed first; the half C-5 measured did not close until declared citation landed. Retained for the reasoning, which stands.**

*Step (b)* gave `executive-reporting` its own verdict, computed over the run-state record: `NOT CERTIFIED` on variant `'A'` (basis 12 assessed / 1 negative) and `CERTIFIED` on the coverage-complete variant `'C'` (12 / 0). *Step (c)* relabelled the echoed field from `verdict` to `priorVerdict`, so the computed finding and the cited prior no longer share a name.

**What closed, and it is the part that had consequence.** `runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:117` read `certificationSummary.some((e) => e.label === 'verdict' && /^certified/i.test(e.value))` — so the outcome this entry point published to its caller said *certified* **because its input said so**, never because the engine assessed anything. That was the echo's only load-bearing consumer, and it is the D-012 sentence *"reports a certification verdict it did not compute"* in its literal, shipping form. It now reads `certificationVerdict.verdict`. Found only because the relabel would otherwise have turned a live predicate into a `.some()` that can never match — **the silent break was the thing that made the real defect visible**, which is an argument for changing a name rather than adding one.

**What did not close, MEASURED and not assumed, as instructed.** C-5 still fails, on the same leaf:

```
C-5 FAIL — COPY-THROUGH: executiveReporting.certificationSummary[0].value
  A="NOT CERTIFIED"  B="CERTIFIED"     -> ECHOED
  certificationSummary[0].label        -> A="priorVerdict"  B="priorVerdict"  INVARIANT
  certificationVerdict.verdict         -> A/B identical                       INVARIANT (computed)
```

The expectation was that relabelling would clear it. **It cannot, by construction.** C-5 keys on a leaf *path* and its *value*; the label is a different leaf (`[0].label`), and no change to it can move a value-flow predicate applied to `[0].value`. The prediction was wrong in a way that measuring caught immediately and reasoning would not have.

**The residue is not a defect, and C-5 cannot tell.** A citation preserves its source verbatim — that is what citing *means* — so a value-flow detector flags every correct citation exactly as loudly as an undeclared echo. This is the same shape as the limit C-5 already declares in its own source for numbers and booleans: value matching cannot recover provenance. **Two facts should not be blurred:** the value *is* copied (C-5 is not misfiring), and the copy is now correct (the defect is gone). C-5 measures the first and is silent on the second.

**Therefore D-012 does not close on C-5 evidence, and no adjustment was made to it.** Three ways forward, needing a ruling rather than an author's choice, because each changes what a governance gate *means*:

- **(a) Declared citation.** The runtime declares which output paths are citations and which input path each cites; C-5 exempts exactly those, and gains an obligation that can fail — an exempted path whose declared source does not actually carry that value fails, and any *undeclared* copy-through still fails. This strengthens the predicate rather than weakening it, and it has precedent in this repository: `verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js` allowlists per file **and** host with a required rationale. Cost: an exemption list is a place to hide things, so the exempted set must be reported in the gate's own detail.
- **(b) Drop the citation.** `certificationSummary` stops carrying the prior verdict; C-5 clears with no exemption and no new concept. Rejected here only because the accepted scope for step (c) explicitly retains it as `priorVerdict`.
- **(c) Leave C-5 red.** Honest, and free. The cost is that a gate everyone knows to ignore stops being a gate, and D-009 already records that a red gate cannot carry a fault proof.

**Not available: transforming the value so the string comparison misses.** Appending provenance text to `"NOT CERTIFIED"` would clear C-5 and destroy the citation, which is defeating the instrument rather than satisfying it.

**Two adjacent facts, recorded because they are absences rather than results.** (i) `canonical-authoring-composer.ts:62` builds the ExecutionPackage's gate expressions as `label + '==' + value`, so the EP now receives `priorVerdict==NOT CERTIFIED` where it received `verdict==NOT CERTIFIED`. **No test asserts any gate expression**, and no gate recomputes the package content hash from source — `verify-customer-readiness.js:157` compares `manifest.contentHash` against `ev.packageContentHash`, both read from the *same emitted artefact*, so it is a self-consistency check and cannot detect a content change. The composer change moved nothing **because nothing looks**, which is not the same as nothing changed. (ii) `measure-copy-through.mjs` was labelling every input-sensitive leaf `copy-through` on the strength of `A !== B` alone — it reported `certificationVerdict.reasons[0].reason` as copy-through when that string is constructed by `test-management-intelligence` and appears in no input. **The instrument built to catch manufactured findings was manufacturing them.** It now separates `ECHOED` (differs *and* each value is a supplied leaf — the only defect), `DERIVED` (differs, no supplied leaf — correct computation) and `INVARIANT`, matching C-5's real predicate.

| **D-015** | **THE CANONICAL DOMAINS DECLARE 115 CERTIFICATION CRITERIA AND EVIDENCE NONE OF THEM — and the property that says so was itself passing vacuously.** Measured 2026-08-04 by counting `certificationCriteria` across the thirteen bound `DomainContract`s (**109 declared**) and across the thirteen results of a canonical run (**0 carried**). **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-05 AFTER ADR-0075: the composition is fourteen domains and the figure is 115** — `observation-interpretation` adds six (`deterministic`, `immutable-output`, `capability-neutral`, `no-repository-access`, `no-execution`, `no-ratio`). **The entry's finding is unchanged and its denominator is not: the number this entry is closed against is 115, and `CRITERIA_DESIGN_REPORT.md` carries the per-group breakdown the closure work will use.** The `109` above is preserved as what was measured on the date it was measured, not corrected away — it was right about a thirteen-domain composition that no longer exists. Every domain states, in its contract, what certifying it would require — `deterministic-reporting`, `evidence-references-only`, `canonical-values-preserved`, `no-external-publication`, and 105 more — and **nothing anywhere in the canonical runtime compares any of them against what the domain did.** A criterion is declared in one place and satisfied nowhere, and no mechanism exists that could notice. | **This is D-007's axis — declaration versus implementation — in the runtime's certification surface itself, and it is the third named instance of the same seam after healing's plane/stage drift and the verdict echo.** Its specific harm is that the criteria are *read as coverage*: a contract listing ten certification criteria is taken by a reviewer, a certification report, and the next author as evidence that ten things are checked. Zero are. Worse, this sits directly beneath the FT-037 verdict: step (b) made `executive-reporting` compute `CERTIFIED`/`NOT CERTIFIED` over the domains' self-reported outcomes, and those outcomes are `certified: true` literals (D-012) assessed against criteria nothing evaluates. **A verdict computed correctly over inputs that are decoration is still decoration** — the arithmetic is now honest and what it aggregates is not. | Functional Testing capability — canonical runtime · Platform governance (the D-007 class) | **Not repaired here, and deliberately not repaired inside C-3.** Making 109 criteria evidenced is a capability — a per-criterion evaluator, its evidence, and the contract change to carry it — not a property adjustment, and building it under a gate's C-numbering would bury a capability inside a scenario. **Sequencing: Section F, alongside the agent layer, not Section C — RULED 2026-08-04 on the ownership argument.** The reason is ownership, not size: agents carry criteria *contributions* (an agent that checks determinism is what makes `deterministic-reporting` evidenceable), so building the evaluation mechanism before the agents exist would produce a second criteria model that the agent layer then has to reconcile with — the duplication CHARTER §4 forbids, and the same mistake as computing a verdict before anything could contribute a negative to it. Section C is publication rework and shares no surface with this. **Until then C-3 stays red and states the true ratio**; it must not be relaxed to `criteriaDeclared > 0`, which is where it started. |

| **D-016** | **FOUR OF THIRTEEN CANONICAL DOMAINS HAVE NO STATE OF THEIR OWN TO FAIL ON — they are 1:1 projections of their input, and a projection cannot have a finding.** Established 2026-08-04 by designing a failure condition for each of the eight domains that lacked one, tracing each before writing it, and discarding the ones that could not fire. **Reachable and implemented (4):** `tenant-resolution` (the running capability is not among the tenant's enabled capabilities — it held both halves of an entitlement check and performed neither), `story-intelligence` (no acceptance criterion survived normalisation), `repository-intelligence` (a reuse candidate names an asset the repository lacks), `automation-intelligence` (two or more reuse assets available, all candidates assigned the first — reuse selected by position, not matching). **Unreachable and REPORTED, not adjusted (4):** `application-strategy-resolution` re-decides domain 1's strategy from the same candidate list with the same deterministic engine, so divergence is impossible **in this composition** — every other field is a `.map`, a Decision Engine result, or a literal (`executionConstraints: []`; ten of thirteen knowledge-model slots literal `[]`); `test-design-intelligence` builds two scenarios per acceptance criterion by `forEach`, so criterion coverage is 2:1 by construction; `automation-architecture` derives components, modules and graph edges 1:1 from the plan; `executive-reporting` emits one evidence reference per executed component and already **throws** on an incomplete run-state record. | **A domain that cannot fail contributes nothing to a verdict, and there are four of them.** Section B step (b) made `executive-reporting` compute `CERTIFIED`/`NOT CERTIFIED` over what the upstream domains reported about themselves; four of the twelve assessed can only ever report success, so the verdict's basis is narrower than `domainsAssessed: 12` suggests. This is not the same as D-012's *unconditional* `certified: true` — these four genuinely have nothing to assess — but the effect on the verdict is identical, and the honest reading of a `CERTIFIED` result is *"nine domains found nothing and four could not look."* **Two further instances fell out of the tracing, recorded where they belong rather than papered over:** the `?? requirementId + ':ac:' + index` fallback at `test-design-intelligence` is dead, because `story-intelligence` builds `traceabilityReferences` by mapping the same array — **a new D-013 instance** alongside `?? 'unknown'` and `?? testCase.id`; and `automation-architecture` **declares `testManagement` on its input and reads it nowhere**, the only unconsumed input in the sequence — **D-007's axis**, a declaration that has stopped meaning anything. | Functional Testing capability — canonical domains | **Do not manufacture conditions for the four.** A branch that cannot be taken shipped as coverage is the exact defect D-013 records, and three of the four discarded conditions were plausible enough to have been written. The reasoning for each rejection is recorded **in the code at the failure-decision site**, not only here, so the next author meets it before re-reaching for the same condition. **The real repair is upstream of all four:** a domain that only projects its input has no findings to contribute because it does no assessment, and giving it one means giving it work — which is the criteria-evaluation capability D-015 sequences to Section F. Until then, `basis.domainsAssessed` should be read with this entry beside it. Every implemented condition is proved BOTH ways in `canonical-failure-conditions.test.ts` — fires on its named input, silent on the reference input — because one direction alone would prove nothing. |

| **D-017** | **THE DOMAIN WHOSE JOB IS RESOLVING THE TENANT HELD BOTH HALVES OF AN ENTITLEMENT CHECK AND PERFORMED NEITHER.** `tenant-resolution` receives `enabledCapabilities` on its input and copies it to its result (`domains/tenant-resolution.ts`, `enabledCapabilities: [...input.enabledCapabilities]`); `ctx.capability.capabilityId` names the capability actually executing and is already in scope at the same statement — it is read three lines later to build the Platform Event. **The two were never compared.** Nothing else in the sequence compares them either: searched across all thirteen domains, `enabledCapabilities` is written once, read once for the copy, and never tested. A run therefore resolves connectors, organises a suite, executes, raises defects and publishes to the customer's tool **without anything having established that the tenant is entitled to the capability doing it**. Found 2026-08-04 while designing Pass 2 failure conditions — by looking for something this domain could legitimately fail on, not by a gate. | **This is an authorisation gap in the runtime that SURVIVES retirement**, which is what separates it from a defect in the agent-catalogue path. It is adjacent to **C-07.11** (*tenant scope cannot be asserted by a caller-supplied field*) without being the same criterion, and the distinction matters: C-07.11 governs where tenant scope COMES FROM — the authenticated principal, never a supplied field — and is enforced at the boundary. This is the next question, unasked: **given a correctly-derived tenant, is that tenant entitled to this capability?** [ADR-0069](../docs/adr/ADR-0069-capability-one-connector-realisation.md) FT-001 states the requirement plainly — *"the run would have no tenant scope, no capability entitlement and no evidence profile"* — so entitlement is a declared responsibility of this domain, and the implementation resolves the tenant and skips the entitlement. **D-007's axis: a declaration that was true of the design and never became true of the code.** The blast radius is the whole sequence, because every later domain treats domain 1's result as settled. | Functional Testing capability — canonical runtime · Platform security | **Reported by the runtime as of Pass 2, and that is deliberately NOT the same as enforced.** `tenant-resolution` now returns `certified: false` with category `entitlement` when the running capability is absent from the resolved list, so the condition reaches the certification verdict instead of being invisible — proved both ways in `canonical-failure-conditions.test.ts` (fires on `no-entitlement`, silent on the reference input). **The run still proceeds**, because refusing mid-sequence is a lifecycle decision belonging to the governance triad (R-12.2), not to domain 1, and wiring a refusal into a domain would put an enforcement point where no gate expects one. **What is still owed:** (a) an architectural ruling on where capability entitlement is ENFORCED — the triad, the entry point, or the boundary alongside C-07.11 — since a security control that only reports is not a control; (b) a criterion in document 07 or 08 that this can be traced to, because at present it is enforced by one runtime branch and no rule, which is the inverse of CHARTER §6; (c) an EP-side counterpart, since the Execution Plane accepts a package and executes it without re-deriving entitlement either. Until (a) is ruled, **do not upgrade the branch to a refusal** — an unratified enforcement point is harder to remove than a missing one. |

| **D-018** | **A REPAIRED INSTRUMENT RETURNED THE SAME WRONG VALUE FOR AN UNRELATED REASON — AND WOULD HAVE READ AS MORE CREDIBLE THAN THE FIRST.** `retirement-inventory.mjs` reported `orphanCount: 0` because a surviving entry point imported the package barrel, so the surviving closure was all 52 modules and no orphan could exist (D-011). That cause was found, fixed, and the closure fell to 41. **It still reported 0**, now because all three retirement targets were themselves inside the surviving closure — `authoring-bridge.mjs` imports `FunctionalTestingOrchestrator`, `buildCatalogue` and `createFunctionalTestingEngine`, and `registry/reasoning-publication.ts:36` imports the `EngineState` type — and `RETIRING \ SURVIVING` cannot find what is reachable only through the retiring set while `SURVIVING ⊇ RETIRING`. Two unrelated causes, one value, and the second arrived **wearing the credibility of the first repair**. Cut at the retirement targets, the same tool reports **10 orphans**. | **The danger is not the wrong number; it is that the second wrong number is more persuasive than the first.** The first zero carried a `VALIDITY: DEGENERATE` banner the tool printed about itself. The second carried `VALIDITY: measured`, a demonstrably repaired mechanism, and a closure that had visibly moved — every signal a reader uses to decide whether to trust a measurement pointed the wrong way. Had the tool been believed at that point, the conclusion *"retirement orphans nothing"* would have been identical to the discredited one, differently wrong, and backed by evidence of repair. **A set difference returns an arithmetically correct answer to whatever question its inputs encode, and says nothing about whether that was the question.** | Platform governance — the class | **This is the same law as the `authoring-bridge.mjs` misclassification (D-011, D-007 instance vi), reached independently by a different instrument, and the pairing is the evidence CHARTER R-13.7 clause 2 rests on.** There, one entry point in the wrong set produced *"the canonical runtime cannot seal an ExecutionPackage"* — arithmetically correct, premise false. Here, an unexamined set relation produced *"nothing is orphaned"* — arithmetically correct, premise false. Neither was a computation error and neither could be caught by checking the arithmetic. The third instance is the C-5 fault probe that fired `UNDECLARED` and `FALSE CITATION` while claiming to prove `STALE`. **What generalises: a measurement's inputs encode a question, and nothing in the result reports which question was asked.** The available control is to disbelieve a result that would be convenient — the zero was disbelieved twice, which is the only reason the second cause was found — and, where the result is structural, to state the set relation being assumed and check it. `retirement-inventory.mjs` now publishes `repointObligationCount` beside `orphanCount` for exactly that reason: the second is meaningless while the first is non-zero, and publishing them apart would let a reader repeat the error. **A fourth instance arrived within one turn of this entry being written, which is the strongest evidence available that it is a default rather than an incident** — see below. |

**D-018 instance (iv) — the class recurred while the entry describing it was still being written, and the instrument was a probe I had authored to answer the question it then answered wrongly.** Investigating whether the canonical runtime has a home for governance-triad agents, a grep for the runner's stage callbacks used the pattern `^\s*[a-zA-Z-]*:\s*(_?ctx, emit)`, which cannot match a **quoted** key. The triad's three callbacks are written `'architecture-review': (_ctx, emit) => {`, so the grep returned eight of twelve stages. That eight-item list was then **hardcoded into a probe** as `RUNNER_CALLBACKS`, and the probe faithfully reported `NO CALLBACK FOR: architecture-review, policy-review, guardrail-review, execution-planning` — a confident, specific, machine-produced finding, derived entirely from the failed grep. The conclusion drawn from it, *"the canonical runtime has no home for stage 4–6 agents"*, was **wrong**: all twelve stages are implemented, at `canonical-runner-capability.ts:141–168`. Caught by reading the source region before writing the finding down, which is the only control that was ever going to catch it. **The measurement encoded a question the grep had already answered wrongly, and reported the answer with the authority of a measurement** — the same law as the barrel closure and the set relation, now with a regex as the faulty input. The true finding was worse than the false one and is recorded as **D-019**: the triad stages are all implemented, and none of them can decline.

| **D-024** | **A DECLARATION THAT WAS UNIMPLEMENTABLE WHEN WRITTEN, IS NOW IMPLEMENTABLE, AND IS STILL UNWIRED.** `dev-change-engine/src/agents/sync-and-reporting.ts` declares `failureHandling: 'A defect that cannot be published yields published:false with a reason; a real bug is never silently unfiled.'` The code beneath it returns `published: true` **unconditionally**. This is not the ordinary declaration-drift of D-007, where a statement stopped being true: **the statement was never implementable.** Until ADR-0072 the publication SPI returned `{ defectId: string }` with no failure arm, so an agent that wanted to yield `published: false` had nothing to derive it from. The author declared the correct behaviour and the platform could not express it. ADR-0072 makes it expressible; the wiring is a behaviour change owned by the Dev-Change capability and is recorded in that ADR's §4 work list, not performed here. | **THE FOURTH INSTANCE OF THE TRUTHFUL-NEGATIVE PATTERN, and the one that shows the pattern has a specific cost beyond silence: it produces DECLARATIONS THAT LOOK LIKE COMMITMENTS AND ARE NOT.** A reviewer reading `failureHandling` sees a stated guarantee about a real bug never being silently unfiled. There is no such guarantee, and there could not have been. The other three instances hid a missing capability; this one advertises the capability that is missing. **A reader is worse off than if the field were blank** — the same harm as D-013's three-state `publicationStatus` and D-012's echoed verdict, arriving through prose rather than through a type. **It surfaced exactly where the contagion corollary predicts** — immediately downstream of the layer just made honest, found by reading what sat on top of the repaired SPI rather than by looking for defects in Dev-Change. That makes it the corollary's first confirmation rather than an illustration of it. | Dev-Change capability (the wiring) · Platform governance (the class) | **Not wired here, deliberately.** The decision — what a Dev-Change run should do when a defect cannot be filed — belongs to the capability that owns it, and a wrong negative-path decision stays invisible until something downstream disagrees, which for a capability worked on elsewhere may be far off. **Recorded so the wiring is a decision someone takes rather than a discovery someone makes.** The generalisable question is larger than one agent: **every `failureHandling` declaration in the platform was written against an SPI that could not fail**, so the same audit applies to all of them. **ATTRIBUTED TO SECTION F, ruled 2026-08-04**: it is the same shape as D-015's 109 declared-but-unevidenced certification criteria and D-019's twenty triad agents that declare a stage nothing runs them at — all three are declaration-versus-behaviour work, and F is where that lands. The audit asks one question of each declaration: *could the platform have honoured this when it was written, and can it now?* A declaration that was never implementable is a different defect from one that stopped being true, and this register has instances of both. **AUDIT PERFORMED 2026-08-05 — `SECTION_F2_FAILUREHANDLING_AUDIT.md`. D-024 IS AMENDED, NOT CLOSED, AND ITS OWN INSTANCE IS STILL LIVE AND STILL CORRECTLY UNWIRED.** Measured over **624 production declarations**: 49 reach an SPI operation in their handle; **19 are honoured, 7 are keepable and not kept, 22 are still unimplementable, 2 were resolved by reading, and 575 call no SPI at all.** **THE HYPOTHESIS HELD AND ITS SCOPE DID NOT.** *"Every declaration was written against an SPI that could not fail"* is right about the class and wrong as a census — the correct statement is **29 of the 49 that reach one**, and the unbounded phrasing hid three things the count surfaced: the 22 cluster on **four SPIs ADR-0072 never touched (D-057)**; a fourth answer to this entry's own question exists — **a declaration about an operation the agent does not perform (D-058)**; and **575 declarations are outside this instrument entirely** and can still be unimplementable through their OWN return type, which is D-043's class and a second audit with a different instrument. **`repository.reuse-analysis` is the worked example of that remainder**: no `toolContracts`, no SPI call, and *"reported as unknown rather than zero"* over a type that had no `unknown` until D-045's repair added the `undecidable` count. **THREE DECLARATIONS BECAME KEEPABLE UNDER ADR-0074 AND NOT ONE WORD OF TWO OF THEM CHANGED** — `sync.design-discovery` and `repository.search.*` were correct sentences a type could not carry; `automation.search.*` was **rewritten**, because its sentence described the defect rather than merely outrunning the type. **A declaration can be unkeepable and a declaration can be wrong, and only one of those is fixed by widening a type.** |

| **D-023** | **THE PUBLICATION SPI CANNOT REPORT A FAILED PUBLICATION — the tool boundary can say *published* and nothing else.** Traced from `capability-framework/src/adapters.ts`: `linkTraceability → { linked: true }`, `publishResult → { published: true }`, `publishEvidenceReference → { published: true }`, `publishDefect → { defectId: string }`. **Four of the five publication operations return a type-level literal `true`** — an adapter cannot report failure because `published: false` does not type-check. The fifth, `publishTests → readonly string[]`, can express partial success only by returning a short list, and the caller reads it through `externalRef: publishedTestIds[index] ?? testCase.id`, whose `??` makes a missing acknowledgement indistinguishable from a present one (D-013). The five hardcoded `status: 'published'` sites in `synchronisation.ts` are therefore **the only value the SPI permits a caller to derive**, not an oversight. | **NO LAYER BETWEEN THE CUSTOMER'S TOOL AND THE EXECUTIVE REPORT CAN REPRESENT A FAILED PUBLICATION INTO THAT CUSTOMER'S SYSTEM OF RECORD.** The exposure is platform-wide, not Functional-Testing-local: the adapter contract is framework, and every capability that publishes implements it. It is masked today only because the reference adapters always succeed — **which is also what makes the repair free exactly once.** After a real ADO connector lands, the same repair must be made while the platform is producing wrong data in a customer's system of record, and the wrongness is of the worst kind: a report asserting successful publication of results that were never accepted. This is the gating fidelity work P-69.2 blocks retirement on, so the window is now. | **Platform — `@dbiz/capability-framework`** (the adapter contract) · every publishing capability | **ADR-0072, ruled 2026-08-04: the SPI change lands BEFORE Section C's publication semantics.** Ruling (b) of the Section C rulings — *`PublishedObject.status` reflects the connector's actual response* — is unimplementable as given until it does. **Raised as its own entry rather than folded into D-013**, which is a Functional Testing entry about three unreachable fields; this is a platform contract that cannot express a category of truth. D-013(i)'s `'partial'`/`'failed'` branches remain unreachable until this lands, and Section C would otherwise have closed with the domain whose subject is publication still unable to describe a failed one. |

| **D-039** | **THIS REGISTER'S OWN DEFECTS ARE NEARLY REPRODUCED BY THEIR FIXES — twice this section, caught in DESIGN both times.** *(i)* **ADR-0072's negative-path test.** R-13.7 requires a property be shown to fail; no adapter in the tree refuses; so the only writable test constructs a failing adapter and asserts it returns what it was told. **That is C-3's tautology (D-015) in a new file** — written to satisfy the rule against unfalsifiable properties, and unfalsifiable. Caught before writing; the test is sequenced to its first real consumer. *(ii)* **The 109-criteria mechanism.** Part 1 — *an agent declares what it evidences* — would have produced **144 agents declaring `certificationContributions: []` and a mechanism with nothing flowing through it**: **D-033's shape (a field nobody reads) inside the mechanism built to close D-015 (declarations nothing evidences)**, and precisely the outcome part 3 was added to prevent. Caught by reading all 109 before building. | **A FIX INHERITS ITS DEFECT'S SHAPE BECAUSE IT IS BUILT IN THE SAME VOCABULARY.** D-015 is *declarations nothing evidences*, so its fix is a declaration — and a declaration with no evidencer is D-015 again. R-13.7 is *properties that cannot fail*, so its evidence is a test — and a test with nothing to observe cannot fail. **The remedy is expressed in the same terms as the disease, which is why it reproduces it: the author reaches for the mechanism the defect made salient.** That both instances were caught in design rather than in review is the useful part — **each was found by asking what would actually flow through the new mechanism**, which is the same question as *does this value match what its producer knows*, asked of a thing not yet built. | Programme process | **NOT A REASON TO DISTRUST THE FIXES, and this is the entry's point.** Both were caught, both cheaply, both before code. **What it argues for is a specific check at design time, not more caution**: *if this mechanism ships, what flows through it on the first real run — and if the answer is "nothing", the mechanism is a declaration about a declaration.* Both instances answer "nothing" and both were stopped there. **The register's own contents are the best available source of that question**, which is an argument for reading it before designing a fix rather than after — the same order that found D-038 and the criterion-namespace collision. |

| **D-038** | **NONE OF THE 109 CERTIFICATION CRITERIA IS THE KIND OF THING AN AGENT CAN EVIDENCE BY DOING ITS WORK.** Measured 2026-08-04, before building the criteria mechanism, by reading all 109 rather than sampling. They fall into three groups and **not one of them is "an agent performed work that satisfies this"**: *(i)* **STRUCTURAL — ~51.** `deterministic` / `deterministic-policy` / `deterministic-publication` (13, one per domain), `immutable-output` (13), `capability-neutral` (13), `redefines-nothing` (6), `evidence-references-only` (5), `canonical-values-preserved` (1). These are properties of the domain's own function — of its return value or its determinism — and are true or false of the DOMAIN, never of an agent inside it. *(ii)* **NEGATIVE — ~28.** `no-execution`, `no-automation`, `no-repository-access`, `no-repository-modification`, `no-alm-synchronisation`, `no-healing`, `no-defect-creation`, `no-synchronisation`, `no-report-generation`, `no-external-publication`, `no-automation-generation`, `no-runtime-framework-invocation`, `no-application-brand`. **An absence cannot be evidenced by acting.** An agent that ran and did not execute has evidenced nothing; the absence is a property of what the whole domain did NOT do, observable only by watching the domain. *(iii)* **CONSUMPTION — ~30.** `decision-engine-consumed`, `connector-spi-consumed`, `execution-result-consumed`, and nine more. These are facts about what reached the domain, established at its boundary, not by work inside it. | **PART 1 OF THE DESIGN — *an agent declares what it evidences* — HAS ALMOST NOTHING TO ATTACH TO.** The design assumed criteria are things agents DO. Read end to end, **they are things the domain IS, things nobody did, and things that arrived.** The instruction anticipated the failure mode from the other side — *"a domain where every criterion is structural is a signal the reading went shallow"* — and the reading says thirteen domains out of thirteen are close to exactly that, which is why it is reported rather than written up as thirteen shallow classifications. **The negative group is the sharpest.** `no-execution` cannot be evidenced by an agent under any design: the only proof is that no execution call was made, which is a statement about the domain's whole run and is exactly what a governance gate — not an agent — is positioned to make. **Building part 1 as designed would have produced 144 agents declaring `certificationContributions: []` and a mechanism with nothing flowing through it** — D-033's shape inside the mechanism built to close D-015, which is the outcome part 3 was specifically added to prevent. | Functional Testing capability — canonical runtime | **REPORTED BEFORE BUILDING. Parts 1, 2 and 3 are NOT built, and part 3 must not be softened to accommodate this — the finding is that parts 1 and 2 target the wrong producer.** **What the measurement suggests, and it is a design question rather than a repair:** the three groups have three different evidencers. **Structural** criteria are evidenced by the domain about itself — a domain can assert its own output is frozen. **Consumption** criteria are evidenced at the composition boundary, where what was passed in is known. **Negative** criteria are evidenced by observation of a run — which is what `run-functional-completeness`'s C-properties already do for a handful of them, by scanning. **If that split is right, `certificationContributions` on an agent is the wrong mechanism for all three**, and the field's scheduled transition to REQUIRED should not be taken until it is settled — a required field with no legitimate content is worse than an optional one. |

| **D-040** | **AN-3 — the agent-naming gate's uniqueness rule — CANNOT FAIL, found by its own R-13.7 probe on the day it was registered.** The probe injected an exact duplicate of `ALL_AGENTS[0]` into the built catalogue, the source of truth the gate reads. The gate exited non-zero, and **AN-3's branch never executed**: `AgentCatalogue.register` (`capability-framework/src/agent.ts:327`) refuses a duplicate id at registration, so the run died with `AgentCatalogueError: story.retrieval: already registered` before the gate read anything. Even had registration allowed it, `catalogue.all` returns a `Map`'s values keyed by id — two agents sharing an id could not both survive to be counted. **The property is real, is enforced, and is enforced twice, neither time by this gate.** AN-1 and AN-2 were proved falsifiable in the same run, each firing its own branch with the other two still green. | **THE SECOND TIME THIS SESSION R-13.7 CLAUSE 2 HAS CAUGHT A RED GATE WHOSE BRANCH UNDER TEST DID NOT RUN** — the first was `assert.throws` catching `StageError` in the refusal-reason test. **Both would have been recorded as proofs under clause 1 alone**, which is the argument for clause 2 restated as evidence rather than as reasoning. The finding here is a *vacuously green* rule in the D-015/D-016 class, but with the opposite cause: D-015's declarations had no evidencer, and AN-3's property has two evidencers stronger than itself. **A gate reporting a property that is guaranteed by construction contributes no detection and reads as though it does.** | Platform governance — `governance/verification/verify-agent-naming.js` | **AN-3 IS NEITHER DELETED NOR WEAKENED, and the probe's finding is printed in the gate's own output and header.** Deleting it would erase the record of why it is redundant, and the next author would add it back. **Weakening it to something falsifiable would be the D-009 pressure hazard (D-041) acted on** — adjusting a rule to make its proof convenient. The honest statement is that the platform's uniqueness guarantee is *stronger* than a gate: it is a type-level and structural impossibility, not a check that runs after the fact. **CATEGORY — DISTINCT FROM D-013, recorded before the 135 migration so the two are not merged by the next reader.** D-013 is *a declared state nothing produces*: `publicationStatus`'s `'partial'` and `'failed'` were reachable in the type and unreachable in fact, because no producer beneath them could emit the value. **AN-3 is the opposite direction — a check whose SUBJECT cannot reach it.** The value it looks for is not merely unproduced; it is destroyed twice before the gate reads, by `register`'s refusal and by the `Map`'s keying, both of which are guarantees rather than absences. **The remedies differ accordingly.** D-013's is *give the producer the ability to emit the state* — ADR-0072 did exactly that, and the unreachable branches became reachable. **There is no equivalent move here, and making one would be a regression**: the only way to let AN-3's branch execute is to remove one of the two constructions that prevent it. **A declared state nothing produces is a gap to close; a check whose subject cannot reach it is a redundancy to label.** Confusing them costs in one direction only — treating AN-3 as D-013 argues for weakening `register`. |

| **D-041** | **A RED GATE IS EXPENSIVE TO REGISTER (D-009), WHICH CREATES STANDING PRESSURE TO WEAKEN GATES SO THEY CAN BE REGISTERED. This is a hazard, not an incident — it has no instance yet and will recur every time a new gate lands red.** D-009's circularity: a red gate cannot record a fault proof, because the clean leg of clean→faulted→clean cannot be established while the gate is red. **The consequence is directional.** A gate that lands green registers, proves and closes; a gate that lands red registers, escalates and carries an unclosable proof obligation. The cheapest way to make a red gate registrable is not to fix the code it indicts — **it is to soften the rule until the code passes.** This session reached that fork twice and took the other branch both times: the nine `design-sync.*` agents were renamed rather than the rule amended to admit compound prefixes, and AN-3 (D-040) was left unfalsifiable-and-labelled rather than rewritten into something that could go red. | **IT IS THE FOURTH INSTANCE OF D-009'S CIRCULARITY AND THE FIRST WHERE THE TEMPTING RESOLUTION WAS TO ADJUST THE RULE RATHER THAN THE CODE.** The three prior instances were about what could be *recorded*; this one is about what gets *written*. **The pressure acts before the gate exists, on its author, while the rule is still cheap to change** — which is exactly when weakening it is invisible, because there is no history of the stronger rule to diff against. A rule relaxed at authoring time leaves no evidence it was ever stricter. **The shape was refused four times this session** — admitting a compound prefix, exempting the declared citation, softening part 3 of the criteria design, and adding a fourth here — and it presented each time as a reasonable accommodation of the specific cases that violated it. | Programme process · every future gate author | **NAMED SO IT IS RECOGNISED AT THE FORK RATHER THAN RECONSTRUCTED AFTERWARDS.** The question to ask when a new gate lands red: *am I changing the rule because the rule is wrong, or because the rule is inconvenient to register?* **The two are indistinguishable from inside the change** — both produce a gate that passes — and distinguishable only by whether the rule's original form was written down first. **Therefore: a gate's rules are recorded before it is first run, and any subsequent relaxation states which run prompted it.** D-009 itself is not closed by this and is not closeable here; the hazard exists because D-009 exists, and it stays open for as long as D-009 does. |

| **D-042** | **THE FIFTEEN SCENARIO-TECHNIQUE AGENTS DESTROY `Requirement.confidence`, AND THE SCENARIOS THEY PRODUCE ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL ACROSS IT — INCLUDING THE FINGERPRINT.** Measured 2026-08-04 in the batch-1 reading of the 135. `Requirement.confidence` is documented *"Present when reasoning proposed it and code accepted it."* `Scenario` has five members — `id`, `requirementId`, `technique`, `title`, `fingerprint` — and **carries no provenance of any kind**. A requirement at confidence 0.2 and the same requirement at confidence 1.0 yield scenarios differing only in the id derived from the requirement id; `fingerprint` is `sha(technique:norm(statement))` and is **equal across both**. **`test.ai-edge-case` is the only one of the fifteen that reads `confidence`** — `(r) => r.confidence < 1` — and it uses it as an applicability TRIGGER, never propagating it. | **`test.duplicate-removal` KEYS ON `technique:fingerprint`, SO IT WILL COLLAPSE A SCENARIO DERIVED FROM A MODEL-INFERRED REQUIREMENT INTO ONE DERIVED FROM A STATED ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, KEEPING WHICHEVER ARRIVED FIRST.** Which one survives is array order, and nothing downstream can tell which it got. **The dropped field is the only one in the model that says whether a human or a model
