asserted the requirement** — so the boundary destroys precisely the provenance that a reviewer of AI-derived test coverage would ask for first, and it destroys it silently. **The shape is `canonicalSpecOf`'s and the difference is the record**: D-036 states exactly what the canonical publication boundary does not carry and calls it a recorded capability reduction, so a reader knows. **Nothing records this one.** An undocumented information-destroying boundary is worse than a documented one by exactly the amount a reader would have to rediscover. | Functional Testing capability — the fifteen `test.*` technique agents · `Scenario` (model) | **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED — the sweep finding governs.** Adding a member to `Scenario` would be a model change inside the retiring closure's only consumer, and the canonical runtime builds its own scenarios in `test-design-intelligence` without these agents. **The question F3 must answer when it places these fifteen: does a canonical `Scenario` carry requirement provenance, and if not, is the reduction recorded the way D-036 records its own?** The narrow repair — add `confidence` to the fingerprint — would stop the collapse and is NOT recommended blind: it changes deduplication behaviour, and two scenarios that differ only in a confidence score may well be duplicates. **The finding is the silence, not the collapse.** |

| **D-043** | **`test.certification` RETURNS `certified: true` ON ZERO REQUIREMENTS, AND ITS OWN REASON STRING STATES THE NUMBER THAT REFUTES IT.** Measured 2026-08-04: `coverage-analysis({scenarios: [], requirements: []})` returns `{"covered":0,"total":0,"uncovered":[]}`, and `certification({uncovered: [], scenarioCount: 0})` returns **`{"certified":true,"reason":"0 scenario(s) covering every requirement"}`**. The assumption is that an empty `uncovered` means coverage is complete. **It is true only because `test.coverage-analysis` cannot report that it failed to compute coverage**: that agent's `failureHandling` declares *"Coverage that cannot be computed is reported as unknown, never as complete"*, and its return type `{ covered: number; total: number; uncovered: readonly string[] }` **has no `unknown`**. `uncovered: []` is the value for *everything is covered* and for *there was nothing to cover*, and the verdict reads the second as the first. | **THE SIXTH INSTANCE OF THE TRUTHFUL-NEGATIVE PATTERN AND THE FIRST INSIDE A VERDICT RATHER THAN AT A TOOL BOUNDARY.** The five prior instances — stage (D-019), domain (D-012), publication SPI (D-023), dev-change declaration (D-024), design-sync SPI (D-028) — all sit at a boundary where a negative arrives from outside and cannot be expressed. **This one is entirely internal**: nothing failed, no tool refused, no external system was consulted. The agent computed the number that contradicts its conclusion, **formatted it into the sentence it published**, and concluded anyway. That makes it the sharpest available statement of the class: *the value was present, in scope, and in the output — and was not consulted by the branch that decided.* | Functional Testing capability — `test.certification` (retiring closure) | **RECORDED SPECIFICALLY TO PREVENT REINTRODUCTION — THE CANONICAL SIDE ALREADY RESOLVES IT THE OTHER WAY.** `domains/test-design-intelligence.ts:266`: *"Emptiness reaches the verdict from story-intelligence, which can see its cause. This domain would only be able to report the symptom."* **The canonical runtime routes emptiness to the producer positioned to know why it is empty; the agent path concludes on it.** So this is not an open defect on the live path and must not be logged as one — **it is a defect in a unit awaiting placement, and the entry's whole purpose is that placement does not carry it across.** The pre-landing check states the test: *does this value match what its producer is positioned to know?* `test.certification` receives an empty list and knows nothing about why it is empty, which is exactly the condition under which it must not render a positive verdict. |

| **D-044** | **THREE CHECKS IN `authoring.quality-review` RECORD EVIDENCE THAT FLATLY ASSERTS THE PROPERTY THEIR OWN VERDICT DENIES — ON EVERY FAILURE.** Measured 2026-08-05 in the batch-3 per-file reading. The agent runs twelve checks over an authored test case, each recording `{ name, passed, detail }`. **Nine build `detail` as a ternary describing the state actually found. Three pass an unconditional string literal** — `authoring.ts:785` (`atomic-steps`), `:812` (`automation-readiness`), `:815` (`validation-layers`). Measured on a step with an empty target and no validation layer: `failed automation-readiness detail: "every step names a target an automation harness can resolve"`, and the same for the other two. **Separately, all three use a bare `Array.prototype.every` over `t.steps`, so a test case with ZERO steps passes all three** — and the two defects compound: a zero-step case passes with evidence asserting facts about steps that do not exist. | **DISTINCT FROM D-043, AND HARDER.** In D-043 the reason string *contained the number that refuted the verdict* — `"0 scenario(s) covering every requirement"` — so a reader who did the arithmetic could catch it, and the value was at least a true fact wrongly interpreted. **Here the evidence is not a misread fact; it is an assertion of the opposite of what was found**, requiring no arithmetic and offering no way to tell from the string which outcome occurred. **A-2, which is why it is not cosmetic: `authoring.quality-review` feeds `authoring.quality-score` and reaches the review board's snapshot through `AuthoringQuality`, where `governance.review.quality` reads it.** A `detail` string is exactly the kind of field that survives excerpting into a report while the boolean beside it does not — so the surviving artefact of a failed check can be a sentence stating the check's subject is satisfied. | Functional Testing capability — `authoring.quality-review` (retiring closure) | **NOT REPAIRED — the sweep finding governs: the file's only caller is the retiring closure.** **The diagnosis matters more than the defect, and it is a FIX rather than a REDESIGN.** Nine of twelve sites follow the correct pattern; three did not. There is no design flaw in `check(name, passed, detail)` — it is used correctly nine times in the same function by the same author. **Three sites that did not follow the pattern, inside one function**, which is the cheapest class of defect to carry across a migration and the easiest to reintroduce, because nothing in the type system or the tests objects: `check` accepts any string for any verdict, and no test asserts that a failing check's detail describes a failure. **What F3 must carry across is the ternary form, not the three exceptions.** The bar answer: the agent assumes a check's verdict and its evidence cannot disagree because the same expression produced both — **they were not produced by the same expression.** The verdict is computed; the detail is written; nothing relates them. |

| **D-045** | **AN UNREACHABLE CUSTOMER REPOSITORY AND AN EMPTY ONE ARE THE SAME VALUE, AND THE PLAN CERTIFIED FROM IT IS "CREATE EVERYTHING".** Measured 2026-08-05 in the batch-4 per-file reading; the whole chain is in `agents/repository-and-authoring.ts`. **(i)** The eight `repository.search.*` agents declare `plane: 'EP'` and `toolContracts: ['ProjectAdapter', 'TestManagementAdapter']` — **these are the agents that actually reach a customer tool** — and each declares `failureHandling: 'An unreachable repository yields no matches and is reported; it never yields "no duplicates exist".'` **The return type is `readonly RepositoryMatch[]`, so there is no "and is reported".** Measured: an unreachable tool, an empty index, and an index whose titles share no tokens over two characters (`similarity()` returns `0` when either token set is empty) are all `[]`. **(ii)** `repository.duplicate-detection` filters `[]` to `[]`. **(iii)** `repository.reuse-decision` hits `if (!best) return { kind: 'create', reason: 'no existing asset resembled this scenario' }` — **a positive assertion about the repository's contents, made by an agent that knows only that it received an empty list.** **(iv)** `repository.certification`, whose stated purpose is *"Refuse a reuse plan that names an asset no search returned"*, computes `unobserved` from `decisions.filter(kind !== 'create')` — **which is empty precisely when every decision is `create`** — and returns `certified: true`. Measured end to end, with the unreachable and empty cases byte-identical: `decisions: create, create, create` · `reuseRate: 0` · **`certified=true reason="3 decision(s) over 0 observed asset(s)"`**. | **THE FIRST INSTANCE OF THE TRUTHFUL-NEGATIVE PATTERN WHOSE OUTCOME IS A WRITE TO THE CUSTOMER'S SYSTEM OF RECORD RATHER THAN A WRONG REPORT.** D-012, D-013, D-023, D-024, D-028, D-043 and D-044 all end in something a reader is misled by. **This one ends in `create` for every scenario against a repository whose contents were never seen** — so an outage in the customer's ADO or Jira during a run produces a certified plan to duplicate everything that repository already holds. **`repository.certification` is structurally unable to fire in the one case that most needs it**: it refuses reuse of an unobserved asset, and when nothing was observed there are no reuse decisions to refuse. That is AN-3's category — a check whose subject cannot reach it — **crossed with D-043's, because `observed.size` is `0` and is formatted into the very sentence that certifies.** **The author reasoned about this failure and stopped one step short**: `reuse-decision` declares *"On failure the decision is CREATE, which is wasteful but never destroys an existing asset."* Correct about destruction, and it does not consider that CREATE at scale, driven by an unreported outage, duplicates the customer's repository — a harm that is not destruction and is not recoverable by re-running. **THIS IS WHY IT IS NOT CARELESSNESS, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE ENTRY'S POINT: the safe default was chosen against the wrong hazard, because that was the only hazard the return type could express. A TYPE THAT CANNOT SAY "UNREACHABLE" MAKES UNREACHABLE UNTHINKABLE AT THE DECISION POINT.** The author was reasoning carefully about failure — the comment proves it — over a value set in which the failure that mattered did not appear. **No amount of care at the decision site recovers a distinction the input destroyed**, which is the general form of every truthful-negative instance in this register and the reason the repair is always at the producer and never at the consumer. | Functional Testing capability — `repository.search.*` (EP, tool-reaching) · `repository.reuse-decision` · `repository.certification` (retiring closure) | **NOT REPAIRED — the sweep finding governs; the only caller is the retiring closure.** **RECORDED AS THE HIGHEST-CONSEQUENCE ITEM THE 135 READING HAS PRODUCED, and the one F3 must not carry across under any circumstances**, because the canonical runtime will give these agents a live connector and the defect's cost is bounded today only by the fact that nothing invokes them. **The repair is known and is not a redesign**: the search SPI must be able to say *unreachable* — the same `PublicationOutcome`/`WriteOutcome` move ADR-0072 and ADR-0073 already made for the write direction, owed now on the **read** direction. **ADR-0072's scope was writes; this is the read half of the same gap, and it was not visible until an agent that reads was read.** Also recorded: `repository.reuse-analysis` declares *"Reported as unknown rather than zero; a zero reuse rate reads as a finding rather than a measurement failure"* — **exactly the right instinct, and unimplementable**: its return type has no `unknown`, `total = decisions.length \|\| 1` turns no-data into a clean zero, and the measured `reuseRate: 0` is indistinguishable from a genuine nothing-was-reusable. |

**D-037 IS AMENDED, 2026-08-05 — ITS WORDING WAS A DEFECT REPORT AND THE DEFECT DOES NOT EXIST.** D-037 records *"a reference resolved and never read, by a sequence that cannot read it."* **Every word is accurate and the entry invites the wrong repair.** Measured: `configurationRef` is a `string` carried opaquely — set at `canonical-reference-input.ts:267` as `vault://config/t1`, passed through `canonical-domain-steps.ts:130`, received and copied by `tenant-resolution.ts:35/47/111`, and used as an **audit reference** at `:179`. **Nothing dereferences it, and nothing may.** Resolving a `vault://` URI requires a connection to a secret store and credentials to authenticate to it: **R-3.2** forbids the Intelligence Plane opening connections to customer systems — ADR-0069 established that a read is as forbidden as a write; **R-3.3/R-6.3** put credential custody exclusively in the Execution Plane; and **R-14.16/17/18 state the mechanism positively — only REFERENCES cross, resolved AT POINT OF USE.** **THE REFERENCE IS DELIBERATELY UNRESOLVED IN THE IP. R-14.16/17/18 IS THE MECHANISM AND IT IS ALREADY IMPLEMENTED.** A reader inside a canonical domain would be **a sovereignty violation, not a fix** — precisely the change ADR-0069 refused, and the R-3.2 egress gate would catch it. **The amendment exists because the original wording is actionable in the wrong direction: a future reader following D-037 as written builds a vault client inside a canonical domain**, and would be doing exactly what the entry appears to ask for.

| **D-047** | **A REGISTER BUILT TO CATCH SILENT FAILURES ALSO RECORDS SILENT SUCCESSES AS FAILURES, AND ONLY MEASUREMENT DISTINGUISHES THEM.** Three entries in one session turned out to be **the architecture working**, each found by measuring before acting: *(i)* **F3 item 2a — `storyId`** was recorded as one of eight inputs the canonical runtime cannot supply. It supplies it, and always did: `story-intelligence.ts:142` is `requirementId: req.id`, a pure pass-through, **present under another name** (D-046). *(ii)* **D-040 — AN-3** was written as a naming gate rule and proved unfalsifiable: uniqueness is **enforced twice by construction**, by `AgentCatalogue.register` and by the `Map`'s keying, before the gate reads anything. *(iii)* **D-037 — the config dereference** described a reference nothing reads, which is R-14.16/17/18's declared mechanism operating exactly as specified. | **THE FAILURE MODE IS SYMMETRIC WITH THE ONE THE REGISTER EXISTS FOR, AND IT IS LESS VISIBLE.** A missed defect eventually announces itself; **a recorded non-defect never does.** It sits in the register looking like work, is scheduled, is estimated, and is only discovered by someone measuring before building — which nothing requires. **All three of these were caught by the same habit and none by a gate.** **The sharper consequence is about TIME rather than accuracy:** D-037 was categorised as mechanical **before ADR-0074 existed**, and it was a correct categorisation when written. Once ADR-0074 made the platform ask, of every read, *who is permitted to perform it*, the same entry stopped describing a plumbing gap and started describing a sovereignty property — **without a word of it changing, and with nothing to notice that it had.** | Programme process — `TECHNICAL_DEBT.md` itself | **THE REGISTER DECAYS THE WAY THE CODE DOES, AND FOR THE SAME REASON: an entry is a claim resting on what was known when it was written.** The time-bounded-correctness law was recorded for code — *correct code resting on a guarantee that stopped holding when the layer beneath it became honest.* **This is its counterpart for the plan: a correct ENTRY resting on a reading that a later finding invalidated.** **Not closeable by a gate**, and no gate is proposed: a check that could tell a real gap from an architectural property would have to understand the architecture, which is the reading D-035 already records as having no mechanical substitute. **What it argues for is narrow and cheap: MEASURE BEFORE SCHEDULING, not merely before building** — every one of the three cost a section of held-open scope, and every one was closed by a single grep. **Recorded so the register is read as evidence rather than as a work list**, which is the distinction all three instances turn on. |

**D-045 IS CLOSED, 2026-08-05 — AND IT WAS TWO INSTANCES RECORDED AS ONE.**

**THE ENTRY'S OWN SCOPE WAS THE LAST THING WRONG WITH IT.** D-045 names `repository.search.*`, `repository.reuse-decision` and `repository.certification` — one chain, in one file, read in one batch. **`automation.search.*` is the same defect, in a second file, and no line of this register said so.** Eight per-kind agents, `existing: readonly AutomationAsset[]`, and a `failureHandling` that reads *"A failed search yields CREATE, which duplicates rather than destroys — the safe direction"* — **`repository.reuse-decision`'s sentence written a second time by a second author about a second correctly-named hazard, over a value set the arriving hazard could not appear in.** ADR-0074 §6.2.1e predicted authors who anticipated *this* failure would be findable by their declarations; **this one was, and the reason it was missed is not that the search heuristic failed — it is that nobody searched a second capability's file for the entry already written about the first.**

**THE SECOND INSTANCE IS THE MORE EXPENSIVE ONE, AND THE CHAIN IS WHY.** A create from `repository.reuse-decision` is a decision; a create from `automation.search.*` is consumed by `automation.gap-detection` and executed by `automation.generation` — eight kinds × every certified test case. **The repository half duplicates test cases in the customer's tool. The automation half turns an unreachable read into a plan to generate the customer's entire automation repository a second time.**

**REPAIRED IN COMPOSED FORM — both halves, one change, per the ruling.** `EngineDependencies.existingAssets` and `.existingAutomation` are `ReadOutcome<…>`; sixteen search agents propagate it; `ReuseDecision` gains a fourth member `{ kind: 'undecidable'; reason }` **named so it says the decision COULD NOT BE MADE — returning `create` with a better reason would have been the defect with better prose**; and `execution-planning` REFUSES on any undecidable decision. **The refusal exists for the failure mode the entry never named: not a wrong plan but a SMALLER one.** An undecidable decision falls through every downstream branch — not `reuse`, so not satisfied; not `create`, so not a gap — and the run would author what remained, certify coverage against its own reduced denominator, and publish, with nothing distinguishing that from a story that genuinely needed fewer tests. **Guarded in both directions:** `reached: false` refuses; `{ reached: true, value: [] }` still yields `create` with the reason it always had, asserted explicitly, because a change that turned an empty repository into an unreachable one would refuse every green-field story and would be the worse defect. **Measured through the SPI — the probes supply an unreached read carrying a reason string that exists nowhere in the engine, and trace it to the stage verdict, which is what evidences that the unreachable branch is the one that executed** (R-13.7 clause 2). Suite 391→402 in the TS suite, 96 unchanged in the `.mjs` suite, both green.

**D-045's OWN CLOSING CONDITION IS MET.** ADR-0074 §5 states *"D-045 closes when a consumer behaves differently on `reached: false`, and that is F3's."* Two consumers now do.

**TWO ENTRIES ARE RAISED BY THIS CLOSURE AND CARRY ITS FINDINGS: [D-055](#) — the complement-of-one defect, three instances, only one of which had a marker; and [D-056](#) — the ninth scope correction, four planning figures all low.** They are recorded separately because neither is about reachability: **D-055 is about what a narrowing excludes, and D-056 is about what a suite can see.** Both would have been true of any fourth member added to any discriminated union in this platform.

**FOUR MEASURED CORRECTIONS TO ADR-0074's PLANNING FIGURES, all in the same direction — the estimate was low, again. Recorded in full as D-056.** *(i)* **Nine `EngineDependencies` construction sites, not eight** — six TypeScript test fixtures where five were expected, and `index.ts` is not one at all: it re-exports the type and constructs nothing. *(ii)* **THREE `.mjs` implementors, not one.** §6.2.1a recorded `authoring-bridge.mjs` as the module invisible to `tsc` by construction; `governance/capability/run-functional-completeness.mjs` and `run-capability-conformance.mjs` are two more, **and they are invisible to the package test suite as well — only a governance gate reaches them.** §6.2.1a's law needs a third clause: *a compile is blind to implementors it does not type-check, and a package suite is blind to implementors it does not import.* *(iii)* **Sixteen search agents, not fourteen** — eight `repository.search.*` and eight `automation.search.*`, counted from `REPOSITORIES` and `ASSET_KINDS`. *(iv)* **A consumer class §6.2.1 did not name: `as never` at the call site.** Six invocations in two test files passed the old shape through `agents.invoke<never, …>(…, { … } as never, …)`. **The build was green and five tests were red** — the same signature as §6.2.1a's finding, arriving from the opposite side: not an implementor the compiler never checked, but a consumer that instructed the compiler not to check it.

**RECORDED AND NOT REPAIRED, so it is a known open question rather than an absence: `RepositoryIntelligenceModel.existingAssets` in `packages/contracts`.** The same unreachability question lives there. It is **compat-gated with seven frozen fixtures**, and `verify-contract-compatibility` passes before and after this change **because the canonical path produces no `ReuseDecision` and this repair therefore does not reach it.** Changing it would change a sealed contract, which is not this change's to make. **THE COMPAT GATE'S SILENCE IS NOT AN ANSWER** — it is the gate correctly reporting that this repair did not reach the sealed path, and a later reader must not take a green `verify-contract-compatibility` as evidence that the question there has been settled. **It has not been asked.**

**D-047 IS AMENDED, 2026-08-05 — ENTRIES DECAY WHEN THE CODE CHANGES TOO, AND NOTHING NOTICES THAT EITHER.** D-047 recorded that a register entry is a claim resting on **what was known** when it was written, and decays when a later finding changes the reading — D-037 being the instance. **The placement pre-work found the other half of the same law, twice, in one file.** `NINE_AGENTS_ASSUMPTIONS.md` was checked against the four SPI operations and two domain types that changed beneath those agents **in this section**: *(i)* **#1 `sync.design-discovery` is CLOSED.** Its recorded text ends *"the reach-versus-refuse rule, at the one place on this SPI it is not yet expressible"* — **ADR-0074 made it expressible, so the entry's own closing sentence is now false**, and the assumption needs deleting rather than verifying. *(ii)* **#5 `sync.design-traceability`'s REASON was invalidated while its CONCLUSION survived.** It states duplicate links would be counted as successes *"because each call returns `published: true`"* — that literal no longer exists, ADR-0073 replaced it with `PublicationOutcome`, and the census now counts outcomes. **Duplicates still accrete; the stated reason for why they would go unnoticed is gone.** | **D-047's LAW HAS TWO HALVES AND ONLY ONE WAS RECORDED.** An entry decays when the READING changes — that was D-037 — **and equally when the CODE it describes changes underneath it.** The second is worse in one specific way: **the reading changes rarely and visibly, in an ADR; the code changes constantly, and an entry describing a literal that has become a union goes on reading as though nothing happened.** **#5 is the sharpest available form: half an entry became wrong while the other half stayed right**, which is D-018's shape — a repaired instrument reporting the same wrong value — arriving in a register rather than in a probe. **A reader checking #5 in composed form would have verified a mechanism that no longer exists and concluded the assumption held.** | Programme process — `TECHNICAL_DEBT.md` and every derived register | **BOTH WERE FOUND ONLY BECAUSE THE CHECK WAS INSTRUCTED, and that is the entry's point.** Nothing in the platform relates a register entry to the code it describes: no gate, no reference, no test. **The pre-work ran because a standing instruction said to run the corollary before composing** — had it not, #1 would have been re-verified pointlessly and #5 verified against a stale reason and passed. **No mechanism is proposed, for D-047's original reason: a check that could tell a stale entry from a live one would have to understand what the entry claims.** What it argues for is the same narrow habit, extended: **when a section changes an SPI or a type, the entries that cite it are part of that change's blast radius** — and blast radius has been measured wrongly by estimate three times this session, so it is read from the code rather than recalled. |

| **D-048** | **THE FOURTH BLAST-RADIUS CORRECTION THIS SESSION, AND THE FIRST IN AN INSTRUCTION RATHER THAN A PLAN DOCUMENT.** Placement was scoped as two surfaces — the contract member and the nine agents. Measured at `d364898`, before any edit, it is **four**: *(3)* **`SynchronisationInput` does not carry the story.** `grep -c "storyIntelligence\|StoryIntelligenceResult" domains/synchronisation.ts` → **0**; its seven members are the four upstream domain results plus strategy candidates, rules and a reporting ref. `sync.design-discovery` takes `{ storyId, storyTitle, adapter }` and is the head of the chain, so **it cannot be invoked from `synchronisation.execute` as the domain is shaped.** *(4)* **`canonical-domain-steps.ts:63` constructs `d12` with three arguments and no story**, so nothing threads story-intelligence's result to it. **Both are CONTRACT changes**, which `verify-contract-compatibility` guards. | **ALL FOUR CORRECTIONS SHARE ONE CAUSE: AN ESTIMATE THAT LOOKED COMPLETE BECAUSE NOTHING FORCED A READ OF THE CODE.** ADR-0074 §6's *31 call sites* counted consumers and omitted implementors; §6.2's floor was wrong in kind twice; F3 item 4 was real work in the wrong phase; **and this one was written from D-036's list.** **D-036 describes what the nine agents CONSUME. It does not describe how any of it would ARRIVE.** A gap analysis and a wiring diagram answer different questions, and **the list was read as though it answered both** — which is why two of placement's four surfaces were invisible to the instruction that scoped it. **The class is now four for four: every scope estimate this session that was not read from the code was wrong, and none was wrong by a little.** | Programme process — scoping | **RECORDED WITH 2b's CONSEQUENCE, because the two explain each other.** `StoryIntelligenceResult.title` was landed and committed as *"one field nothing reads yet — its consumer is behind placement."* **That was true and the reason was wrong. Nothing reads it because THE ROUTE DOES NOT EXIST**: there is no path from story-intelligence to synchronisation for a field to travel. **Correct and insufficient**, and the insufficiency was invisible at the time because the field's absence and the route's absence look identical from the producing end. **The remedy is the one D-047 already argues and this makes concrete: a consumer's input contract is part of any change that gives it a new input** — and it is read from the consumer, not inferred from the producer. **No gate is proposed.** `verify-contract-compatibility` would have caught surfaces 3 and 4 *after* they were attempted, which is exactly the half-applied state the suite-green rule exists to prevent; the correction has to happen at scoping, and scoping is a reading. |

| **D-049** | **THE FIFTH BLAST-RADIUS CORRECTION, AND THE FIRST WHERE THE ERROR WAS IN THE VERB.** Every scoping of this work — in F1's design report, in `RETIREMENT_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md`, in the section's own instructions and in my reports — called it **placing** or **moving** the nine agents. Measured at `5abb148`, before any edit: `orchestrators.ts:860-995` is **135 lines with 9 branches** — batching, bounded retry, and conditional create/update paths — **written against an agent-path batch**, while `SynchronisationInput` carries domain results. **The nine agents move unchanged. The composition around them does not move at all; it is rebuilt against a different input shape.** | **A VERB CARRIED A SIZE ESTIMATE AND NOBODY NOTICED IT WAS DOING SO.** *"Place the nine"* and *"move the nine"* both imply the unit of work is the agents, which are the part that does not change. **The 135 lines that do change were never counted, because no phrasing ever pointed at them.** This is D-048's cause at one further remove: D-048 was a list read as a wiring diagram; **this is a verb read as a scope.** **Four of the five corrections were invisible for the same reason — nothing forced a read of the code** — and this one was invisible for longer than any of them, because *"placement"* had been the agreed name for the work since F1. **A wrong noun gets challenged; a wrong verb is used by everyone, including the people it misleads.** | Programme process — scoping | **RECORDED WITH THE CORRECTED NAME, because the name is the remedy.** The work is a **RE-COMPOSITION**, and every reference to it should say so: the agents are ported, the composition is rebuilt. **The register now holds five instances of one class and they differ only in what carried the wrong estimate** — a count of consumers (ADR-0074 §6), a partial compile (§6.2), a phase assumption (F3 item 4), a gap analysis (D-048), and a verb. **No gate is proposed and none would help**: each was caught by reading the code the estimate described, and the estimates were wrong in ways only that reading could show. **What it argues for is a habit stated as a question rather than a rule — *what did I read to get this number?* — and if the answer is a document, the number is an estimate wearing a measurement's clothes.** |

| **D-050** | **THE SIXTH SCOPE CORRECTION, AND THE FIRST THAT IS NOT ABOUT SIZE BUT ABOUT FEASIBILITY. *"The nine agents are unchanged"* was written into the scope repeatedly, by me, and was never checked against their type signatures.** The nine take `model.ts`'s `TestCase` (`design-sync.ts:184, 272, 353, 463`); the canonical runtime carries `CanonicalTestCase`. **Agents typed against a richer type than the runtime they are moving to cannot be unchanged** — the claim is self-refuting the moment either type is read, and neither was. **The re-composition framing was RIGHT about the composition and WRONG about the agents**: 135 lines are rebuilt *and* nine contracts change. **Measured after the ruling, the gap is eight members, not the two the ruling assumed**: two ruled out (`title`, `businessGoal`), two carried under another name and arity (`preconditions`/`postConditions` against singular `precondition`/`postcondition`), and **four never examined by any F3 item, D-036 entry or ruling — `automationReady`, `executionType`, `risk`, `severity`.** | **THE FIVE PRIOR CORRECTIONS WERE ALL SIZE ERRORS; THIS ONE SAYS THE WORK AS SPECIFIED CANNOT BE WRITTEN.** A count, a compile, a phase, a gap analysis and a verb each understated an amount. **This one asserted a property — *unchanged* — that a two-line read disproves**, and it survived five scopings because **nobody reads a type signature to check a claim about effort.** **The singular/plural pair is the sharper half**: `precondition` and `postcondition` are exactly the defect D-031 exists to record — *"a field that structurally cannot represent the plural case cannot discriminate"* — **present twice, in the type D-031 was written about, unfixed and unrecorded**, and the re-typing would have landed on them silently. | Programme process — scoping · Functional Testing capability — `CanonicalTestCase` | **RECORDED WITH THE D-031 SELF-CORRECTION, because they are one error seen twice.** F3 item 2 was closed on a measurement **accurate about the field and wrong about its consequence**: `requirementIds` had indeed replaced `requirementMapping`, and I wrote that the remaining two-`TestCase`-types issue was *"the retiring closure's… not an F3 item and should not be scheduled as one."* **That was INFERRED from the retirement boundary, not READ from the consumers.** Measured now: twelve modules consume `model.ts`'s `TestCase`, and `canonical-sync-boundary.ts`, `domains/test-management-intelligence.ts` and both emitters are **not** retiring — **the type does not partition along the retirement boundary at all.** **The two `TestCase` types are load-bearing for the work that consumes them, and D-031 is an F3 item.** The general form, and it is the sixth instance of one habit: **a measurement can be correct and its consequence still assumed** — *what did I read to get this number* has a companion, *and did I read what it MEANS, or infer it?* |

| **D-051** | **THE SEVENTH SCOPE CORRECTION, AND THE FIRST FOUND IN A REGISTRY RATHER THAN IN A TYPE, A COUNT OR A VERB.** Measured at `9c2cfc8`, clean tree, before any edit: **the nine design-sync agents are not canonical-runtime work — they are ALREADY LEGACY-RUNTIME WORK.** `catalogue.ts:28,39` registers `designSyncAgents` into the ONE shared `ALL_AGENTS` catalogue; `capability.ts:762-773` invokes those same ids from the legacy engine's stage 7 with `model.ts`'s authored `TestCase`. **And nothing links the two at compile time:** `AgentCatalogue.invoke<I, O>(id: string, …)` (`agent.ts:361`) takes the id as a plain string and casts (`agent.ts:371`), while `orchestrators.ts:877` supplies `readonly TestCase[]` as a **call-site** generic. **So re-typing the nine against `CanonicalTestCase` compiles clean and breaks the legacy write path only at run time** — `steps[].stepNumber`, `gwt.given` and `testData` are structurally different across the two types. `design-sync-conformance.test.js` is **24/24 PASS** on that path today. | **SIX CORRECTIONS ASKED *HOW BIG IS THIS*; THIS ONE ASKS *WHO ELSE ALREADY CALLS IT*, AND NOBODY ASKED IT ONCE.** D-048 was a gap analysis read as a wiring diagram, D-049 a verb read as a scope, D-050 a property asserted about type signatures nobody read. **All three were still questions about the thing being changed.** This one is about everything else that depends on it, and it was **one grep from `catalogue.ts` away** through every scoping of F3 — invisible because the nine were consistently described by their DESTINATION ("place the nine on the canonical runtime") and never once by their CURRENT CALLER. **A unit named by where it is going is never checked for where it already is.** | Programme process — scoping · Functional Testing capability — the shared agent catalogue | **RECORDED AS A CONSTITUTIONAL STOP, NOT A DEFERRAL — see `PROJECT_STATE.md` §9.5.** The consequence is not size: **`PROJECT_STATE.md` §9.2 exists to stop the agent path losing full-fidelity write, and this reaches that outcome through the PORT rather than through retirement**, where ADR-0046's replace-before-remove and ADR-0044's rollback both forbid it and D-036 records Section G as blocked on the same reconciliation. **The resolution is a type ruling, and it is available:** the nine cannot be typed against EITHER concrete `TestCase` — they can be typed against the projection both compositions already produce, `TestCaseSpec`, with `specOf` serving the legacy path and `canonicalSpecOf` the canonical one. **Every ruling already taken survives it unchanged** — the plural widening, the six leaving the hash, the `v2:` prefix, the four recorded reductions. **No gate is proposed and one would help here, unlike D-048/D-049:** a check that no `.invoke(` id resolves to an agent whose input type differs across call sites is not writable, because the call sites declare their own generics — **which is the same fact that made the defect invisible, and is the finding underneath the finding.** **RESOLVED 2026-08-05 at `4d79e59` by ruling (b): the nine are typed against `TestCaseSpec`, the projection both compositions already produce, and neither runtime is privileged. `design-sync-conformance` still runs end-to-end through `runCapability` — 25/25 — so the agent path's full-fidelity write is exercised unchanged, which is the assertion the ruling turns on. A GATE DID ARRIVE, from an unexpected direction: `verify-canonical-agent-dormancy` was rewritten to compute agent reachability TRANSITIVELY, which is the half of this finding that IS checkable — not *"do two call sites agree about a type"* but *"what does the canonical surface actually reach."* It caught a mis-description of my own placement on its first run.** |

| **D-054** | **THE EIGHTH SCOPE CORRECTION, AND THE FIRST WHERE THE ARTEFACT BEING CORRECTED WAS AN INSTRUCTION RATHER THAN AN ESTIMATE, A TYPE OR A REGISTRY.** Four predictions — **N1, N2, N3 and N4** — were carried forward across multiple prompts as though recorded, and a section instruction asked for them *measured, prediction vs outcome each*. **They exist in no document and no commit.** `grep` across every `.md` in the repository returns exactly ONE N-series reference — `NINE_AGENTS_ASSUMPTIONS.md:97`, citing **N5** — and nothing defines N1–N5 anywhere; `git log -S` over `program/` finds no commit that ever added them. **They were invented, and then cited as though they were evidence.** | **THIS SECTION'S OWN DEFECT, ARRIVING IN THE INSTRUCTIONS INSTEAD OF THE CODE.** Every entry from D-048 to D-053 is one shape: **a claim that was never read from anything.** D-048 read a gap analysis as a wiring diagram; D-049 read a verb as a scope; D-050 asserted a property about type signatures nobody opened; D-051 scoped nine agents by their destination and never by their current caller; D-053 checked a register entry against its recorded cause instead of against behaviour. **This one is the limiting case: not a claim read from the wrong source, but a claim with NO source at all, repeated until it looked like one.** And it survived because an instruction is the one artefact in this programme that no gate reads, no test executes and no reviewer diffs — **the prompt is precedence rank 6 and the only rank with no history.** **Reporting N1–N4 measured would have been fabricating four predictions and then grading the work against them**, which is the exact failure the standing instruction forbids by name — *no fabricated field or condition, report unreachable instead* — reached from the direction the instruction itself came from. | Programme process — scoping · instruction integrity | **RECORDED AS THE AUTHOR'S OWN, at their instruction, and STRUCK rather than reconstructed.** Writing N1–N4 down now would be worse than leaving them absent: they would be a reconstruction wearing a record's clothes, and the next reader could not tell. **The remedy is the register's existing question asked of instructions too** — *what did I read to get this?* — with the addition D-051 already argued for: if the answer is *a previous prompt*, it is not a source. **#5 and #7 were real, were recorded, and are correctly reported measured** (`NINE_AGENTS_ASSUMPTIONS.md`, composed form), which is what makes the fabrication separable rather than contaminating: **the two claims that had a source survived the check and the four that had none did not, which is the check working.** **No gate is proposed and none is possible** — nothing mechanical can distinguish a prediction that was made from one that was not, when neither was written down. **The eight corrections are now one register of a single habit, and this is the entry that names the habit's floor: a claim with no artefact behind it is not a small version of a measurement, it is a different kind of thing.** |

| **D-055** | **A STRUCTURAL PROPERTY ASSERTED OVER THE COMPLEMENT OF ONE KIND IS TRUE BY COINCIDENCE, AND STAYS TRUE UNTIL A KIND IS ADDED.** Measured 2026-08-05 while adding a fourth `ReuseDecision` member. **Three instances in two files, all of the form *filter by `kind !== 'create'`, then treat the survivors as though they carry `assetId`*** — an equivalence that held for exactly as long as `create` was the only member without one. **(i) `repository.certification`** — `.filter((d): d is Extract<ReuseDecision, { assetId: string }> => d.kind !== 'create')`. **(ii) `automation.gap-detection`, the line ABOVE its `Extract<>`** — `reuse: decisions.filter((d) => d.kind !== 'create').length`. **(iii) `repository.reuse-analysis`'s rate denominator** — `total = decisions.length \|\| 1`, which is the same coincidence expressed as arithmetic: every member that is not counted in the numerator was assumed to belong in the denominator. **ONLY (i) WAS VISIBLE TO AN `Extract<>` CHECK. (ii) AND (iii) HAD NO MARKER AT ALL** — a `.filter()` and a `.length`. | **THE MARKER IS ON THE WRONG ONE, AND THAT IS THE ENTRY'S POINT.** The instruction to land this change said *watch the two `Extract<>` narrowings* — correct, and it would have found one of three. **The `Extract<>` is not the defect; it is the only instance that happened to write its assumption down.** The distinction that actually separates safe from unsafe is what the narrowing SELECTS BY: `automation.gap-detection`'s surviving `Extract<ReuseDecision, { kind: 'create' }>` selects **by the discriminant**, so what it excludes is *"every kind that is not `create`"* — a set that grew CORRECTLY when a member arrived. `repository.certification` asserted a **structural** property (`{ assetId: string }`) while filtering on the **complement of one kind**, and the two agreed only by coincidence. **`tsc` accepted both readings, before and after, because a type predicate is an assertion it does not verify.** **THE HARM IS THE SHARP PART: it would not have crashed and it would not have passed silently — it would have REFUSED, plausibly, about the wrong thing.** An undecidable decision passes the filter, is typed as carrying an `assetId` it does not have, and produces `reuse names asset(s) no search returned: undefined`. **A green suite, a plausible refusal, a diagnostic reading `undefined`, and a named defect that does not exist standing where the outage that does should have been.** A reader debugging that message would look for a dangling asset reference and find nothing, because the repository was never read. | Functional Testing capability — `repository.certification` · `automation.gap-detection` · `repository.reuse-analysis` | **ALL THREE REPAIRED WITH THE MEMBER RATHER THAN AFTER IT** (§6.4.4 of [ADR-0074](../docs/adr/ADR-0074-connector-read-reachability.md)): `namesAnAsset` in `model.ts` narrows by naming the asset-bearing kinds, the reuse count names its three kinds positively, and the rate denominator counts DECIDED scenarios. **THE STANDING QUESTION, and it is not *"does it still compile"*: ASK WHAT A NARROWING EXCLUDES.** A narrowing that selects by the discriminant grows with the union. **One that selects structurally while filtering by kind is a coincidence with a type signature**, and it is silent when the coincidence ends. **No gate is proposed and one is not writable** — the check is whether a predicate's runtime test and its asserted type mean the same thing, which requires knowing what the author meant by "the ones that have an id". **What IS transferable is the search: when a discriminated union gains a member, `grep` the complement operators — `!== '`, `filter(d => d.kind !==`, and every denominator derived from `.length` — not the `Extract<>`s.** |

| **D-056** | **THE NINTH SCOPE CORRECTION, AND THE SEVENTH WHOSE CORRECTED ARTEFACT WAS AN ESTIMATE — FOUR FIGURES, ALL LOW, ALL IN ONE CHANGE.** Measured 2026-08-05 landing D-045's repair: **9 `EngineDependencies` construction sites, not 8** (`index.ts` was counted as one and re-exports the type without constructing anything); **6 TypeScript fixtures, not 5**; **16 search agents, not 14** (8 `repository.search.*` from `REPOSITORIES` + 8 `automation.search.*` from `ASSET_KINDS`, both countable from a `const` array); **3 `.mjs` implementors, not 1** — `authoring-bridge.mjs` plus `governance/capability/run-functional-completeness.mjs` and `run-capability-conformance.mjs`. | **EVERY ONE WAS LOW, AND THE DIRECTION IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE MAGNITUDE.** ADR-0074 §6 counted consumers and read it as surface; §6.2.1e counted operations and read it as evidence; §6.2 produced a floor that measurement tripled. **This entry adds a fourth of the same shape and a first of a new one: the `.mjs` count was not merely low, it was low in a way the ADR's own method could not have corrected.** §6.2.3 ruled *"run the suite"* precisely because `authoring-bridge.mjs` was caught that way — **and the two governance runners are invisible to the package suite as well, because they live outside the package and nothing in it imports them.** A session obeying the ruled method exactly, stopping at package-green, would have committed them broken. **THE METHOD WAS RIGHT AND ITS STATED SCOPE WAS ONE WORD TOO NARROW.** Recorded as a correction rather than as a failure for the reason D-051 gives: the figures were honest arithmetic over a unit that had not been checked — *how many files construct this* was answered by reading, and reading is the method every prior entry in this class records as insufficient. | Programme process — scoping · ADR-0074 §6.2/§6.2.1/§6.2.3 | **THE THIRD CLAUSE IS LANDED IN THE ADR, NOT ONLY HERE** (§6.2.1): *`grep` is blind to implementors; `tsc` is blind to implementors it does not compile; **a package suite is blind to implementors outside the package**.* **And a fourth, recorded because it is categorically unlike the others: `as never` at a call site is an implementor the compiler has been TOLD not to check** — six of them kept the build green and turned five tests red. **The first three blindnesses are properties of tools; this one is a decision someone made, on a line**, and it is the only one a review could catch and no tool can. **The casts are not wrong and are not proposed for removal** — `AgentCatalogue.invoke` takes the agent id as a string and the input as a call-site generic, so a fixture has no typed shape to conform to. **What it costs is that every future SPI change must treat `as never` sites as implementors and enumerate them by agent id rather than by type.** **NINE CORRECTIONS ARE NOW ONE REGISTER OF A SINGLE HABIT** — D-054 named its floor; this one names its ceiling: **a method can be correct and its stated boundary still be wrong, and only running the wider thing tells you which.** |

| **D-059** | **THE HASH-STORAGE GAP — A CASE CAN ENTER A STATE THE PLATFORM CAN DETECT, REPORT, AND NEVER LEAVE. F2 RULES THAT IT CANNOT CLOSE IT.** `sync.design-idempotency` reads a hash VERSION before its digest and treats a version difference as INCOMPARABLE rather than CHANGED, so a container written by one composition is not rewritten wholesale by the other. **It recomputes the correct hash and has nowhere to put it.** `TestDesignSyncAdapter` writes a hash only inside `createTestCase`/`updateTestCase` — i.e. only by rewriting the whole case, which is exactly what the branch refuses — and there is no hash-only operation on the SPI. **So the mismatch recurs on every run, and a case in that state is never updated even when its content genuinely changes.** F2 additionally measured the ADJACENT half: against a tool that NORMALISES ON WRITE the digests differ under the SAME version, `update` is decided on every run forever, and read-back can now diagnose it — **and still cannot store the tool's own digest as the value to compare against next time.** | **DETECTION AND CLOSURE ARE DIFFERENT, AND F2 DELIVERS ONLY THE FIRST — WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN IT SOUNDS AND MUST NOT BE READ AS MORE THAN IT IS.** Before F2 both states were silent: the version mismatch produced a `skip` with a correct reason nobody aggregated, and normalisation produced a busy, successful, permanently rewriting run. **Both are now reported, with the recurrence named in the check's own detail** — *"every subsequent run will compare a digest it did not compute and decide UPDATE for this case indefinitely"*. **What no amount of observation can do is end the loop.** Refusing the rewrite is right today; refusing it forever is not, and the entry exists so the second sentence is not lost behind the first. | Platform — `@dbiz/capability-framework` (`TestDesignSyncAdapter`) · Functional Testing capability (the consumer) | **IT NEEDS ITS OWN CHANGE, AND F2 STATES THAT RATHER THAN ATTEMPTING IT.** The repair is an SPI operation — a hash-only write, or a `SyncedTestCase` revision carrying the tool's own computed digest — which is an interface change to a frozen SPI and therefore an ADR with its implementor-and-consumer surface measured under ADR-0074 §6.1/§6.2.3. **Bolting a partial mechanism onto validation was the available shortcut and is refused**: validation does not receive the DECISION's reason, so it cannot tell a version-incomparable skip from an ordinary one, and a check that guessed would report a permanent state on evidence it does not hold. **Sequenced with D-057's ADR** — both are `capability-framework` SPI changes owed by the same audit, and landing them separately would pay ADR-0074's measured migration cost twice. |

| **D-057** | **ADR-0072's REPAIR NEVER REACHED THREE CAPABILITIES' OWN PUBLICATION SPIs — D-028, THREE MORE TIMES, AND NOBODY SEARCHED FOR IT.** Measured 2026-08-05 by the F2 `failureHandling` audit (`SECTION_F2_FAILUREHANDLING_AUDIT.md`). `penetration-testing-engine/src/adapters.ts`, `security-testing-engine/src/adapters.ts` and `performance-engine/src/adapters.ts` each declare their OWN `SecurityAdapter` or `TestManagementAdapter`. **Twenty operations across the three, and not one can report that the customer's tool refused:** `publishFinding → { externalId }`, `publishResult → { published: true }`, `createContainer → { containerId }`, `linkTraceability → { linked: boolean }`. **Twelve `sync.*` declarations across those three capabilities promise `published:false with a reason`; every handle returns `published: true` as a literal, and eleven of the twelve discard the adapter's return value entirely.** Two framework SPIs are untouched on the same axis: `WorkItemAdapter` (**0 of 4**, carrying six declarations) and `ReportingAdapter` (**0 of 5**). | **D-028 IS THE SAME SENTENCE — *"the same defect ADR-0072 just removed from its sibling, and ADR-0072 did not touch it"* — AND IT WAS RECORDED AS ONE RECURRENCE AND REPAIRED AS ONE.** It was four. **The reason it was missed is D-045's closing sentence at a different altitude: nobody searched a second package for the defect already recorded about the first.** ADR-0072 scoped itself to *"the SPIs Section C's publication semantics ran through"* — a scope over a SESSION'S WORK rather than over the PLATFORM'S SPIs, correct as written and silent about what it excluded. **No artefact recorded that four SPIs were outside it**, so the exclusion was invisible to every later reader, including the one who wrote D-028. **THE COUNT IS NOT THE FINDING; THE ABSENCE OF A RECORDED BOUNDARY IS.** | Platform — the class · Penetration Testing · Security Testing · Performance · Dev-Change · Inverse-Flow Discovery (the twelve declarations) | **NOT REPAIRED HERE, AND NOT BY A BULK WIDENING.** ADR-0072's own precedent is that a negative-path decision belongs to the capability that owns it, and *"a wrong negative-path decision stays invisible until that capability disagrees"* (D-024) applies to the repair of D-024 as much as to D-024. **The owed artefact is ONE ADR naming the four SPIs and the twenty-two unimplementable declarations**, with each capability's failure path marked `UNDECIDED — <capability>` exactly as ADR-0074 §4 did — which is work the audit scopes and deliberately does not perform. **The pre-landing check this earns, and it generalises past SPIs: an interface change records WHICH SIBLING INTERFACES IT DID NOT TOUCH, and why.** ADR-0074 §6.1 already asks *who implements this, not only who calls it*; this adds *what else has this shape, and is it in scope*. **AMENDED 2026-08-06 BY [ADR-0085](../docs/adr/ADR-0085-tenant-test-repository-disposition.md) §4.3 — THE SHAPE'S THIRD OCCURRENCE, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT SETTLES THAT THE COUNT IS NOT THE FINDING.** D-028 recorded the shape as one recurrence and it was four; D-057 recorded three capabilities' **own** publication SPIs — sibling interfaces in other packages, plausibly out of sight of an ADR scoped to *"the SPIs Section C's publication semantics ran through"*. **This occurrence has no such excuse: `TestManagementAdapter.createContainer` and `.createGrouping` sit in the CANONICAL FRAMEWORK INTERFACE, four lines from `publishTests`, `linkTraceability` and `findExistingTests` — three operations ADR-0072 and ADR-0074 widened, in the same file both ADRs edited.** Three of five widened, two passed over, **and the exclusion was not a package away; it was two lines away, twice, and no artefact recorded that it was made.** ADR-0085 widens both to `WriteOutcome<T>`, **adds the container/grouping discovery operation the split left unrepresentable**, and marks every consumer `UNDECIDED — <capability>` rather than deciding failure paths on three capabilities' behalf. **The pre-landing check above is therefore now earned twice, and the second time proximity did not help** — which is the argument for making it mechanical rather than diligent. |

| **D-068** | **A TEST THAT DOES NOT RUN IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A TEST THAT DOES NOT FAIL, AT EVERY LEVEL THE PLATFORM INSPECTS.** Observed 2026-08-05 during B1. Two readings of the same workspace suite, minutes apart: **`functional-testing-engine` 409 tests / `fail 0` / `EXIT 0`**, and **413 tests / `fail 0` / `EXIT 0`**. The four were not failing — **they were not running**, from a `dist` built before the framework change propagated. **Both readings exit 0. Both report `fail 0`. Both are green by every signal the platform reads.** The only thing that separates them is a total, and **nothing reads the total** — not the package script, not `run-all.js`, not any gate. | **D-008's CLASS WITH A MECHANISM D-008 DOES NOT COVER.** D-008 says *a control that has never been seen to fail is indistinguishable from one that cannot*, and its remedy is fault injection — plant a violation, observe the control fire. **That remedy assumes the control RUNS.** Here the control was never invoked at all, and non-invocation renders as success at every level: exit code, failure count, and the summary lines every prior session in this programme has quoted as evidence. **Fault injection cannot detect a probe that was not loaded.** It is also D-045's shape — *not a wrong result, a smaller one* — and D-015's vacuous green, arriving in the **measurement apparatus** rather than in a check. Its reach is every count this programme compares: **a stale `dist` reduces the denominator on both sides of a before/after comparison, so the delta reads zero while the coverage falls.** | Programme measurement discipline · the governance runner · every package `test` script | **A GATE READING THE TOTAL IS WRITABLE, AND IT IS CHEAP — VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED.** Every one of the 15 workspace projects emits a uniform machine-readable `ℹ tests N` (16 totals, `functional-testing-engine` emitting two because its script runs `.js` then `.mjs`). **So the mechanism is: parse the totals, compare against a committed baseline, fail on any DROP.** Not on any change — a drop. **Do NOT close it with a hard-coded expected total per package**: that is exactly the stale literal this register keeps finding, and B1 has just replaced two of them in the framework suite. The check is *did a total fall without a recorded reason*, and a rise needs only the baseline updated. **THE HONEST SCOPE NOTE: this surfaced through my own stale build, not through a platform defect** — and it is recorded because **the convention caught it and no mechanism would have.** The platform's *rebuilt-from-source* rule (ADR-0074 §6.2.3) is currently the only thing standing between this programme and a silently shrinking suite, and a convention is one reader's discipline, not a control. **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — `governance/verification/verify-suite-integrity.js`, built between ADR-0076 phases B2 and A1 and deliberately BEFORE the largest remaining change.** Three checks: every source test file has a compiled counterpart; **no compiled suite declares fewer tests than its source** (D-068's exact mechanism, and it needs NO baseline because the source IS the expectation, so the check cannot itself go stale); and no package declares fewer than a committed baseline (`suite-totals.json`, **1399 declared tests across 14 packages**). **It checks a DROP and never a pinned count** — a rise passes, is reported, and is re-cut with `--relock`, for the same reason the closure gate reports an added ADR rather than absorbing it. **PROVED BY THREE PLANTED FAULTS BEFORE IT WAS TRUSTED (D-008), each firing its intended branch and only its intended branch:** a deleted compiled suite → checks 1 and 2 fail; **four `test(` sites removed from the BUILT file only — D-068's mechanism reproduced exactly → check 2 alone fails**; three removed from source *and* dist → check 3 alone fails, checks 1 and 2 stay green. **Registered in `run-all.js` and PROVED in `proofs.json` through the registry's own harness**, so it is not a gate the programme merely has on disk. **Measured: governance 156 → 157 checks, and the failing-check set is otherwise byte-identical to a stashed clean tree.** |

| **D-067** | **NO TEST IN THE PLATFORM HAD EVER PLACED A NON-`ok` OUTCOME AT `architecture-review` — WHICH IS WHY A GREEN SUITE WAS CONSISTENT WITH A TRIAD STAGE NOTHING COULD HEAR.** Measured 2026-08-05 while closing D-066. Before the probe ADR-0076 §6 B1 required, `architecture-review` appeared in `capability-framework/test/framework.test.ts` **only inside `GOVERNANCE_TRIAD` iterations** — presence checks that delete a stage and assert the run is refused. **Stage 4 was never exercised negatively, in the framework suite or in any of the five capabilities'.** Confirmed by the repair's own blast radius: adding `architecture-certified` to the gate list moved **zero** assertions in any capability suite, and only two arity literals in the framework's own (`8` → `9`), **neither of which asserted a progression that depended on stage 4 being unheard**. | **THE ADR PREDICTED `progressedTo` WOULD SHIFT FOR ALL FIVE CAPABILITIES AND IT DID NOT MOVE ONE ASSERTION — and the reason is the finding, not a reprieve.** Every capability's `architecture-review` emits `ok`, so a gate that reads `ok` changes nothing they assert. **The consequence ADR-0076 §5 describes is real at runtime and invisible to the suites, for exactly the reason the defect survived: the case was never written.** This is D-008 read from the other side — *a control that has never been seen to fail is indistinguishable from one that cannot* — applied to a **stage** rather than to a gate. **A negative case that no test constructs is a branch no suite can report on**, and the platform's five capability suites collectively contained none for one third of its governance triad. | Platform — all five capabilities' conformance suites | **THE FRAMEWORK-LEVEL CASE NOW EXISTS AND THE PER-CAPABILITY ONES DO NOT.** The probe asserts the property over `GOVERNANCE_TRIAD` rather than over `architecture-review` alone — naming one stage would prove the instance and not the property. **What is still absent: a negative case at stage 4 in each capability's own suite**, which cannot be written until that capability decides what its architecture review refuses ON — `UNDECIDED — <capability>` per ADR-0076 §4.4, and deliberately not decided here. **Recorded so the absence is a known gap rather than a green column**, and so the next reader does not mistake B1's zero-movement result for evidence that the five capabilities were already sound at stage 4. **THE OWED WORK, NAMED: one case per capability placing a NON-`ok` outcome at stage 4. Five cases, none writable here.** |

| **D-075** | **THE FIFTEENTH SCOPE CORRECTION — A GATE WAS CLASSIFIED AS *LOSING ITS SUBJECT* WHEN IT WAS ABOUT TO DETECT THE THING IT WAS BUILT FOR, AND THE ACCEPTED ADR AUTHORISING THE CHANGE FORBIDS IT IN TWO PLACES.** Measured 2026-08-05 on a rebuilt tree while executing Section G. Section G's shape report §3 named **two** gates that lose their subject, §9.4 added a **third**, and the restated instruction carried all three. **There is a fourth, and it is on none of the lists: `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js`, registered `gating: true` at `run-all.js:115`.** Two of its eight properties fall to G and **neither depends on the cross-package `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` question the instruction ties this file to**: **RC-4** (`:38`, `:91`) requires `fs.existsSync(FTE/src/capability.ts)` — **PART 1 alone deletes it**; **RC-3** (`:85`) fails if `authoring-bridge.mjs` names the canonical bridge — **PART 3 makes it do exactly that.** The gate's model (`runtime-cutover-readiness.ts`, ten preconditions) records **nine of ten UNMET** — including `realRequestTranslatorImplemented: false`, the translator PART 3 needs to build a `CanonicalCapabilityInput` at all — and carries a named verdict for precisely this state: **`inconsistent-premature-cutover`**. | **THE SEVERITY IS INVERTED, AND THE INVERSION IS THE FINDING.** The instruction names the vacuous green (`:85` falling back to `''` on a deleted gateway) as *"the worse failure"*. **Measured: if the cross-package deletion is declined — and it should be — that failure mode CANNOT OCCUR.** What occurs instead is RC-3 going **RED because the live authoring path really has been rerouted**, which is the gate working correctly. **A gate going vacuously green is a control that stopped measuring; a gate going correctly red that is then re-pointed to restore green is a control that was OVERRULED — and only the second requires someone to decide to do it.** **ADR-0061, the accepted authority G runs under, withholds this in two places and contradicts itself in a third:** §6 **step 4** names `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness` among five gates to **MIGRATE**, and step 4 precedes step 6; §6 **step 7** puts *"routing the live gateway"* **out of scope**, *"repository canonicalization does not perform it"*; and its acceptance banner states it does **NOT** authorise deleting legacy before behavioural equivalence is verified (`behaviouralEquivalenceDemonstrated: false`). **The contradiction: §6 step 6 places "re-point `authoring-bridge.mjs`" IN scope while step 7 places "routing the live gateway" OUT of scope, and ADR-0049 §2 states in its own words that the live path IS `ip-execute-gateway.mjs → authoring-bridge.mjs`. For this one file the two steps are the same act** — D-069's class, a scope error inside an ACCEPTED ADR, and RC-3 is the control built to catch it. **No symbol scan could have found this: the gate references no FTE symbol, only a file PATH and the ABSENCE of a string** — D-007's seam arriving inside the shape report's own measurement method. | Programme governance · ADR-0061 §6 (steps 4/6/7) · ADR-0049 (PROPOSED, nine of ten preconditions unmet) · `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` | **STOPPED BEFORE THE FIRST DELETION, PER THE BINDING'S OWN CONDITION, AND WHAT IS OWED IS ONE RULING RATHER THAN WORK** — recorded in [`SECTION_G_SHAPE_REPORT.md`](SECTION_G_SHAPE_REPORT.md) §10. **The cross-package scope decision the instruction required IS TAKEN and did not need the stop: `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` is NOT deleted inside G** — ADR-0061 §6 step 6 defers it (*"already slated for M5"*), a **surviving** module declares it in the governed package-governance contract (`src/contract/package-governance.ts:166`), and `verify-package-governance.js:518` reads it unguarded so the gate would **throw** rather than fail. **The owed ruling is whether the programme performs operational cut-over now with nine preconditions unmet, or stops at repository canonicalization.** **PART 1 WITHOUT PART 3 IS NOT AVAILABLE** — the bridge imports all three deleted modules, so Part 1 and Part 3 are one change *because the bridge makes them one*, which is the identical coupling ADR-0061 §6 failed to see. **Recommended: amend or supersede ADR-0049 FIRST, and MIGRATE this gate per §6 step 4 rather than delete it** — one ADR, against an irreversible change to the platform's only live authoring path. **Do NOT close this by re-pointing the gate:** it is the one control that distinguishes *the cut-over was authorised* from *the cut-over happened*. |

| **D-080** | **THREE PLACES WHERE ADR-0077 DESCRIBES A MECHANISM SLIGHTLY OFF FROM WHAT IMPLEMENTING IT REQUIRES — ONE CLASS, THREE INSTANCES, NONE CHANGING THE DECISION.** Found 2026-08-05 while executing §6 steps 1–2. **(i) §4.6's table splits CU-3 and CU-4 into *implemented* + *bound* and folds CU-5's implemented half away**, listing only a combined `CU-4b/5b bound` row — so the transport's implemented half, which `verify-runtime-enablement` RE-1 measures and which holds, had no id. Implemented as **CU-5 (measurable, dispatch-only)** plus separate `CU-4b`/`CU-5b`, giving fourteen declared preconditions. **(ii) §7 records RC-5 among the properties that are "unchanged"** — its PROPERTY is unchanged (readiness admits no shortcut) but its CHECK reads the model's source text, and the model's source changed shape, so the regex had to be re-expressed (`p.met` → `p.satisfied`, `CU-\d+` → `CU-[0-9]+[a-z]?`). A property and the check that detects it are not the same object, and §7 priced them as one. **(iii) §8 classifies `retirement-inventory.mjs` as "read or executed … unmodified by decision"**, but RC-3′ needs per-entry reachability the tool did not publish, so it gained an additive `livePathReach` block. | **THIS IS THE CLASS D-069 NAMED, AT ITS SMALLEST AND MOST BENIGN SIZE, AND IT IS RECORDED AT THAT SIZE DELIBERATELY.** Each is a drafting gap in a **frozen** document, discovered only by building the thing it describes; none changes the decision, so **per the acceptance banner none is written back into the ADR.** The generalisation worth keeping: **an ADR that specifies a gate's properties is specifying an implementation, and the distance between "the property is unchanged" and "the check is unchanged" is exactly where §7's estimate went wrong** — the same shape as D-069's *"a composition's surface is the units plus the type distance between them"*, applied to a control rather than to a composition. | [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §4.6 · §7 · §8 · `governance/capability/retirement-inventory.mjs` | **CLOSED BY IMPLEMENTATION, RECORDED RATHER THAN AMENDED.** Fourteen preconditions are declared and the reference suite asserts the CU-3/CU-3b split explicitly, so the omission cannot recur silently. **The ADR is NOT amended** — a frozen decision that absorbs every implementation detail found while executing it becomes a running log, and the property that makes it worth reading is that it did not move. |

| **D-081** | **ADR-0077 §4.8 STATES RC-4′ AS A CONJUNCTION OF THREE, AND THE LITERAL READING WOULD MAKE IT RED AT STEP 1 — WHICH §6 STEP 1's OWN SENTENCE CONTRADICTS.** Measured 2026-08-05 implementing the gate. §4.8 says RC-4′ *"asserts all three of: (1) the canonical entry point exists…; (2) **the legacy modules are absent**; (3) the CU-6a evidence artefact exists…"*. **Conjunct (2) is FALSE on every tree before §6 step 6, so a literal RC-4′ is red from step 1 to step 6** — while §6 step 1 says *"RC-3′ **is** red at this point"*, naming exactly one red. Two clauses of the same ADR describe different numbers of failing properties at the same boundary. | **A CONJUNCTION OF THREE IS NOT AN ORDERING CLAIM; IT IS A DESCRIPTION OF THE END STATE.** §4.8's own heading — *"replace-before-remove becomes an ORDERING claim evidenced, not a coexistence claim observed"* — is the decision, and the ordering it describes is an **implication**: `canonicalPresent AND (legacyPresent OR equivalenceEvidenceCurrent)`. That form is green in both legal states (both present, before; legacy removed with current evidence, after), **red exactly on the state the property exists to forbid** (legacy removed with no current evidence), and it never becomes vacuous, because after the deletion it requires live evidence rather than an absence. The literal conjunction would also have made RC-4′ **permanently unfalsifiable in the other direction** — nothing distinguishes "removed correctly" from "removed at all" once (2) is a requirement rather than a case. | [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §4.8 vs §6 step 1 · `governance/verification/verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` | **IMPLEMENTED AS THE IMPLICATION, WHICH IS WHAT THE HEADING DECIDES; THE CONJUNCTION IS THE DRAFTING.** Measured on this tree: RC-4′ **PASS** — *"both compositions present — the replacement is available and nothing has been removed"*, with RC-3′ the single red, exactly as §6 step 1 says. **Not amended:** the decision (ordering, evidenced) is unchanged, and §4.8's third conjunct survives intact as the condition on the removed case. **Owed at §6 step 6:** the evidence artefact's `corpusDigest` must match what was measured, or RC-4′ goes red — the intended behaviour, and it will read as an obstruction to whoever changes the corpus. |

| **D-082** | **ADR-0077 §4.8 SPECIFIES A PROBE WHOSE EXPECTED OUTCOME IS *GREEN*, AND THE FAULT-PROOF HARNESS CANNOT EXPRESS ONE.** Measured 2026-08-05. §4.8's re-anchored probe set ends with *"plant a comment naming the canonical bridge → **GREEN** — the probe that proves the name-match defect is gone"*. `record-fault-proofs.js` records a proof only when `cleanRunExit === 0 && faultedRunExit === 1 && causeNamedInOutput`; **every probe it can hold is a probe that must go RED.** A negative control — *this input must NOT trip the gate* — has no shape in the harness, and `verify-governance-self-validation` §3 audits for exactly that triple, so a green-expecting entry would be recorded as a weak proof rather than as a passing one. | **THE PROGRAMME'S PROOF HARNESS PROVES ONLY ONE OF THE TWO THINGS R-13.4 ASKS FOR.** It proves a control fires on a real violation; it has no way to record that a control does **not** fire on a lookalike. That asymmetry is invisible while every gate's failure mode is under-detection — and it becomes load-bearing the moment a gate is repaired for **over**-detection, which is precisely what debt D-079 was: RC-3 fired on a comment. **A repair whose whole content is "this no longer fires" cannot be evidenced by a harness that only records firing.** | `governance/verification/record-fault-proofs.js` (runner, `proved` predicate) · `verify-governance-self-validation.js` §3 · [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §4.8 | **RUN AS A HAND-RECORDED NEGATIVE CONTROL AND THE RESULT IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** The exact comment RC-3's own fault proof used (`// premature-cutover probe: createRuntimeEntryPointBridge`) was planted above the same anchor import and the migrated gate re-run: **RC-3′'s verdict line and its detail line are byte-identical planted and unplanted**, and the bridge was restored byte-identical. **D-079's defect is gone, demonstrated rather than argued.** **OWED, and it is a harness change rather than a gate change: a negative-control mode in `record-fault-proofs.js`** (`expectPass`, requiring `faultedRunExit === 0` and an unchanged verdict line) so this class of repair can be registered instead of witnessed. Until then the control is re-run by hand at §6 step 5. |

| **D-083** | **D-009's CIRCULARITY ARRIVED WHERE IT WAS PREDICTED: A CORRECTLY RED GATE CANNOT CARRY A RECORDED PROOF, AND FOUR PROBES WENT UNPROVED THE MOMENT RC-3′ WENT RED.** Measured 2026-08-05 at §6 step 2's boundary. `record-fault-proofs.js` requires a **clean leg** — the gate must exit 0 on the repository as it stands — before any fault it plants counts as detected. RC-3′ is red between ADR-0077 §6 steps 1 and 5 **by design and by ruling**, so all four re-anchored cut-over probes record `clean=1 faulted=1` → `proved: false`, and `verify-governance-self-validation` §3 lists them among its weak proofs (8 → 12 entries; the gate was already red for the other eight, so no gate changed state). | **THE PRESSURE D-041 NAMED IS NOW STANDING ON THIS GATE, AND IT IS BEING DECLINED IN WRITING.** The cheap resolutions are both available and both are weakenings: **delete the four probes** so the registry is tidy (losing the coverage that makes step 5's green meaningful), or **re-point RC-3′ to green** so the clean leg exists (overruling the control at the moment it detects the thing it was built for). **Neither is taken.** The probes are kept, anchored to the CURRENT source so none is inert, and each expects a SPECIFIC property's `FAIL RC-n` line rather than the gate's summary — because a probe matching the summary would "detect" the red that was already there, which is the incidental-detection weakness the harness's own header warns about. | debt D-009 (circularity) · debt D-041 (pressure to weaken) · `record-fault-proofs.js` · `verify-governance-self-validation.js` §3 · [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §6 steps 1–5 | **OPEN BY DESIGN UNTIL §6 STEP 5, AND SELF-CLOSING THERE.** The four probes prove themselves the instant the re-composition turns RC-3′ green; nothing else is owed. **The proof set for this gate went 5 → 4** — the fifth is D-082's negative control, which the harness cannot hold. **Recorded as a number rather than absorbed:** four probes registered and unproved is an honest state; four probes deleted would have been a quiet reduction in what step 5 has to satisfy. |

| **D-084** | **CU-6a IS MEASURED, AND IT DOES NOT HOLD — THE CANONICAL COMPOSITION PRODUCES NO PACKAGE BODY AT ALL, ON EVERY ENTRY OF THE DECLARED CORPUS.** Measured 2026-08-05 on a rebuilt tree, both paths, ADR-0077 §6 step 3; artefact `governance/capability/authoring-equivalence-evidence.json`, `corpusDigest 7d955bbe…e264`. Six corpus entries — the four bridge-executing gates' F1 fixtures plus the two `selectorDiscovery: null` refusal variants two of them derive — run through `authorViaFTE` and through `createCanonicalRunnerCapability(...).runThroughRunner(...)` with the SAME tail (the bridge's own `groundOperations`, `publishReasoningResults`, `assembleExecutionPackage`). **Two independent causes, both measured, neither in §4.7's seven. (i) THE EVIDENCE GATE.** The canonical runner emits **one** audit event vocabulary — `stage.completed`, twelve entries. The registry's declared evidence names are **agent-level** events the legacy engine emits (`story.requirement-extraction`, `test.positive`, `automation.manifest`, `selector-intelligence.certifyAuthoring`, …): **18 declared, 0 matched.** `reasoning-publication.ts:151` matches declared names against events the run recorded; `reasoning-result-registry.ts:375` refuses any capability publishing with no evidence (`CERTIFICATION_FAILURE`). `requirement-intelligence` is first in declared order, so the chain is blocked at its head and **no body is projected**. **(ii) THE GROUNDING VOCABULARY.** `groundOperations` (authoring-bridge.mjs:383–393) dispatches on the closed set `navigate | input | select | click | assert`; `CanonicalTestCase.steps[].action` is **prose** (`test-management-intelligence.ts:172–175`: *"establish precondition: …"*, *"verify: …"*). Seven distinct canonical actions, **zero** in the grounding vocabulary. E-3 measures **125/155/234 legacy operations → 0 canonical** wherever selector discovery is present. | **E-7 IS A STOP CONDITION AND THIS IS THE STOP.** ADR-0077 §4.5: *"a difference not in §4.7 fails CU-6a, and the only way to make it pass is to amend §4.7."* Cause (ii) is the one the ADR pre-classified: §4.5 E-3 says grounding *"is the bridge's own reasoning and is unchanged by the re-composition, **so a difference here is a defect, not a design difference**"* — and it was measured against the bridge's OWN function, exported for the measurement rather than copied, so the difference cannot be an artefact of a second implementation. **Cause (i) is the larger finding and the ADR does not anticipate it at all.** §4.3 lists `publishReasoningResults` under *"harvest.state RE-TYPED"* and everything else UNCHANGED — but the registry does not only consume `state`; it consumes `audit`, and it consumes it as **proof that the reasoning happened**. Re-typing the harvest supplies the *values*; nothing supplies the *evidence*, and the registry is built to refuse exactly that. **§6 step 4 is scoped to ten fields of `ReasoningHarvest.state`; the measured obligation is larger than the step that was written to discharge it.** | ADR-0077 [§4.5 E-3/E-7](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) · §4.7 · §6 steps 3–5 · `reasoning-publication.ts:151` · `reasoning-result-registry.ts:375` · `capability-model.ts:402–450` · `test-management-intelligence.ts:172–175` · `authoring-bridge.mjs:383–393` · debt D-041 | **RULED 2026-08-05 — ROUTE (b): AMEND §4.3 AND §6 STEP 4, THEN RE-MEASURE. §4.7 STAYS AT SEVEN.** **(a) admit both to §4.7 — REJECTED**, on the one thing §4.7 is for: its seven are capabilities the canonical path **does not have and will not**; these two are things the re-composition **must supply** and the ADR did not scope. **Admitting them would declare a defect equivalent, which is exactly what E-7 forbids** — the difference set is the entire content of the word *equivalent*, so an entry naming a gap rather than a design boundary empties it. **(b) amend the migration — TAKEN.** §4.3 gains **§4.3.1** (publication is not unchanged; the registry consumes `audit` as proof the reasoning happened); **§6 step 4** re-scoped from *ten fields* to *ten fields AND the evidence the declared names expect*, with three sub-routes scoped and **not** decided; **§6 step 5a** records the grounding vocabulary as a defect (**D-086**). **(c) halt — not taken.** **What was NOT done, in writing:** no tolerance widened, no difference classified into a neighbouring §4.7 entry, no narrower corpus substituted to obtain a passing artefact — D-041's standing pressure, declined at the first opportunity anyone had to yield to it. **Still re-measurable, because nothing is deleted or re-composed — which is the whole reason step 3 preceded step 5.** Artefact now TRACKED (**D-085**); the scoping pattern recorded as **D-087**. |
