| **D-085** | **THE ONE EVIDENCE ARTEFACT THAT CANNOT BE REGENERATED IS STORED UNDER THE RULE THAT ASSUMES ALL OF THEM CAN.** Measured 2026-08-05 producing the CU-6a artefact. `.gitignore:115` ignores `governance/**/*-evidence.json`, and **`git ls-files 'governance/capability/*-evidence.json'` returns 0** — every evidence artefact in the tree is an untracked, regenerable build product, deliberately and correctly, because every one of them can be regenerated by re-running its gate. **`authoring-equivalence-evidence.json` cannot.** ADR-0077 §5 states it in the ADR's own words: *"CU-6a is measurable **once**, on a tree where both paths exist, and RC-4′(3) preserves that measurement by digest rather than by re-running it."* After §6 step 6 deletes the legacy modules there is no second implementation, so the artefact is not a cache of a computation — **it is the only record that the computation ever happened.** | **RC-4′(3) IS LOAD-BEARING AND ITS SUBJECT IS NOT UNDER VERSION CONTROL.** RC-4′ permits the legacy modules to be absent *only where* the CU-6a evidence is present and current; on a clean clone the artefact is absent, so after the retirement RC-4′ reads `LEGACY REMOVED WITHOUT CURRENT EQUIVALENCE EVIDENCE` and the gate is red for everyone but the machine that ran the measurement. **The failure is not that the gate breaks — it is that the gate breaking is the *good* outcome.** The bad one is the artefact being regenerated on a post-deletion tree by someone clearing a red gate: with only one path left there is nothing to compare, and whatever such a run wrote would be a document rather than a measurement. | `.gitignore:115` · [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §4.8 RC-4′(3), §5 · `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` §5 · debt D-084 | **SETTLED 2026-08-05, ON RULING, AND THE REASONING LANDED IN THE DECISION RATHER THAN IN A COMMENT.** `.gitignore` now carries `!governance/capability/authoring-equivalence-evidence.json` — verified scoped: `git check-ignore` reports the negation as the last matching pattern for this path and `governance/**/*-evidence.json` still ignores every other artefact. The exception sits alongside the two already carved for `governance/verification/proofs.json` and `governance/closure/baseline.json`, for the same reason: **consumed evidence, not produced output.** The reasoning is in **ADR-0077 §5** — including the dangerous repair, regenerating the artefact on a post-deletion tree where nothing is left to compare — because a `.gitignore` comment is not where anyone looks before clearing a red gate. **RESIDUE, OPEN AND THE PROGRAMME OWNER'S:** whether a generator that can only ever be run once should be able to overwrite its own artefact at all. Today `measure-authoring-equivalence.mjs` can, and after §6 step 6 doing so silently destroys the record. |

| **D-086** | **THE GROUNDING VOCABULARY IS CLOSED AND THE CANONICAL STEP IS PROSE — A DEFECT, RULED AS ONE, NOT A DESIGN DIFFERENCE.** Measured 2026-08-05 (ADR-0077 §6 step 3), ruled 2026-08-05. `groundOperations` (`authoring-bridge.mjs:383–393`) dispatches on the closed set `navigate \| input \| select \| click \| assert`. `CanonicalTestCase.steps[].action` is **prose** — `test-management-intelligence.ts:172–175` emits *"establish precondition: …"* and *"verify: …"*. **Seven distinct canonical step actions, zero in the vocabulary; 125 / 155 / 234 legacy grounded operations become 0.** Measured against the bridge's **own** `groundOperations`, exported for the measurement rather than copied, so it cannot be an artefact of a second implementation. | **ADR-0077 §4.5's E-3 PRE-CLASSIFIED THIS EXACT CASE AND THE RULING HELD TO IT:** grounding *"is the bridge's own reasoning and is unchanged by the re-composition, **so a difference here is a defect, not a design difference**."* **It was NOT admitted to §4.7.** §4.7's seven are capabilities the canonical path does not have and will not; this is a thing the re-composition must supply. Admitting it would have declared a defect equivalent — the failure E-7 exists to refuse, at the first opportunity anyone had to commit it. | ADR-0077 [§4.5 E-3](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) · §6 step 5a (added by amendment) · `authoring-bridge.mjs:383–393` · `test-management-intelligence.ts:172–175` · debt D-084 | **OPEN — SCOPED INTO ADR-0077 §6 STEP 5a, REPAIR NOT YET CHOSEN.** Two: **widen the vocabulary** to recognise what the canonical step carries, or **have the canonical step carry a structured action beside its prose** — a typed field grounding dispatches on, leaving the prose as the readable statement it already is. The second keeps dispatch closed and puts structure where the authoring decision is made; the first moves requirement→control interpretation into the bridge, which is closer to the AI Selector Intelligence `authoring-bridge.mjs:344–351` records as **deferred**. **The prohibition that makes this a defect rather than a choice:** no repair may map prose to an action by pattern-matching the prose. A bridge that infers `click` from the word *"submit"* has authored an operation nothing certified. |

| **D-087** | **THIRD INSTANCE IN THREE SECTIONS: A STEP'S SCOPE WRITTEN AGAINST A PARTIAL PICTURE OF WHAT IT TOUCHES — AND THIS ONE CHANGED THE DECISION.** Recorded 2026-08-05 on the ruling of ADR-0077 §6 step 3. Three instances, same shape, escalating: **(i) ADR-0061 §6** scoped *"re-point `authoring-bridge.mjs`"* as repository canonicalization while §6 step 7 put *"routing the live gateway"* out of scope — **one file, two steps, and for that file they are the same act** (D-076). **(ii) ADR-0077 §4.8** stated RC-4′ as a conjunction of three including *"the legacy modules are absent"*, which the literal reading makes red at step 1 — contradicted by §6 step 1's own sentence (D-081). **(iii) ADR-0077 §6 step 4** scoped the harvest re-founding to **ten fields of `ReasoningHarvest.state`**, when the registry also consumes `harvest.audit` **as proof the reasoning happened** and refuses any capability publishing without it. **0 of 18 declared evidence names matched on the canonical path; no package body was produced at all.** | **THE ESCALATION IS THE FINDING, AND D-080 IS WHY IT NEEDS ITS OWN ENTRY.** D-080 records three drafting gaps in the same ADR and closes with *"none changes the decision, so per the acceptance banner none is written back."* **This one does change the decision** — §4.3 asserted publication was unchanged and it is not — so it was written back, and ADR-0077 is amended rather than annotated. **The distinguishing question is available before the work starts and was not asked in any of the three: what does this step's subject CONSUME, as opposed to what does it PRODUCE?** All three scoped by produced values — a file's exports, a gate's properties, a harvest's fields — and all three were wrong about a consumed one: the gateway's routing, step 1's own ordering, and the registry's evidence channel. **The values were re-typed and the proof was left behind**, which is the same shape as D-069's *"a composition's surface is the units plus the type distance between them"* with the distance running through a second input rather than a type. | Programme governance · [ADR-0061](../docs/adr/ADR-0061-canonical-functional-capability-runtime-adoption.md) §6 · [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §4.3.1, §4.8, §6 step 4 · debt D-069 (the class) · D-076 · D-080 · D-081 · D-084 | **OPEN — AND THE COUNTER-MEASURE IS A QUESTION, NOT A GATE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN AUTOMATED.** No gate in this platform can read an ADR's step scope against the consumption graph of the module it names; D-011 already records that the suite verifies artefacts and not reasoning. **What is available:** before scoping a step that re-founds a component, enumerate that component's INPUTS and name which of them the step supplies — the measurement that would have caught (iii) is one grep for what `reasoning-publication.ts` reads off `harvest`, and it takes a minute. **Recorded as a standing instance count rather than closed:** a fourth occurrence in ADR-0077 would make this a property of the document rather than of three steps, and that is a different finding with a different repair. |

| **D-088** | **THE REASONING RESULT REGISTRY'S PROOF-OF-REASONING IS BOUND TO THE RUNTIME BEING RETIRED — ITS DECLARED EVIDENCE NAMES ARE LEGACY AGENT IDENTITIES, AND STEP 4 IS WHERE THIS SURFACED, NOT WHERE IT ORIGINATED.** Measured 2026-08-05 (ADR-0077 §6 step 3). `CAPABILITY_DECLARATIONS[*].evidence` names 18 audit-derived strings — `story.requirement-extraction`, `test.positive`, `automation.generation`, … — matched by substring against the run's audit events. **They match `agent.<id>.invoked`. They ARE the fifteen legacy agents debt D-035 named**, and §6 step 6 deletes every one of them. `reasoning-result-registry.ts:375` refuses any capability publishing with no evidence, so **after the retirement the registry's proof-of-reasoning has no producer**. Measured both paths on one tree: legacy **16 of 18** matched, canonical **0 of 18**. | **THIS WAS TRUE BEFORE THIS SESSION AND NOBODY HAD ASKED, WHICH IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE 0-OF-18.** ADR-0067 built the registry so that *"an output nothing proves was produced is a claim"* — a property this register regards as one of the platform's strongest. **What ADR-0067 did not state is WHOSE events constitute proof**, and the implementation answered that question by naming the only runtime that existed. So the registry's central guarantee is expressed in the vocabulary of a runtime three ADRs have since resolved to retire, and **no gate could have detected it**: every declaration resolves, every name matches on the live path, and the suite is green. It becomes visible only when a SECOND runtime publishes — which is to say, only during a cut-over, which is the one time the answer matters. **ADR-0067 owes the general statement: proof-of-reasoning is a property of the CAPABILITY, and the events that constitute it are a declaration the capability owns, not a list of the agents that happened to implement it.** | [ADR-0067](../docs/adr/ADR-0067-reasoning-result-registry.md) — the registry, and the ADR this bears on · `registry/capability-model.ts` (11 of 22 capabilities) · `registry/reasoning-publication.ts:151` · `registry/reasoning-result-registry.ts:375` · debt D-035 (the fifteen agents) · D-084 · D-087 | **OPEN AGAINST ADR-0067, AND NOT CLOSABLE BY ADR-0077 §6 STEP 4.** Step 4b(b) makes the declarations **additive** — both vocabularies live between steps 5 and 6, legacy reduced at step 7 — which restores a working platform and **leaves this question exactly where it is**: after step 7 the names will be canonical domain identities instead of legacy agent identities, and the registry will be bound to the *new* runtime in the same way it was bound to the old one. **The migration fixes the instance; only ADR-0067 can fix the class.** What is owed is one statement in ADR-0067 §4 and a shape for `evidence` that names the capability's own reasoning rather than its current implementation's units — recorded here rather than attempted in passing, because deciding what constitutes proof for twenty-two capabilities is the expensive half and belongs with an owner. |

| **D-089** | **A LIVE VACUOUS GREEN INSIDE THE REGISTRY ADR-0067 BUILT TO PREVENT IT — `grounded-authoring` DECLARES TWO EVIDENCE NAMES NOTHING HAS EVER EMITTED, AND PASSES.** Measured 2026-08-05 on both paths. `CAPABILITY_DECLARATIONS['grounded-authoring'].evidence` declares `selector-intelligence.bestExecutable` and `selector-intelligence.certifyAuthoring`. **Nothing in this repository emits either — not the legacy engine, not the canonical composition, not the bridge.** `0 of 2` on the **legacy** path, which is the live path today, and `0 of 2` on the canonical one. The capability certifies anyway, because its publisher supplies `composedFrom(…)` evidence instead of `auditEvidence(…)`, and `reasoning-result-registry.ts:375` only requires the evidence list to be non-empty. The two unmatched names are recorded in `unobservedEvidence` — **a field written at `reasoning-result-registry.ts:399` and read by nothing, in no gate and no test.** | **THIS IS PASSING TODAY, ON THE LIVE PATH, AND IT IS NOT A STEP-4 RISK.** It is the vacuous-green class (D-011, D-015) inside the mechanism whose entire purpose is to refuse claims — and it is worse than the instances those entries record, because **the registry DETECTED it, wrote it down, and nothing reads what it wrote.** `unobservedEvidence` is the platform noticing correctly and reporting into a void; D-013's shape one level out, where the value contradicting the verdict is not merely present in the output but was computed *for the purpose of* contradicting it. **The two names also demonstrate the escape route:** a publisher on `composedFrom` clears the non-empty gate with no audit at all, which is why ADR-0077 §6 step 4 forbids switching a publisher to `composedFrom` *because its audit evidence stopped matching*. **That prohibition governs the migration and does nothing about this instance, which predates it.** | [ADR-0067](../docs/adr/ADR-0067-reasoning-result-registry.md) · `registry/capability-model.ts` (`grounded-authoring`) · `registry/reasoning-publication.ts` (`composedFrom`) · `registry/reasoning-result-registry.ts:375,399` · debt D-011 · D-015 · D-041 · D-088 | **OPEN — AND THE REPAIR IS NOT "DELETE THE TWO NAMES", WHICH IS THE OBVIOUS ONE AND IS A WEAKENING.** Three questions, in order, and the first is the real one: **(i) is `grounded-authoring`'s proof genuinely its certified upstream inputs**, in which case `composedFrom` is correct and the two audit names are a leftover that should never have been declared — **or is it the selector-intelligence reasoning it is named for**, in which case the evidence is genuinely missing and the capability has been certifying without it since ADR-0067; **(ii) `unobservedEvidence` is written and read by nothing — either a gate reads it or it should not be computed**, and *"declared evidence nothing emits"* is exactly the condition it was built to surface; **(iii) whether a declaration whose names no producer emits should be REPRESENTABLE at all** — D-012's preferred form, applied to the evidence declaration rather than to the readiness input. **Deleting the names answers none of the three and makes the instance invisible.** |

| **D-090** | **CU-6a RE-MEASURED AFTER §6 STEPS 4 AND 5: THE FIRST TWO CAUSES ARE CLOSED AND TWO MORE ARE MEASURED, SAME CLASS, STILL OUTSIDE §4.7.** Measured 2026-08-05 on a rebuilt tree, both compositions present. The evidence gate is cleared — the canonical run's first refusal moved `requirement-intelligence` → `coverage-analysis` → `repository-composition`, i.e. six capabilities now certify that previously could not — and the grounding vocabulary is repaired. **CAUSE-3 — THE CANONICAL COMPOSITION COMPOSES NO AUTOMATION REPOSITORY.** Four registry capabilities (`repository-composition`, `manifest-generation`, `repository-digest-generation`, `dependency-graph-generation`) read `state.automation` and `state.automationManifest` — **emitter outputs the legacy engine materialised at execution-planning**. The canonical composition produces an automation ARCHITECTURE (`architectureComponents`, `logicalModules`, `dependencyGraph`, `materializationPlan`) and never materialises an asset. The emitters SURVIVE the retirement, but `composeRepository`/`repositoryInventory` take the legacy `TestCase` and read it through `businessTaxonomy` and `defaultEmitter.emit`, which `CanonicalTestCase` does not satisfy. **CAUSE-4 — NO OBSERVED-OUTCOME CHANNEL.** `CanonicalCapabilityInput` declares none and `ExecutionInput` names only the automation architecture, so `reflectViaFTE`'s closed loop cannot reason over the Execution Plane's returned outcomes at all. Measured suite state after steps 4–5: **428 pass / 0 fail** (TS) and **82 pass / 14 fail** (`.mjs`) — nine authoring-bridge tests on cause 3, five closed-loop tests on cause 4. | **THE PATTERN D-087 NAMED IS NOW AT FOUR INSTANCES INSIDE ONE ADR, AND THAT IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE TWO GAPS.** D-087's standing count said a fourth occurrence in ADR-0077 *"makes this a property of the DOCUMENT rather than of three steps, and that is a different finding with a different repair."* **It has occurred.** §4.3 enumerated what the re-composition changes by walking the bridge's own call sequence — translate, run, ground, publish, assemble — and every gap since has been a CONSUMER of that sequence that the walk could not see: the registry's audit channel, the grounding vocabulary, the emitter-derived automation state, the observed-outcome channel. **Four gaps, one method, and the method is reading a producer's code to enumerate a consumer's needs.** Cause 4 is the more serious of the two: it is not a migration gap but a **capability the canonical composition structurally cannot have**, and unlike §4.7's seven it was never declared. | ADR-0077 [§4.3](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) · §4.5 E-7 · §4.7 · §6 steps 4–5 · `registry/reasoning-publication.ts` (the four automation publishers) · `emitters/repository.ts` · `domains/execution.ts` (`ExecutionInput`) · debt D-084 · D-086 · D-087 | **CAUSE-3 IS CLOSED (2026-08-05, §6 step 5b): the automation repository is composed from the canonical architecture through the SURVIVING emitters, and the canonical path now PRODUCES A PACKAGE BODY.** `CanonicalTestCase` is projected into the `TestCase` shape `composeRepository`/`repositoryInventory` read, with ADR-0069 P-69.2's four reductions left at their empty values because they are §4.7's declared entries 1–4 and deriving them would convert a declared difference into a fabricated equivalence. **Measured after: E-1, E-2, E-5 and E-6 hold on every corpus entry**; suites 428/0 (TS) and 91/5 (`.mjs`), from 82/14. **What remains is no longer migration wiring — it is E-3 and E-4, which are differences in WHAT THE TWO RUNTIMES AUTHOR** (234 vs 4 grounded operations; 6 of 9 coverage dimensions measured vs 3 of 9), and they are a distinct question from causes 1–4. **CAUSE-4's CHANNEL CAN BE SUPPLIED ENTIRELY FROM THE INTELLIGENCE PLANE — MEASURED 2026-08-05, AND IT CORRECTS MY OWN EARLIER REPORT.** I reported that `ExecutionAdapter` carries *"three writes and no read"* as evidence the channel was structurally absent. **That framing assumed the Intelligence Plane must PULL. It does not — the Execution Plane PUSHES.** Measured: the EP POSTs observed outcomes to `/v1/evidence`; `ip-execute-gateway.mjs:597–601` reads `body.outcomes`, runs them through `guardReturnedOutcomes` (the sovereignty guard), joins them to the cached F1 context by `correlationId` and hands them to `reflectViaFTE`. **The data already crosses the boundary, already passes a guard, and already arrives in this plane.** The gap is entirely internal: `reflectViaFTE` receives `observed` and `CanonicalCapabilityInput` has no field to put it in. **NO EP-SIDE CONTRACT CHANGE IS REQUIRED, and this is NOT a fifth instance of the cross-plane ceiling.** The owed work is IP-side and bounded: an optional observed-outcomes field on `CanonicalCapabilityInput`, a way for the `execution` domain to REPORT observed outcomes instead of executing its own on the reflection pass, and the existing execution→healing→defect→synchronisation→reporting thread carrying them. **The one design decision inside it:** the canonical `execution` domain currently derives `outcomeSummary` from components it ran through the runtime connector, so it must be able to distinguish *"I executed this"* from *"I am reporting what the Execution Plane executed"* — which is ADR-0071's could-not-reach/said-no distinction at a third boundary, and is the part that needs an ADR rather than a patch. · **STOPPED AT §6 STEP 5a AND RETURNED TO THE PROGRAMME OWNER — E-7 APPLIED AS RULED, AND STEPS 6–7 ARE NOT STARTED.** The instruction was explicit: *"an eighth difference stops the change and comes back to me."* **It is not classified into §4.7, no tolerance is widened, and the corpus is unchanged** (`corpusDigest 7d955bbe…e264`, unmoved across all three measurements). **The control agrees:** RC-4′ permits the legacy modules to be absent only where the equivalence evidence is present AND `equivalent: true`; it is `false`, so the deletion is refused by the gate as well as by the ruling. **The tree is left mid-change deliberately** — §6 records that no valid boundary exists between steps 4 and 6, and stopping inside it with 14 red tests is the honest state, not a broken one. **Both compositions still exist, so CU-6a remains measurable** — which is what makes any of the three continuations available. |

| **D-091** | **HIGHEST-SEVERITY HARNESS FINDING IN THE PROGRAMME — THE INSTRUMENT THE WHOLE CUT-OVER RESTS ON PRODUCED A GREEN EQUIVALENCE PROOF BETWEEN ONE IMPLEMENTATION AND ITSELF, ON THE LAST TREE WHERE THE COMPARISON WAS POSSIBLE.** Found 2026-08-05 executing §6 step 5a. §6 step 3 reads *"unrepeatable after step 5 and is the reason it precedes it"*; §6 step 5 reads *"Re-measure CU-6a."* **Both cannot be true.** What step 5 removes is the bridge's route to the legacy composition; what step 6 removes is the composition. Between them the legacy modules are still on disk and still constructible, so the measurement is available — and the first re-measure after step 5 silently compared the canonical path against ITSELF, because the harness reached "legacy" through `authorViaFTE`, which step 5 had just re-pointed. | **A MEASUREMENT THAT RUNS ONE RUNTIME TWICE REPORTS EQUIVALENCE, AND IT IS THE MOST DANGEROUS SHAPE A FALSE GREEN CAN TAKE HERE** — it would have certified CU-6a at the exact moment CU-6a stopped being measurable, on the one artefact the programme cannot re-derive. It was caught only because the E-property results moved in a way the causes did not explain. **The repair inverts which side is reconstructed, and that inversion is worth keeping as the general rule: reconstruct the thing being RETIRED, and measure the thing being ADOPTED as it actually ships.** The harness now builds the legacy composition directly and calls the live `authorViaFTE` for the canonical side, so the adopted path is measured rather than modelled. | [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §6 steps 3, 5 · `governance/capability/measure-authoring-equivalence.mjs` · debt D-087 (the class) · D-090 | **REPAIRED IN THE HARNESS AND AMENDED IN THE ADR — §6 step 3 now reads "unrepeatable after step 6" and carries the harness obligation.** This was NOT left as an editorial note: unlike D-080's three, the wrong lifetime **changed what the instrument measured**, so it changes the decision's evidence and the freeze admits it. **THE OBLIGATION, GENERAL AND OUTLIVING THIS ADR: reconstruct the thing being RETIRED; measure the thing being ADOPTED as it actually ships.** The harness now builds the legacy composition directly and calls the live `authorViaFTE` for the canonical side. **The programme-owner-facing consequence: CU-6a IS STILL MEASURABLE TODAY.** Every continuation from D-090's stop retains the ability to re-measure, and that ability ends at step 6 and not before. **What has no counter-measure and is recorded as such:** nothing in this platform could have detected a self-comparison. It was caught because the E-property results moved in a way the known causes did not explain — a human reading numbers, which is D-011's limit arriving at the instrument rather than at the code. |

| **D-092** | **ADR-0077 §4.5's E-3 IS EQUIVALENCE-AS-SAMENESS, WHICH §4.1 OF THE SAME ADR RULES UNSATISFIABLE BY CONSTRUCTION — AND IT PASSES ONLY WHERE IT MEASURES NOTHING.** Measured 2026-08-05, both compositions, one fixture. E-3 requires *"identical operations — same count, same order, same `action`, same `selector`, same `testCaseId`."* **`testCaseId` is the decisive clause:** legacy ids are `tc-REQ-1-r1-accessibility-1`, canonical are `REQ-1:sc:0:pos` — generated by different modules under different schemes. So E-3 requires the two runtimes to author **identically named test cases in identical numbers**. Measured on the execution-contract fixture from ONE acceptance criterion: **legacy 22 test cases → 234 operations; canonical 2 → 4.** That ratio is not incidental — it is the ratified design difference ADR-0077 **§2.4 already records** (ADR-0049 §5: the canonical authors **abstract** packages by ADR-0039 design, the legacy **concrete** steps). **AND THE PASSING CASES ARE THE TELL: E-3 held on exactly the three corpus entries with no `selectorDiscovery`, where both sides ground ZERO operations.** It passes when it has nothing to compare and fails whenever it does. | **THE REASONING ERROR IS ONE INFERENCE AND IT IS VISIBLE IN E-3's OWN JUSTIFYING CLAUSE:** *"Grounding is the bridge's own reasoning and is unchanged by the re-composition, **so** a difference here is a defect."* That is **f unchanged ⇒ f(x) unchanged**, which holds only if `x` is unchanged. `x` is the test cases — **the output of the runtime being swapped.** The ADR reasoned about the function and forgot the argument. **This is §4.1's own finding one level in.** §4.1 wrote *"equivalence-as-sameness is unsatisfiable by construction, which is why the precondition was never defined"*, and §4.5 exists to replace sameness with a measurable property — *a property of the artefact the gateway signs, modulo a declared difference set.* **That framing is sound and is SATISFIED: E-1, E-2, E-5 and E-6 hold on every corpus entry today.** E-3 then reintroduced sameness for one property, against the one input the cut-over changes by design. **A vacuous green is the signature: the class this register records at D-011 and D-015, here inside the precondition that gates the irreversible operation.**
>
> **THE DEFECT, RECORDED VERBATIM AS DIAGNOSED, BECAUSE THE AMENDMENT RESTS ON IT:**
>
> *"E-3's justifying clause is **f unchanged ⇒ f(x) unchanged**, which holds only if `x` is unchanged, and `x` is the test cases — the output of the runtime being swapped. The ADR reasoned about the function and forgot the argument."*
>
> **That is D-087's question — *what does this step's subject CONSUME, as opposed to what does it PRODUCE?* — unasked in the clause that most needed it.** D-087's four instances are all migration steps; this is the same omission inside a **precondition**, where the cost is not a rework cycle but an unsatisfiable gate on an irreversible operation.
>
> *"E-3 passed on exactly the three corpus entries grounding zero operations: it passes when it has nothing to compare. A vacuous green inside the precondition gating the irreversible operation."*
>
> **THE STRUCTURAL DISTINCTION, WHICH IS THE AMENDMENT'S BASIS:** *"E-1/E-2/E-5/E-6 measure the artefact's **CONTRACT**; E-3/E-4 measure its **CONTENT VOLUME**, which a differently-designed runtime necessarily produces differently. A content-volume property can be a floor or a ratio, never an identity."* | ADR-0077 [§4.5 E-3](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) · §4.1 · §2.4 · [ADR-0049](../docs/adr/ADR-0049-canonical-runtime-cutover.md) §5 · ADR-0039 (abstract authoring) · debt D-011 · D-015 · D-084 | **THE DEFECT IS IN E-3's DEFINITION — NOT IN THE CANONICAL AUTHORING, AND NOT IN §4.5's PREMISE.** The canonical authoring is ratified by ADR-0039/0044/0069 and §2.4 records the abstract-vs-concrete difference as **known before this ADR was drafted**; §4.5's premise is sound and four of its six properties hold. **CONSEQUENCE, STATED WITHOUT HEDGING: CU-6a AS DEFINED CANNOT RETURN `equivalent: true` ON ANY CORPUS ENTRY THAT GROUNDS AN OPERATION. ADR-0077 defined a precondition its own subject cannot meet.** Not repaired here — an E-property is part of the Decision, so re-founding E-3 is an amendment to §4.5 and the programme owner's. **The shape a repair would take, scoped not proposed:** E-1/E-2/E-5/E-6 measure the artefact's CONTRACT; E-3/E-4 measure its CONTENT VOLUME, which is what a differently-designed runtime necessarily produces differently. **A content-volume property can only be a floor or a ratio, never an identity** — and whichever it becomes must be stated so it cannot pass vacuously on an empty operation set. |

| **D-093** | **HALF THE CLOSED-LOOP SUITE WAS SATISFIED BY REPORTING THE LOSS HONESTLY; THE OTHER HALF NEEDS THE LOSS NOT TO EXIST — AND THAT LINE IS WHERE HONEST REPORTING STOPS BEING SUFFICIENT.** Measured 2026-08-05. `reflectViaFTE` re-pointed onto the canonical composition, which has no observed-outcome channel (D-090 cause 4). **Six tests failed; three now pass and three cannot.** The three that RECOVERED — *the certification verdict is read structurally* · *an EMPTY outcome map is reported as ungrounded rather than certified silently* · *outcomes naming an UNKNOWN test case are reported, not silently ignored* — assert that the pass **tells the truth about what it saw**, and were satisfied the moment `join` reported `matched: 0` with a stated reason instead of crashing. The three that CANNOT — *the reflection pass JOINS the outcomes* · *outcomes actually reach the engine* · ***THE REGRESSION*: a wholly failing run does not certify like a wholly passing one** — assert that the pass **saw something**, and no amount of accurate reporting can satisfy them. | **THIS GENERALISES PAST ITS CAUSE AND IS THE REASON IT IS ITS OWN ENTRY.** This register's dominant repair pattern is *make the negative expressible* — D-013's vacuous `published`, D-023/D-024/D-028/D-057's unreportable refusals, D-043's unmeasurable coverage, ADR-0071/0072's whole subject. **That pattern has a boundary and this measurement locates it exactly: expressibility repairs a claim about a capability; it cannot repair the absence of the capability.** Two thirds of an assertion set can be about honesty and one third about substance, and only running it distinguishes them. **The clearest single instance is `THE REGRESSION`** — written to prevent a certification verdict that does not vary with the run it certifies, and now red for exactly that reason. A verdict that reports *"I certified nothing and here is why"* is honest **and still a verdict formed without reference to the run**, which is why CAUSE-4 was ruled a GAP and refused admission to §4.7: a difference set that admits *"the verdict does not vary with the run"* is not a difference set. | Platform — the class · `test/closed-loop.test.mjs` · `authoring-bridge.mjs` (`reflectViaFTE`) · debt D-090 · D-013 · D-024 · ADR-0071 · ADR-0072 | **OPEN, AND CARRIED AS A TEST TO APPLY BEFORE THE NEXT EXPRESSIBILITY REPAIR: of the assertions this change would satisfy, how many are about the SYSTEM TELLING THE TRUTH and how many are about the SYSTEM DOING THE WORK?** If the second set is non-empty, expressibility is a partial repair and must be reported as one rather than closed. **The three-of-six split is kept as the measured figure** because a ratio makes the boundary concrete in a way the principle does not: the same change was a complete repair for one half of one file and no repair at all for the other. |

| **D-094** | **THE TWO RUNTIMES CLASSIFY THE SAME SENTENCE DIFFERENTLY — LEGACY `business-rule`, CANONICAL `acceptance-criterion` — AND CARDINALITY HID IT.** Measured 2026-08-05, before asserting E-4′(2), on the condition that it not rest on cardinality. On `intent-conservation` the criterion statements match **exactly, in set and in order** (3 and 3, identical text). On the address fixtures the cardinality also agrees (**1 and 1**) and the statement text is identical — **but the `kind` differs**: legacy acceptance-criterion statements **0**, canonical **1**. **A covered-set identity resting on cardinality would have reported PASS over a classification divergence.** It also explains the E-4 dimension difference measured earlier: `byRequirementKind('acceptance-criteria', …)` and `byRequirementKind('business-rule', …)` route on exactly that `kind`, so **the same sentence lands in a different coverage dimension on each side.** | **E-3's ERROR IN A SMALLER FIELD, CAUGHT BECAUSE THE OWNER REFUSED TO LET IT BE ASSERTED UNMEASURED.** E-3 asserted identity of a derived artefact without checking its inputs; E-4′(2) would have asserted identity of a covered SET by counting it. **The condition — *measure, then assert or report the gap* — is the general form and is worth more than this instance.** **AND THE FINDING THAT REFRAMES E-4 ENTIRELY: `canonical ⊆ legacy` IS FALSE, IN THE CANONICAL's FAVOUR.** The canonical measures the `acceptance-criteria` dimension (0 of 1, a stated shortfall); the legacy reports it `0/0, applicable:false` — it does not measure it at all. **Every prior framing of E-4, this ADR's included, assumed a reduction. It is a DIFFERENT SUBSET, and on the criterion dimension the canonical is the more honest of the two.** The dimension NAME set is identical on all six entries. | ADR-0077 [§4.5.1](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) E-4′(2) · `agents/story-and-test.ts` (legacy classification) · `domains/observation-interpretation.ts` (canonical) · `emitters`/`byRequirementKind` · debt D-092 | **RULED 2026-08-05: THE REQUIREMENT'S `kind` IS CONTRACT, NOT CONTENT — SO D-094 NEEDS ITS OWN RESOLUTION AND IS CORRECTLY UPSTREAM OF E-4′.** The ground is what the value does: **it crosses the plane boundary** in `metadata.storyAnalysis.requirements[].kind`, **and it steers which coverage dimension the criterion is measured in.** *A value that crosses and changes what gets measured is not content.* **THE DIVERGENCE, STATED AS WHAT IT IS: the two runtimes ASSERT DIFFERENT THINGS ABOUT THE SAME SENTENCE — identical text, different `kind`** — and it is not a coverage difference, a volume difference or a naming difference. **Asserting E-4′(2) on cardinality would have passed it** (1 and 1 on every affected entry), which is why the owner's condition to measure before asserting is the load-bearing part of this entry. **OPEN, and it is now the ONLY remaining failure in the whole of CU-6a** — E-1, E-2, E-3′, E-4′(1), E-4′(3), E-5, E-6 all hold, and E-4′(2) fails on three fixtures and their two derived variants. **MEASURED 2026-08-05 — NEITHER CLASSIFICATION IS RATIFIED, SO THIS IS A DIVERGENCE AND NOT A DEFECT.** `Requirement.kind` is a four-way union in `model.ts` with **no ADR, no architecture document and no criterion behind it**; ADR-0039 §88 names *"dependency/business-rule/risk identification"* as a **capability of Story Intelligence** and says nothing about what the classification means or which value wins; and **`contract/execution-package.ts` does not mention `kind` at all** — so the field crosses the plane boundary inside `metadata.storyAnalysis.requirements[].kind` **ungoverned by the contract that carries it. That is D-095's exact shape at a second field, which makes D-094 and D-095 one finding measured at two places.** **AND THE TWO MODELS MEAN DIFFERENT THINGS BY THE FIELD.** Legacy: `story.requirement-extraction` marks every criterion `acceptance-criterion`, then `story.business-rule-extraction` **overwrites** it to `business-rule` on a modal marker — `/\b(must|shall|only if|never|always|at least|no more than|cannot)\b/` — so `kind` is a **SEMANTIC CLASSIFICATION** and the two values are mutually exclusive with business-rule winning. Canonical: criteria come from the acceptance-criteria text and business rules from the story statement, in two independent lists with no reclassification — so `kind` is **PROVENANCE**. **They are not two answers to one question; they are answers to two different questions sharing a field name.** By its own definition each is right: the sentence contains *"cannot"* and states a prohibition (legacy), and it was written by the customer as an acceptance criterion (canonical). **The sentence is genuinely both, and the models disagree about precedence** — the legacy destroys the criterion classification to record the rule; the canonical never has to choose. **RULED 2026-08-05 — `Requirement.kind` MEANS PROVENANCE. THE CANONICAL'S CONSTRUCTION IS CORRECT. AND THE RULING FOUNDS THE MEANING RATHER THAN CORRECTING AN IMPLEMENTATION.** Three grounds. **(i) The legacy's reclassification DESTROYS information**: overwriting `acceptance-criterion` with `business-rule` is *why* the `acceptance-criteria` coverage dimension reports `0/0, applicable:false` — **a classification that empties a measurement in order to record a different fact is losing one to state another.** **(ii) The alternative reintroduces modal-marker STRING MATCHING into a certified domain**, which debt D-086's binding prohibition forbids by name — *no repair may map prose to a classification by pattern-matching the prose.* **(iii) The sentence is genuinely both, and a model that MUST CHOOSE is wrong about what it models**; provenance and semantics are two facts about one statement, and a four-way union that forces them into one field can only carry one. **THE RULING ESTABLISHES AN AUTHORITY THAT DID NOT EXIST — it is not a finding that one implementation drifted from a standard, because there was no standard.** `Requirement.kind` was a four-way union in `model.ts` with no ADR, no architecture document and no criterion behind it, and both runtimes were implementations of an unratified field. **The canonical requires no change; it already conforms to the meaning now founded.** **E-4′(2) CLEARED ON RE-MEASURE**, and the reason is the ruling rather than a tolerance: the covered criterion set is the **criteria list**, not `requirements` filtered by `kind` — reading the criterion set off a provenance field was the conflation this ruling ends. **CU-6a IS NOW MET: `equivalent: true`, 0 undeclared differences, `corpusDigest` unmoved across seven measurements.** **E-4′(3) IS RESOLVED (ruled 2026-08-05, adopted-side-only) and now holds on all six entries; the measurement it produced is preserved in the standing correction below rather than lost with the gate.** |

| **D-098** | **THE INTELLIGENCE PLANE MINTS EVIDENCE REFERENCES INTO THE EXECUTION PLANE FOR TRACES THAT DO NOT EXIST — INV-1 INVERTED, ON THE AUTHORING PASS.** Measured 2026-08-05. On an authoring pass — no execution has happened, and none can have — the `execution` domain emits **four `EvidenceReferenceHandle`s** reading `locatorRef: 'execution-plane://component:…/trace'`, `custody: 'execution-plane'`, `contentHashRef: 'ref:component:…'`. **The Execution Plane never produced those traces. The handles were constructed on this side, they name a custodian that holds nothing, and they carry a content-hash reference to content that was never hashed.** INV-1 is that evidence stays custodied in the Execution Plane and **only references cross**; this is the same sentence read backwards — **references fabricated by the plane that is supposed to receive them.** **AND THE OUTCOME HALF, WHICH IS THE MILDER FINDING:** the same pass reports `provenance: composed`, `outcomeSummary {total: 4, passed: 4, failed: 0}`, `status: 'passed'`, and that result reaches `executiveReporting.certificationVerdict`, which `reflectViaFTE` reads — **so the runtime certifies against a run it did not observe on BOTH passes, not only the reflection one.** The CAUSE-4 repair does not close either half. | **THE REPAIR DOES NOT CLOSE IT, AND THIS IS RECORDED RATHER THAN FOLDED IN.** ADR-0077 §6 step 8 gives the domain a way to say *"I am relaying what the Execution Plane observed"*; **it gives it no way to say *"execution has not happened yet"***, because that case is not a provenance — it is the absence of a result, and the platform's shape for it is the stage emitting `notApplicable` (ADR-0071). The authoring pass reaches the `execution` stage unconditionally, so it produces a composed result whether or not composing one means anything. **Measured contrast, same tree, same fixture:** an authoring pass reports 4 passed with 4 evidence references; a reflection pass whose Execution Plane returned nothing reports **4 skipped, 0 evidence references, `join {supplied: 0, matched: 0}`** — the honest shape, reached only because `observedOutcomes` was supplied. **The repair made the RELAY path truthful and left the AUTHORING path exactly as it was.** The evidence-reference half is the sharper finding: INV-1 says evidence stays custodied in the Execution Plane and only references cross, and these are references to nothing, minted on this side. | `domains/execution.ts` (composed branch) · `canonical-runner-capability.ts` (`execution` stage, unconditional) · ADR-0071 (`notApplicable`) · INV-1 · debt D-090 · D-013 | **OWED AND NAMED — NOT SCOPED INTO ADR-0077 §6 STEP 8.** The question it poses is whether the `execution` stage should run at all on an authoring pass, and that is a composition-arrangement question: §6 step 8's channel is about *whose outcomes*, this is about *whether there are outcomes to have*. **Folding it in would be the scope error D-087 counts, at the fifth opportunity.** The shape of the repair is already visible and is deliberately NOT taken here: the `execution` stage emits `notApplicable` when `observedOutcomes` is `undefined`, so the authoring pass produces no execution result and every downstream domain sees an absence rather than a fiction — **which is a change to what eight domains receive, and needs its own measurement of who reads `execution` and what they do with nothing.** |

| **D-097** | **AN ABSENT READ WAS READ AS AN ABSENT CHANNEL — THE SAME ERROR CLASS AS THE REST OF THIS SECTION, REASONING ABOUT ONE DIRECTION AND NOT MEASURING THE OTHER.** Recorded 2026-08-05 as a correction to my own CAUSE-4 cost report. I reported *"`ExecutionAdapter`'s three operations — `publishResult`, `publishEvidenceReference`, `publishDefect` — are all writes; there is no read"* and concluded **"no domain can receive an outcome it did not produce, and none of the fourteen has a port through which one could arrive."** The premise was true and the conclusion did not follow: **it holds only if the Intelligence Plane must PULL. The Execution Plane PUSHES.** Measured: the EP POSTs observed outcomes to `/v1/evidence`; `ip-execute-gateway.mjs:597–601` reads `body.outcomes`, runs `guardReturnedOutcomes` over them, joins them to the cached F1 context by `correlationId` and hands them to `reflectViaFTE`. **The data already crosses the boundary, already passes a sovereignty guard, and already arrives in this plane.** | **THIS IS D-087's METHOD RULE AT A THIRD ALTITUDE, AND IT COST A WRONG STRUCTURAL CLAIM RATHER THAN A REWORK CYCLE.** D-087: *reading a producer's code to enumerate its consumers finds only the consumers the producer already knows about.* Here: **reading an SPI's operations to enumerate a capability's inputs finds only the inputs that arrive through that SPI.** I enumerated the pull surface — the adapter — and concluded about the channel. **The push surface was one file away and is already governed, guarded and in production.** The finding that makes it worth an entry rather than a correction: **it changed a REPORTED CONCLUSION, not an implementation.** The programme owner was told a capability was structurally impossible and that completion would need a cross-plane contract; both were wrong, and neither would have been caught by any gate, because a gate cannot check a sentence in a report. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs:597–601` · `domains/execution.ts` (`ExecutionInput`) · `canonical-capability.ts` (`CanonicalCapabilityInput`) · debt D-087 · D-090 · D-011 | **CORRECTED AND RECORDED. CAUSE-4's CHANNEL IS IP-SIDE AND BOUNDED; IT IS NOT A FIFTH CROSS-PLANE CEILING.** The general form, added to D-087's method rule: **when concluding that a capability CANNOT receive something, enumerate every direction it could arrive from — not only the one you were looking at.** A pull surface and a push surface are different enumerations and only one of them was made. **The remaining ADR-shaped question is unaffected and is real** — the `execution` domain must distinguish *"I executed this"* from *"I am reporting what the EP executed"* — but it is domain SEMANTICS, not an absent port. |

| **D-096** | **STANDING CORRECTION — THREE MEASUREMENTS IN A ROW PUT THE CANONICAL RUNTIME AHEAD OF THE ASSUMPTION MADE ABOUT IT, AND THE ASSUMPTION HAD NEVER BEEN MEASURED UNTIL NOW.** Recorded 2026-08-05, ADR-0077 §6 steps 3–5. **(i)** The canonical MEASURES the `acceptance-criteria` coverage dimension — 0 of 1, a stated shortfall — where the legacy reports it `0/0, applicable: false` and does not measure it at all. **(ii)** On all six corpus entries the canonical carries **ZERO** dimensions marked not-applicable without a stated reason; the legacy carries **two to four** (`acceptance-criteria`, `workflow`, and on some entries `business-rule`, `risk`, `boundary`). **(iii)** `canonical ⊆ legacy` on measured dimensions is **FALSE — in the canonical's favour**; the dimension NAME set is identical on all six and what differs is which are measured. **The anti-vacuous device written to catch the adopted runtime caught the RETIRING one.** | **EVERY FRAMING IN THIS PROGRAMME ASSUMED A REDUCTION, INCLUDING ADR-0077's OWN AND INCLUDING EVERY REPORT I WROTE BEFORE MEASURING IT.** §2.4 is titled *"What the canonical is already known not to reproduce"*; §4.7's seven are all losses; the shape report counts absences; the E-3/E-4 framing was *"the canonical produces less."* **That framing was never wrong exactly — the four P-69.2 reductions and the three shape-report absences are real — but it was never checked in the other direction**, so a difference in the canonical's favour had no place to be recorded and was invisible to every reader. **The mechanism is worth naming because it is not carelessness: a retirement's whole vocabulary is loss, so its instruments are built to count what is missing and are structurally unable to report a gain.** It is D-011's limit — *the suite verifies artefacts, not reasoning* — pointed at a framing rather than at a claim. | ADR-0077 §2.4 · §4.5.1 · §4.7 · `SECTION_G_SHAPE_REPORT.md` §6 · debt D-092 · D-094 · D-011 | **KEPT AS A STANDING CORRECTION, NOT CLOSED, BECAUSE IT IS ABOUT HOW THE NEXT COMPARISON WILL BE FRAMED RATHER THAN ABOUT THIS ONE.** The rule it earns: **when a migration's instruments are built to count losses, measure the reverse direction explicitly before stating what the successor does not do** — the assumption is free to make and expensive to leave unchecked, and here it was carried through an ADR, a shape report and five reports before a measurement contradicted it. **Not a licence to assume the canonical is better either:** all three findings are narrow, all three are about coverage-dimension honesty, and none of them touches §4.7's seven, which remain real losses. |

| **D-095** | *(D-094 is the same finding at a second field — see below.)* **A GOVERNED CONTRACT THAT DOES NOT GOVERN THE THING CROSSING IT — AND THAT, NOT THE EXECUTION PLANE'S ABSENCE, IS THE CEILING.** Raised 2026-08-05, not answered, on instruction. **MERGED 2026-08-05 WITH D-094, WHICH IS THIS FINDING MEASURED AT A SECOND FIELD.** D-094 began as a classification divergence and resolved into the same sentence: `metadata.storyAnalysis.requirements[].kind` **crosses the plane boundary and `contract/execution-package.ts` does not mention it** — exactly as `traceability.links[].requirementIds` and `selectedTestIds` cross and are not mentioned. **Two fields, one defect: the cross-plane contract declares SECTIONS and does not govern the VALUES inside them.** That is why D-094 could be settled by an Intelligence-Plane ruling and D-095 cannot — `kind` is produced and consumed on this side of the boundary as well, so this plane has standing to found its meaning; the EP's join keys are consumed on the far side, and nothing here has standing over them. **THE OWED WORK IS ONE CONTRACT QUESTION, NOT TWO:** the cross-plane contract must declare, per section, which of its VALUES the far side depends on — join keys and classifications alike — so a change to either is detectable on this side instead of being discovered by a customer. **Stated in its own terms because the obvious reading is the wrong one:** this is NOT *"the EP is outside this plane, so we cannot measure it."* It is that **`contract/execution-package.ts` declares `traceability` at SECTION level — `consumedBy: 'EP reporting and result synchronisation'` — and `requirementIds` appears NOWHERE in the file.** So the contract names a consumer and does not name the keys that consumer joins on. **Even with an Execution Plane present, the answer would live in EP code rather than in the contract**, and establishing it would mean reading the other plane — the sovereignty violation the boundary exists to prevent. **The absence of the EP is not what blocks the measurement; the contract's silence is.** Fourth time at this ceiling. CU-7 asserts *external contracts unchanged*, and the re-composition changes IDENTIFIER SCHEMES that cross the plane boundary in the signed package: `traceability.links[].requirementIds` moves `REQ-1-r1` → `REQ-1:ac:0`, and `traceability.links[].testCase` / `selectedTestIds` move `tc-REQ-1-r1-accessibility-1` → `REQ-1:sc:0:pos`. **Measured on this side: the execution contract declares `traceability` as `consumedBy: 'EP reporting and result synchronisation'` — a SECTION-level declaration — and `requirementIds` appears NOWHERE in `contract/execution-package.ts`.** So the Intelligence Plane's own governed contract **does not record which fields the Execution Plane keys on**, and the Execution Plane's repository is outside this plane and must not be read from it (CLAUDE.md §4, architecture 19/20). | **THE FINDING IS NOT "THE EP IS ABSENT" — IT IS THAT THE CONTRACT DOES NOT CARRY THE ANSWER.** Even with the EP present, the answer would live in EP code rather than in a governed cross-plane contract, so it could only be established by reading the other plane — which is the sovereignty violation the boundary exists to prevent. **A contract that names a consumer but not the keys that consumer joins on cannot support a claim that its values are unchanged.** CU-7 is currently `measured-met` from three gates (`verify-contract-compatibility`, `verify-http-surface-parity`, `verify-execution-contract`), and **all three measure SHAPES**. *"External contracts unchanged"* is a claim about values as well as shapes, and the value half has never been measurable from here. | ADR-0077 §4.6 (CU-7) · `contract/execution-package.ts:132–134` · `registry/reasoning-publication.ts` (`traceability-composition`) · [20-cross-plane-contracts.md](../docs/architecture/20-cross-plane-contracts.md) · CLAUDE.md §4 | **RAISED, NOT ANSWERED — and the resolution is a CONTRACT change, not a measurement.** What is owed is that the cross-plane contract **declare its join keys**: which fields of `traceability` the Execution Plane matches on across runs, so an identifier-scheme change is detectable on this side of the boundary instead of being discovered by a customer whose prior-run references stop matching. **Until that exists, CU-7's `measured-met` should be read as *shapes unchanged*, and the evidence artefact should say so** rather than letting a three-gate green stand for a claim two of whose halves nothing measures. **NOT scoped into ADR-0077** — it is a cross-plane contract question that outlives this cut-over, and folding it in would repeat exactly the scope error D-087 counts. |

| **D-099** | **THE ROLLBACK MACHINERY SURVIVED THE THING IT ROLLS BACK TO.** Recorded 2026-08-05 at ADR-0061 §6 step 6. `rollbackToLegacy()`, `selectImplementation()` and `buildParallelValidationReport()` remain exported from `src/activation.ts`; `capability.ts` and `orchestrators.ts` are deleted. So `rollbackToLegacy` returns a state whose `selectImplementation` says `legacy` and there is no legacy to select, and `buildParallelValidationReport` compares two runtimes of which one exists. **The activation STATE MODEL still describes a two-implementation world that the tree no longer contains.** `INITIAL_ACTIVATION_STATE.legacyAvailable` is `true` and is asserted `true` by a surviving test. | **NOT A DEAD-CODE FINDING — A MODEL THAT OUTLIVED ITS DOMAIN, WHICH IS WHY IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN DELETED.** `activation.ts` is not one of the nine orphans: nothing in the retiring closure reaches it, so no inventory reports it and no gate goes red for it. It is reachable, compiles, passes its tests, and every one of those greens is about a transition that can no longer be performed. **The danger is precisely that it looks maintained.** A reader of `verify-capability-activation` sees deterministic reversible activation/rollback proved by an executing suite; what is proved is that the FUNCTIONS are deterministic, not that the rollback is possible. Widening §6 step 6 to take `activation.ts` was refused deliberately — enlarging a deletion set mid-deletion is what `SECTION_G_SHAPE_REPORT` §9.1 stopped the first attempt for. | `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/activation.ts` · `test/canonical-capability-conformance.test.ts` (the surviving `legacyAvailable` assertion) · `governance/verification/verify-capability-activation.js` AC-7 · debt D-100 · D-015 | **OPEN. The repair is a decision about the activation model, not a deletion:** either the two-implementation state model is retired with ADR-0044's reversibility clause, or it is re-founded on what activation now means when there is one implementation. **Until then AC-7 prints the loss on its own line rather than folding it into a pass**, so a reader of the activation gate is told the rollback path is gone instead of inferring it from a green. |

| **D-100** | **ADR-0061 §6 STEP 6 FALSIFIES A RECORDED PROPERTY OF AN ACCEPTED ADR THAT THE AUTHORISING ADR NEVER NAMES.** Measured 2026-08-05 while re-pointing the gate set. **AC-7 is an ADR-0044 acceptance property** — *"the legacy implementation remains present (capability.ts + orchestrators.ts)"* — and step 6 deletes exactly those two files. **ADR-0077 §7 enumerates its reach and ADR-0044 is in none of it:** ADR-0049 superseded in full, ADR-0061 amended at §6 steps 6–7, ADR-0046 enabled. ADR-0044 is not mentioned anywhere in the document. Contrast ADR-0046, whose LR-3/LR-4 assert the same retention and which ADR-0077 §7 explicitly ENABLES — **the identical property, at two ADRs, one covered and one not.** | **D-087's SHAPE A FOURTH TIME, AND PER D-087's OWN CLOSING NOTE THAT MAKES IT A PROPERTY OF THE DOCUMENT RATHER THAN OF THREE STEPS.** D-087 records the distinguishing question as available before the work and unasked: *what does this step's subject CONSUME, as opposed to what does it PRODUCE?* ADR-0077 §7 scoped its impact by the decisions it PRODUCES a change in, and missed an ADR that CONSUMES the deleted files as a recorded property. **The reason it was invisible is D-077's blind spot exactly:** AC-7 references no FTE symbol — it is two `existsSync` calls on file PATHS — so the seven-file symbol inventory that scoped this step could not see it, and neither could the retirement inventory, which measures imports. | [ADR-0044](../docs/adr/ADR-0044-functional-testing-capability-activation.md) AC-7 · [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) §7 · `governance/verification/verify-capability-activation.js` · debt D-087 · D-077 · D-076 · D-099 | **PARTLY CLOSED 2026-08-05 — THE ADR HALF IS DISCHARGED; THE STATE-MODEL HALF IS NOT, AND IS D-099.** AC-7 measures the same ordering claim LR-3 carries (absent only where ADR-0077 is ACCEPTED and the authoring-equivalence evidence is current), so the gate is honest and no weaker than before — it can no longer be satisfied by two files merely existing. **But a gate re-pointed to match a deletion is not the same as an ADR amended to authorise it, and conflating the two is how a control comes to certify its own bypass** — so the repair was withheld from the gate and taken as a decision. **[ADR-0077 §7.1](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md) now names ADR-0044, amends it at §4's reversibility clause and AC-7 only, and states the ground: reversibility was the safety property standing in for evidence that the replacement worked, and CU-6a replaced it with a measurement RC-4′(3) preserves by digest.** §8 carries ADR-0044 in its amended-decisions list; the gate's comment and its `ROLLBACK PATH GONE` line cite §7.1 instead of claiming the amendment is owed. **WHAT REMAINS OPEN, and §7.1 says so in the ADR rather than leaving it to this register: the two-implementation activation STATE MODEL is not re-founded** — `rollbackToLegacy()`, `selectImplementation()`, `buildParallelValidationReport()` and `INITIAL_ACTIVATION_STATE.legacyAvailable = true` still describe a world the tree no longer contains. **That is D-099, it is an ADR-0044 question, and enlarging this amendment to take it would be the scope error D-087 counts.** **A defect found while writing the amendment and repaired with it:** the gate's evidence artefact recorded `AC-7 observed: legacyPresent` — **false** after the retirement — inside a document whose `verificationStatus` was `verified`, so the printed check and the emitted evidence disagreed about the same property with only the evidence readable downstream. It now records the property with `legacyPresent`, `legacyRetired` and the naming authority beside it. |

| **D-101** | **THE PLATFORM's REASONING REGISTRY STILL TAKES ITS INPUT SHAPE FROM ONE CAPABILITY's PACKAGE.** Recorded 2026-08-05, ADR-0061 §6 step 6 / obligation 4. `registry/reasoning-publication.ts` imported `EngineState` from `capability.ts` — a **type-only** dependency, so it cost nothing at runtime and announced itself as nothing, while being a surviving module bound to a retirement target. ADR-0077 §6 step 4a severed it by declaring `HarvestState` **structurally**: exactly the sixteen fields the publishers read, where `EngineState` carried thirty-two. **The severance is complete and the module needed no change at step 6** — which is why the inventory reports zero re-pointing obligations against a registry that had one. | **THE OBLIGATION THAT REMAINS IS ALTITUDE, NOT COUPLING.** ADR-0067 founds the Reasoning Result Registry as a **platform-level** consumer; `HarvestState` is declared inside `packages/functional-testing-engine`. Step 4a removed the dependency on a runtime's INTERNAL type; it did not move the declaration to the altitude ADR-0067 puts the registry at. **A platform contract living in one capability's package is the same defect one level up from the one just repaired** — and it is invisible for the same reason the original was: a type-only import across a package boundary costs nothing at runtime and no gate measures where a declaration lives. | [ADR-0067](../docs/adr/ADR-0067-reasoning-result-registry.md) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/registry/reasoning-publication.ts` (`HarvestState`) · ADR-0077 §6 step 4a · debt D-069 · D-087 | **OPEN — an ADR-0067 question, deliberately not folded into §6 step 6.** Where a platform contract is declared is not an FTE decision, and moving `HarvestState` while deleting the legacy runtime would have bundled a platform-contract relocation into a capability retirement. **That is the scope error D-087 counts, at the fifth opportunity, and it is refused here for the same reason D-098 was.** |

| **D-102** | **A NUMBER HANDED TO YOU IS AN ESTIMATE UNTIL YOU MEASURE IT, AND THAT INCLUDES A NUMBER FROM THE PROGRAMME OWNER.** Recorded 2026-08-05, by the programme owner, against the session that executed ADR-0061 §6 step 6. Asked before the first deletion whether the budget would carry all four parts, the answer was yes. **That estimate was made against a restated Part 2 — *"three gates lose their subject"* — and the restatement was MINE.** Measured after the deletion, **the gate surface was six**, two of them accepted-ADR **property** gates (ADR-0044 AC-7, ADR-0046 LR-3/LR-4) asserting on `fs.existsSync` of a **file path** — invisible to every symbol inventory in this programme. **The deletion set, the 6 944 lines, the exclusions, the 218-test drop and the suite totals were all measured. The one figure that was not measured was the one that came from me, and it is the one that was wrong.** The same shape recurred at Part 4: the restated *"eight subjectless dimensions, six survivors"* measured as **nine** subjectless (`domains` omitted, and `verify-functional-completeness.js:107` gates on it) and **zero** survivors as written — [`PART_4_CENSUS_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](PART_4_CENSUS_DESIGN_REPORT.md) §0. | **THE BLIND SPOT IS D-077's, BUT ITS LOCATION IS NEW AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ENTRY.** D-077 records that a symbol scan cannot see a file path; here the blind spot arrived **in the estimate rather than in the measurement**, where no gate, inventory or report looks. **An estimate is not evidence, and a restatement is not a measurement — CHARTER §3 and CLAUDE.md §5 say a prompt does not override the repository, and a NUMBER in a prompt is a prompt.** The programme's whole method is evidence over assertion; this is the one channel where an assertion routinely enters unchallenged, because it arrives from the authority the work is done for and it arrives as an aside rather than as a claim. **The cost was a session's last part**, and the cost of the check is a `grep`. | Method · programme governance · debt D-077 · D-087 · D-100 · [`NEXT_ACTION.md`](NEXT_ACTION.md) | **OPEN AS A STANDING RULE, NOT AS A REPAIRABLE ITEM.** **Every figure that enters a plan is measured before it is committed to, whatever its source — including the programme owner's.** The measurement is named in the plan beside the figure, so a later reader can tell a counted number from a quoted one. **Where a figure cannot be measured before the work, the plan says so rather than carrying it silently**, which is C-0.4 (*NOT MEASURED is FAIL*) applied to an estimate. Specifically, and cheaply: **a deletion's gate surface is measured by a PATH search across `docs/adr/` and `governance/` for every file the deletion set names**, not by a symbol inventory — the search that would have found AC-7 and LR-3 before the deletion rather than after it. |

| **D-103** | **A GATE WENT GREEN ON A COUNT OF THINGS THAT NO LONGER EXIST — `verify-canonical-agent-dormancy`, PASSING AFTER THE DELETION, REPORTING "135 agents remain dormant" FROM A HARD-CODED LITERAL.** Measured 2026-08-05, at ADR-0061 §6 step 6, and **found by the deletion rather than by the shape report**. Its four properties are all satisfied more easily after the deletion than before it: **DM-1** (*only `agents/design-sync.ts` is reachable from the canonical surface*) passes because it is the only agent module that still exists; **DM-3** (*it exposes exactly the nine ruled ids*) passes unchanged; **DM-4** (*no `AgentCatalogue` handle on the canonical surface*) passes because `catalogue.ts` was deleted. **And the RESULT line is a string literal on the PASS branch** — `'RESULT: PASS — only the ruled design-synchronisation placement is reachable; 135 agents remain dormant.'` **135 = 144 − 9, computed once by a human and frozen into the output.** Measured on the post-deletion tree: `src/agents/` contains **one file, nine agents, and zero dormant ones.** The gate reported 135 dormant agents in a repository that contains none. | **THE VACUOUS-GREEN CLASS (D-011, D-015) IN ITS PUREST FORM, AND SHARPER THAN THE ONE THAT WAS PREDICTED.** The restatement anticipated *a gate whose subject is gone*; what the deletion actually produced is worse — **a gate whose every property is satisfied BY the absence, printing a quantity of the absent thing that was never computed at all.** A reader of that output learns that 135 agents are dormant. There are nine agents. **The number was never a measurement: it was correct on the day it was typed and became false without anything changing that could be observed**, which is D-007's declaration-versus-implementation seam inside a gate's own verdict line. **Two general facts it establishes.** (i) *A gate's pass-branch OUTPUT is a declaration like any other and must be derived, never authored* — every count in a verdict line is computed from what the run observed, or it is not printed. (ii) **A gate that gets EASIER to pass when its subject is removed is not detecting anything**, and the test for it is available before any deletion: ask of each property whether removing the subject would turn it red or green. | `governance/verification/verify-canonical-agent-dormancy.js` (deleted at `0675630`) · ADR-0069 P-69.2 · debt D-011 · D-015 · D-007 | **CLOSED BY DELETION, RECORDED AS A CLASS.** The gate is gone with its subject and its removal is justified in `run-all.js` where the entry was. **What is NOT closed is the class**, and it is recorded here because this instance was found by an irreversible operation rather than by an instrument: **no gate in this platform checks another gate's pass-branch output for authored constants.** The check is mechanical — a numeric literal inside a `RESULT: PASS` string is a finding — and it is cheap enough that its absence is the finding. **Applied immediately at Part 4:** `PART_4_CENSUS_DESIGN_REPORT.md` §1 measures every candidate census dimension for whether it takes more than one value, and reports four as CONSTANT rather than retaining them. |

| **D-104** | **TWO PIECES OF EVIDENCE WERE SAVED BY THE BINDING'S OWN PROCEDURE AND BY A TOOL REFUSING — NEITHER BY ANYONE INTENDING TO SAVE THEM.** Recorded 2026-08-05, both at ADR-0061 §6 step 6. **(i) The CU-6a artefact was untracked, together with ADR-0077 itself.** `governance/capability/authoring-equivalence-evidence.json` sat under `.gitignore:115` (`governance/**/*-evidence.json`) alongside twelve other untracked files including the accepting ADR. **The baseline procedure for a clean-tree diff is `git stash -u`, and it would have taken both** — and a prior stash in this programme had already done exactly that to an untracked ADR. The artefact records `measure-authoring-equivalence.mjs` running **both** runtimes; one of them is now deleted, so **the measurement cannot be retaken**, and RC-4′ permits the legacy modules to be absent *only where* that artefact is present and its digest current. They were committed first, alone, at `2e89e85`. **(ii) The A1 board repairs were never in git history at all**, and were preserved only because `git rm` refused to remove a file with uncommitted changes; they were committed at `de1a784`, one commit before the tests carrying them were deleted. | **THE FAILURE WOULD HAVE BEEN SILENT IN BOTH CASES, AND THAT IS WHY THEY ARE ONE ENTRY.** Neither loss announces itself: a stash that swallows an untracked evidence file leaves a clean tree and a green build, and the artefact's absence surfaces later as a **red RC-4′ on someone else's clone** — which then invites the repair that destroys the evidence for good, regenerating it on a post-deletion tree where there is nothing to compare (ADR-0077 §5, debt D-085). **The controlling fact is that the save came from procedure, not from judgement.** Nobody reasoned *"this file is unrepeatable, commit it first"* until after the binding's commit-before-stash step forced the question, and nobody reasoned about the A1 repairs at all — a tool declined an unsafe operation. **Evidence that survives by luck is evidence that will eventually not.** | `.gitignore:115` and its negation · `governance/capability/authoring-equivalence-evidence.json` · commits `2e89e85`, `de1a784` · ADR-0077 §5 · debt D-085 · D-011 | **OPEN, AND THE REPAIR IS A PROPERTY OF THE IGNORE RULE RATHER THAN OF A PROCEDURE.** The negation is on disk carrying its reasoning, so the artefact is tracked — **but a rule that ignores a whole class and excepts one member by hand will lose the next unrepeatable artefact the same way**, because nothing distinguishes *regenerable output* from *consumed evidence* except a comment. **What is owed is the distinction made structural**: unrepeatable evidence lives where the ignore rule does not reach, or carries a marker the rule reads. Recorded with D-085's wider question — whether a generator that can only ever run once should be able to overwrite its own artefact at all — which is still not decided. **And the narrow, immediate one: before any destructive git operation in a tree with untracked files, the untracked set is READ, not stashed.** |

| **D-105** | **A DECLARED CONNECTOR OPERATION IS DRIVEN BY NO RUN THE PLATFORM CAN MAKE, AND UNLIKE ITS FOUR NEIGHBOURS IT CARRIES NO REASON.** Measured 2026-08-05 building Part 4, across a five-run workflow set sweeping both axes the harness can vary (`referenceInput` × `referenceDependencies`). **`TestManagementAdapter.findExistingTests` is invoked by none of them** — union **18 of 19** declared canonical operations. It was previously reported inside a two-item C-4 failure whose *other* item carried the whole explanation: *"defect publication requires an execution failure to publish about."* **That reason is now discharged and was never about this operation** — under `referenceDependencies('F')` the canonical path does call `publishDefect`, so the excuse was a property of which inputs the census ran, not of the runtime. Removing it leaves `findExistingTests` with **no stated reason at all**, against four sibling operations (`updateTestCase`, `createSharedStep`, `createSharedParameter`, `attachDesignArtefact`) that each carry one and are asserted NOT to be driven. | **THE ASYMMETRY IS THE FINDING.** `CANONICAL_DESIGN_SYNC_NOT_DRIVEN` exists precisely so that *"this operation is not invoked"* is a **declaration with a reason** rather than a gap — and C-4b asserts negatively against it, so driving one would be caught. `findExistingTests` sits in neither list honestly: it is in the DRIVEN list and is not driven. **So the canonical path either has a reuse-discovery step it does not perform, or it has an operation in its declared surface that belongs in the not-driven list with a reason.** Which of those it is decides whether this is a capability gap or a declaration defect, and **the census cannot tell them apart** — it observes the absence and cannot observe the intent. **Related but not the same as D-012's axis:** that is a declaration the implementation contradicts; this is a declaration nothing exercises, which is the *reachability* half of the same seam. | `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/repository-intelligence.ts` · `domains/test-management-intelligence.ts` · `governance/capability/run-functional-completeness.mjs` C-4 · `CANONICAL_DESIGN_SYNC_NOT_DRIVEN` · debt D-007 · D-012 | **OPEN — AND IT IS A DECISION OWED, NOT A DEFECT TO FIX. THE TWO CAUSES ARE NOT TWO REPAIRS OF ONE FAULT; THEY ARE TWO DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT THE PLATFORM, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS TRUE.** **(a) THE CANONICAL PATH DOES NO TOOL-SIDE REUSE DISCOVERY.** Then nothing is broken, the declaration is wrong, and the entry belongs **in ADR-0077 §4.7's neighbourhood** — it is a capability the canonical path does not have and will not, which is what §4.7's seven entries *are*. **It is NOT admitted to §4.7 here.** §4.7 is closed at eight and E-7 makes extending it an **amendment to ADR-0077**, never a register edit or a gate edit; and CU-6a is already measured and met, so an entry admitted now would be widening a difference set *after* the precondition it defines was satisfied. **What is owed is the amendment, or a ruling that this is not a §4.7 difference.** **(b) A CERTIFIED DOMAIN IS MISSING A CONNECTOR CALL IT SHOULD MAKE.** Then `repository-intelligence` reasons about reuse from the supplied `RepositoryIntelligenceModel` alone while a discovery operation sits declared and undriven, and the repair is a **behaviour change to a domain certified under ADR-0039** — its own ADR, its own conformance evidence, not a census edit. | **THE DISTINCTION IS THE ENTRY'S WHOLE CONTENT, AND THE CENSUS STRUCTURALLY CANNOT SETTLE IT.** It observes an absence; it cannot observe an intent. **Under (a) the platform is honest and its declaration is wrong; under (b) the declaration is right and the platform is incomplete** — opposite verdicts on the same measurement, and a tenant is told a different thing about reuse in each. **WHAT SETTLES IT IS A DOCUMENT, NOT A RUN:** whether ADR-0039's `repository-intelligence` domain contract obliges tool-side discovery, or obliges reasoning over a supplied model. That question is upstream of this census and was never asked of it. **THE PRESSURE, NAMED SO THAT TAKING IT IS A DECISION:** (a) is one line in `CANONICAL_DESIGN_SYNC_NOT_DRIVEN` and turns C-4 green immediately. **It is D-041 exactly** — the operation stops being counted as missing without anything having been answered — and it is worse than the ordinary case, because the four siblings each carry a reason that was *ruled*, so a fifth entry would inherit their credibility without having earned it. **DO NOT MOVE IT TO THE NOT-DRIVEN LIST TO CLEAR THE GATE.** Until it is ruled, C-4 fails and names this entry: **a red property naming an open decision is the correct state**, and it is D-012's own recommendation applied to a second finding. **Not repairable inside Part 4** — re-pointing a census is not where the platform decides whether it performs reuse discovery. |

| **D-106** | **THE IP's OWNERSHIP RECORD — THE THING ADR-0079 WOULD ASSERT AGAINST — IS UNTRACKED RUNTIME STATE, AND THE GENERATOR ALREADY EMITS FROM IT FAITHFULLY.** Measured 2026-08-06 during the P-78.8 mapping, before ADR-0079 was drafted. **(i) The pairing has exactly one home:** `tenants/<slug>/tenant.json` → `.onboarding.tenantId`, established once at `tenant-repository.ts:229–233` where `slug = slugify(organisationName)` and `tenantId = tnt-${randomBytes(6).toString('hex')}`. **No derivation exists in either direction and one was searched for** — six random bytes are not a function of a name and the name is not recoverable from them; every resolution in the plane is a SCAN. **(ii) `/tenants/` is `.gitignore:76`.** The registry is untracked, unversioned, single-replica local state, with no second copy by ADR-0032's design. **(iii) The generator does NOT invent the pair:** `solution-export.ts:453–455` (was `:400–402` before an out-of-band edit displaced the block on 2026-08-06; content unchanged) emits `tenantId: env.onboarding.tenantId` and `slug` from the SAME `TenantEnvelope`, so `config/connectivity.json` already carries the registry's own answer. Measured on disk: `tnt-42d3e7e9d324 / carlislehomes`, `epToken.version: 9` — **the exact pair the EP session reports regeneration restoring and the live IP rejecting.** | **THE OPTION AS POSED IS ALREADY IMPLEMENTED, WHICH MOVES THE DEFECT UP A LEVEL RATHER THAN CLOSING IT.** *"The generator must emit the correct pairing from the registry"* is satisfied in code today; what is wrong is **the registry it reads**, not the reading. So the hand-correction at EP `50d2433` is **a correction to an Intelligence-Plane record, applied in the customer's plane** — the one place it can neither be reviewed by DBiz nor survive the regeneration that owns the file. **That is the sovereignty defect, and it is worse than the data loss it was reported as:** the loss is recoverable by re-applying the edit; the misplacement means the IP's own registry stays wrong indefinitely while every regeneration looks like the thing that broke it. **The failure mode is documented in the code that now prevents new instances and cannot repair this one:** `tenant-repository.ts:216–227` refuses a twin slug precisely because *"a solution package generated from one then 403s against an EP token issued by the other"* — added after this tenant existed. **A second, independent finding on the same axis:** the reverse lookup is implemented TWICE with DIFFERENT safety, `resolveSlugByTenantId` (`registration.ts:430`) fail-closed on ambiguity (`hits.length === 1`) and `knownTenant` (`ip-execute-gateway.mjs:488`) returning the FIRST match — the seal-time path holds the unsafe one. **And a third:** `isolation.storagePartition` is the **tenantId** (`tnt-42d3e7e9d324`) while `tenantPartition()` returns **`t/<slug>`** — the declared partition identifier and the actual storage partition disagree, which is directly load-bearing on any key shape ADR-0079 rules. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/solution-export.ts:447–489` · `tenant-repository.ts:216–227`, `:229–233`, `:725–728` · `registration.ts:425–433` · `ip-execute-gateway.mjs:485–498`, `:565–580` · `platform-providers/src/tenant/tenant-context.ts:58–60` · `.gitignore:76` · EP commit `50d2433` (customer plane) · ADR-0032 · ADR-0079 (drafting) | **OPEN — A DECISION OWED BEFORE ADR-0079 IS DRAFTED, AND IT IS NOT THE BINARY IT WAS POSED AS.** *"Emit from the registry"* is already done, so the live choice is between **(a) correcting the IP registry entry and letting regeneration restore the corrected pair** — which returns the record to the plane that owns it and makes regeneration safe rather than destructive — and **(b) declaring `config/connectivity.json` tenant-owned**, which the file's own content argues against: it carries IP endpoints, transport, TLS and polling alongside the identity, so tenant-ownership would hand the customer four generator concerns to protect one field. **The evidence points at (a); it is recorded here and NOT taken in passing**, because correcting a tenant identity is a lifecycle operation on a PROVISIONED tenant at `epToken.version: 9` and it invalidates issued credentials. **What must not happen either way: ADR-0079 asserting write-time ownership against this registry without stating that the registry is untracked.** A store whose authorisation rests on an unversioned, unreplicated file is making a weaker claim than the ADR's language would imply, and the gap belongs in the decision rather than in this register. **— CONDITION MET, 2026-08-06. [ADR-0079](../docs/adr/ADR-0079-retrievable-package-store.md) is ACCEPTED and its §5.1 states it inside the decision**, in the terms this entry set: *the assertion is exactly as strong as an operator-editable local file, and no stronger.* Its §5.3 additionally carries the third finding above — `isolation.storagePartition` versus `tenantPartition()` — as an unresolved consequence rather than resolving it in passing. **THIS ENTRY REMAINS OPEN AND ITS SUBSTANCE IS UNCHANGED:** what was met is the *disclosure* obligation, not the defect. The registry is still untracked at `.gitignore:76`, the live pairing is still wrong, and the choice between (a) and (b) is still owed. **A gap that is now correctly described is not a gap that is closed** — and the reason to say so explicitly is that an ADR citing this entry reads, at a glance, like the entry has been discharged by it. **— CORRECTED 2026-08-06 BY [D-109](#), ON THIS ENTRY'S OWN LOAD-BEARING DESCRIPTION.** This entry calls the registry *"single-replica local state, with no second copy by ADR-0032's design."* **A second copy was measured, and the two disagree:** the live registry holds `carlisle-homes` / `tnt-eb7e75f1d0de` while the local dev registry at `tenants/` holds `carlislehomes` / `tnt-42d3e7e9d324`. **Two consequences for what is written above.** (i) *"the live pairing is still wrong"* is now specific rather than general — **the pair recorded in this entry as measured on disk is the ORPHAN**, and the id the EP and the live registry both hold is `tnt-eb7e75f1d0de`.
