(ii) **The choice between (a) and (b) is unchanged in shape but no longer the whole question**: correcting *a* registry entry presumes one registry, and there are two, so **which record survives is prior to where it is corrected.** That ruling is deferred pending an enumeration of both registries — D-109's piece (1). **Nothing here is retracted:** the durability finding (untracked, unversioned, no fallback copy) stands exactly as written, and is made worse rather than better by a second replica that no gate compares against it. |

| **D-107** | **THE CLASS, IN ITS STRONGEST FORM — REACHED 2026-08-06 AND PROMOTED TO LEAD THIS ENTRY, BECAUSE IT GOVERNS EVERY INSTANCE BELOW AND THE ORIGINAL HEADLINE IS ONLY THE FIRST OF THEM:** **A CLAIM THAT COULD HAVE BEEN CHECKED IN ONE RUN IS ASSUMED TO HAVE BEEN CHECKED, *PRECISELY BECAUSE* IT COULD HAVE BEEN.** **THE COST COMPARISON IS THE EVIDENCE, AND IT RUNS THE WRONG WAY:** D-107's own instance took a **parser run across 71 documents**; D-117's took a **cross-plane read and a deployment probe** against a live tenancy; and the third took **four lines against an artefact already in hand** — `composeExecutionPackage` then `certifyPackageForSealing`. **The two expensive checks were run. The cheap one was not.** And the cheapness is not incidental to why: an expensive check is *scheduled*, argued for, and its absence is conspicuous — **a four-line check is never scheduled, because it looks like something that must already have happened.** **THE CONSEQUENCE, RECORDED AS THE ENTRY'S GOVERNING FINDING: THE PREMISES THAT SURVIVED LONGEST IN THIS PROGRAMME WERE THE ONES EASIEST TO TEST.** Not the subtle ones, not the ones needing instrumentation nobody had built — **the ones whose test was so cheap that its absence was invisible**, and which therefore travelled unchallenged into frozen documents, governed prohibitions and accepted ADRs. **The repair follows from that and is not more verification: it is that a claim SHALL carry how it was established.** A prohibition resting on a claim about a control's SUBJECT shall say *measured* or *inferred*, because **an inference that reads as a measurement is one nobody re-tests** — and the cheaper it would have been to test, the more completely it reads as measured. **The original finding follows, and it is instance 1 of the class rather than the class itself.** **AN ADR'S STATUS IS READ IN TWO PLACES, WRITTEN IN THREE FORMATS, AND VALIDATED IN NEITHER — SO A THIRD OF THE PROGRAMME'S DECISIONS HAVE NO USABLE STATUS IN THE FROZEN CLOSURE BASELINE.** Measured 2026-08-06 while accepting ADR-0079, in two stages, and **the second stage refuted the first**. **(i) THE INDEX.** Comparing every one of the 71 ADR files' `Status:` line against its `DECISIONS.md` row found **three stale rows, all in the same direction — `ACCEPTED` on disk, `PROPOSED` in the index: [ADR-0070](../docs/adr/ADR-0070-execution-package-retrieval-inversion.md), [ADR-0076](../docs/adr/ADR-0076-declaration-typing-and-independent-review.md), [ADR-0077](../docs/adr/ADR-0077-canonical-authoring-cutover.md)** — while four others indexed `PROPOSED` (ADR-0037, ADR-0038, ADR-0066, ADR-0075) agree with disk and are correctly open. **ADR-0070 is the load-bearing instance:** ADR-0078 and ADR-0079 are both written as executing its §6 steps, so the index reported the parent decision as unsettled while two accepted children discharged it; **ADR-0077's work had already landed at `a335a75`.** All three corrected, each marked `(corrected 2026-08-06 — D-107)`. **(ii) THE REFUTATION — this entry was first written claiming *"nothing measures that field."* THAT IS FALSE.** [`emit-closure-package.mjs:116`](../governance/closure/emit-closure-package.mjs) parses it with `/\*\*Status:\*\*\s*([A-Z]+)/` and writes the result into **`ARCHITECTURE_BASELINE.md`, a closure register**. Measured across all 71: **`ACCEPTED`/`PROPOSED` 40 · `UNKNOWN` 22 · `A` 8 · `P` 1.** `**Status:** **ACCEPTED**` (the bold-wrapped form used by ADR-0077, ADR-0078, ADR-0079 and 19 others) fails the regex because `[A-Z]+` cannot match the leading `*` → **`UNKNOWN`**; `**Status:** Accepted` (title case, ADR-0070 among them) matches exactly one letter → **`A`**. **31 of 71 decisions — 44% — are recorded in the frozen baseline as either nothing or a single letter, and every gate is green over it.** | **A READER THAT CANNOT FAIL IS WORSE THAN NO READER, AND THIS ONE IS SHAPED SO THAT ITS FAILURE VALUE IS LEGAL.** `UNKNOWN` is not an error path — it is the `?? 'UNKNOWN'` default, emitted into a **closure register** and frozen there. The closure gate then verifies that register's *hash*, so **the parse failure is not merely tolerated, it is notarised**: whatever the regex produced becomes the baselined truth about what this programme decided. **`A` is the sharper half.** `UNKNOWN` at least announces that something is missing. **`A` is a value that looks like data** — it is in the column, it is not blank, it is not `UNKNOWN`, and it is the first letter of a word the parser silently truncated. A reader scanning `ARCHITECTURE_BASELINE.md` sees a populated cell. **Nothing anywhere distinguishes *"this ADR is accepted"* from *"the regex matched one character."*** **THE ROOT CAUSE IS THAT THE FORMAT DRIFTED UNDER A PARSER WHOSE FAILURE MODE IS SILENCE.** The regex was written for `**Status:** ACCEPTED`, which 40 ADRs still use. Later authoring conventions added bolding and title case — **both perfectly readable to a human, both invisible to the parser** — and because the failure value is legal, **no ADR ever failed to land on account of it.** The 22 `UNKNOWN`s accumulated one accepted decision at a time over months. **This is CHARTER §17.1.1's shape: a control-shaped thing reporting on a subset of its subject while presenting as complete** — and unlike the instances that rule caught in gate runners, this one is in the **closure package**, the artefact whose entire purpose is to be the durable record after everyone has stopped looking. **The two halves are ONE defect at two altitudes, which is why they are one entry:** `DECISIONS.md` reads the field by hand and drifted; the baseline reads it by regex and mis-parses. **Neither validates, and the field is the only one in either artefact that is a claim about another file rather than a copy of one.** | `governance/closure/emit-closure-package.mjs:116` (the regex) · `program/ARCHITECTURE_BASELINE.md` (36 `ACCEPTED`, 4 `PROPOSED`, 22 `UNKNOWN`, 8 `A`, 1 `P`) · `program/DECISIONS.md` status column and the three `(corrected 2026-08-06 — D-107)` markers · `docs/adr/ADR-0070`, `ADR-0076`, `ADR-0077` `Status:` lines · commit `a335a75` · D-065 (the ADR **count** drift — this is the **content** half) · CHARTER §17.1.1 | **OPEN. THE THREE ROWS ARE CORRECTED; NEITHER READER IS.** **What is owed is one repair, not two** — a single canonical status accessor that both `DECISIONS.md`'s check and the closure emitter use, and **whose unparseable case is an ERROR rather than a value.** Three properties, each learned from a specific instance here: **(1) it reads THE FILE, never a second list** — a register of expected statuses would be the third source of truth CLAUDE.md §5 forbids; **(2) an unrecognised status FAILS THE EMIT** — `UNKNOWN` and `A` must both become build failures, because the whole finding is that a legal failure value let 31 ADRs drift unnoticed; **(3) it must fail on the UNDER-CLAIMING direction too** — all three stale index rows read `PROPOSED` for an accepted decision, and under-claiming looks like caution rather than error, so nothing would ever have queried it. **NOT DONE HERE, AND THE EXCLUSION IS DELIBERATE:** this touches the closure emitter, so it lands with a fault proof under R-13.4/R-13.7 and CHARTER §18, and adding it while recording the finding is the scope error **D-087** counts. **A NARROW WORKAROUND WAS TAKEN AND IS NAMED SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR THE FIX:** ADR-0079's own status line is written in the parseable form, so the decision being accepted today is recorded correctly rather than as `UNKNOWN`. **That fixes one row of 31 and repairs nothing** — the other 30 are unchanged, and **the correct end to fix is the parser, not the documents.** No FROZEN ADR is edited to suit a regex. **The failure it sets up if left:** a decision indexed or baselined as `PROPOSED`/`UNKNOWN` reads as *available to reopen* — an invitation to re-litigate a settled ruling, or to author a second decision over ground the first already closed, which is CLAUDE.md §5's duplicate-creation failure arriving through the programme's own records instead of through a prompt. **Not repairable by discipline:** ADR-0077 was accepted, amended, executed and merged across two days by a process that touched the index twice and re-emitted the baseline, and the status field survived all of it wrong in both places. **— THE CLASS IS WIDER THAN THE STATUS FIELD, AND THE RULE IT EARNS IS RECORDED HERE AS THE ENTRY'S GENERAL FORM.** **A NUMBER IN A STATE FILE, A REGISTER OR AN ADR IS AN ESTIMATE UNTIL IT IS MEASURED.** Every such figure is a **claim about another artefact**, and the programme has no mechanism that compares any of them to their subject — so each is correct on the day it is typed and decays silently thereafter, with nothing observable changing. **It is CHARTER §17.1(i) read one altitude up:** that rule requires a gate's PASS-branch output to be derived rather than authored; this says the same of every population, count and citation a *document* asserts about the tree. **INSTANCES ON DISK, EACH CORRECTED AGAINST ITS SUBJECT RATHER THAN AGAINST ANOTHER DOCUMENT:** conformance criteria **417 → 422** (`NEXT_ACTION.md`, corrected against `baseline.json` and `verify-programme-closure`); registered gates **73 → 75** (`NEXT_ACTION.md`, corrected 2026-08-06 against the gate's own *"75 gates registered, 75 baselined"*); and **the sharpest, because it is not in a state file at all** — **ADR-0070 §6 step 4 and P-70.6's retirement scope, where EVERY figure and EVERY citation has drifted**: *"eight address-holding references"* measures **10 across 7 files**; *"four conformance tests at `:88,103,109,115`"* measures **seven tests at `:96,102,107,112,119,124,129`**, and **none of the four cited lines is a test boundary**; *"a registered fault proof at `record-fault-proofs.js:1455`"* measures **five proofs at `:1535–1580`**, and **line 1455 is a proof about a different module entirely** (`runtime-execution-spi.ts` executing a browser in the Intelligence Plane). **THAT LOCATION IS WORSE THAN A STATE FILE AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE POINT: a stale state-file number MISINFORMS A READER; a stale citation in a MIGRATION STEP MISDIRECTS AN ACTION.** P-70.6 insists the retirement is *"a scoped step with its own evidence … never a side effect of deleting a binding"* — and an implementer following its citations would retire the wrong things, miss four of the five fault proofs, and report the step complete. **THE PROGRAMME OWNER'S RUNNING COUNT IS FOURTEEN FIGURES CORRECTED BY THIS RULE. IT IS RECORDED AS THEIRS AND IS NOT VERIFIED HERE BY CENSUS — deliberately, because asserting a total for the rule that says totals are estimates until measured would be the defect performing itself.** Counting them is a measurement this entry owes, not a number it may quote. **WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY CLOSE THE CLASS:** figures that are claims about the tree are **generated into state files, or cited by symbol and count with the measurement's date**, never typed — the same repair D-107's parser needs, applied to prose instead of to a regex. **— THIRD INSTANCE 2026-08-06, AND IT ADDS A COROLLARY THE FIRST TWO DID NOT SUPPORT: THE CHEAPER THE TEST, THE LONGER IT GOES UNTAKEN.** `M5_CUTOVER_PRECONDITION_REPORT.md` §5 prohibited retiring `verify-package-governance` §13 with the gateway, on the stated ground that *"its subject is the **sealing point**, which moved — not the gateway, which is one implementation of it."* **Measured: false.** The gate's mandatory sections are `packageId`, `tenantId`, `schemaVersion`, `metadata.storyAnalysis`, `automation.manifest` and eight more — **all gateway fields** — and `SECTION_OWNERSHIP`'s own repair strings name the gateway's internals by module path. **The canonical package, which satisfies `parseExecutionPackage`, draws 18 blocking findings from it.** **THE CLAIM WAS AN INFERENCE ADOPTED AS A PROHIBITION, AND NOTHING IN THE SECTION DISTINGUISHED IT FROM THE MEASURED FACTS AROUND IT** — a prohibition inherits the authority of the section it sits in, which is how it reached a document governing a cut-over. **The corollary is what makes this instance worth more than the count:** D-107's own instance needed a parser run across 71 documents and D-117's needed a cross-plane read and a deployment probe, **but this one needed FOUR LINES against an artefact already in hand** — `composeExecutionPackage` then `certifyPackageForSealing` — **and had never been run.** *"The gate had never been run against a canonical package"* is not an oversight explained by cost; **a claim that could have been checked in one run is assumed to have been checked, precisely because it could have been.** **THE RULE ADDED TO THIS ENTRY'S REPAIR: a prohibition resting on a claim about a control's SUBJECT SHALL state how that subject was established** — measured, or inferred — because an inference that reads as a measurement is one nobody re-tests. **Ruled (a) on the measurement:** the gate retires with its subject (CHARTER §17.1.1 (ii)) and M5 is the deletion originally scoped. Report: [`M5_CUTOVER_PRECONDITION_REPORT.md`](M5_CUTOVER_PRECONDITION_REPORT.md) §7-§8. |

| **D-108** | **THE PLANE'S TENANT REGISTRY CONSTRUCTS ITS OWN TENANT-SCOPED PATH, WHICH R-07.2 FORBIDS, AND ADR-0079 DELIBERATELY DID NOT REPAIR IT.** Recorded 2026-08-06 at ADR-0079 §4 P-79.1 and §6. [`tenant-repository.ts:91-100`](../../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts) — `FileTenantConfigStore.dir()` — composes `resolve(this.rootDir, safe)` itself. **R-07.2:** *"A store SHALL NOT construct its own tenant-scoped path."* **R-07.3** requires exactly one constructor and names it: [`artefactPath`](../../packages/platform-providers/src/storage/storage-provider.ts). The code is **careful — the slug is defended twice before it reaches `resolve`** — so this is not a traversal defect and is not reported as one; **it is a single-constructor violation, and the property R-07.3 buys is lost by there being two constructors at all, regardless of how good the second one is.** | **THE REASON IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN FIXED IS THE REASON IT IS DANGEROUS TO FIX.** Moving the tenant registry onto `artefactPath` **changes where every tenant manifest lives** — it is a data migration against [ADR-0032](../docs/adr/ADR-0032-tenant-configuration-repository-ssot.md)'s single source of truth, on a **PROVISIONED tenant at `epToken.version: 9`**, and folding it into the introduction of a new and unrelated store is the scope error **D-087** counts. **What ADR-0079 did instead is the part worth preserving: it made the violation a NAMED DEFECT WITH A SUCCESSOR PATTERN rather than a precedent.** The new sealed-package store is the **first production consumer of `artefactPath`** (P-79.1), so the next reader looking for how a store obtains its location finds one correct example and one entry saying the other is wrong — **where, had the store hand-rolled its path too, they would have found two agreeing implementations and concluded that hand-rolling is how it is done here.** A second instance is not twice the debt; it is the point at which a violation becomes a convention. **This entry exists so the count stays at one.** | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts:91-100` · `packages/platform-providers/src/storage/storage-provider.ts` (`artefactPath`, `tenantPartition`) · R-07.2, R-07.3 · [ADR-0079](../docs/adr/ADR-0079-retrievable-package-store.md) §4 P-79.1, §6 *"Not in this sequence, deliberately"* · ADR-0032 · ADR-0010 §6 · D-087 · D-106 (the same file, on the durability axis) | **OPEN AND OWED, WITH ITS SCOPE ALREADY RULED.** It is **not** repaired inside ADR-0079 §6 and that exclusion is stated in the ADR rather than left to be inferred. **What is owed is its own change**, sequenced as a data migration under ADR-0032's authority: the manifest location moves, the registry's readers move with it, and the tenant currently on disk is migrated rather than re-provisioned. **The precondition is D-106, not this entry** — the same file's untracked, single-replica, unversioned posture (`.gitignore:76`) means there is no second copy to fall back to if a migration goes wrong, so **moving where it lives while it is still unreplicated is the riskier order.** Durability first, location second. **The standing constraint until then:** no third path constructor. Any new store obtains its location from `artefactPath`, and this entry — not the existing code — is the answer to *"but `tenant-repository` does it this way."* |

| **D-109** | **ONE CUSTOMER IS TWO TENANTS IN TWO REGISTRIES, AND EVERY RECENCY SIGNAL THE PLATFORM EXPOSES POINTS AT THE ABANDONED ONE.** Measured 2026-08-06, from a question about which `tenantId` Carlisle Homes maps to. **The pair:** `carlisle-homes` / **`tnt-eb7e75f1d0de`** — created 2026-08-04T00:53:03Z, `Provisioned`, `epToken.version: 1` (`…WKos`), **11** audit events, held by the **live registry** (the deployment's `/state` mount) and observed through the Tenant Onboarding portal; and `carlislehomes` / **`tnt-42d3e7e9d324`** — created 2026-07-23T14:54:59Z, `Provisioned`, `epToken.version: 9` (`…sSwc`), **52** audit events, `updatedAt` 2026-08-04T08:24:03Z, held by the **local dev registry** at `tenants/` (`.gitignore:76`). **The EP runtime holds `tnt-eb7e75f1d0de`** in `config/identity.json` and `config/connectivity.json` — the hand-correction D-106 records at EP `50d2433`. **The IP's own generated package still emits the other:** `generated/carlislehomes/config/connectivity.json` carries `tnt-42d3e7e9d324`, `slug: carlislehomes`, and an updates endpoint at `/api/tenants/carlislehomes/updates`. **THE RECENCY INVERSION IS THE MEASUREMENT:** on `updatedAt` (08:24Z vs 01:14Z), on token version (9 vs 1) and on audit depth (52 vs 11), the **abandoned** record is the newer, busier, more-rotated one — and [`dashboard.ts:38`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/dashboard.ts)'s `recently-updated` sort places it **first**. | **THIS IS THE 403 D-106 REPORTS, SEEN FROM ITS CAUSE RATHER THAN ITS SYMPTOM, AND IT CORRECTS D-106'S OWN DESCRIPTION OF THE REGISTRY.** D-106 calls the pairing *"untracked, unversioned, single-replica local state, with no second copy by ADR-0032's design."* **There is a second copy. There are exactly two, they disagree, and neither can observe the other** — so the durability finding and this one are different faults on one file. **THE GUARD THAT EXISTS CANNOT SEE THIS PAIR, FOR A STRUCTURAL REASON RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.** [`tenant-repository.ts:222`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts) does catch separator-twins — `identityKey()` collapses hyphens, so `carlislehomes` and `carlisle-homes` share a key — but it scans `this.store.list()`: **one store, one `rootDir`.** A collision across replicas is outside what it can address, and it is a **creation-time** gate, so it never re-asks. **Nothing anywhere revalidates the invariant after creation**, and the invariant it rests on — *one directory per customer, in exactly one registry* — is unenforced across replicas and unstated anywhere. **AND IT IS NOT REPAIRABLE BY A HEURISTIC, WHICH IS WHY IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN PATCHED.** *"Resolve to the most recent tenant"* is the obvious repair and it returns **this** wrong answer: recency measures activity, and the orphan is busier precisely **because** the dev loop kept regenerating against it after the real tenant was provisioned. **Identity has no recency, and the platform has no supersession** — both records read `Provisioned`, and no field in either can say the other exists. **The failure surfaces the only way it can:** a 403 at runtime, in the customer's plane, as [`onboarding-experience.test.ts:72-76`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/test/onboarding-experience.test.ts) already records — *"Nothing detected it until runtime."* | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts:214-227` (`slugify`, `identityKey`, the single-store guard) · `:451-455` (`archive`, manifest retained per R-21.24) · `dashboard.ts:38` · `registration.ts:430` (`resolveSlugByTenantId`, fail-closed) · `ip-execute-gateway.mjs:488` (`knownTenant`, first-match) · `test/onboarding-experience.test.ts:72-76` · `.gitignore:76` · `generated/carlislehomes/config/connectivity.json` · EP commit `50d2433` (customer plane) · ADR-0032 · D-106 (same file, durability axis) · D-107 (the same shape in `DECISIONS.md` — a claim about another file that nothing compares) | **OPEN. THE SURVIVOR DECISION IS DEFERRED BY RULING, 2026-08-06, PENDING AN ENUMERATION OF BOTH REGISTRIES — and the deferral is the correct state, not a delay.** It cannot be settled from a developer machine: the live registry is the deployment's `/state` mount and is unreachable from here, so **which records exist there is inferred from one portal view rather than enumerated.** Choosing a survivor on that basis is the D-041 shape — the question stops being asked without having been answered. **THREE SEPARABLE PIECES, AND THEY ARE NOT ONE CHANGE.** **(1) The scan**, read-only, over **both** registries, keyed on `identityKey` — it is the evidence the decision needs and it does not yet exist. **(2) The retirement**, once ruled: through [`archive()`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts) — `ACTIVE`/`SUSPENDED` → `OFFBOARDING` → `CLOSED`, **manifest retained under R-21.24** — and **never `delete()` or `rm`.** The loser's manifest is an **untracked, single-copy, 52-event audit trail**; destroying it is **D-104's failure mode exactly**, and its `lifecycleState` is `PROVISIONED`, which is not one of `archive()`'s accepted sources — **the transition is itself unbuilt and must not be forced.** Retiring `tnt-42d3e7e9d324` costs **no evidence artefacts** — EP `evidence/` measured **empty, 0 files** — and the July certification documents that cite it remain true **as history**. **(3) The gate**: no two tenants share an `identityKey`, evaluated **over the registry** rather than at creation, and **crossing replicas** — the property this pair violated. It lands as **its own change with its own fault proof** (R-13.4, R-13.7 clause 2, CHARTER §18) and **not folded into ADR-0079 §6**, which is the scope error **D-087** counts and **D-108** refused on this same file. **THE STANDING CONSTRAINT UNTIL THE SURVIVOR IS RULED: nothing regenerates or publishes from `generated/carlislehomes/`.** It was built from `tnt-42d3e7e9d324`; an update-install from this working copy writes that id back into the customer's plane and **reproduces the 403 while looking like a routine regeneration** — which is precisely the destruction path D-106 describes, where the correction lives in the plane that does not own it. **— PIECE (1) IS BUILT AND UNRUN, 2026-08-06.** [`governance/tenant-lifecycle/scan-tenant-registries.mjs`](../governance/tenant-lifecycle/scan-tenant-registries.mjs) — read-only, **not a gate**, exits 0 always. Every registry is DECLARED by the caller; an unreachable one is reported `unreachable` **with its cause** rather than omitted, so *"I was refused"* can never be read as *"there is nothing there"*. It reproduces this entry's local measurement exactly (`tnt-42d3e7e9d324`, `epToken.version: 9`, **52** audit events, `PROVISIONED`). **IT HAS NO NETWORK MODE, AND THAT IS A RULING RATHER THAN A CHOICE.** The first draft read the live registry over `GET /api/tenants`; [`verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js`](../governance/verification/verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js) refused it at **EG-2** — *"tool I/O belongs to the Execution Plane"* (R-3.2, R-6.3, R-14.16) — **and the gate was right, which settles something this entry left open:** *"unreachable from a developer machine"* is not a connectivity problem to be engineered around, it is the egress ban, and **no amount of credential provisioning makes the network route legal.** The live registry is therefore enumerated **where the IP runs**, against its own `/state` mount, and carried back as a file (`--dir /state/tenants --out live.json`, then `--merge live.json`); a merged source keeps its origin and scan digest, and a merged scan that was itself incomplete is carried **with** that fact rather than laundered into a complete-looking comparison. **THREE PATHS PROVED, AND THE THIRD WAS A DEFECT IN THIS TOOL:** a cross-registry collision is detected and stamped `survivorFromRecency: null`; a declared-but-unreachable source forces `INCOMPLETE`; and **a single-registry run reports `INCOMPLETE`, not a clean `COMPLETE`** — corrected during authoring, where it first reported *"no identityKey is shared"* from one registry. That is **the vacuous-green class D-011/D-015/D-103 count**: one store is exactly the blindness recorded above at `tenant-repository.ts:222`, so a green from it means *"I could not have seen one."* `identityKey` is module-private and therefore **mirrored and re-verified against the source text on every run**, reporting `fatal` on drift rather than grouping on a stale rule. **WHAT IS STILL OWED IS THE EVIDENCE, NOT THE TOOL:** the two-registry run **has not happened**, so the survivor ruling remains exactly as deferred as before, and **the deferral is still the correct state.** **Pieces (2) and (3) are untouched, deliberately** — and (2) remains blocked on its own unbuilt transition: both twins are `PROVISIONED`, which `archive()` does not accept. Suite after this change: **9 red, zero net new**; the egress gate passes standalone. |

| **D-110** | **A GATE THAT READS RAW SOURCE CANNOT TELL A CALL FROM A SENTENCE ABOUT A CALL, AND THE HONEST COMMENT BECAME THE VIOLATION.** Measured 2026-08-06 while landing `GET /api/packages/{hash}` (ADR-0079 §6 step 3). [`verify-http-surface-parity.js:240`](../governance/verification/verify-http-surface-parity.js) classifies a controller as dispatcher-backed with `/route\s*\(/.test(s)` where `s` is the file as `readOf` returned it — **raw, comments and strings included** (`:215`). `PackageController` is self-handled: it calls `handlePackageRetrieval()` and never the dispatcher. Its header comment said exactly that, in the words *"it does NOT delegate to route()"* — and **the gate read the disclaimer as the delegation**, demanded a dispatcher route for the `packages` prefix, and failed **HS-4**. | **THE FALSE POSITIVE LANDS ON THE FILE THAT DOCUMENTS ITSELF MOST CAREFULLY, WHICH INVERTS THE INCENTIVE THE GATE EXISTS TO CREATE.** A controller that said nothing about the dispatcher would have passed; the one that explained precisely why it does not use it failed. **The repository already knows this class and solves it two different ways, which is the finding.** [`verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js`](../governance/verification/verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js) and [`verify-execution-plane-boundary.js`](../governance/verification/verify-execution-plane-boundary.js) strip comments, strings, template text and regexes before scanning, *"so generation (the emitter) and detection (the gates) never false-positive"* — the correct fix, applied at the gate. [`record-fault-proofs.js`](../governance/verification/record-fault-proofs.js)`:44,:55` instead assembles `VENDOR_LITERAL` and `GA_CLAIM` from fragments so the recorder does not trip the gate it feeds — the workaround, applied at the source, with its reasoning written down. **This entry took the second, and says so rather than presenting it as a fix:** the controller's comment now avoids naming the dispatcher and explains why, so the file is honest about being contorted. **Why the gate was not repaired here:** comment-stripping changes what every parity check measures across five controllers and one dispatcher, and folding a detector rewrite into a route's introduction is the scope error **D-087** counts and **D-108** and **D-109** both refused on. | `governance/verification/verify-http-surface-parity.js:215` (`readOf`, no stripping) · `:240` (`isRouteBacked`) · `:271-276` (HS-4) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/package.controller.ts` (the contorted comment) · `registration.controller.ts` (the same self-handled shape, which passes only because it never names the dispatcher) · `record-fault-proofs.js:36-55` (the same workaround, precedent) · `verify-intelligence-plane-egress.js`, `verify-execution-plane-boundary.js` (the correct fix, already implemented elsewhere) | **OPEN.** The repair is to strip comments and string literals in `verify-http-surface-parity.js` before the `isRouteBacked`, action and prefix extractions, **and to re-run the comparison over the stripped text to confirm the existing 23 actions and 5 prefixes are unchanged** — a detector rewrite is not safe to land on the assertion that it only removes false positives. It carries its own fault proof: a controller whose *comment* names the dispatcher must NOT be classified as backed by it, and one that genuinely calls it must be. **Until then every self-handled controller in this plane is subject to the same trap, and the trap is silent** — it fires only when someone writes the accurate sentence. |

| **D-111** | **MOUNTING A ROUTE IS A CONFIGURATION ACT AND NOTHING GATES IT — SO A ROUTE CAN BE DECLARED, TESTED, GATED AND UNREACHABLE IN EVERY DEPLOYMENT.** Measured 2026-08-06 after landing `GET /api/packages/{hash}`. [`composeApiDeps`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/platform-adoption.ts) is the single composition root: it decides what an `ApiDeps` carries, and `AppModule.register` mounts each conditional controller only when its dependency is set. It sets `signPackage`, `epTokenSecret`, `registration`, `microsoftAuth` and `solutionOutputDir` — **so every conditional controller is mounted except one.** `packageStore` is the only `ApiDeps` field the composition root does not set, and `PackageController` is the only controller never registered anywhere. **Measured across all of `governance/`: ZERO gates reference `ApiDeps`, `composeApiDeps` or `platform-adoption.ts`.** | **THIS IS THE CONFIGURATION-THEATRE SHAPE ONE LEVEL OUT FROM THE ONE ADR-0079 CLOSED, AND THE LEVEL IS WHAT MAKES IT WORSE.** `verify-http-surface-parity.js` proves the SOURCE declares the surface — it reads decorators and dispatcher text and cannot see that a controller is conditionally registered on a dependency nothing supplies. `verify-http-surface.js` opens a socket and drives the ASSEMBLED application — **the one gate that could see this, and the gate that exists because `DELETE /api/tenants/%2e%2e` survived 58 gates** — and it does not probe `/api/packages` (measured: zero references). **The purge gap D-079's sibling entry records was INSIDE a module, where a source-scanning gate could reach it. This one is BETWEEN the module and the deployment, which is exactly where no gate in this suite looks.** By the standing rule — *a control that is declared but not executed is treated as absent* — this route does not yet exist. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/platform-adoption.ts` (`composeApiDeps`, the composition root) · `src/server/app.module.ts` (conditional controller registration) · `src/engine/api.ts` (`ApiDeps.packageStore`, the only unset field) · `governance/verification/verify-http-surface.js` (the socket gate, does not probe `/api/packages`) · `governance/verification/verify-http-surface-parity.js` (source-only) · D-112 (the same route, ungoverned on a second axis) | **OPEN. THREE PARTS, AND THE WIRING ALONE IS NOT THE REPAIR.** **(1)** wire `sealedPackageService(...)` into `composeApiDeps` — everything it needs is already there (`platform.storage`, `repo`, the logger), so this needs **no new configuration key, secret or infrastructure**. **(2)** **A GATE THAT MEASURES COMPOSITION:** every optional `ApiDeps` field a controller depends on is either set by the composition root or recorded as deliberately unmounted with a reason. **Without (2), the next optional dependency repeats this silently** — the wiring fixes one instance, the gate fixes the class. **(3)** extend `verify-http-surface.js` to probe `/api/packages` over the socket, **including the cross-tenant negative**, which today exists only as an in-process test. Lands with its own fault proof (R-13.4, R-13.7 clause 2). **Deliberately not wired in the change that found it: wiring it quietly would have closed the instance and left the class, which is the defect this entry is about.** **— CLOSED 2026-08-06, ALL THREE PARTS, AND THE THIRD PART FOUND A REAL DEFECT.** **(1)** `composeApiDeps` mounts the store via `sealedPackageService()` — not the bare constructor — so retention is running before the route serves a read, with an alert sink that raises at `error` level. **(2)** `verify-composition-root.js` is registered and gating: it DISCOVERS the conditionally-mounted set from source and requires every one to be wired or named in an explicit deliberately-unmounted list with a reason, with no third state; fault proof observed in PATCH mode (CLEAN 0 · FAULT 1 · REPLAY 1 · RESTORED 0) naming the branch and the field. **(3)** six socket probes in `http-surface.security.test.ts`, locked into `verify-http-surface.js`'s required-coverage list with its baseline raised 60 → 72. **THE THIRD PART WAS THE EASIEST TO ARGUE AWAY — the route was already tested — AND IT CAUGHT THE DEFECT.** `PackageController` read `req.principal`, which NOTHING in this application populates, so through the assembled app the route answered **401 to the package's rightful owner** while passing fifteen in-process assertions. The pure handler was correct; what its tests could not cover is whether anything supplies the principal. **That is this entry's own class, one layer further in**, and it is the founding class of the suite that caught it. |

| **D-112** | **A FROZEN ADR ASSERTS THAT TWO GATES GOVERN A ROUTE. NEITHER DOES, AND ONE OF THEM STRUCTURALLY CANNOT.** Measured 2026-08-06. **P-70.5** states the retrieval endpoint *"is therefore governed automatically by `verify-http-surface-parity.js`: the route must be served by `route()` and mapped by a mounted controller under the same verb, or the gate fails"*, and places it in scope for `verify-http-surface.js`. **As built, neither holds.** `route()` is a **pure synchronous** function; retrieval reads through the Storage Provider and is **async**. Serving retrieval through `route()` would make **every existing route** async to serve one new one, so it is served by a dedicated handler plus a self-handled controller — the shape `/api/register` already uses — and **parity excludes self-handled controllers by HS-4's own rule.** The socket gate named alongside it does not probe `/api/packages`. | **THE ROUTE IS CORRECT; THE GOVERNANCE CLAIM ABOUT IT IS NOT — AND THE CLAIM IS IN A FROZEN DOCUMENT.** P-70.5 was written before the store, the handler or the route existed, and it reasoned from the shape of the tenant routes, which are synchronous. **This is the same class as ADR-0070 §6 step 1's four assertions** that ADR-0078 §5 records as *"three of them false on the day they landed"*: a decision describing a mechanism that did not yet exist, contradicted by the disk once it does. **It compounds D-111 on a different axis** — that entry says the route is never mounted; this one says that even mounted it is governed by neither gate its ADR names. **Note what is NOT claimed:** the route is tested (15 assertions, including the four byte-identical refusals and the EP-token revocation check) and it is measured by `verify-data-sovereignty-store.js` at the store layer. **What it lacks is HTTP-surface governance**, which is the layer P-70.5 promised. | `docs/adr/ADR-0070-execution-package-retrieval-inversion.md` (P-70.5) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/package-retrieval.ts` (the async handler) · `src/engine/api.ts` (`route()` is synchronous; the pre-router branch) · `src/server/package.controller.ts` (self-handled) · `governance/verification/verify-http-surface-parity.js:240` (HS-4 scope; see also D-110) · `governance/verification/verify-http-surface.js` · D-111 · D-110 | **OPEN — AND IT IS AN AMENDMENT QUESTION, NOT A REPAIR.** ADR-0070 is **FROZEN**, so P-70.5 cannot be edited: correcting it requires a new ADR, which is the programme owner's call (CHARTER §9). **The same ADR carries a second measurable error** — doc 05 §9's *"the sole Execution-Plane-initiated route today mints a credential and carries no work"*, where **four** EP-initiated routes exist (`/api/register`, `GET`/`POST /updates`, `/installed`); the conclusion survives, the premise does not. **Both are carried to the AD-043 ruling rather than settled here**, because AD-043 will amend the same documents and splitting one amendment across two ADRs is the churn R-18.26 exists to prevent. **Until ruled, the honest statement is that `GET /api/packages/{hash}` is governed at the store layer and not at the HTTP layer** — and it must not be described as *"governed by gates that already exist"* (ADR-0070 §7), which is where this claim originates. **— PARTLY OVERTAKEN 2026-08-06, AND THE ENTRY STAYS OPEN.** The route is now governed at the HTTP layer after all, by a different route than P-70.5 named: six probes in `http-surface.security.test.ts` drive it over a raw socket and are locked into `verify-http-surface.js`'s required-coverage list, and `verify-composition-root.js` asserts it is mounted at all (D-111). **So the GAP is closed and the STATEMENT is still false** — P-70.5 says parity governs it and parity does not, structurally. **Recorded as carried into the ADR-0080 ruling** (its §5.2 states both this and doc 05 §9's count error and repairs neither). **The reason to keep this open after the coverage landed:** an entry closed because its symptom was cured, while a frozen document still asserts the wrong mechanism, is how a false statement outlives the condition that made anyone look at it. |

| **D-113** | **EP-TOKEN ROTATION IS NOT ENFORCED ON `/api/application-templates`, AND THE ROUTE THAT ESCAPES IT IS THE ONE THAT ESCAPES IT BY ARRIVING FIRST.** Measured 2026-08-06 while diagnosing OBL-002. The revocation check — `epp.version !== deps.repo.epTokenVersion(epp.slug)`, the whole of rotation-without-a-denylist — sits at [`api.ts`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/api.ts) **below** the application-templates branch, which returns at its own `if`. **Executed and confirmed, not inferred:** a `carlislehomes` EP token at **v3**, against a tenant whose current version is **v9**, is served **200** by that route. Six rotations that were supposed to kill it did not. **RECONFIRMED 2026-08-06 ON THE LIVE DEPLOYMENT, ON THE SURVIVING TENANT, WITH A TOKEN REVOKED MINUTES EARLIER BY AN ORDINARY OPERATOR ROTATION — which closes the one way this entry could have been discounted.** The first measurement used `carlislehomes`, the abandoned twin whose record has since been destroyed (**D-114**), so a reader could fairly have asked whether the hole was an artefact of a broken record. It is not. The operator rotated `carlisle-homes` **v1 → v2**; against `https://inteligenceplane.dbizsolution.com`, the superseded **v1** is refused **`401 {"error":"ep token revoked — regenerate it"}`** on `GET /api/tenants/carlisle-homes/manifest` and on `/updates` — **and served `200` by `GET /api/application-templates` in the same minute.** Rotation works everywhere it is reached and is not reached there. **The stale credential keeps that access until its own expiry, 2026-09-03**, which no operator action shortens. | **THE ESCAPE IS POSITIONAL, WHICH IS WHY NO REVIEW OF EITHER PIECE WOULD FIND IT.** Both the branch and the check are individually correct; what is wrong is that one is written above the other, and nothing in the file says the order carries a security property. **`package-retrieval.ts` already learned this exact lesson and wrote the check out by hand** — its step 2 says a superseded token *"is refused HERE or it is not refused at all"* — but it reasoned about the route that inherits NOTHING, and did not ask which routes inherit only SOME of the checks. `/api/application-templates` is that route: it takes authentication from the router and revocation from nowhere. **The exposure is genuinely small and is stated rather than used to dismiss the entry** — the catalogue names no customer, holds no credential, and is byte-identical for every caller, so a stale token gains only platform metadata. **The defect is that rotation has a hole at all**, because rotation's value is that operators can believe it: a revocation mechanism with one silent exemption is worse than a documented partial one. **It is also the reason OBL-002's diagnosis could exclude rotation** — measured, not assumed — so the entry is load-bearing for that report either way. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/api.ts` (the templates branch returns above the revocation check) · `src/engine/ep-token.ts` (`parseEpPrincipal`, the version-in-id mechanism) · `src/engine/package-retrieval.ts` (step 2 — the same check, written out, for the route that inherits nothing) · `src/engine/auth-refusal.ts` (OBL-002, which found this) · D-111 · D-112 | **OPEN — AND THE REPAIR IS NOT "MOVE THE BRANCH DOWN".** Reordering would close this instance and leave the class: the next route added above the check inherits the same hole, and the diff that adds it looks like a routing change. **The repair is to make revocation unskippable rather than well-positioned** — resolve the principal and apply the EP-version check in ONE place every transport funnels through, so a route cannot be authenticated without also being revocation-checked, and a new branch cannot opt out by being written earlier. `resolveCaller` in `createServer` and the controllers' shared adapter are now the single funnels per transport, which is where it belongs. **Deliberately NOT fixed in the change that found it:** OBL-002's change is the refusal taxonomy, and folding an authorisation change into it would put a security-behaviour change inside a diff reviewed as a diagnostics change. **A gate is owed with it** — that every route reaching an EP principal has passed the version check — because without one this is a positional property again, just positioned better. |

| **D-114** | **THE TENANT REGISTRY IS THE ONLY RECORD OF ITS OWN HISTORY, IT IS UNTRACKED AND SINGLE-COPY, AND ONE OF ITS RECORDS HAS NOW BEEN DESTROYED.** Measured 2026-08-06 while diagnosing OBL-002. **What existed:** `tenants/carlislehomes/tenant.json` — `tnt-42d3e7e9d324`, `epToken.version: 9` issued 2026-07-30, **52 audit events**, read by a session earlier the SAME DAY (its figures are quoted in that session's own record). **What exists now:** `tenants/` contains **only** `carlisle-homes/tenant.json` — `tnt-eb7e75f1d0de`, created **2026-08-06T01:23:28Z**, **2 audit events**, no `lifecycleState`, **no `epToken` field at all**. **Measured: zero remnants of `carlislehomes` anywhere in the tree**, and `generated/` is absent entirely. `tenants/` is `.gitignore:76`, so **no git copy exists and it is not recoverable from this machine.** | **D-109 PREDICTED THIS EXACT LOSS AND PRESCRIBED THE CONTROL THAT WOULD HAVE PREVENTED IT.** Its ruling: the loser's manifest is *"an untracked, single-copy, 52-event audit trail; destroying it is **D-104's failure mode exactly**"*, retirement is *"through `archive()` — manifest retained under R-21.24 — and **never `delete()` or `rm`**"*, and its `PROVISIONED` state made even `archive()` *"itself unbuilt and not to be forced"*. **The record was not archived. It is gone.** **THE FINDING IS NOT THE DELETION, IT IS THAT NOTHING COULD HAVE STOPPED IT.** There is no gate, no backup, no replica and no write-ahead record over `tenants/`: the registry that R-07.1 makes the root of tenant isolation, that ADR-0032 declares the single source of truth, and that **ADR-0079 P-79.2's write-time ownership assertion resolves through**, has **weaker durability than any artefact the platform governs**. §5.1 of ADR-0079 says the assertion is *"exactly as strong as an operator-editable local file, and no stronger"* — this entry is what that sentence costs when the file is edited. **AND IT SILENTLY BROKE THE LIVE CREDENTIAL:** `epTokenVersion()` reads `onboarding.epToken?.version ?? 0`, so the replacement record returns **0**, and the revocation check refuses every `ep:carlisle-homes:vN` token with **401 `ep token revoked — regenerate it`** regardless of signature or expiry. **One deletion invalidated the Execution Plane's credential with no message anywhere naming the cause.** | `tenants/` (`.gitignore:76`) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts` (`epTokenVersion`, `?? 0`; `delete`; `archive` and its accepted source states) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/api.ts` (the revocation check) · `governance/tenant-lifecycle/scan-tenant-registries.mjs` (D-109 piece 1, read-only) · D-109 (predicted this) · D-106 (the durability axis) · D-104 (the class) · ADR-0032 · ADR-0079 §5.1 | **OPEN — AND THE REPAIR IS DURABILITY, NOT A RULE ABOUT BEING CAREFUL.** D-109's `archive()` discipline was already written down and did not prevent this, so restating it is not a repair. **FOUR PARTS, SMALLEST FIRST, EACH LANDING WITH ITS OWN FAULT PROOF.** **(1) A WRITE JOURNAL.** `FileTenantConfigStore` appends every mutation — slug, operation, before-hash, after-hash, timestamp — to an append-only file **outside** the per-tenant directory, so removing a tenant directory cannot remove the evidence that it existed. This is the cheapest part and it is the one that turns an unexplained absence into a readable event. **(2) DELETION BECOMES UNREACHABLE FROM BUSINESS CODE.** `repo.delete()` is the only supported destructive path and R-21.24 already says manifests are retained; it should not be callable outside a lifecycle transition that has recorded its authority. Structural impossibility over discipline (C-0.1) — the discipline was tried. **(3) A GATE OVER THE REGISTRY'S DURABILITY POSTURE**, asserting the journal exists, is append-only, and is not inside `tenants/`. Its fault proof plants a mutation that bypasses the journal. **(4) THE REPLICATION QUESTION — ADR-0032's, and NOT settled here.** Whether the registry is tracked, mirrored to the Storage Provider, or backed up is a sovereignty decision about customer-derived configuration and needs its own ADR; **D-106 already carries it and this entry does not pre-empt it.** **WHAT IS NOT PROPOSED: recovering the lost record.** It is unrecoverable, the surviving record carries the CORRECT identity `tnt-eb7e75f1d0de`, and D-109's survivor question is now moot in the one direction that matters — **so the loss cost the audit trail, not the identity.** Saying that plainly is not mitigation; the 52 events are gone and no part of this repair brings them back. |

| **D-115** | **ADR-0080 P-80.5 DERIVES PENDING WORK FROM STATE THIS PLANE DOES NOT HOLD — MEASURED ONE HOUR AFTER THE ADR WAS ACCEPTED, WHILE BUILDING IT.** P-80.5 rules that *"pending work SHALL be derived from runs without evidence (R-20.12), and never from a record of what an Execution Plane has collected"*, and §6 step 3's completion condition **(b)** requires proving the derivation in **both** directions — *a run gains evidence and leaves the collection; a run without evidence survives repeated polls unchanged*. **Neither half is constructible. Measured across all 15 packages: nothing persists runs, and nothing persists evidence.** **(i) Runs:** the only durable per-tenant store is `tenant-repository.ts` (tenant envelopes, lifecycle, EP-token metadata, capability update events) and it holds **no runs**. Five engines keep `Map<runId, RunOutcome>` accumulators that are **process-lifetime, lost on restart and not tenant-indexed**; `reasoning-result-registry.ts` says of itself *"It is NOT a persistence database. It lives for one execution and is discarded with it."* The sealed package store's layout has a `run` segment and **hard-codes it to the constant `'sealed'`**, which its own header admits *"is neither a capability nor a run"*. **(ii) Evidence:** `POST /v1/evidence` shape-checks a reference, **writes one JSON line to stdout** and returns 202; `evidence-return-channel.ts` is a pure function that retains nothing. **(iii) The binding P-80.5 names by rule is schema-only.** `EvidenceReferenceSchema.packageHash` (R-20.12, C-20.10) is imported **only** by `contracts/test/*`, `compat/harness.mjs` and `emit-schema.ts` — **no engine, gateway or store ever parses an evidence reference through it**, and the type the engine actually uses (`EvidenceReferenceHandle`) has **no `packageHash` field at all**. | **THIS IS ADR-0078 P-78.6's SHAPE, EXACTLY, ONE DECISION LATER — AND THAT IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE GAP.** P-78.6 recorded that the package store *"does not exist: no persistence, no hash index, no ownership record"* and correctly **gated ADR-0070 §6 step 2 on deciding it** rather than building it in passing. **ADR-0080 did not make the same check before ruling the derivation**, so an accepted, FROZEN proposition now names a mechanism with nothing on disk behind it — the class ADR-0078 §5 records as *"three of them false on the day they landed"*, occurring in the ADR written to close that lineage. **WHY THE ROUTE WAS NOT SHIPPED ANYWAY, WHICH IS THE OPERATIVE DECISION.** The route, the collection shape, the inherited authorisation and the records-nothing-on-read property are all buildable and provable today; only the derivation is not. Shipping them behind an unimplemented `PendingWorkSource` port would mount an endpoint that **returns an empty collection to every caller, forever** — and under R-05.27 an empty collection is a **SUCCESS**, a positive assertion that no work is pending. **A port with no implementation would therefore tell every Execution Plane, with a 200, that it has nothing to do, and be indistinguishable from the truthful answer.** That is worse than an absent route, which at least 404s. **The declared-but-unbuilt shape (R-11.2) with a success status on it.** | `docs/adr/ADR-0080-work-request-exchange.md` (P-80.5, §6 step 3 condition (b)) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts` (the only durable per-tenant store; no runs) · `packages/contracts/src/evidence.ts:44-58` (`packageHash`, declared and unconsumed) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/execution.ts:38` (`EvidenceReferenceHandle`, no `packageHash`) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/runtime/evidence-return-channel.ts` (pass-through) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs:585-611` (evidence to stdout) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/registry/reasoning-result-registry.ts:31-36` · `packages/platform-providers/src/storage/sealed-package-store.ts:85-86` (`run` = `'sealed'`) · ADR-0078 P-78.6 (the same shape, handled correctly) · ADR-0079 (what deciding it looked like) | **OPEN — A DECISION IS OWED BEFORE ADR-0080 §6 STEP 3 CAN BE BUILT, AND IT IS NOT THIS ENTRY'S TO TAKE.** The question is **what record of runs and evidence the Intelligence Plane keeps**, and it is a sovereignty decision before it is an engineering one: a per-tenant record of runs and received evidence is **customer-derived C3 data persisted in the DBiz plane**, so it inherits **every** obligation ADR-0079 had to discharge for the package store — R-06.4's four conditions, a declared retention read by code, a scheduled purge with its unreadability proof, and a document-06 gate with its own subject. **Folding that into *"build the exchange"* is the scope error D-087 counts and which D-108, D-109 and ADR-0080 §5.2 have each already refused on this same axis.** **Three things the decision must settle, and none is a wire-shape detail:** **(1)** whether *"a run exists"* is recorded at authoring time by this plane, or inferred from the sealed package store — the store can enumerate hashes per tenant but **cannot say which represent outstanding work**, because it holds no run and deliberately no delivery state; **(2)** whether **received evidence** is recorded at all in this plane, given `POST /v1/evidence` currently logs to stdout and R-12.5 makes stages 10-12 the Intelligence Plane's — *an evidence record is the one signal that legitimately crosses back*, so this is not the P-70.3 violation it superficially resembles; **(3)** whether R-20.12's `packageHash` binding is **enforced** rather than declared, since P-80.5's derivation is only as sound as that link, and the link is currently consumed by nothing. **UNTIL IT IS RULED, `GET /api/tenants/{slug}/work` SHALL NOT BE MOUNTED** — and ADR-0080 §6 steps 3, 4 and 5 stay unperformed. ADR-0080 is FROZEN; this is recorded here and in `PROJECT_STATE.md`, **never written back into it.** **— RULED 2026-08-06 AS Q1 = B, Q2 = B, Q3 = B, AND DRAFTED AS [ADR-0082](../docs/adr/ADR-0082-run-and-evidence-record.md), STATUS PROPOSED, STOP FOR ACCEPTANCE.** A run is recorded **at authoring time** from the package stage 7 just sealed; **received evidence IS recorded**, replacing the stdout line; and **R-20.12's `packageHash` binding is enforced at ingress AND LANDS FIRST.** **ONE ADR, because the three answers are not independent:** a run record without an evidence record derives nothing, an evidence record that cannot be joined derives nothing, and either store without its retention, purge and gate is a customer-data store this plane is not authorised to hold — **three ADRs would produce three partial authorisations, and the first one accepted would create a C3 store whose obligations were scheduled.** **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS CARRIED VERBATIM INTO THE DECISION (P-82.3), AS THE RULE A LATER READER IS HANDED INSTEAD OF THE ARGUMENT:** *ask what changes when an Execution Plane re-fetches a package it already holds* — **under a delivery record something changes, and that is the defect; under an evidence record nothing changes**, because pending-ness never depended on fetching. **And the decisive reading with it: R-05.28 forbids the collection record in the SAME SENTENCE that requires the evidence record**, so the evidence record is **presupposed, not tolerated**, and **P-70.3 and R-05.28 are one rule seen from two sides.** **§4.4's boundary lands as a CONSTRAINT rather than a note (P-82.4):** references and hashes, **never payloads** — a record that accreted payloads would be an **unauthorised C1 store** converting a required signal into a **sovereignty breach**, and since C1 in this plane is *"ephemeral, never persisted"* it is a **condition on the store's existence**, not a labelling exercise. **Q3'S ORDERING IS IN §6 AS STEP 1 (P-82.5):** unbound evidence is unattributable, so **no run ever leaves the collection** and `/work` returns **the same work forever** to an EP that has already completed it — **a permanently non-empty falsehood, the fail-open port's mirror image one layer down and equally invisible**; both are Successes under R-05.5, both byte-identical to a truthful answer, and **neither reddens a gate.** **§3's full discharge is P-82.7**, and the `retentionObligationDays` note stays as written — two sides of the boundary, two numbers, both correct, traced to its single consumer before the note was written. **ONE THING THE DRAFTING SETTLED THAT THE REPORT DID NOT:** the doc-06 gate is **scoped to one subject by construction** and fails closed when that subject is absent, so ADR-0082 P-82.8 **extends it to a second enumerated subject rather than adding a sibling gate** — *"which stores does document 06 govern?"* must have **one** answer from **one** enumeration, and a second gate makes the answer *"however many gates someone happened to write"*, **which is exactly D-126's shape.** **`GET /api/tenants/{slug}/work` STAYS UNMOUNTED and ADR-0080 §6 steps 3–5 STAY UNPERFORMED until ADR-0082 is accepted** — the ADR-0078 P-78.6 precedent, which is the one this entry records ADR-0080 as having not followed. |

| **D-116** | **DECISION TRACEABILITY IS CARRIED BY A LABEL NOBODY IS OBLIGED TO WRITE, AND THE ONE CHECK OVER IT RUNS IN A SINGLE DIRECTION ON ONE OF THREE SPELLINGS — SO FOUR ACCEPTED ADRs CLAIM TO CLOSE DECISIONS THE ARCHITECTURE STILL LISTS AS OPEN.** Measured 2026-08-06, recording the catch that landed with ADR-0080. **THE CATCH.** [`verify-adr-completeness.js:87`](../governance/verification/verify-adr-completeness.js) builds `closedByAdr` from `/\*\*(?:Closes\|Resolves):\*\*\s*([^\n]+)/i` — **a structural label**. ADR-0080 was authored with the prose `**Answers:** **AD-043**` ([`ADR-0080:9`](../docs/adr/ADR-0080-work-request-exchange.md)), which reads correctly to a human, names the right decision, and **the gate cannot see**. Doc 05's v1.2 amendment header carries `**AD-043 is CLOSED**`, which `:102` *does* see — so `declaredClosed` held AD-043 and `closedByAdr` did not, and **the architecture would have declared a decision CLOSED with no ADR traceable to it.** A `**Closes:** **AD-043**` label was added **at acceptance**, and only then did the gate pass. **THE FINDING IS NOT THE MISSING LABEL — IT IS THAT A GATE AT ACCEPTANCE, NOT REVIEW, IS WHAT STOOD BETWEEN A CORRECTLY-REASONED ADR AND AN UNTRACEABLE CLOSURE.** The ADR was authored, read and accepted carrying prose that satisfied every human reader and no machine. **THE GENERALISATION, MEASURED, AND IT IS WORSE THAN THE CATCH.** The check is `declaredClosed → closedByAdr` and **has no converse**: an ADR may claim to close a decision the architecture never records as closed, and nothing looks. **Four do today.** `ADR-0001` **Closes:** AD-001 — the register lists AD-001 as **open at M1.6 in four documents** ([02](../docs/architecture/02-reference-architecture.md):162, [03](../docs/architecture/03-intelligence-plane-architecture.md):271, [04](../docs/architecture/04-execution-plane-architecture.md):160, [19](../docs/architecture/19-repository-ownership.md):139). `ADR-0015` **Closes:** AD-008, AD-009 — both **open at M1.2** in [04](../docs/architecture/04-execution-plane-architecture.md):161-162, [05](../docs/architecture/05-cross-plane-communication.md):188-189 and [20](../docs/architecture/20-cross-plane-contracts.md):179. `ADR-0030` **Closes:** AD-018 — [21](../docs/architecture/21-tenant-lifecycle.md):246 says *"**Resolved** by ADR-0030"*, a **third spelling the gate reads in neither direction**. **AND THE DECISION REGISTER — THE ACTUAL SSOT OF DECISION STATE — IS NOT READ AT ALL.** AD-043's row reads `\| … \| **CLOSED 2026-08-06** — [ADR-0080](…) \|`; `:102` matches only `**AD-nnn is CLOSED**`, so **the row did not register** and AD-043 was seen solely because of incidental prose in an amendment header. **A future ADR that closes a decision and updates only the register — the natural and correct place — leaves this gate silent.** | **THE PROGRAMME'S DECISION LEDGER IS NOT MEASURED; IT IS SPOT-CHECKED IN ONE DIRECTION ON ONE OF THREE SPELLINGS.** This is **D-007's class** — declaration-versus-implementation drift — landing on the decision layer itself, and it is load-bearing: `verify-programme-closure` freezes the ADR set and the closure baseline records which decisions are closed. **Four ADRs and the architecture disagree right now, both sides ACCEPTED and FROZEN, and every gate is green.** The gate's PASS reads *"every decision is traceable to a complete ADR"* and means *"every decision spelt `**AD-nnn is CLOSED**` or `**Resolves:**` in an architecture document is named by an ADR spelt `**Closes:**`"* — **a property scoped until it cannot fail**, which R-13.7 clause 1 legislates against. It is also **D-107's shape one decision later**: *an ADR's status read in two places, written in three formats, validated in neither.* Same defect, different field. | [`governance/verification/verify-adr-completeness.js:85-113`](../governance/verification/verify-adr-completeness.js) (`:87` ADR side — `src.match`, **first occurrence only**, so a second `**Closes:**` line in one ADR is unread; `:100-102` architecture side; `:110-112` the one-directional check) · [`docs/adr/ADR-0080-work-request-exchange.md:9-12`](../docs/adr/ADR-0080-work-request-exchange.md) (the `Answers:` prose and the at-acceptance note recording it) · `docs/adr/ADR-0001-platform-language-and-runtime.md`, `ADR-0015-degraded-operation-mechanism.md`, `ADR-0030-tenant-lifecycle-management-orchestration.md` (the four unreciprocated claims) · [`docs/architecture/05-cross-plane-communication.md:179`](../docs/architecture/05-cross-plane-communication.md) (the register row the gate does not read) · D-007 (the class) · D-107 (the same defect on ADR status) | **OPEN — RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, BECAUSE THE REPAIR IS TWO DECISIONS AND NEITHER IS THIS ENTRY'S.** **(1) The gate is the smaller half:** add the converse check, read the decision-register tables, accept the three spellings — or **rule one spelling canonical and migrate**, which is the better answer and the larger change. **(2) The larger half is an architecture ruling this register does not own: ARE AD-001, AD-008, AD-009 AND AD-018 CLOSED?** Their ADRs are ACCEPTED and say so; four architecture documents say otherwise. **Tightening the gate first would convert four silent disagreements into four red gates with no ruling behind them**, and reconciling them by editing whichever side is cheaper is precisely how a second source of truth gets written. **The decisions are settled first, in the architecture; the gate is tightened second, so it reddens on drift rather than on an unruled backlog.** Until then, **`verify-adr-completeness`'s PASS SHALL NOT be cited as evidence that the decision ledger is consistent** — it measures one direction of one spelling, and this entry names what it misses. |

| **D-117** | **CORRECTED 2026-08-06 BY MEASUREMENT, AND THE CORRECTION IS NOW THE HEADLINE: THIS ENTRY'S LOAD-BEARING CLAIM — *"NOTHING PARSES WHAT THIS PLANE EMITS, SO NOTHING CAN CONTRADICT IT"* — WAS ALREADY UNTRUE ON THE DAY IT WAS WRITTEN.** **`SealedPackageStore.put` is a consumer of the published contract, in production code, on the serving path** — it reads `provenance.tenantId`, `provenance.contentHash.value` and `validity.notAfter`, all three the contract's own vocabulary — **and it has been refusing the gateway all along.** Silently, and only because **nothing has ever called it** (D-122). **THE PARSE EXISTED; IT HAD NO CALLER.** That is a materially different defect from the one recorded below, and it is worse in one respect and better in another: the contract was never uncontradicted — it was **uncontradictable**, because the single consumer positioned to contradict it was unreachable. **The corollary reverses this entry's remedy.** A consumer that *agrees with the contract and refuses what diverges* is not evidence that the contract is wrong; it is the contract working. **D-121 settled it as none of (a), (b) or (c): the contract is not wrong, the producer is, `CONTRACT_VERSION` stays at 1.0.0, and nothing is amended.** The original finding follows and is retained in full, because its (i) and (iii) are unaffected and correct. **THE EXECUTION-PACKAGE CONTRACT IS PUBLISHED, AND THE PACKAGES THIS PLANE ACTUALLY SENDS DO NOT SATISFY IT — MEASURED FROM THE EXECUTION PLANE, WHICH IS THE ONLY PLACE IT COULD BE.** Measured 2026-08-06 while the EP tried to build its executor from the published schema, as C-20.4 intends. **(i) THE SHAPES SHARE NO REQUIRED FIELD NAME.** `OperationSchema` ([`contracts/src/execution-package.ts:46-52`](../packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts)) **requires `operationId` and `kind`**. What is emitted — `groundOperations()` on the live authoring path, and the smoke body — is `{ id, action, target\|selector, value?, expect, testCaseId, authoredFor, groundedControl? }`. **Neither `operationId` nor `kind` is ever produced, by any path, and no mapping exists at the boundary.** The published and emitted descriptions are of different objects. Searched: `operationId` appears in the schema source, an `observation-set` field, `canonical-authoring-composer.ts:52`, and a **documentation example** in `customer-success/src/api-reference.ts:310` — **nowhere in the gateway or the bridge.** **(ii) THE DIVERGENCE IS UNDETECTED BECAUSE THE SCHEMA IS ENFORCED ONLY AGAINST ITSELF.** ***— THIS IS THE CLAUSE THE HEADLINE CORRECTS. It is true of `parseExecutionPackage` and false of the contract, and the difference is the whole finding: the enforcement that existed was not a parse. `SealedPackageStore.put` enforces three of the contract's own fields without calling the parser, so a search for the parser's call sites could not see it.*** `parseExecutionPackage` has exactly one family of call sites: [`contracts/compat/harness.mjs`](../packages/contracts/compat/harness.mjs), **against fixtures the harness authors**. `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` — the module that serves packages — **never parses one through the schema it publishes.** The compatibility gate is green because it validates the contract against fixtures written to the contract. **(iii) AND THE DECLARED CARRIER DECLARES NOTHING EXECUTABLE.** Even where the schema is obeyed, `kind` is `z.string().min(1)` — **no enum** — and `parameters` is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` — **no shape.** A fully conforming package can tell an executor nothing about what to do. The emitted `action` **does** have a closed vocabulary — `navigate`, `fill`, `click`, `assertText` — and it exists **only inside `groundOperations()`**, published nowhere. **(iv) NO SECTION DECLARES WHAT IS MATERIALISED.** `automation.assets` (`EmittedAsset = { language: 'gherkin'\|'typescript'\|'json', body: string }`), `automation.manifest`, `metadata.*`, `traceability`, `executionMetadata`, `certification` and `manifest` are enumerated in `SECTION_OWNERSHIP` and are **absent from the published contract entirely.** | **THIS IS D-115'S SHAPE ON THE CONTRACT THAT CARRIES EVERYTHING ELSE — *declared and unconsumed* — and it is the third instance in three days.** D-115: `EvidenceReferenceSchema.packageHash`, declared, consumed by tests only. **D-117: the whole execution-package schema, declared, consumed by its own harness only.** In both, a gate is green because the artefact is checked against something authored to match it. **C-20.4 EXISTS SO THAT BOTH PLANES VALIDATE AGAINST THE ONE ARTEFACT, AND ONLY ONE PLANE DOES.** The Execution Plane validated against the published contract and was correct to; this plane emits without validating and has never been contradicted. **THE MEASUREMENT COULD ONLY COME FROM THE OTHER SIDE, WHICH IS THE GOVERNANCE FINDING.** No gate here compares emitted to published, so the divergence was invisible from inside — and it surfaced only because a customer-owned plane read the contract and found it did not describe what it receives. **A contract that only its author validates is a document, not a contract.** | [`packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts:46-52`](../packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts) (`operationId`, `kind` required; `parameters` unconstrained) · [`packages/contracts/schema/execution-package-v1.0.0.json`](../packages/contracts/schema/execution-package-v1.0.0.json) (the published artefact) · [`packages/functional-testing-engine/authoring-bridge.mjs:667-711`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/authoring-bridge.mjs) (`groundOperations` — `id`/`action`, and the four-value vocabulary) · [`packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs:357`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs) (the smoke body, same shape) · [`packages/contracts/compat/harness.mjs`](../packages/contracts/compat/harness.mjs) (the only enforcement, against its own fixtures) · [`packages/functional-testing-engine/src/contract/package-governance.ts`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/contract/package-governance.ts) (`SECTION_OWNERSHIP` — the sections the contract does not carry) · D-115 (the same *declared-and-unconsumed* shape) · D-007 (the class) | **OPEN — AND THE CHOICE IS A CONTRACT DECISION, NOT A RENAME.** Two closures, and **the Intelligence Plane must pick one rather than let the EP pick:** **(1) MAP AT THE BOUNDARY** — the gateway projects `GroundedOperation` onto `{ operationId, kind, parameters }` before sealing, and `kind` becomes the published vocabulary. Keeps the contract as written; makes the internal shape genuinely internal. **(2) AMEND THE PUBLISHED CONTRACT** to describe what is sent, with the `kind` enum and a `parameters` shape per kind, at a moved version. **Whichever is chosen, two things are owed with it and are the reason this is not a small change:** **(a) the vocabulary and parameter shape SHALL be published**, because an executor's whole behaviour derives from them and today they are readable only by reading this plane's source — which is the boundary violation the EP correctly refused to commit; **(b) `parseExecutionPackage` SHALL be called on the emission path**, not only in the harness, or the next divergence is found the same way — by a customer. **NOT REPAIRED HERE, DELIBERATELY:** this changes the wire format between two sovereign planes and is exactly the change CLAUDE.md §4 forbids authoring in one step across both. **It is also NOT a reason to unblock the EP's executor** — until one of the two lands, an executor on either shape is built on something no contract guarantees. **— AMENDED 2026-08-06: THE CLASS ABOVE THIS INSTANCE, WHICH IS WHY THE GATE WAS GREEN THROUGHOUT.** This entry records that the shapes disagree. **It does not yet record why nothing caught it, and that is the transferable half.** **`parseExecutionPackage` has exactly three classes of call site and no others:** its own definition, the barrel re-export, `packages/contracts/compat/harness.mjs` (nine calls) and `packages/contracts/test/contracts.test.ts`. **No engine, no gateway, no store, no emitter and no route parses a package through it.** The authoring path assembles an object and emits it; `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` serves it; nothing between them consults `ExecutionPackageSchema`. **And the documents the harness parses are the corpus the harness authors, to the contract's own shape** — so **the gate validates the contract against documents written to the contract**, and a producer that never parses cannot be contradicted by a parser that only ever sees documents written for it. **THE ONE FIXTURE THAT EXERCISES OPERATIONS MAKES IT WORSE:** `compat/fixtures/v1.0.0/execution-package.full.json` uses `kind` values **`browser.navigate`** and **`api.invoke`** — **tool names**, which `OperationSchema`'s comment forbids **in the same file** (*"Capability-named, never tool-named (R-7.3, R-14.14)"*). **The corpus does not merely fail to catch the drift; it encodes a breach of the rule stated beside it.** **THIRD INSTANCE IN THREE DAYS, AND THIS ONE IS ON THE CONTRACT CARRYING EVERYTHING ELSE** — after `frameworkVersions` (never written; empty `devDependencies` shipped to every wizard-onboarded tenant) and `EvidenceReferenceSchema.packageHash` (declared, consumed by nothing — D-115). **WHAT THE AMENDMENT MUST THEREFORE CLOSE, OR IT MOVES THE FICTION INSTEAD OF REMOVING IT: the gateway parses what it serves.** `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` SHALL run every package through `parseExecutionPackage` before serving, so a producer that stops conforming fails **at the producer, in the run that produced it** — not days later by reading two files side by side. **And the corpus SHALL gain a fixture captured from a real authored package**, so backward compatibility is measured against something this plane actually produced rather than against its own restatement of the schema. **Both land with the amendment, not after it — otherwise the schema is merely unconsumed at a new version.** **— CORRECTED 2026-08-06 BY MEASUREMENT, AND THE ENTRY WAS TRUE ABOUT THE SHAPES AND WRONG ABOUT THE CONSEQUENCE. THE HEADLINE ASSERTS THAT THE PACKAGES THIS PLANE *SENDS* DO NOT SATISFY THE CONTRACT — AND THIS PLANE SENDS NOTHING.** The gateway is not on the path the Execution Plane calls. `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` binds **`127.0.0.1` only**, on its own port, in its own process, and `process.exit(1)`s if `DBIZ_ENV` or `NODE_ENV` is `production` — **it refuses to serve a production deployment by its own design** (ADR-0049 M5, stated in its header). Measured on the live deployment with a deliberate control: `POST /v1/execute`, `POST /v1/evidence` and `POST /zzz-deliberately-nonsense-path` return **byte-identical 335-byte responses**, and the bytes are **Azure Blob Storage's `UnsupportedHttpVerb` page** — `/v1/*` never reaches the application at all. `POST /api/packages/notahash` returns NestJS's own `{"statusCode":404,"message":"Cannot POST …"}` in the same minute, proving the application is up and the fallthrough is specific rather than an outage. **So the divergence between the gateway and the contract is REAL and is NOT A DIVERGENCE ON THE WIRE, because that wire is not connected.** **AND THE SECOND CORRECTION IS THE LARGER ONE: the claim that *nothing parses what this plane emits, so nothing can contradict it* IS NO LONGER TRUE, AND WAS ALREADY NOT TRUE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN.** `SealedPackageStore.put` — production code, on the serving path, deployed — **reads `provenance.tenantId`, `provenance.contentHash.value` and `validity.notAfter`, all three of them the contract's own vocabulary**, and refuses a body that lacks them. **Measured**: a package authored by the live path through the running gateway, handed to a real store in `carlisle-homes`' partition, is refused — `SealedPackageWriteRefused: sealed body carries no provenance.tenantId` — while a contract-conforming control body is **accepted and served back** by the same store in the same run. **A consumer of the published contract has existed all along, it is the one the tenant-isolation property is derived from, and it has been silently refusing the gateway** — silently only because nothing has ever called it (**D-122**). **THE ENTRY'S REPAIR DIRECTION IS THEREFORE INVERTED: option (1) "map at the boundary" and option (2) "amend the published contract" both presume the emitted shape is owed a description. It is not owed one — it never crosses.** The repair is at the producer, and ADR-0049 M5 already schedules it. See [`D-121_DECISIVE_TEST_MEASURED.md`](D-121_DECISIVE_TEST_MEASURED.md) and D-121's own amendment. **WHAT SURVIVES THE CORRECTION UNCHANGED, AND IS STILL THE ENTRY'S VALUE: (i) the shapes genuinely disagree; (ii) `parseExecutionPackage` is still absent from every emission path; (iii) the compatibility gate still validates the contract against documents written to the contract; (iv) the `kind` vocabulary and `parameters` shape are still published nowhere.** None of those depended on the wire being connected. |

| **D-118** | **THE VERSION-NEGOTIATION MECHANISM R-20.24 REQUIRES EXISTS, IS TESTED, AND IS NOT WIRED TO THE ONLY FUNCTION THAT PARSES A PACKAGE — AND A TEST ASSERTS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE PARSER DOES.** Measured 2026-08-06 while scoping the OBL-004 amendment. `version.ts` declares `SUPPORTED_MAJORS = [1]`, `majorOf()` and `isSupported()`, for R-20.24: *"The IP must support every deployed version."* **`parseExecutionPackage` calls none of them** — it compares `parsed.contractVersion !== CONTRACT_VERSION` and throws, **exact match or refuse**. [`contracts.test.ts:215`](../packages/contracts/test/contracts.test.ts) asserts **`isSupported('1.4.2') === true`** with the comment *"a later minor within a supported major must parse"* — **and `parseExecutionPackage` throws on it.** Two functions in one package disagree about one rule, and only the predicate is tested. | **THIS IS LOAD-BEARING ON THE OBL-004 AMENDMENT AND IS WHY THE VERSION MOVE IS NOT A ONE-LINE EDIT.** Moving `CONTRACT_VERSION` to `1.1.0` makes **every retained `v1.0.0` fixture fail on the VERSION CHECK ALONE**, before any field-shape question — so `verify-contract-compatibility` reddens for a reason unrelated to the amendment's substance, **and the wrong repair is then obvious and available: re-author the retained fixtures, which would destroy the only evidence the R-19.11 promise has.** **AND IT REVEALS THE PROPERTY HAS NEVER BEEN EXERCISED.** The corpus holds exactly **one** version directory, `v1.0.0`, **equal to the current version** — so *"every retained fixture still parses"* has only ever asserted that current-version documents parse at the current version. **R-19.11's promise to customers — that an older Execution Plane may run against a newer Intelligence Plane — has never been measured across a version boundary, because no boundary has ever existed.** The mechanism to honour it was built, tested in isolation, and left unconnected: **the same class as D-117 and D-115.** | `packages/contracts/src/version.ts:14-27` · `packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts:131` (exact-match refusal) · `packages/contracts/test/contracts.test.ts:215` · `packages/contracts/compat/harness.mjs:116` (negotiation tested against the predicate only) · `packages/contracts/compat/fixtures/` (one directory, equal to current) · D-117 · D-115 | **OPEN — AND IT IS A PRECONDITION OF THE OBL-004 AMENDMENT RATHER THAN A CONSEQUENCE OF IT.** `parseExecutionPackage` SHALL accept any version whose **major** is in `SUPPORTED_MAJORS` — the predicate that already exists and is already tested — and refuse only an unsupported major, which is what R-20.24 and R-19.11 say and what `:215` already asserts. **THE ORDER IS NOT NEGOTIABLE: wire the tolerance FIRST, then move the version.** Wiring it is a fix to a **fail-closed** refusal and is safe on its own; moving the version first reddens the gate for the wrong reason and invites the repair that destroys the evidence. Only after it does 1.1.0 leave the v1.0.0 fixtures parsing — **and a `v1.1.0` fixture directory landing beside the retained `v1.0.0` one makes the compatibility property non-vacuous for the first time.** **— AMENDED 2026-08-06 ON LANDING THE TOLERANCE. THE SECOND HALF IS RECORDED ABOVE THE FIRST, BECAUSE IT IS THE LARGER FINDING AND IT IS NOT YET CLOSED.** **R-19.11's compatibility promise — that a customer's Execution Plane may run OLDER than the Intelligence Plane — HAD NEVER BEEN MEASURED ACROSS A VERSION BOUNDARY, BECAUSE NO BOUNDARY HAD EVER EXISTED.** The corpus held exactly **one** fixture directory, `v1.0.0`, **equal to the current version**, so *"every retained fixture still parses"* asserted only that current-version documents parse at the current version. **The gate was green because the condition it guards had never occurred** — the same shape as D-117 one level out: there, a parser that only ever saw documents written for it; here, a compatibility property that only ever saw one version. **The parser disagreeing with its own predicate was the visible symptom; this is the structural cause, and a symptom fixed above a cause that stands is how the next instance arrives silently.** **WIRED, 2026-08-06:** `parseExecutionPackage` now calls `isSupported()` and refuses only an unsupported MAJOR (R-20.24, R-19.11). Demonstrated: `1.4.2` parses and **the parsed version is the CALLER'S, not rewritten to this build's** — rewriting would be the silent reinterpretation R-20.1 forbids; `2.0.0` is refused with a message naming the supported majors.
