The predicate/parser disagreement is now **impossible to reintroduce silently**: a test asserts the parser and the predicate agree on **every** input the predicate is tested with, in the same file. **STILL OPEN, AND THIS IS THE PART THE WIRING DOES NOT CLOSE: the boundary still does not exist.** `verify-contract-compatibility` reports **1 version(s)** and will keep reporting it until a `v1.1.0` fixture directory lands beside the retained `v1.0.0` one at step 2. **Until then the promise is honourable in code and still unmeasured in evidence**, and this entry stays open to say so. |

| **D-119** | **THE PLATFORM'S CREDENTIAL REMEDIATION ASSUMES A VAULT RESOLVER THE GENERATED SOLUTION DOES NOT CARRY.** Measured 2026-08-06 **from the Execution Plane**, and the instruction that prompted it was **WITHDRAWN on that measurement.** The remediation directs an operator to blank the plaintext credential at `.env:24`, place it under `.secrets/`, and confirm with **`npm run readiness`**. **That script does not exist in the generated solution.** It is an Intelligence-Plane operational note describing a check the emitted Execution Plane never received. | **THE REMEDIATION IS NOT MERELY INEFFECTIVE — IT BREAKS A WORKING CREDENTIAL TO ACHIEVE NOTHING, AND IT IS THE FAIL-OPEN SHAPE IN AN OPERATIONAL PROCEDURE.** Blanking `.env:24` for `.secrets/` **moves plaintext to plaintext**: no resolver in the generated solution reads `.secrets/`, so the value lands on a disk **nothing reads**. **(i)** The credential stops working. **(ii)** The check that would have caught it does not run, so the operator is told to satisfy a gate that **cannot fail** — *configuration theatre*, which document 06's R-06.12 rationale names as the predecessor's actual defect and which this programme has now found at three layers in three days. **(iii) It is wrong for EVERY tenant, not this one** — it is emitted from the same generator, so any tenant following it moves a working secret to an unread location. **This is D-115's fail-open asymmetry expressed as a procedure rather than a port:** the instruction *appears* to harden the deployment, measurably weakens it, and **nothing on either side reports the difference.** | the generated solution's `package.json` (no `readiness` script) · `.env:24` · `.secrets/` (no reader) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/solution-export.ts` and `packages/platform-core/src/solution-generator.ts` (the emitters that would carry a resolver) · D-115 (the same asymmetry, in a port) · document 06 R-06.12 | **OPEN — AND IT IS A FORK, NOT A FIX.** Exactly one of two things is true and the platform must choose: **(a) the generated solution SHALL carry a vault resolver** — the emitted EP reads `.secrets/` or a real secret backend through a declared mechanism, and `readiness` becomes a script it actually has; or **(b) the remediation is WRONG and SHALL be withdrawn from every tenant-facing artefact that carries it**, not merely from the conversation it appeared in. **There is no third option in which the instruction stands as written**, because it names a check that does not exist and a location nothing reads. **It is an Intelligence-Plane obligation although the symptom is in the customer's plane:** the IP emits the solution *and* authors the remediation, so both halves of the contradiction are DBiz-owned and **neither is repairable from the Execution Plane**. **Nothing is to be changed in the customer's plane on the strength of this entry** — the instruction is withdrawn, the credential stays where it is, and the fork is settled here first. |

| **D-120** | **THE COMPATIBILITY CORPUS ENCODES A BREACH OF THE RULE STATED BESIDE THE SCHEMA IT VALIDATES.** Measured 2026-08-06 while scoping the OBL-004 amendment. `compat/fixtures/v1.0.0/execution-package.full.json` is **the only retained fixture carrying any operations at all**, and its `kind` values are **`browser.navigate`** and **`api.invoke`**. `OperationSchema` — **in the same file that defines the field** — declares: *"Capability-named, never tool-named (R-7.3, R-14.14)."* **`browser` and `api` are tools.** | **THE CORPUS DOES NOT MERELY FAIL TO CATCH THE DRIFT OBL-004 RECORDS; IT ENCODES A VIOLATION OF THE RULE THE SCHEMA STATES BESIDE IT.** That is worse than an incomplete corpus, and it is worse in a specific way: the fixture is **evidence**, regenerated and re-parsed on every gate run, so the violation is asserted as conforming **nine times per run** by `verify-contract-compatibility` — a control affirming the thing its own subject forbids. **It is D-117's mechanism seen from the corpus end.** D-117 records that the gate validates the contract against documents written to the contract; this records what those documents happened to say. **A corpus authored to satisfy a schema will encode whatever the author believed the schema meant, and nothing in the loop can contradict it** — `kind: z.string().min(1)` accepts `browser.navigate` happily, so the rule in the comment was never enforceable and the fixture was never wrong by any executing check. **The rule lived in prose beside a field typed to ignore it.** | `packages/contracts/compat/fixtures/v1.0.0/execution-package.full.json` · `packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts:46-51` (`OperationSchema`, and the comment it breaches) · `packages/contracts/compat/harness.mjs` (parses it on every run) · D-117 (the same mechanism, from the parser end) · D-118 · OBL-004 | **OPEN — AND THE FIXTURE IS TO BE RETAINED, NOT RE-AUTHORED.** **It SHALL NOT be silently corrected when the v1.1.0 amendment lands.** Two reasons, and the second is the one that would be lost. **(i)** It is the **only** evidence of what the v1.0.0 contract admitted, and R-19.11's backward-compatibility measurement is about exactly that — re-authoring it would destroy the boundary's far side to tidy the near side, which is the repair D-118 warns against in the same sequence. **(ii)** It is the **artefact this finding is about.** A fixture corrected in place leaves the register pointing at a file that no longer shows the defect, and the next reader concludes the entry was overstated. **What SHALL happen instead:** the fixture stays byte-identical, a comment beside it in the corpus records what it is evidence of, and the **v1.1.0** fixtures — captured from a real authored package per D-117 — carry the capability-named vocabulary. **The two directories then show the correction as a boundary rather than as an edit**, which is what a compatibility corpus is for. |

| **D-121** | **SETTLED 2026-08-06 AS NONE OF (a), (b) OR (c) — THE CONTRACT IS NOT WRONG, THE PRODUCER IS; `CONTRACT_VERSION` STAYS AT 1.0.0 AND NOTHING WAS AMENDED. THE FIELD COUNT IN THIS HEADLINE WAS ALSO WRONG: EIGHT DIVERGENCES, NOT SEVEN, AND THE EIGHTH IS THE ONE THAT CARRIES THE LESSON.** The eighth is **`validity.reusableWhileUnavailable`** — **nested inside a section that IS emitted and is itself incomplete.** The seven are a top-level field census's answer, and **the census was the instrument**: it answers *"is this key present?"* and returns the same value for a section that is complete and one that is present-and-partial. **The divergence is one level deeper than the entry that found it, and the depth was a property of the instrument rather than of the defect.** This is CHARTER §17.1's shape at a different target — §17.1 asks whether a number moves when the thing it counts moves; this asks whether a census **can range over the dimension the defect lives in**. An instrument that cannot is not wrong, it is silent, **and silence reads as absence**. The general obligation this argues for: **a census reports the depth it ranged to, so that its silence is distinguishable from a clean result.** The field itself is not incidental either — `reusableWhileUnavailable` is the field R-22.5 binds (*a cached package is not exempt from expiry*), so it is exactly the kind of field whose absence defaults to whatever a consumer assumes. The original finding follows unaltered. **THE PACKAGE THIS PLANE SERVES IS NOT THE `ExecutionPackage` CONTRACT — SEVEN OF ELEVEN REQUIRED FIELDS ARE NEVER SENT, AND THE OPERATION MISMATCH OBL-004 RECORDS IS ONE VISIBLE SLICE OF IT.** Measured 2026-08-06 while implementing the accepted v1.1.0 amendment, **before the version was moved**. `ExecutionPackageSchema` requires `contractVersion`, `runId`, `correlationId`, `capabilityId`, `operations`, `directives`, `gates`, `evidenceRequirements`, `provenance`, `validity` and `proceed`. **The live authoring path and the smoke fallback both emit** `packageId`, `tenantId`, `capability`, `contractVersion`, `authoredBy`, `issuedAt`, `validity`, `proceed`, `authoredFor`, `operations` (plus `schemaVersion`, `executionContextVersion`, `metadata`, `certification`, `manifest`). **Shared: FOUR** — `contractVersion`, `operations`, `validity`, `proceed`. **Required and never sent: SEVEN** — `runId`, `correlationId`, `capabilityId`, `directives`, `gates`, `evidenceRequirements`, `provenance`. **And `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` does not import `@dbiz/contracts` at all**: it hardcodes `const CONTRACT = "1.0.0"` as a string literal, so it would not follow a version move even if it parsed. | **THIS ENLARGES OBL-004 RATHER THAN ADDING TO IT, AND IT INVALIDATES THE SCOPE THE AMENDMENT WAS RULED ON.** OBL-004 was measured at the operation element — *"not one required field name in common"* — and that is true. **The same sentence is true one level up, of the package itself**, and nobody looked because the operation mismatch was sufficient to explain the symptom. **The consequence for the accepted amendment is decisive: P-81.5 — *the gateway parses what it serves* — CANNOT LAND AS SPECIFIED.** Adding `parseExecutionPackage` to the serving path would reject **every package the gateway currently emits**, on seven top-level fields, before reaching an operation. So the amendment as scoped would have produced a contract whose *operation element* describes what is emitted, enclosed in a *package shape* that still does not — **and would have moved `CONTRACT_VERSION` to 1.1.0 to say so.** **That is why nothing was landed:** a published version is a public act, and burning 1.1.0 on a partial correction would encode the remaining fiction at a new version with a compatibility boundary built under it. **It is also D-117's mechanism at full size:** nothing parses, so nothing could contradict — the operation shape and the package shape drifted independently, for the same reason, and neither was visible to any gate. | `packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts:99-114` (the eleven required fields) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs:127` (`const CONTRACT = "1.0.0"`, hardcoded), `:346-359` (smoke body), `:383-399` (refusal body) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/authoring-bridge.mjs:735-760` (the live path's emitted shape) · D-117 (nothing parses) · D-118 (the boundary) · D-120 (the corpus) · OBL-004 | **CLOSED 2026-08-06 BY THE DECISIVE TEST — RULED, NOT DEFERRED. Nothing is owed on the contract; what remains is D-122, which is a different question.** The three options that follow were the ruling's subject and all three are refuted or moot; they are retained because the reasoning that killed them is the reasoning that keeps the contract at 1.0.0. **Three options, and they are not equivalent.** **(a) AMEND THE WHOLE PACKAGE SHAPE at v1.1.0** — the contract describes the served envelope end to end, `runId`/`correlationId`/`capabilityId` reconcile against `packageId`/`tenantId`/`capability`, and `directives`/`gates`/`evidenceRequirements`/`provenance` are either declared optional or emitted. **Largest, and the only one after which P-81.5 can land.** **(b) AMEND THE OPERATION ELEMENT ONLY, and state plainly that the enclosing package remains undescribed** — smaller and honest, but it spends the 1.1.0 version on a partial correction and leaves P-81.5 unimplementable, so **D-117 is renumbered rather than closed**, which the ruling that authorised this work explicitly refused. **(c) TREAT THEM AS TWO ARTEFACTS** — decide that what the gateway serves is a *different, IP-internal envelope* that legitimately is not `ExecutionPackage`, and that the contract describes what the Execution Plane will be given once the retrieval path carries it. **This is the option that must be measured rather than assumed**, because if it is true then OBL-004's premise is wrong in the other direction and the contract needs no amendment at all — only a consumer. **The measurement that settles (c): what the Execution Plane actually receives on the wire today, and from which endpoint.** It is answerable only from the Execution Plane, which is where OBL-004 was found. **Nothing was changed. `CONTRACT_VERSION` is 1.0.0, the corpus is byte-identical, `verify-contract-compatibility` is PASS at 7 fixtures / 1 version / 9 properties both before and after.** **— SETTLED 2026-08-06 BY THE DECISIVE TEST, AS NONE OF (a), (b) OR (c). REPORT: [`D-121_DECISIVE_TEST_MEASURED.md`](D-121_DECISIVE_TEST_MEASURED.md).** The test was *seal one package into the store for `carlisle-homes` and hand the EP its hash*. **It cannot be constructed, and the reason is the answer: the package cannot be sealed.** Executed, not reasoned — an EP token minted for `carlisle-homes`, `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` started against the real `tenants/` record, `POST /v1/execute` → **200, a package authored on the live path**, and those exact bytes handed to a real `SealedPackageStore` over a real `FilesystemStorageProvider` in that tenant's partition. **Result: `SealedPackageWriteRefused: sealed body carries no provenance.tenantId`.** **Control, same store, same call: a contract-conforming body is accepted and `store.get` returns `found`** — so the store partitions and serves correctly, and the refusal is about the package. **(c) IS REFUTED RATHER THAN UNPROVEN:** retrieval cannot carry the gateway envelope, because the only door to the Execution Plane requires `provenance` and the envelope has none — `put` reads `provenance.tenantId`, `provenance.contentHash.value` and `validity.notAfter`, **all three the contract's vocabulary, and the ownership assertion P-79.2 and C-07.11 rest on is built on the very field it refuses.** **(a) IS REFUTED IN THE SAME STROKE, WHICH WAS NOT EXPECTED:** amending the cross-plane contract to describe the served envelope would write an artefact that **never crosses a plane boundary** into the contract that governs crossing it. The envelope is not underdescribed; it is internal. **(b) is moot.** **THE FOURTH ANSWER: THE CONTRACT IS NOT WRONG AND NEEDS NO AMENDMENT — THE PRODUCER IS**, and ADR-0049 M5 already schedules its retirement in the gateway's own header. **`CONTRACT_VERSION` STAYS AT 1.0.0; NOTHING IS AMENDED; P-81.5 IS NOT UNBLOCKED BY A VERSION MOVE AND NEVER WAS.** **AND THE FIELD COUNT IN THIS ENTRY IS CORRECTED: `parseExecutionPackage` on the authored package returns EIGHT issues, not seven.** The eighth is **`validity.reusableWhileUnavailable`** — nested inside a section that IS emitted and is itself incomplete. **A top-level field census cannot see that, and the census was the instrument**, so the divergence is one level deeper than the entry that found it. **What remains open is not a contract question at all — it is D-122.** |

| **D-122** | **NOTHING HAS EVER WRITTEN TO THE SEALED PACKAGE STORE. `SealedPackageStore.put` HAS ZERO CALL SITES OUTSIDE TESTS, IN THE ENTIRE TREE — SO `GET /api/packages/{hash}` IS A DEPLOYED, AUTHENTICATED, TENANT-PARTITIONED ROUTE OVER A STORE THAT IS PERMANENTLY EMPTY.** Measured 2026-08-06 while attempting D-121's decisive test. Searched every non-test, non-`dist`, non-`node_modules` source in both the TypeScript and `.mjs` trees: the only `put` calls are `onboarding-session.ts` (a different store) and `registration.ts:269` (`putOtc`). **`ApiDeps.packageStore` is read in exactly two places — `app.module.ts:38` to decide whether to mount `PackageController`, and `api.ts:412` to pass it to the read handler.** `sealedPackageService()` constructs the store and starts its purge driver; **nothing fills it.** **AND THE TWO HALVES ARE NOT MERELY UNCONNECTED — THEY ARE INCOMPATIBLE AT THE WRITE BOUNDARY.** A package authored by the live path through a running `ip-execute-gateway.mjs`, handed to a real store in `carlisle-homes`' partition, is **refused**: `SealedPackageWriteRefused: sealed body carries no provenance.tenantId`. The gateway seals its package and **returns it in the `POST /v1/execute` response body** (`send(res, 200, { package: pkg, … })`) — it has no code path to the store, and could not use one if it had. **AND EVERY BODY THAT HAS EVER ENTERED THE STORE WAS HAND-AUTHORED BY A TEST**, to the published contract, with `operations: []` — `sealed-package-store.test.ts:52`, `package-retrieval.test.ts:47`, `http-surface.security.test.ts`. | **THIS IS D-117'S MECHANISM AT THE FOURTH LEVEL, AND IT IS THE LEVEL THAT MAKES THE OTHER THREE UNFALSIFIABLE.** D-115: `packageHash` declared, consumed by tests only. D-117: the execution-package schema, consumed by its own harness only. D-118: a compatibility property that had only ever seen one version. **D-122: a store whose only writer is its own test suite** — so the retrieval path's proofs are all proofs about bodies the tests wrote, exactly as the compatibility corpus is. **IT IS ALSO WHY D-121 COULD NOT BE SETTLED BY MEASURING THE WIRE.** *"What does the Execution Plane receive?"* has no factual answer: nothing has ever been on that wire, and **which shape crosses is decided by a writer that does not exist.** The question looked empirical and was a design decision nobody had noticed was unmade. **AND THE EMPTINESS IS UNOBSERVABLE FROM OUTSIDE, BY DESIGN.** C-05.11 / P-70.4 make unknown-hash and unowned-hash **one signal**, correctly — so *"the store is empty"* and *"that package is not yours"* are byte-identical to every caller. The Execution Plane cannot distinguish them; **neither can an operator over HTTP.** The only way to learn the store has never held anything is to read the source or the mount. **C-05.11'S EXTENSION, RECORDED AS ITS OWN FINDING: THE PROPERTY WAS REASONED ABOUT AS A CUSTOMER-FACING CONFIDENTIALITY GUARANTEE, AND IT IS ALSO AN OPERATOR-FACING BLINDFOLD.** *"The store is empty"*, *"not yours"*, *"expired"* and *"that tenant was offboarded"* are one signal to **every** principal — **and there is no privileged one for whom it separates.** A global principal is **refused outright** at [`package-retrieval.ts:216-221`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/package-retrieval.ts), deliberately and correctly under P-79.2, because a route with no slug gives *may-access* nothing to range over — **so a platform operator has strictly LESS visibility of this surface than a tenant does, not more.** That is why D-122 went unnoticed while the route was deployed, authenticated, tenant-partitioned, gated and green. **THIS IS NOT A DEFECT IN C-05.11 AND SHALL NOT BE REPAIRED BY WEAKENING IT** — a discriminating refusal for operators is exactly the oracle P-70.4 exists to deny, reachable by anyone who obtains an operator credential. **The repair belongs on the WRITE side**, where *has anything ever been written* can be asserted without any caller learning anything about any package. C-05.11's text needs no change; what is owed is that the **consequence** be recorded where the next reader of the criterion meets it, which is doc 05 and is an amendment by ruling. **THE FAIL-OPEN/FAIL-CLOSED DESIGN LAW BESIDE R-05.27 IS NOT BREACHED, AND THAT IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE DEFENCE.** The law admits a declared-but-unimplemented port *only if its empty case fails CLOSED*; this one returns the single 404 refusal, so it is lawfully mounted. **The law was written about the READ side and does not reach the WRITE side** — a route can satisfy it perfectly and still be permanently incapable of succeeding, and nothing in the law or in any gate distinguishes *correctly empty* from *never fillable*. | `packages/platform-providers/src/storage/sealed-package-store.ts` (`put`, no non-test caller) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/platform-adoption.ts:206-224` (`sealedPackageService`, mounted read-only) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/app.module.ts:38` · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/api.ts:412` · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs:330-340` (`sign`, `contentHash` at top level, no `provenance`), `:645` (`listen(PORT, "127.0.0.1")`) · `packages/platform-providers/test/sealed-package-store.test.ts:52` and `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/test/package-retrieval.test.ts:47` (the only bodies ever stored) · [`D-121_DECISIVE_TEST_MEASURED.md`](D-121_DECISIVE_TEST_MEASURED.md) · D-121 · D-117 · D-118 · D-120 · D-111 | **OPEN — AND IT IS A RULING BEFORE IT IS A BUILD, BECAUSE THE WRITER DECIDES WHAT CROSSES.** Building a seal-time path is not a wiring task: **whatever calls `put` fixes the shape the Execution Plane will parse for as long as the contract lives**, which is precisely the decision D-121 was raised to obtain. **The measurement narrows it rather than reopening it:** `put` already requires `provenance.tenantId`, `provenance.contentHash.value` and `validity.notAfter`, and C-07.11's isolation is derived from the first — **so any admissible writer emits the contract's provenance, and the contract is therefore the answer by construction rather than by preference.** **WHAT IS OWED WITH IT, AND THE ORDER MATTERS: (i)** the writer SHALL run `parseExecutionPackage` before `put`, so P-81.5 lands at the producer where the run that broke it can be named — and it can land now, because it is no longer blocked on a version move; **(ii)** the compatibility corpus SHALL gain a fixture **captured from a real sealed package**, which D-117 already owes and which only becomes possible once one exists; **(iii)** the fail-open/fail-closed design law is owed a companion for the write side, or the next declared-and-unfilled store repeats this exactly and is lawful while doing it. **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT IN THE CHANGE THAT FOUND IT.** Authoring the writer would settle the cross-plane shape question by fiat, in a diff reviewed as a plumbing change — the same failure D-121 refused when it declined to spend `1.1.0` on a partial correction. **A store with no writer is honest; a writer chosen by whoever happened to implement it is not.** **— CLOSED 2026-08-06. THE STORE HAS A WRITER, AND A PACKAGE THIS PLANE AUTHORED HAS BEEN WRITTEN TO IT AND RETRIEVED BACK THROUGH THE REAL ROUTE — the first time in this programme.** `createSealedPackageWriter` (`sealed-package-writer.ts`) is the component this entry ruled on. **Its write surface enumerates ONE EVENT** — `onPackageSealed`, no `write`/`put`/`save`/`record` and **no options bag carrying a discriminator**, asserted as a property rather than left to review — because a `kind: 'fetch'` parameter is a third event wearing a field's clothes and is how a delivery record (P-70.3, forbidden) enters a store that looks like it only holds packages. **THE ORDER OF ITS CHECKS IS PART OF THE DESIGN:** the **publication gate first** (nothing is parsed, resolved or written for a run that may not publish), then **P-81.5's `parseExecutionPackage`** — which closes the PATH rather than one producer, so a future second producer cannot reach the store without satisfying the published contract — then ownership through the **one canonical fail-closed resolver**, never a derivation. **PROVED, POSITIVE CONTROL FIRST:** a conforming package is published and **retrieved back through `handlePackageRetrieval` with an EP principal**, envelope intact, signature beside it; a not-admissible verdict refuses **before anything is written**; the D-121 gateway shape is refused by P-81.5; an unresolvable `provenance.tenantId` is refused; a missing signature is refused loudly. **THE WRITER IS CONSTRUCTED IN THE COMPOSITION ROOT AND IS NOT MOUNTED ON A ROUTE, WHICH IS STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED:** publication is not an HTTP operation — nothing outside this plane may ask for a package to be published — and driving it from the authoring path is the **ADR-0049 M5 cut-over**, separately authorised. **So the store has a writer and no production caller yet**, and that boundary is where this entry now sits. **— REPORTED 2026-08-06 BEFORE BUILDING: [`D-122_WRITER_RULING_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](D-122_WRITER_RULING_DESIGN_REPORT.md). ALL FOUR QUESTIONS ANSWERED, THREE OF THEM BY SOMETHING ALREADY ON DISK. NOTHING WAS BUILT.** **(i) WHAT AUTHORS AN ACCEPTABLE PACKAGE — `composeExecutionPackage`, measured, not preferred:** driven against a real store in `carlisle-homes`' partition it is **ACCEPTED**, **retrieved byte-identical**, and **`parseExecutionPackage` PARSES the retrieved body**; the controls refuse a foreign `tenantId`, the gateway envelope, and `{}`. It emits all eleven required fields, hashes with `sha256-jcs-v1` over the JCS canonical form rather than `sha256` over transport bytes, and **already ends with `return parseExecutionPackage(pkg)`** — so P-81.5 is discharged at that producer and what remains is one line at the write site. **(ii) WHERE THE WRITE BELONGS — stage 7, and the architecture already says so:** doc 12's own stage table gives stage 7, Execution Planning, the job *"author the sealed execution package"*. **The other two candidates are unavailable rather than merely worse** — *at certification* is **impossible by the lifecycle's ordering** (certification is stage 11; the EP executes at stage 8, so the package would be written after the run it exists to enable), and *a separate publication step* is a thirteenth stage, which R-12.18 forbids. **What is genuinely open is only whether STORING is part of AUTHORING**, and P-70.1 binds them: *"exists **and is retrievable**"* is one obligation, so **stage 7 as built does not discharge it.** **(iii) WHAT DECIDES RETRIEVABILITY — two fail-closed discriminators already exist and neither was built for this:** certification (the bridge throws before composing) and ownership resolution (**a reference run's `tenantId: 't1'` resolves to no tenant, so `put` refuses it — measured**, which means **a reference run cannot pollute a customer partition by construction, today, before anything is built**). Recommended ruling: **they are sufficient, introduce no third**; `proceed: false` **IS** retrievable (a typed refusal is authored precisely so the EP learns not to execute — R-12.14), and `directives.mode` does not discriminate (R-04.6: dry-run *"differs only inside the adapter"*). **(iv) CAPABILITY'S OR THE PLATFORM'S — the platform's, and nearly forced:** C-11.11 forbids framework code branching on a capability identity and a per-capability publication rule is that branch; R-12.18 makes stage 7 the platform's stage. The capability's contribution — `proceed` and the certification verdict — is per-run, already expressed, and already sufficient. **AND THE REPORT FOUND A FIFTH THING THAT BLOCKS THE BUILD RATHER THAN COMPLICATING IT — D-123: the detached signature R-20.29 obliges the EP to verify has NO CARRIER under pull.** **SO THE RULING CAN BE MADE NOW; THE BUILD SEQUENCES BEHIND D-123.** **— RULED 2026-08-06, AS REPORTED: `composeExecutionPackage` · stage 7 · the two existing discriminators · the platform, not the capability. THE BUILD IS BLOCKED BY D-123 AND SHALL NOT PROCEED.** **AND THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS A FINDING RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE IS RECORDED AS THE RULING'S OWN CONTENT: THREE OF THE FOUR ANSWERS CAME FROM DISK, AND STAGE 7'S ALTERNATIVES ARE *UNAVAILABLE*, NOT *WORSE*.** A ruling that selects between live options is a judgement, and the next reader may reasonably revisit it when circumstances change. **A ruling that records which options were never available is a measurement, and revisiting it requires refuting the measurement.** *At certification* is not a weaker option — **it is a contradiction**: certification is stage 11 and the Execution Plane executes at stage 8, so the package would be written **after the run it exists to enable**. *A separate publication step* is not heavier — **it is a thirteenth stage, which R-12.18 forbids in terms.** **Neither was rejected on cost, and neither can return on a cost argument.** The same holds for (i): the gateway is not a worse producer than the composer, it is **not a producer of this artefact at all** — it does not import `@dbiz/contracts`, and its `sha256` content hash is not a value `ALGORITHM_VERSIONS` admits. And for (iii): the two discriminators were not chosen over a third — **they already hold, fail-closed, measured, before anything is built.** **THIS IS WHY THE RULING WAS WORTH REPORTING BEFORE BUILDING AND WOULD HAVE BEEN WORTHLESS AFTER:** built first, all four would have arrived as *"this is how it was implemented"*, indistinguishable from a preference, and the unavailability of the alternatives would never have been established. Report: [`D-122_WRITER_RULING_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](D-122_WRITER_RULING_DESIGN_REPORT.md). |

| **D-123** | **THE DETACHED SIGNATURE R-20.29 REQUIRES THE EXECUTION PLANE TO VERIFY HAS NO CARRIER UNDER PULL — SO A WRITER BUILT TODAY WOULD PRODUCE PACKAGES THE EP IS CONTRACTUALLY OBLIGED TO REFUSE.** Measured 2026-08-06 while reporting D-122. **R-20.29** states that verification on retrieval is **two checks** and *"both SHALL pass before a retrieved package is executed: the content-hash match of R-20.28, and **the validity of the detached signature over the canonical form** (R-20.22)."* **Check two is unperformable as built.** `SealedPackageStore.put(ctx, sealedBody)` takes **one** artefact and reads no signature field; `GET /api/packages/{hash}` returns `JSON.parse(result.body)` and **nothing else — no envelope, no header, no second route**; and `ExecutionPackageSchema` correctly has **no signature field**, because R-20.22 makes the signature **detached**. The only `PackageSigner` in the tree is the SPI's injected port, and the `SignatureEnvelope` it produces is carried as the **second argument of `transport.execute(pkg, signature)`** — the push call. **The one `signPackage` actually wired in the deployed tier (`api.ts:275`) signs a SOLUTION UPDATE MANIFEST under ADR-0035 and is a different artefact entirely**, named here so it is not mistaken for this one. | **THE SIGNATURE WAS AN ARGUMENT OF THE CALL THE INVERSION DELETED, AND NEITHER ADR NOTICED BECAUSE NEITHER WAS LOOKING AT ARITY.** ADR-0070 changed **who initiates**; from that direction nothing had moved, so it stated no home for the signature. ADR-0079 stores *"the sealed body and its provenance. Nothing derived"* (P-79.5) — and **a detached signature is not derived from the body**, so it falls outside that sentence rather than being excluded by it. **It is a second artefact with no home in either decision.** **THE FAILURE IT WOULD PRODUCE IS THE EXPENSIVE KIND:** a pipeline that authors, stores, serves and hash-matches correctly, whose **every delivery fails the second check** — and which would present as an **Execution Plane defect**, on the plane that cannot see why. **IT IS ALSO WHY `put`'s SINGLE-ARTEFACT SIGNATURE IS NOT A DETAIL:** `execute(pkg, signature)` takes two arguments and `put(ctx, body)` takes one, so the substitution ADR-0070 §6 steps 4–5 describe **drops the signature silently**, at a call site that type-checks. | package fixes what retrieval returns. **— LINK 2 (THE CARRIER) IS BUILT; LINK 1 IS REPORTED 2026-08-06: [`D-123_LINK1_PACKAGE_SIGNING_REPORT.md`](D-123_LINK1_PACKAGE_SIGNING_REPORT.md).** **AND THE REPORT FOUND WHAT WAS NOT ASKED FOR, WHICH IS THE MOST URGENT OF IT: THERE ARE TWO COMPETING SIGNATURE SHAPES AND THEY SHARE NO FIELD NAME ON THE TWO FIELDS THAT MATTER.** `SignatureEnvelope { signature, signingKeyId, algorithm }` in the functional-testing engine against `DetachedSignature { value, keyId, algorithm }` in the onboarding engine — **only `algorithm` agrees.** **THIS IS D-117'S SENTENCE AT A DIFFERENT ARTEFACT** (*the shapes share no required field name*) and it survives for D-117's reason: **nothing has ever carried a signature across the boundary, so nothing could disagree.** **It is live rather than historical**, because the writer built for §6 step 3 takes `signature: unknown` and serialises whatever it is given — **so the first component to sign a real package fixes the shape the Execution Plane must parse for as long as the contract lives.** That is **D-122's ruling shape one artefact down**, and neither type is in `@dbiz/contracts`, which is why they could drift. **THE OTHER ANSWERS:** the manifest signer is the **same key, same mechanism, different artefact domain** — not a forgery risk, because `digestV1` binds the domain into the signed value, but **one revocation blast radius**, so distinct key identifiers per domain are recommended (ADR-0007 §6 already provides the mechanism). **SIGN AT AUTHORING**, because the signature attests **origin** — ADR-0007 already rejected signing at retrieval for that reason and publication is nearer retrieval than authoring — **and D-122 genuinely could not have answered this**: reading *exists and is retrievable* as ONE obligation left no distinct authoring moment for a signature to attach to. **The writer already assumes it**: `PackageSealedEvent` takes the signature as an INPUT, so a sign-at-publication design would have needed a signer injected into the writer and the built shape has no such dependency. **— LINK 1 BUILT 2026-08-06, ALL THREE PARTS TOGETHER.** **(a) THE SIGNER, AT AUTHORING:** `createPackageSigner` structurally satisfies the canonical runtime's `PackageSigner` port without importing it (the capability engine is not a dependency of this tier — D-128's rule-lift reasoning), and **it enforces the provenance agreement**: a package naming a key this plane does not hold is **refused**, because this is the only place both values are ever in hand and letting it through turns an authoring defect into `signature-invalid` in the customer's plane. **(b) ROTATION:** `publishVerificationKeys` emits `verification-keys-changed` on the update channel the EP already polls — **no new route, no inbound connection, no customer redeployment** (R-08.16, ADR-0007 §6 discharged by reuse). **Idempotent BY COMPARISON over the key ids actually sent**, read back off the events, rather than by a *distributed* marker — a marker is a second record of the same fact and can disagree (ADR-0079 alternative D's reasoning at a different subject). **A tenancy that has never been sent one is emitted to, which is the only path by which the already-registered tenancy can obtain a key at all.** **(c) MINT-ON-EMPTY AS A REFUSAL**, on the DERIVED condition. **PROVED, EACH WITH ITS POSITIVE CONTROL:** a plane with no tenancy mints freely; an existing key loads without consulting the authorisation; **absent key + tenancies then REFUSED, naming the count and the resolving action**; **whole-volume loss mints again — and that is the property**, because the harm died with the evidence; a package naming a different key is refused; a never-sent tenancy is emitted to; a second sweep emits nothing; a **changed** set emits again (rotation, not a one-shot backfill) carrying **both** keys, since ADR-0007 §6 keeps several valid concurrently and sending only the new one would break every package still in its validity window; **only `keyId`, `publicKeyPem` and `algorithm` cross**; and **distribution is AUDITED**, because a trust event that leaves no audit line cannot be reconstructed after an incident. **107/107 · 89/89 · 47/47 · 379/379 · 210/210; closure, composition-root, HTTP-parity and document-06 all PASS.** **THREE THINGS RECORDED WITH IT, BECAUSE EACH IS A REASON RATHER THAN A DETAIL.** **(i) THE SIGNER'S REFUSAL IS THE INVARIANT'S JUSTIFICATION, NOT A CONVENIENCE.** The signer is **the only place both values are ever in hand** — the package's `provenance.signingKeyId` and the key actually held — so it is the only place a mismatch can still be fixed. Letting it through turns an authoring defect into `signature-invalid` **in the customer's plane**. **That is mint-on-empty's shape at a second site: a defect surfacing where nobody did anything wrong**, and it is why both refusals are loud and early rather than tolerant. **(ii) ROTATION'S MARKER REFUSAL IS THE SECOND APPLICATION OF THE FIRST-RUN REASONING.** Idempotence could have been a `distributed` flag on the tenant record; it is instead a **comparison over the key ids actually sent**, read back off the events this plane emitted — because **a marker is a second record of the same fact that can disagree with the first**. **Twice in one change the temptation was a token and twice the answer was to derive from what already exists**, which is the transferable part: a token is a new thing that can be wrong, and a derivation cannot disagree with its own source. **(iii) A CHANGED SET CARRIES BOTH KEYS, AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS WHY.** ADR-0007 §6 keeps several keys valid **concurrently** so rotation needs no customer redeployment — **so sending only the new key breaks every package still inside its validity window under the old one.** That failure would appear **only in production and only for packages already issued**: a test that rotates and then signs afresh passes, because the packages it verifies were signed after the rotation. **The defect is invisible to any test that does not hold an artefact from before the change** — the same blindness the write-ordering proof had to crash a provider to escape. **— RULING 1 BUILT 2026-08-06: ONE DETACHED-SIGNATURE SHAPE, DECLARED IN `@dbiz/contracts` AND NOWHERE ELSE.** `DetachedSignatureSchema { algorithm, keyId, value }` — passthrough, so additive change still survives (R-20.4, C-20.7) — with `SIGNATURE_ALGORITHMS` refusing an **unknown algorithm at the boundary** rather than carrying it through as opaque. **BOTH CONSUMERS CONVERGED ON IT:** `package-signing.ts` re-exports it, and the SPI's `SignatureEnvelope` is now a type ALIAS — **the name is kept deliberately**, because renaming a type across a conformance suite in the same change that unifies it would make the convergence unreviewable. **One shape, two names, one declaration.** **THE FIELD NAMES `{ algorithm, keyId, value }` WON BECAUSE THEY ARE THE ONES ACTUALLY PRODUCED** — a working signer emits them — and converging toward the artefact that exists costs nothing, while converging toward the unwired port would rewrite a working signer to match something that has never run. **THE ONE ARGUMENT AGAINST IS ANSWERED RATHER THAN OMITTED:** `SignatureEnvelope` called it `signingKeyId`, matching `provenance.signingKeyId`. That symmetry is real and is now preserved as an **INVARIANT** — `signatureMatchesProvenance()` — rather than as a shared spelling, because **two fields with one name still hold two values and nothing compared them.** **THE TYPE SYSTEM FOUND EVERY CONSTRUCTION SITE**, which is itself the proof the two shapes were incompatible: four compile errors across two conformance suites, each a place where a `SignatureEnvelope` literal could not satisfy the contract. **PROVED:** a well-formed signature parses; an unknown algorithm is refused; **the retired `SignatureEnvelope` shape is REFUSED rather than passed through** — which needed asserting precisely because passthrough admits unknown fields, so it must fail on the ABSENCE of the required ones rather than pass on the extras; additive fields survive; and the provenance agreement compares **values, not spellings**. **107/107 · 89/89 · 47/47 · 368/368 · 210/210; closure, contract compatibility and document-06 all PASS.** | **— REPORTED 2026-08-06: [`D-123_SIGNATURE_CARRIER_AND_INVERSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](D-123_SIGNATURE_CARRIER_AND_INVERSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md). THE ENTRY IS ENLARGED BY ITS OWN MEASUREMENT: THE CHAIN HAS THREE MISSING LINKS, NOT ONE, AND THE CARRIER IS THE MIDDLE ONE.** **(1)** No signer on the write path — the only `PackageSigner` is wired inside a **generated string**, and the deployed tier's signer signs an ADR-0035 manifest. **(2)** The carrier — this entry. **(3) The generated Execution Plane is instructed to verify against a LITERAL PLACEHOLDER** — `signatureVerificationKeyRef: '<FILL: IP public verification key ref + keyId>'` — recorded as **D-125**. **So the ruling cannot be *"pick a carrier"*: any carrier ruled today lands into a chain broken on both sides of it, and closing only the carrier produces exactly this entry's failure while appearing to have fixed it.** **THE OPTIONS ARE COSTED AND TWO ARE EXCLUDED BEFORE COST.** *Embedding* is a **major** contract version by ADR-0007 §7's forward obligation (*"detached to embedded … would be a major contract version"*) and breaks content addressing, since `hashableContent` excludes only `provenance.contentHash`. *A header alone* is rejected for the artefact's own reason: **a signature that lives only in an HTTP response does not survive caching to disk**, and doc 20 §2.2 makes cacheability and replay the point of sealing. *Signing on the read path* changes what the signature asserts and puts the platform's highest-value key on the highest-traffic route. **RECOMMENDED: a sibling artefact AT REST plus a retrieval envelope ON THE WIRE — two halves of one answer, not alternatives**, because ruling only the wire shape rebuilds this same gap one layer down the moment the EP caches a package. **A concrete defect the sibling scheme introduces and must land with:** `purgeExpired` skips every name failing `HASH_RE`, so a `<hash>.sig` sibling **would never be purged** and signatures would outlive their packages — an R-06.13 / C-06.8 breach created by the fix. **AND AD-016'S APPARENT CONTRADICTION IS RESOLVED RATHER THAN ARBITRATED:** ADR-0007 closes AD-016's **model** (asymmetric, DBiz-held signing, verification-only distribution, key id on the package, overlapping rotation); what R-20.29 correctly calls **open** is the **distribution** leg — and D-125 is that leg, as a `<FILL:` marker in emitted customer configuration rather than as a documentation ambiguity. **Neither frozen document is wrong; the sentence distinguishing them is what is missing.** **ALSO SETTLED SO IT IS NOT RE-RAISED:** signing over the hex content hash rather than the canonical bytes is **correct** — doc 20 §5 specifies detached signatures precisely so verification needs no re-serialisation, and `digestV1(domain, canonical)` already binds the domain into the value being signed, so an execution package's signature is not interchangeable with a solution manifest's. **— RULED 2026-08-06: SIBLING AT REST + ENVELOPE ON THE WIRE, BOTH. DRAFTED AS [ADR-0081](../docs/adr/ADR-0081-execution-package-signature-carrier.md), STATUS PROPOSED, STOP FOR ACCEPTANCE.** **TWO DESIGN CONSEQUENCES THE RULING DID NOT ANTICIPATE, AND BOTH ARE LOAD-BEARING.** **(1) THE SIBLING IS A PARALLEL `run` SEGMENT, NOT A `<hash>.sig` SUFFIX** — a suffix forces `HASH_RE` to be **loosened** inside `purgeExpired`, and that pattern is the same shape as the one guarding `keyFor`, where it stops a non-hash segment reaching the storage layer. **The naive retention fix would weaken a control P-79.2's addressing rests on**, and a reviewer relaxing it in one loop invites the next reader to relax it in the constructor. A parallel segment keeps **both** artefact segments a bare hash, turns the purge hole into an **explicit enumeration change** rather than a relaxed pattern, and leaves ADR-0010 §6's tenant-leading prefix untouched so `purgeTenant` stays a prefix operation. **(2) THE BODY IS THE COMMIT POINT — signature first, body second**, so the body's presence **implies** the signature's. A crash between them leaves a signature with no package: inert, never served, purgeable. **The reverse order leaves a package that cannot be verified and would still be found** — the one state this entry exists to prevent. **The general form: a partial write SHALL fail toward the absence of the thing that is SERVED, never toward the absence of the thing that PROVES it.** **AND A PACKAGE WHOSE SIGNATURE IS ABSENT IS REFUSED** into P-79.6's single expression — **ADR-0078's taxonomy stays at four; no fifth class and no oracle.** **The signature has no `validity.notAfter` and therefore no declarable retention under R-06.9**, so its retention **is** the package's and the purge deletes the pair; an orphaned signature is purged unconditionally. |

| **D-124** | **DOC 12 ASSIGNS THE SEALED EXECUTION PACKAGE TO STAGE 7, AND STAGE 7 AS BUILT EMITS A COUNT — THE PACKAGE IS COMPOSED OUTSIDE THE TWELVE-STAGE LIFECYCLE ENTIRELY.** Measured 2026-08-06 while reporting D-122. [`12-capability-orchestration.md:23`](../docs/architecture/12-capability-orchestration.md) gives stage **7**, *Execution Planning*, plane **IP**, the job **"Author the sealed execution package"** — in the stage table, as that stage's whole description. The canonical runner's stage 7 ([`canonical-runner-capability.ts:277-281`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/canonical-runner-capability.ts)) emits `{ planned: true, components: N }` — **a count, and `notApplicable` when there is no architecture to plan from.** The package is composed at [`runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:111`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts), **after `runThroughRunner` has returned all twelve stages.** | **THE ARTEFACT THE ARCHITECTURE PLACES INSIDE THE LIFECYCLE IS PRODUCED OUTSIDE IT, SO NONE OF THE LIFECYCLE'S GUARANTEES REACH IT.** A stage result is sealed by a module-private brand and cannot be constructed outside its producing stage (R-12.10, R-12.11); **the package is not a stage result**, so it inherits none of that. **This is why D-122's question (ii) needed a ruling rather than a lookup:** the place doc 12 names is empty, and the place the package is actually built is not governed by the document that names it. **IT IS ALSO WHY THE ORDERING ARGUMENT MATTERS —** composing after stage 12 means the package is authored after `certification` (stage 11) has already run, which is coherent only because the runner's stage 8 is not the Execution Plane's stage 8 (R-12.5). **A real cross-plane run cannot have that ordering**, and nothing currently states so. | `docs/architecture/12-capability-orchestration.md:23` · `packages/capability-framework/src/stages.ts:34-57` (the twelve, and `STAGE_PLANE`) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/canonical-runner-capability.ts:277-281` · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:102,111-112` · [`D-122_WRITER_RULING_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](D-122_WRITER_RULING_DESIGN_REPORT.md) §3 | **OPEN — RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, AND IT IS D-122'S RULING THAT DECIDES IT.** Two closures and they are not equivalent: **(1)** stage 7 authors and stores the package, and the bridge stops composing — which makes doc 12 true as written and puts the artefact inside the lifecycle's guarantees; **(2)** doc 12's stage table is amended to say what stage 7 actually does, and the package is declared a post-lifecycle artefact — **which would need to explain what governs it instead, and there is currently no answer.** **(1) is the recommendation and it is the same change D-122 rules on**, so this SHALL NOT be closed separately: repairing it independently would author the writer as a refactor, which is precisely what D-122 refuses. **— REPORTED 2026-08-06, AND THE DEFECT IS ONE FIELD RATHER THAN A PLACEMENT: [`D-124_COMPOSITION_PLACEMENT_REPORT.md`](D-124_COMPOSITION_PLACEMENT_REPORT.md).** Of the three domain outputs `composeExecutionPackage` consumes, **two are available at stage 3** — `automationArchitecture.architectureComponents` and `automationIntelligence.validationRequirements`, both assigned in the runner's `context` stage — **and exactly one is not: `gates`, derived from `executiveReporting.certificationSummary`, which is produced at STAGE 11.** **The package is composed after all twelve stages because ONE of its fields depends on a stage that runs after the stage that is supposed to author it.** **AND IT IS WORSE THAN MISTIMED, WHICH IS WHAT DECIDES THE REPAIR.** R-20.7 defines gates as conditions the **EP carries and the IP evaluates**; a `certificationSummary` is what the IP **concludes after a run** — so the package's gates currently describe **a run that has already happened.** **In a real cross-plane run the derivation is impossible, not merely late:** `STAGE_PLANE` makes stages 8 and 9 the **Execution Plane's**, so a package that must be authored before stage 8 can never carry a field derived from stage 11. **The reference path works only because it simulates all twelve stages in-process — the runner's stage 8 is not the EP's stage 8 — and the bridge then composes a package "for dispatch" from the outputs of a run that has already completed.** **That is precisely the ordering D-122 ruled IMPOSSIBLE when it rejected *"write the package at certification"*: the rejected option is what the implementation does.** **SO THE REPAIR IS NOT TO MOVE THE COMPOSER — IT IS TO SEVER `gates` FROM STAGE 11**, after which composition at stage 7 is trivially available. **What `gates` should be derived from is a CAPABILITY DECISION** — certification *criteria* declared before execution rather than a certification *summary* produced after — and the report deliberately does not take it. **This does NOT mean the packages authored so far are wrong for the reference path**, where the simulated run genuinely has concluded; it means **the derivation cannot survive contact with a real Execution Plane**, and nothing has yet put it there (D-122). **— THE HEADLINE, RULED AND RECORDED 2026-08-06: THE TWO ARTEFACTS ARE BYTE-INDISTINGUISHABLE AND DIFFER ONLY IN *WHEN* THEY WERE BUILT.** That is why nothing could see it — **not a missing check, but two things identical in every dimension an artefact carries.** A package authored from a completed run and a package authored to enable one have the same fields, the same hash domain, the same schema and the same content; **composition time is not a property of the package**, so no gate, test or parse could have disagreed. The store has never been written to, so the ordering has never had a consequence — **and an ordering with no consequence produces no evidence of itself.** **SEVERANCES 1 AND 2 HAVE LANDED; 3 IS REPORTED AND NOT TAKEN.** **(1)** `gates` now derives from `automationIntelligence.validationRequirements` — **stage 3**, the source this composer already reads for `evidenceRequirements`, because ***"what SHALL be captured" and "what SHALL be true of it" are ONE DECLARATION SEEN FROM TWO SIDES.*** **Proved with both controls: the gates no longer move when the stage-11 summary changes, and DO move when the stage-3 declaration changes.** **The GRAMMAR is untouched and is a CAPABILITY DECISION** — R-20.7 fixes who carries and who evaluates, not the vocabulary, and a closed `expression` enum is a **contract** change at a moved version (D-121's cost). The expression carries the requirement **verbatim**, and the source says it is a declaration and not yet an evaluable expression. **What severance 1 fixes is the TEMPORAL defect only.** **(2)** Composition is now **unconditional**; the certification check moved from gating *authoring* to gating *publication* (dispatch). **Nothing is weakened** — the same verdict blocks the same crossing — and an uncertified run now produces an inert artefact, which is the honest representation of *authored and refused*. **(3) IS BUILT 2026-08-06 — the publication gate, with all three conditions and its fault proof.** `decidePublication()` in `capability-framework` decides admissibility from the **governance triad alone**, because that is the only certification that exists before stage 8 and **the Execution Plane needs the package before stage 8.** **THE REASON, RECORDED AS THE REASON AND NOT AS A CAVEAT: THIS IS NOT STRONG-GATE VERSUS WEAK-GATE, IT IS WEAK GATE VERSUS NO PATH** — nothing publishes today, so a presence-only gate is not a regression from a stronger one; it is the first gate there has ever been on a path that does not otherwise exist. **CONDITION 1:** every non-judged leg refuses, **`not-applicable` included** — CHARTER §17.1's `NOT MEASURED` is never a pass, and admitting on it would make the gate satisfied **by the absence of its subject** (§17.1.1). The three non-judged dispositions stay **distinguishable** in the record because they are different facts. **`Verdict` gained a typed `disposition`** (`judged` / `not-applicable` / `refused` / `absent`), because `certify()` previously carried that distinction **only in a prose `reason`** — a consumer needing it had to parse a sentence a producer happened to write, which is D-013's shape. **CONDITION 2:** `legs` travels **inside** the decision and the decision travels on `RuntimeExecutionOutcome`, where a consumer sees it — not in an audit trail. The record is **enumerated from the triad, not from the verdicts**, so a stage nobody rendered a verdict for produces a **leg marked `absent`** rather than vanishing from the count. **CONDITION 3, AND IT CARRIES THE WEIGHT:** nothing is called *certified* — **ADMISSIBLE** means the plane has **not found a reason to refuse**; *certified* would mean it **established soundness**, which a presence-only review has not. **The word is asserted as a property, not left to review** — and that property **caught the emitted reason string**, which read *"ADMISSIBLE IS NOT CERTIFIED"*. **A disclaimer still puts the word in every emitted verdict**, where it is excerpted, logged and eventually read without its negation; the distinction now lives in the module's documentation, which travels to a reader deciding whether the gate is sufficient, rather than in the message, which travels to everyone else. **FAULT PROOF, BOTH LEGS:** positive control — all three legs judged → **ADMISSIBLE**, three legs carried; and **nine faults** — each triad leg `not-applicable`, `absent` and `refused` in turn → **NOT admissible**, each naming the leg and its disposition. Plus the **subject-removal test on the gate itself**: an empty outcome carrying `certified: true` → **NOT admissible**, because a gate reading only that boolean would have admitted. **89/89 framework, 210/210 engine, 361/361 onboarding; closure PASS.** **AND THE FULL CERTIFICATION STILL GATES THE REFERENCE PATH** — where all twelve stages run the stage-11 verdict exists and is strictly stronger, and dropping it to *use the new gate* would weaken a path that can afford the stronger check to match one that cannot. Report: [`SEVERANCE_3_PUBLICATION_GATE_REPORT.md`](SEVERANCE_3_PUBLICATION_GATE_REPORT.md). |

| **D-125** | **CORRECTED 2026-08-06 WHILE IMPLEMENTING ITS OWN FIX, AND THE CORRECTION SHARPENS IT: THE PLACEHOLDER IS FAIL-CLOSED AND IS STILL WRONG, BECAUSE IT ASKS THE CUSTOMER FOR A VALUE ONLY DBiz HOLDS.** The first version of this entry said the EP is *"instructed to verify against a literal placeholder"*, implying it would **proceed** on one. **It would not.** [`solution-export.ts:628`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/solution-export.ts) emits the requirement *"Register-then-run entrypoint — config load+validate (**refuse start on any unresolved `<FILL:>`**) … accept no work before readiness (R-16.9/10/11)"*, and `:474-477` records the `<FILL:>` convention as a deliberate choice: *"a placeholder is worse cosmetically and better operationally … so the EP says 'this is not configured' instead of failing mid-run."* **The device is correct and the boot guard is correct.** **THE DEFECT IS WHICH LIST THE MARKER WAS IN.** Every other `<FILL:>` in the package names something the **customer** holds — their CA trust bundle, their KMS key, their EP signing key — so `:587`'s instruction *"Complete the `<FILL: …>` placeholders … the Intelligence Plane holds none of them"* is actionable. **The IP's package verification key is DBiz-held and appears in NO artefact the customer possesses.** So this one marker sat in a list headed *complete these* naming a value its reader **cannot obtain**, under a sentence asserting DBiz holds none of them. **The consequence is not an unsafe boot — it is a permanent one:** the EP refuses to start, forever, and the refusal is indistinguishable from an operator who has not finished configuring. **That is D-122's write-side shape again — a control that satisfies its law perfectly and can never succeed** — and it is why P-81.4 is sequenced FIRST rather than treated as tidy-up. The original finding follows unaltered. **THE GENERATED EXECUTION PLANE IS INSTRUCTED TO VERIFY EVERY EXECUTION PACKAGE AGAINST A LITERAL PLACEHOLDER STRING, AND THE INSTRUCTION AND THE PLACEHOLDER ARE EMITTED BY THE SAME FUNCTION.** Measured 2026-08-06 while reporting D-123. [`solution-export.ts:509`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/solution-export.ts) writes `signatureVerificationKeyRef: '<FILL: IP public verification key ref + keyId>'` into every generated `config/security.json`; `:483` writes `verificationKeyRef: '<FILL: IP signature verification key reference + keyId>'` into `config/connectivity.json`; and `:631` writes the runtime instruction *"verify-before-execute: … detached-signature verification against `security.signatureVerificationKeyRef`; failure halts as a refusal (R-08.13–15)"* — **naming the field it just filled with a placeholder.** **THE KEY EXISTS AND IS NOT THE PROBLEM.** `loadOrCreateSigningKey` persists an ed25519 pair at boot ([`platform-adoption.ts:193`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/platform-adoption.ts)) and `SigningKey.publicKeyPem` is commented *"published to the EP as trust material"*. **Nothing publishes it.** | **THIS IS THE THIRD MISSING LINK IN D-123'S CHAIN, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT SHIPS.** Links 1 and 2 are absences inside this plane; **this one crosses the sovereignty boundary as emitted customer configuration.** A customer security team reading `config/security.json` — which doc 20 §5 makes an explicit design goal, *"a customer security team can read a package in a text editor"* — finds a security control whose key reference is an unfilled template marker. **AND IT IS AD-016'S OPEN LEG, IN CONCRETE FORM.** ADR-0007 closes the signing **model**; R-20.29 says custody and *"customer-side trust material"* remain open. This is what open means on disk: not an undecided policy, but a `<FILL:` marker in the artefact the policy was supposed to reach. **THE FAILURE ORDERING IS WHAT MAKES IT WORTH ITS OWN ENTRY:** a carrier ruled and built without this changes **nothing observable** — the EP still cannot verify, every retrieval still ends in R-20.30's `signature-invalid`, and the build would appear to have closed D-123. **It is also a defect independently of D-123:** even under the retired push model, an EP receiving a signature beside a package could not verify it. | IT IS RULED WITH D-123, NOT BEFORE OR AFTER IT.** Publishing a verification key is not a wiring task: **it is where DBiz-held trust material enters a customer tenancy**, so it inherits R-08.15 connectivity) and the rotation model ADR-0007 §6 already sets. **THE ROTATION QUESTION IS THE ONE TO RULE ON, NOT THE PUBLICATION:** ADR-0007 makes multiple keys concurrently valid so rotation needs
