tenant record:** `config/security.json` now emits `signatureVerification: { source: "registration-grant:configuration.packageVerificationKeys", resolveBy: "provenance.signingKeyId", … }`, and **the two `<FILL:>` markers that remain in that file are the EP signing key and the customer-held KMS key — both genuinely the customer's.** 352/352 engine tests pass; `tsc` clean. **AND THE CONTROL SURFACED WHAT THIS DOES *NOT* FIX, WHICH MATTERS MORE THAN WHAT IT DOES.** `config/connectivity.json` still carries **four IP-owned `<FILL:>` markers** — `executeEndpoint`, `evidenceEndpoint`, `oauthTokenEndpoint`, `telemetryEndpoint` — and the boot guard refuses start on **any** unresolved marker. **So the generated Execution Plane still cannot boot, and P-81.4 removed one blocker of five rather than the last one.** Those four are a different case and are correctly still markers: `solution-export.ts:470-477` records that nothing in the IP serves `/v1/*` and that composing them from the gateway origin *"manufactured four confident, well-formed, wrong URLs"* — they are honest *not-yet-served* markers, where this one named a value that **exists and was simply unpublished**. **Stated so that no reader takes this build as unblocking the Execution Plane.** **— RE-MEASURED 2026-08-06 WHILE REPORTING D-123 LINK 1, AND THE `<FILL:>` IS GONE:** generating a real solution for `carlisle-homes` from the repository's tenant record, `config/security.json` emits `signatureVerification { source: "registration-grant:configuration.packageVerificationKeys", resolveBy: "provenance.signingKeyId", … }`, and **both remaining `<FILL:>` markers in that file are genuinely the customer's** — their EP signing key and their KMS key. **WHAT REMAINS IS NARROWER AND SHARPER: ROTATION IS NOT BUILT, AND THE GRANT IS THE ONLY CARRIER.** P-81.4 rules the set is *rotated over the update-event channel the EP already polls*; **nothing emits a verification-key event**, and **registration is a once-per-tenancy event.** **THE DECIDING FACT: THE ONE REGISTERED TENANCY HOLDS NO VERIFICATION KEY AT ALL** — `carlisle-homes` registered **before the `packageVerificationKeys` field existed**, so its grant cannot have carried one. The grant is returned once and never persisted, so this plane cannot read what that tenancy holds — **but it can know what it was not sent.** **So link 1 without a distribution path produces packages the only existing customer CANNOT VERIFY AT ALL**, with no fix short of re-registering — which would mint a new EP credential and is exactly the redeployment coupling ADR-0007 §6 exists to avoid. **ROTATION THEREFORE RIDES WITH LINK 1**, not because rotation is due — no key has rotated — but because **the carrier for an already-registered tenancy does not exist**, and link 1 is the change that makes that absence observable. **— BUILT 2026-08-06 WITH LINK 1, AND THE CARRIER NOW EXISTS.** `verification-keys-changed` rides the ADR-0035 update channel; the composition root sweeps at boot and is **idempotent by comparison**, so a restart emits nothing and a tenancy that has never been sent a key is emitted to. **`carlisle-homes` obtains a verification key on the next boot of a deployment holding a signing key** — which is the whole reason this rode rather than followed. **THE RULING THIS IMPLEMENTS, RETAINED BECAUSE ITS REASONING IS WHY THE BUILD TOOK THIS SHAPE: RULED 2026-08-06 AS ADR-0081 P-81.4, AND THE RULING IS THE KEY *SET*, NOT THE KEY.** Carried by `RegistrationGrant.configuration` at ADR-0036's trust-establishment moment (verification keys are non-secret by R-08.15, so nothing INV-2 protects crosses), rotated over the update channel the EP already polls, and **`solution-export` emits a reference and a resolution rule rather than a PEM or a `<FILL:` marker.** **Sequenced FIRST among ADR-0081 §6's steps** — it is the only step whose absence makes the others unobservable. **STOP FOR ACCEPTANCE.** |

| **D-126** | **SEVEN ACCEPTED ADRs ARE ABSENT FROM THE ADR INDEX, AND THE THREE GATES OVER ADRs ARE ALL STRUCTURALLY UNABLE TO SEE IT.** Measured 2026-08-06 while adding ADR-0081's index row. `DECISIONS.md` §5 is the ADR index — CHARTER §2 item 8 — and comparing every `ADR-nnnn-*.md` on disk against it finds **73 on disk, 8 unindexed**: **ADR-0060, ADR-0063, ADR-0071, ADR-0072, ADR-0073, ADR-0074, ADR-0080**, and ADR-0081 before this session added its row. **ADR-0080 is the load-bearing instance** — accepted 2026-08-06, the subject of D-115's open ruling, and absent from the index a reader consults to find it. **WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT, AND ALL THREE REASONS ARE DIFFERENT.** `verify-adr-completeness` reads the **files**, never the index, so it is satisfied by an ADR that no index names. `verify-change-control-completeness`'s connectivity property scans `program/` **wholesale** — deliberately, and correctly, to avoid the curated-subset defect its own header records — so **an ADR mentioned once in `PROJECT_STATE.md` counts as connected while being absent from the index entirely.** And `verify-programme-closure` counts ADRs **on disk against the baseline**, never against the index. **Three gates over ADRs, and the index is not the subject of any of them.** | **THIS IS D-107 AT ITS OWN ROOT, AND THE DIRECTION IS THE FINDING.** D-107 recorded the index carrying a **stale status** for three ADRs — a wrong value, which at least exists to be compared. **This is absence, and absence is the harder direction**: a missing row cannot be stale, cannot be diffed, and produces no output for any reader to doubt. **The subject-removal test (CHARTER §17.1.1) explains it exactly** — every property over the ADR estate is satisfied *more easily* when a row is missing, because each is a property over files or over a baseline, and a file that no index names is a file like any other. **AND IT IS THE CONNECTIVITY PROPERTY'S OWN TRADE-OFF ARRIVING AS A COST.** That property was widened from a hand-listed subset to a wholesale `program/` scan precisely because the narrow version *"cried wolf"* and reported five connected ADRs as orphans. The widening was right, and it bought a blind spot: **being mentioned anywhere in programme state is now indistinguishable from being indexed.** **A control loosened to stop false positives stopped detecting a true one, and nothing recorded which detection was traded away.** | `program/DECISIONS.md` §5 (the index, line 85 onward) · `docs/adr/` (73 files on disk) · `governance/verification/verify-adr-completeness.js:49-69` (reads files, not the index) · `governance/verification/verify-change-control-completeness.js:119-130` (wholesale `program/` scan for connectivity) · `governance/verification/verify-programme-closure.js` (disk vs baseline) · D-107 · D-116 (decision traceability by a label nobody must write) · CHARTER §2 item 8 · CHARTER §17.1.1 | **OPEN. ONE ROW ADDED, SEVEN NOT, AND THE EXCLUSION IS DELIBERATE.** ADR-0081's row was authored by the session that authored ADR-0081. **The other seven are NOT back-filled here, for PENDING_ADR_AMENDMENTS.md's own stated reason:** an index row summarises a decision, and **nobody can write the summary of a decision they did not take** — inventing seven would put authored characterisations of other sessions' reasoning into the permanent record of why this platform is built as it is. That is worse than a visible gap. **THE REPAIR IS TO DERIVE THE INDEX, NOT TO REFILL IT** — the same end D-107 already prescribes for the status field: **one accessor that reads the ADR files, and whose disagreement with the index is an ERROR rather than a value.** A derived index cannot go stale and cannot omit; a hand-maintained one has now failed in both directions — wrong status (D-107) and missing row (here). **AND THE CONNECTIVITY PROPERTY IS OWED A NARROWER SIBLING, NOT A REVERSAL:** *is this ADR named by the INDEX* is a distinct question from *is it mentioned in programme state*, and collapsing them is what made this invisible. The wholesale scan stays; the second property is added beside it. **— AND THE PAIR IS NOW VISIBLE, WHICH IT WAS NOT WHEN THIS ENTRY WAS WRITTEN.** Adding ADR-0083 turned `verify-change-control-completeness` RED on *every ADR is referenced by the architecture, another ADR, or programme state* — **before its index row existed.** So the estate holds **two** properties over the same drift, pointing in **opposite directions**: the connectivity property catches a decision **nothing points at** (it exists and the estate does not know), and this entry's membership property catches a decision **the index does not name** (the estate knows and the register does not). **They are one drift — a decision disconnected from the estate — seen from each end, and only the first was covered until now.** That is exactly how eight ADRs sat unindexed while every one of them was *connected*: **the covered direction was satisfied by a single mention anywhere in `program/`.** **A control pair is only a pair once both directions are named; before that it is one control and an assumption.** |

| **D-128** | **THERE IS NO EVIDENCE ROUTE IN THE AUTHENTICATED TIER, SO R-20.12's BINDING HAS NO PRODUCTION INGRESS TO BE ENFORCED AT — AND `EvidenceReferenceHandle` CANNOT CARRY THE HASH EITHER, BECAUSE IT IS CONSTRUCTED BEFORE THE PACKAGE EXISTS.** Measured 2026-08-06 while performing ADR-0082 §6 step 1. **(i) THE INGRESS.** `POST /v1/evidence` exists **only** on `ip-execute-gateway.mjs`, which binds `127.0.0.1`, `process.exit(1)`s on a production environment, and whose `/v1/*` paths **never reach the deployed application at all** (D-121 §5 — Azure Blob Storage's error page answers them). The NestJS tier has controllers for auth, registration, tenants, packages, application templates and health, and **no evidence controller**. So P-82.5's enforcement, built where the route is, hardens a **dev harness**. **(ii) THE HANDLE.** `EvidenceReferenceHandle` is constructed at [`execution.ts:297`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/execution.ts) and [`healing.ts:144`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/healing.ts) — **stages 8 and 9**, inside `runThroughRunner` — while the package is composed at [`runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:111`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts), **after all twelve stages have returned.** Making `packageHash` required on the handle would break both sites **with no value to supply.** | **THIS IS D-124's CONSEQUENCE ARRIVING AS A BLOCKER RATHER THAN AS AN OBSERVATION.** D-124 records that the package is composed outside the lifecycle that governs it; **(ii) is what that costs**: evidence produced at stage 9 cannot reference a package that stage 7 was supposed to have authored, so **R-20.12's binding is unconstructible on the canonical path for a structural reason, not an omission.** **AND (i) IS THE SAME SHAPE AS D-122's WRITE SIDE.** ADR-0082 P-82.5 is correct, is a precondition, and **lands on a path that cannot serve production** — so satisfying it fully would mean either building an evidence route (a decision nobody has taken) or investing in a gateway whose retirement is already scheduled under ADR-0049 M5. **The enforcement that CAN land today is presence and digest shape**, and that is what landed; **resolution to a known run is ADR-0082 §6 step 2's**, because no run record exists to resolve against — claiming resolution now would be the declared-but-unbuilt failure this platform exists to avoid. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/ip-execute-gateway.mjs` (`/v1/evidence`, the only one; `:125` binds `127.0.0.1`) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/` (seven controllers, **no evidence controller**) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/execution.ts:297` and `src/domains/healing.ts:144` (handle construction, stages 8–9) · `packages/functional-testing-engine/src/runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:102,111` (composition after all twelve stages) · `packages/contracts/src/evidence.ts:44-58` (`EvidenceReferenceSchema.packageHash` — already required, and the single source of truth) · ADR-0082 P-82.5, §6 step 1 · ADR-0049 M5 · D-121 §5 · D-122 · D-124 | **OPEN — AND ADR-0082 §6 STEP 1 IS PARTLY DISCHARGED, WITH THE PART THAT IS NOT NAMED RATHER THAN IMPLIED.** **What landed and is proved:** the ingress **refuses** a reference carrying no usable `packageHash` — `422`, before any side effect — measured against a running gateway with **two positive controls accepted (`202`, digest-object and hex-string forms), three subjects refused, and a fourth control refused for a DIFFERENT stated reason** (the artefact-content guard), showing the new check shadows nothing. **`evidence.received` was written exactly twice across six probes: refused, never stored, proved by observation rather than by reading the branch.** **What did NOT land, and why:** the handle field (ii — blocked on D-124) and resolution to a known run (ADR-0082 §6 step 2). **What is owed as a decision, not a build:** whether the evidence route moves to the authenticated tier **before** ADR-0049 M5 retires the gateway, or waits for it. **Until that is ruled, R-20.12 is enforced on a development path only, and this entry is the record that no reader should infer otherwise.** **— RULED 2026-08-06: MOVE THE EVIDENCE ROUTE TO THE AUTHENTICATED TIER. DO NOT WAIT FOR ADR-0049 M5.** Waiting would leave ADR-0082's evidence half unreachable in production until a retirement nobody has scheduled — **the store authorised, built, gated and fed by nothing: a declared capability with no subject**, which is the class this register already counts five times. **AND THIS ENTRY'S CLAIM (ii) IS CORRECTED BY THE D-124 REPORT — THE HANDLE DOES NOT BLOCK THE MOVE, AND I RECORDED THAT IT DID.** Measured: **`receiveEvidence` — the function that ingests EP evidence as handles — has ZERO non-test callers**, and the gateway's `POST /v1/evidence` consumes **`body.reference`, raw JSON from the wire**, implementing its own inline payload check rather than calling it. **`EvidenceReferenceHandle` therefore appears on no ingress path at all.** The ingress consumes a **wire reference**, and `EvidenceReferenceSchema` — the wire contract — **already requires `packageHash`.** **So (ii) is a real blocker on requiring the field of the internal handle, and NOT a blocker on the move.** **WHAT THE MOVED ROUTE CAN CARRY ON DAY ONE:** presence and shape (already landed, transfers unchanged) **plus a real parse through `EvidenceReferenceSchema`, which the authenticated tier can do and the gateway cannot** — so **the move RETIRES a duplicated shape rule rather than adding one**, the duplicate existing only because that file imports no `@dbiz` package (D-121). **WHAT IT CANNOT CARRY:** resolution to a known run, which needs ADR-0082 §6 step 2. **The cost, stated rather than discovered: the route will accept any reference whose `packageHash` is well-formed, including one naming a package this plane never authored** — strictly better than today, when it accepts references naming nothing, and **not the R-20.12 binding in full.** **AND A FIFTH INSTANCE OF THE UNWIRED-DECLARATION CLASS IS ON THE PATH BEING BUILT:** `receiveEvidence` is declared, exported, conformance-tested and called by nothing, while the gateway duplicates its payload guard inline — **so the move is equally an opportunity to wire it and an opportunity to carry the duplicate into the tier that is supposed to be canonical.** Report: [`D-124_COMPOSITION_PLACEMENT_REPORT.md`](D-124_COMPOSITION_PLACEMENT_REPORT.md) §5, §6. **— BUILT 2026-08-06. `POST /v1/evidence` NOW EXISTS IN THE AUTHENTICATED TIER**, served by both transports from one pure handler (`evidence-ingress.ts`), with its auth block **written out rather than copied**: an authenticated principal, **the EP-token revocation check** (the one a new route silently omits), the `tenant:update` permission, and a principal scoped to **exactly one tenant** — a global principal is refused, because a route with no slug gives `mayAccessTenant` nothing to range over. **The tenant comes from the CREDENTIAL and a body claiming another is refused rather than silently re-scoped.** **PROVED OVER A REAL SOCKET THROUGH THE ASSEMBLED APPLICATION** (D-111's lesson), positive control first: a conforming reference is **202**; no `packageHash` → **422** naming the contract; an embedded payload → **422 even though it PARSES**, since the schema is `.passthrough()`; a payload nested in `artefacts[]` → **422**; another tenant claimed → **403**; no credential → **401 with the RFC 6750 challenge**; a platform-admin → **403**; and a **superseded EP token → 401**, on its own tenant with its own positive control so the 401 is rotation being enforced rather than the tenant being unusable. **361/361 and 210/210 green; `verify-http-surface-parity`, `verify-composition-root` and `verify-provider-platform` all PASS.** **AND THE MOVE RETIRED A DUPLICATE RATHER THAN CARRYING ONE:** the payload rule now lives once, in `@dbiz/contracts` beside the schema that defines the artefact, as `carriesEvidencePayload` — the capability's `evidence-return-channel` delegates to it instead of holding its own copy. **The three copies had already drifted:** the capability's omitted `body` and `bytes` and did not look inside `artefacts[]`. **`receiveEvidence` COULD NOT BE IMPORTED, AND THAT IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN A COMPROMISE** — `@dbiz/functional-testing-engine` is not a dependency of the API tier, and adding it would make the platform's tenant surface depend on a **capability engine**, inverting the layering. **A wire-contract rule belongs in the contract**, so the rule was lifted rather than the function imported, and both consumers now run the same check rather than two that agree. **WHAT IS STILL NOT ENFORCED, AND THE ROUTE'S OWN RESPONSE SAYS SO:** resolution of `packageHash` to a **known run** — so the route **accepts any reference whose `packageHash` parses, including one naming a package this plane never authored.** Strictly better than the gateway, which accepted references naming nothing, and **not R-20.12 in full.** It closes with ADR-0082 §6 step 2. **AND NOTHING IS RECORDED** — `recorded: false` is in the 202 body, because P-82.2's durable record is §6 step 3 and a route implying durability it lacks is the declared-but-unbuilt failure in one field. |

| **D-129** | **THE PLATFORM'S HIGHEST-VALUE ASSET IS HELD IN WEAKER CUSTODY THAN THE SESSION SECRET, AND THE TWO ARE RESOLVED TWELVE LINES APART IN THE SAME FUNCTION.** Measured 2026-08-06 while reporting the first-run marker. `composeApiDeps` resolves `SESSION_SECRET` through the **Secret Provider** — Key Vault in cloud, where absence is unambiguous and `require()` throws `MissingSecretError` — and resolves the **package signing key** as `loadOrCreateSigningKey(join(signingDir, 'ep-package-signing.pem'))`: **a file on a mounted state volume, created if missing.** ADR-0007 §2 calls package signing keys *"the platform's **highest-value asset**: their compromise grants reach into EVERY customer tenancy simultaneously"*. **The weaker custody holds the stronger asset.** | **NO DECISION RECORDS THE SPLIT, WHICH IS WHY IT IS DEBT RATHER THAN A CHOICE.** The signing key predates the provider adoption — ADR-0060 §6 M-a adopted the config/secret seam **additively**, so what was already a file stayed a file — and nothing since has asked why. **IT IS NOT MERELY INCONSISTENT; IT IS THE CAUSE OF A SEPARATE DEFECT.** `SecretProvider.require` has exactly the semantics the mint-on-empty repair is reconstructing by hand: **absent throws, and there is no create-if-missing.** The first-run problem is largely an artefact of the key living somewhere that has a create-if-missing idiom at all — provisioning a secret is a deliberate act, so *absent* would unambiguously mean *not provisioned*. **AND THE BLAST RADIUS IS ASYMMETRIC WITH THE PROTECTION:** `SESSION_SECRET` compromise forges credentials against this plane; signing-key compromise **authors packages every customer tenancy will execute.** | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/platform-adoption.ts` (`secrets.require('SESSION_SECRET')` and `loadOrCreateSigningKey(...)`, same function) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/package-signing.ts` (create-on-absent) · `packages/platform-providers/src/**/secret-provider` (`require` throws on absent) · ADR-0007 §2 · ADR-0060 §6 M-a · AD-016 (key custody, open) · [`SIGNING_KEY_FIRST_RUN_MARKER_REPORT.md`](SIGNING_KEY_FIRST_RUN_MARKER_REPORT.md) §5 · D-123 | **OPEN — AND DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED INTO D-123 LINK 1.** Moving the key touches secret provisioning, deployment and key custody — **AD-016's open leg** — and folding it into a signing change is the scope error **D-087** counts. **THE INTERIM IS THE MARKER REPORT'S §3 AND IT REMAINS CORRECT AFTER THE MOVE**, because it is about **distribution** (has any verification key reached a tenancy?) rather than about storage. **What is owed is one ADR** placing the signing key in the Secret Provider, with its provisioning, its rotation and its absence semantics stated — and it should be authored **with** AD-016's distribution leg rather than beside it, since both are answers to *where does trust material live and how does it move*. **— RAISED AS ITS OWN ADR 2026-08-06: [ADR-0083](../docs/adr/ADR-0083-signing-key-custody.md), PROPOSED, STOP FOR ACCEPTANCE.** **THE CAUSE IS RECORDED PLAINLY BECAUSE IT IS THE PART NOBODY WOULD FIND BY REVIEW: NO DECISION RECORDS THE SPLIT.** ADR-0060 §6 M-a adopted the config/secret seam **additively** — correctly, so the migration could land without rewriting working wiring — and **what was already a file stayed a file.** Nothing rejected the Secret Provider for this key; nothing considered it. **The weaker custody holds the stronger asset by RESIDUE, not by choice**, and a reader auditing custody would reasonably look for the decision that put it there and find none. **The absence of the decision is the finding.** **AND IT IS ALREADY CAUSING A SECOND MECHANISM TO BE BUILT BY HAND:** `SecretProvider.require` already has the semantics D-123 link 1's mint-on-empty repair reconstructs — **absent throws, and there is no create-if-missing at all** — so **link 1's repair rebuilds in one place what exists platform-wide.** That is the argument for the ADR, and it means **link 1's repair is INTERIM BY CONSTRUCTION**, which is now said at the site rather than left to look permanent. **P-83.3 retires the derived authorisation WITH ITS SUBJECT** (CHARTER §17.1.1 obligation (ii)) rather than leaving a control satisfied by the absence of what it watches. **— CLOSED 2026-08-06. ADR-0083 ACCEPTED AND EXECUTED, AFTER ADR-0084 AND DELIBERATELY IN THAT ORDER** — with Rule 6's scope recorded first the move reads as **a custody improvement**, which is what it is; in the other order it would have read as **a violation being remediated**, and it never was one. The key is resolved as `PACKAGE_SIGNING_KEY` through the secret backend; **the create-if-missing branch is gone**; and `SigningKeyMintAuthorisation`'s **four properties were DELETED rather than kept** — they would have been satisfied by the absence of what they watched. **What replaced them is stronger and is proved:** an unprovisioned secret refuses, an **empty** secret is treated as absent rather than as a key, and **the module exposes no create-or-get at all** — asserted as a property, so a re-introduction fails a test rather than passing review. **AND THE ADOPTION END-TO-END TEST HAD TO PROVISION THE KEY TO KEEP BOOTING**, which is the change working: a deployment without a provisioned signing key does not start, **and a test is a deployment.** 107/107 · 89/89 · 47/47 · 379/379 · 210/210. |
| **D-130** | **THE STORY-OBSERVATION PROJECTION DROPS FIVE OF THE SEVEN FIELDS ITS OWN CONTRACT DECLARES, AND ONE OF THE TWO IT KEEPS CANNOT DISTINGUISH ABSENCE FROM SUCCESS.** Measured 2026-08-06 against the attachments capability request. **(i) THE PROJECTION.** [`ArtefactObservationSchema`](../packages/contracts/src/observation-set.ts) declares `name · extension · mediaType · bytes · sha256 · retrieved · retrievalError · provenance`. `StoryObservation.artefacts` ([`observation-interpretation.ts:313-319`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/observation-interpretation.ts)) carries **`{ name, retrieved }`** — dropping `sha256`, `mediaType`, `bytes`, `retrievalError` and `provenance`. **`retrievalError` is the field on which *"a story references an attachment that cannot be read"* would be assessed, and the runtime shape has no place to put it**, so a failed retrieval and a retrieval nobody attempted are the same value. **(ii) THE SIGNAL.** `assessCompleteness` emits `signal('attachments-retrieved', input.artefacts.length === 0 \|\| input.artefacts.every((a) => a.retrieved), …)` — so **a story with no attachments and a story whose attachments all downloaded report the identical `present: true`.** **(iii) NOTHING POPULATES EITHER SIDE.** The only producer of a `StoryObservation` in the repository is the fixture (`canonical-reference-input.ts:304-306`); `translateExecutionRequest` obtains it from an injected `fetchRequirement` provider with **no implementation**; and the Execution Plane performs **no work-item attachment retrieval at all**. `retrieved` is a boolean nobody sets, and `classifyArtefact` classifies a list nobody fills. | **THIS IS THE ABSENCE/SILENCE DISTINCTION THE `ObservationSet` CONTRACT ADDED `retrievalError` AND `retrievalGaps` TO PREVENT, DEFEATED IN THE PROJECTION THAT CONSUMES IT.** The contract was written knowing that *"a fact with no resolvable origin cannot be audited, and cannot be argued with"*; the flattening discards exactly the fields that carry origin and failure. **And (ii) is worse than a missing signal, because it is a PRESENT one that reads as a pass:** a story that references no attachment at all reports `attachments-retrieved: present` — the shape D-013 records, a value asserting a state it cannot have observed. **The compounding is the part that matters for any completeness refusal:** a refusal grounded on *"an attachment that cannot be read"* is unbuildable on this shape, and would appear buildable to anyone reading the signal list rather than the projection. | Functional Testing capability — `observation-interpretation` · `@dbiz/contracts` (the contract is correct and unchanged) | **BUILD, NOT A DECISION — RULED 2026-08-06.** ADR-0075 **P-75.8 already records this gap open**, so nothing is owed before the widening can be authored: `StoryObservation.artefacts` takes `ArtefactObservationSchema`'s shape, and **(ii) is fixed WITH the widening rather than deferred to the ADR** — `retrievalError` and a populated `retrievalGaps` make *no attachments* and *all retrieved* different values, and fixing the predicate without the fields would only move the ambiguity. **What this does NOT authorise:** retrieving an attachment. Content crosses **by reference only** (`ArtefactObservationSchema` has no content field and will not gain one), content is **C1** — permitted in this plane never, except R-06.4 §2, **ephemeral, never persisted** — **no attachment store exists and no ADR authorises one** without R-06.4's four conditions, and **WHO FETCHES is EP-side by R-3.2**, which forbids this plane opening a connection to a customer system. **WHETHER anything is fetched at all remains a capability decision and is not taken.** Report: [`STORY_INPUT_CAPABILITY_REPORT.md`](STORY_INPUT_CAPABILITY_REPORT.md) §1. |
| **D-131** | **TWELVE OF THIRTEEN COMPLETENESS SIGNALS ARE COMPUTED ON EVERY RUN AND READ BY NOTHING — THEIR ENTIRE EFFECT IS A COUNT IN AN EVENT.** Measured 2026-08-06. `assessCompleteness` ([`observation-interpretation.ts:260-292`](../packages/functional-testing-engine/src/domains/observation-interpretation.ts)) emits thirteen signals, each carrying its own evidence string — including **`expected-results`** (*"N of M criteria state an expected outcome"*) and **`attachments-retrieved`**, which are precisely the two facts an upstream story-completeness refusal would be grounded on. They land on `ObservationInterpretationResult.completenessSignals`, **whose only downstream reader is `story-intelligence`, which reads `interpretation.acceptanceCriteria` and nothing else** (`:157`). The Platform Event publishes `signalsPresent` as a **count** (`:422`), and that is the whole of their consequence. **And the domain that holds them cannot refuse:** its `execute` has exactly one exit, `certified: true, failure: null` (`:383`) — **no branch.** The one domain holding every completeness signal is the one domain in the sequence with no negative finding. | **D-033'S CLASS, ON THE FACTS A REFUSAL WOULD BE BUILT FROM — AND D-033 ALREADY SUPPLIES THE READING.** *"An unconsumed field is not dead weight, it is an UNTESTED CLAIM"*, and *"the absence of a consumer is not evidence a claim is unwanted, only that nobody has checked it."* These are D-033's **second kind**: a claim whose consumer is the missing capability — and D-130(ii) is the proof, a signal that has been wrong on every empty-attachment story for as long as it has existed, uncaught because nothing ever asked it a question. **The signals are also the deliberate residue of a deleted verdict, which is why the repair is not "wire them up":** the prior implementation divided present by total and published `completeness score: 46%`, and the domain's own header records that this *"reads as a verdict no matter what the surrounding prose says."* The score was removed on purpose and the certification criterion `'no-ratio'` enforces its absence. **So the signals are unconsumed BY DESIGN and unweighed BY DEFAULT, and only the second half is debt.** | Functional Testing capability — `observation-interpretation` | **RECORDED 2026-08-06 AS AN INSTANCE OF THE DECLARED-AND-UNCONSUMED CLASS (D-007's axis, D-033's reading), AND NOT REPAIRED HERE.** **WHO WEIGHS THE SIGNALS IS A CAPABILITY DECISION AND IS OPEN** — the design comment nominates `story.review` and `story.gap-detection`, **neither of which exists in this plane** — and wiring them to a consumer without naming that owner reintroduces the deleted score under a new name, which is the one outcome the `'no-ratio'` criterion was written to prevent. **THE COUNT IS SEVEN, MEASURED, WITH THE ENUMERATION WRITTEN OUT — AND THE CORRECTION IS ITSELF AN INSTANCE OF D-107.** This was first ruled as the **eighth** instance; enumerating the register directly gives seven priors: `requirementMapping` and `DomainOutput.certified` (D-033 i and ii), `automation-architecture`'s unread `testManagement` input (D-016), `frameworkVersions`, `EvidenceReferenceSchema.packageHash` (D-115), the execution-package schema itself (D-117), and `receiveEvidence` (D-128) — **so this is the eighth by inclusion and the priors number seven, and the estimate was carried rather than counted.** **RULED 2026-08-06: the measured enumeration governs, and the estimate is recorded as having been one.** That is D-107's own subject — a figure asserted from memory across a sequence of statements, correct-looking, and never measured until something needed it to be right — and it is the reason the enumeration is written out here rather than the number alone: **a count nobody can reproduce is precisely the defect this class exists to record**, and a class register that carried an unreproducible count would be an instance of itself. Report: [`STORY_INPUT_CAPABILITY_REPORT.md`](STORY_INPUT_CAPABILITY_REPORT.md) §2. |
| **D-132** | **NOTHING IN AN EXECUTION PACKAGE STATES WHICH TENANT, ORGANISATION OR ENVIRONMENT THE RUN IS SUPPOSED TO MEASURE, SO A RUN AGAINST THE WRONG CUSTOMER'S ENVIRONMENT IS UNDETECTABLE RATHER THAN IMPOSSIBLE — RAISED HERE BECAUSE THE PLANE THAT MEASURED IT CANNOT RAISE IT HERE.** The Execution Plane's `IP-AMENDMENT-REQUEST.md` **A-8**, its **highest-priority item and the only one in its set with no workaround on its side**. **A-8 HAS NOT ARRIVED IN THIS PLANE AT ALL** — grep across the repository returns only `GA-8` (a deployment gate) and `AA-8` (an automation-architecture property); no ADR, and no entry in this register, `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md`, `PENDING_ADR_AMENDMENTS.md` or `DECISIONS.md`. **Measured against the contract:** `ExecutionPackageSchema` ([`execution-package.ts:99-114`](../packages/contracts/src/execution-package.ts)) carries `contractVersion · runId · correlationId · capabilityId · operations · directives · gates · evidenceRequirements · provenance · validity` — **no `subject`.** **`provenance.tenantId` IS carried and is NOT the answer:** provenance records *who authored this package*, not an assertion the executor must compare against its configured target before acting, and it carries no `expectedOrigin`. **The evidence is a GREEN run, which is the counter-intuitive half:** `run-fixture-0001`, the first `EXECUTED_CLEAN` run in the programme, four checks performed, zero unperformable — **and nothing in it establishes whose application it measured**, because `op-002` compared the browser URL against a value from the EP's own `.env` and therefore *"confirmed that this plane's configuration agrees with itself."* | **THE GENERALISATION OF A DEFECT THE EP ALREADY CLOSED ONE LEVEL DOWN, AND THE LEVEL IT MOVED TO IS OUTSIDE THAT PLANE'S REACH.** `CORRECT-CHECK-WRONG-SUBJECT.md` records `assertUrl contains "dynamics.com"` reporting **SATISFIED against Microsoft's Entra login page** — the URL read correctly, the comparison performed correctly, the result reported correctly, **and the subject wrong**. Its **Finding 1 inverts the usual instinct and is the reason this outranks breadth: *adding checks to a run whose subject is unestablished increases the risk rather than reducing it*** — each satisfied comparison raises confidence in a conclusion about the wrong thing, so *"four checks against a login form produce a weaker artefact than one, because four make the green result look earned."* The identity-provider case was closable there because a redirect has a hostname that plane can see; **the tenant case is not, because the fact that settles it never crosses.** **THE ASYMMETRY IS THE GOVERNANCE FINDING, AND IT IS D-117'S EXACTLY:** the Intelligence Plane knows what it commissioned and cannot see what a consumer had to invent; the measurement could only come from the other side, and did. | Contract — `@dbiz/contracts` (`ExecutionPackageSchema`) · Execution Plane (the consumer that measured it) | **OPEN — A CAPABILITY DECISION REQUIRING AN ADR, AND EXPLICITLY NOT TAKEN IN PASSING.** This changes the wire format between two sovereign planes, which CLAUDE.md §4 forbids authoring in one step across both, and it is the same class as D-117's two closures — **the Intelligence Plane must choose rather than let the consumer choose.** **What is owed:** whether the package asserts its own subject, in what fields (the EP requests `tenantId` + `expectedOrigin` required, `environment` enumerated), and whether `provenance.tenantId` is extended or a distinct `subject` block is added — **the two are not interchangeable, because provenance describes authorship and a subject block describes an obligation on the executor.** **WHAT THIS ENTRY IS FOR, STATED PLAINLY: the Execution Plane cannot file against this register, and an amendment request that arrives in a document nobody in the receiving plane indexes has not arrived.** It is filed here so the gap is visible from the side that owns the contract. **AND IT IS NOT BLOCKING, WHICH IS PRECISELY THE PROBLEM** — what it leaves open is not a run that stops but *"a run that succeeds against something nobody commissioned."* **Ordering, recorded as a constraint:** A-8 and an application model precede test-case breadth; more cases against the wrong module is more confident wrong answers. Report: [`STORY_INPUT_CAPABILITY_REPORT.md`](STORY_INPUT_CAPABILITY_REPORT.md) §3. |

Solutions generated before a disposition existed carry three valued `testManagement` fields, no `<FILL:>`, and **no statement of whether their test repository already exists** — because there was no field in which to state it. **Their disposition is UNKNOWN.** | **THIS IS THE WIDEST-REACHING CONSEQUENCE OF ADR-0085, AND IT REACHES TENANTS THE ADR CANNOT SEE.** Every other ruling reaches tenants onboarded after it; this one reaches every tenant already deployed. **If silence is read as `create-if-absent`, every existing tenant silently acquires a policy nobody chose** — which is §3's rejected inference (*"a configuration's meaning must not depend on how far its author got"*) **arriving through MIGRATION instead of through design**, and migration is the route a rejected alternative actually returns by: nobody re-argues it, a default is simply chosen for the records that lack the field. **The harm is not hypothetical and it is permanent:** a tenant with an existing Test Plan whose name does not match the composed convention gets a **second plan**, and a container is not renamed once assets hang off it. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/solution-export.ts` (emission) · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/tenant-repository.ts`, `tenant-config.ts` (normalisation of records lacking the field) · `tenants/carlisle-homes/tenant.json` (the one existing record) · ADR-0085 §5, §3 · D-045 (the duplicate-creation harm one level down) | **OPEN — AND THE RULING IS ALREADY TAKEN; WHAT IS OWED IS THE MECHANISM.** ADR-0085 §5 rules that existing solutions **MUST NOT be read as `create-if-absent`** and that their disposition is **recorded as unknown**. The mechanism owed is that normalisation carries `unknown` as a *value* rather than as an absent field, so that a run against an un-migrated tenant **refuses with a reason naming the migration** rather than proceeding under an assumption. **A default installed at this layer would be indistinguishable from a decision, and would be taken by whoever wrote the default on behalf of every tenancy that never took one.** Resolution per tenant is a re-onboarding or an explicit amendment. **Until then, `unknown` is never a pass** — CHARTER §17.1's `NOT MEASURED` rule applied to configuration. |

| **D-134** | **`issueKey` — THE SIXTH DECLARED-AND-UNCONSUMED INSTANCE, AND THE FIRST AIMED AT A HUMAN.** Measured 2026-08-06 during ADR-0085's measurement and **carried from the concurrent ADR-0085 file that was resolved away**, so the measurement survives its container. Emitted at `application-plane.ts:385` and `solution-export.ts:389` into two Execution-Plane files — `config/integrations.json` and `config/capabilities.json` — both carrying `"issueKey": "<FILL: user story / issue key (e.g. CARL-123)>"`. **Measured: no consumer, in either plane.** The only other occurrences are the two generators and one template test asserting the key survives regeneration. **And the consuming plane's synchronisation port lists `issuekey` on `FORBIDDEN_NOUNS` with a conformance test that fails the build on a hit — so the field is emitted twice, required to be filled twice, and NO COMPLIANT CONSUMER FOR IT CAN BE WRITTEN.** | **THE OTHER FIVE COST A READER'S TIME. THIS ONE COSTS A CUSTOMER'S.** `EvidenceReferenceSchema.packageHash` (D-115), the execution-package schema (D-117), version tolerance (D-118), `SealedPackageStore.put` (D-122) and `receiveEvidence` are code declaring a capability nothing calls. **This is a `<FILL:>` — an instruction to a PERSON, asking a customer to go and find a value in their tracker.** > **A PLACEHOLDER IMPLIES A CONSUMER.** It is not a dormant declaration; it is a **claim, made to the customer, that supplying this changes what the platform does. It changes nothing.** **It also sits in the same two files as the absence ADR-0085 exists to name**, and §2.1 records it as one of four mechanisms that presented that absence as settled — a field declared twice and consumed nowhere **trains readers to treat declaration as provision**. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/application-plane.ts:385` · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/engine/solution-export.ts:389` · the consuming plane's synchronisation port `FORBIDDEN_NOUNS` and its conformance test · ADR-0085 §2.1, §4.4 (explicitly **owed**, not ruled) · D-115, D-117, D-118, D-122 | **OPEN — OWED AS ITS OWN RULING, AND DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN INSIDE ADR-0085.** The candidate disposition measured alongside it is **deletion rather than wiring**: the generator's own comment at `application-plane.ts:383` already says *"A per-run target: the slot is generated; the value is entered at the EP, never at onboarding"* — **the generator knew, and nothing downstream read it** — which makes story identity **per-run** and `issueKey` the one instance of this class closable by **removal**. **Measured cost of removal: an Execution Plane reading the key would break — none does.** It is not ruled in ADR-0085 because that ADR rules on **per-tenant** configuration and this is **per-run** identity: *they arrive as one question — "what should the tenant tell us?" — and they are two, and answering them together is how `issueKey` came to exist.* |

| **D-137** | **A PACKAGE WAS ABSENT FROM EVERY SUITE MEASUREMENT THIS PROGRAMME HAS PUBLISHED, AND THE FIGURES LOOKED COMPLETE WITHOUT IT.** Measured 2026-08-06 taking M5's pre-deletion measurement. `packages/tenant-onboarding-web` runs under **vitest**, not `node --test`, so every sweep that harvested `ℹ tests/pass/fail` counted **nothing** for it and reported the remaining packages as the total. It is **8/8 green**, so the omission changed no verdict — **which is precisely why it survived.** The published shorthand (`107/107 · 89/89 · 47/47 · 379/379 · 210/210`) named five suites and was read as the estimate; the workspace has **fifteen**. | **THIS IS THE CENSUS-DEPTH PROBLEM INSIDE THE SWEEP THAT MEASURES IT** (CHARTER §17.1, and the same shape `PART_4_CENSUS_DESIGN_REPORT.md` §1 found in four census dimensions). §17.1 asks whether a reported number MOVES when the thing it counts moves. **This number cannot move for `tenant-onboarding-web` at all** — no result it could produce, green or red, would change the sweep's output, because the harvester's regex does not match its runner's output format. **A suite that cannot fail the measurement is not being measured**, and `NOT RUN` ≡ `FAIL` (C-0.4) applies to a suite the harness never looked at exactly as it applies to one that did not execute. **The harm is latent rather than realised:** the day that package goes red, the sweep still reports green, and the report will be believed because it has been right every previous time. | `packages/tenant-onboarding-web/package.json` (`vitest run`) · every sweep harvesting `ℹ tests\|pass\|fail` from `node --test` · `program/PROJECT_STATE.md` and `program/SESSION_LOG.md` suite shorthands · CHARTER §17.1 · C-0.4 · D-008 (a measurement taken across a concurrently-changing tree) | **OPEN.** What is owed is a suite census that is **DERIVED from the workspace rather than from a runner's output format** — enumerate `packages/*/package.json` with a `test` script, require a result from **each**, and report a package whose result could not be parsed as **`NOT MEASURED`, never as absent**. **A sweep that silently omits what it cannot parse reports the packages it understands as the population**, which is a constant wearing a measurement's clothes. | **D-138** | **A RED CARRIED FOR MANY TURNS AS A MISSING TOOL IS A PATH CONDITION, AND THE FIGURE NEVER RECORDED THE SHELL THAT PRODUCED IT.** Measured 2026-08-06 taking ADR-0082 §6 step 2's baseline. `packages/platform-runtime` has been carried — across sessions, into `PROJECT_STATE.md`, into `NEXT_ACTION.md` and into the prompt opening this session — as **`0/59, spawnSync openssl ENOENT`**, classified a **CHARTER §13 external dependency (tool installation)** and therefore nobody's work. **Re-run against the same commit it is `58/58 PASS`, `fail 0`.** Nothing in the package changed and nothing was installed. **The shell differed:** `openssl` resolves at `/mingw64/bin/openssl` (OpenSSL 3.5.5) under Git Bash and **does not resolve from PowerShell** — both confirmed directly this session. The suite was previously run from PowerShell and the shell was never recorded beside the figure. **The test count also moved, 59 → 58, which nothing in the record explains** and which is a second unexplained variable in the same figure. | **THE MISCLASSIFICATION IS THE COST, NOT THE RED.** As a §13 external dependency it was a blocker with no owner and no action — the one category the CHARTER makes exempt from being worked. It is **a harness defect, it is fixable, and it was never outside the programme's control**, so a package the platform depends on has sat unmeasured-in-effect while looking accounted for. **The general form is worth more than the instance and is recorded as CHARTER §17.1.4: a measurement without its environment recorded is a measurement OF the environment.** The failure is one-directional — an unrecorded environment makes a working thing look broken, the red is attributed to the subject, and the true cause is never in the record to be doubted. **[`D-008`](TECHNICAL_DEBT.md) is the same rule one variable narrower**: it requires the commit-ish beside a measurement; this requires everything else that could have produced it. | Platform governance (the class) · Platform runtime (the instance) | **Do not repair this by installing anything — there is nothing missing.** (1) **Reclassify**: `platform-runtime` is not a §13 stop condition; strike it from the external-dependency list wherever it appears in programme state, since a §13 label suppresses the work. (2) **Record the environment**: extend D-008's evidence envelope from commit-ish to **shell, `PATH`-resolution of every external binary the suite shells out to, and the runner**, and refuse to publish a suite figure without them. (3) **Then** establish why the count moved 59 → 58 — that is a separate unexplained delta and closing (1) and (2) does not answer it. **Until (2) lands, every red in programme state carrying no environment is a hypothesis rather than a finding**, and this entry is the proof that the distinction is not academic. |
| **D-139** | **ADR-0082's Q3 IS PARTIALLY MET: THE RUN RECORD PROVES A HASH IS WELL-FORMED AND NEVER PROVES IT RESOLVES TO A PACKAGE — FORMAT-BINDING IS NOT REFERENTIAL BINDING.** Measured 2026-08-06 against `run-record-store.ts` as built. Q3 was ruled a **precondition, not a follow-up**: `packageHash` binding enforced at ingress, an unbindable reference **REFUSED and not stored**. What `onPackageAuthored` enforces is `HASH_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/` — **64 hex characters**. It does not ask the sealed package store whether anything is stored under that hash. **A well-formed hash that binds to nothing is storable today**, which is the exact condition Q3 was made a precondition to prevent. The store's own comment states the consequence correctly — *"an unattributable run can never leave the collection"* — so the requirement is understood at the site and only half-enforced by it. | **AN UNBINDABLE RUN IS PERMANENT.** Nothing removes a run from the outstanding set except evidence arriving and being attributed to it; a run whose hash resolves to no package can never be discharged, so `/work` would offer it **forever**. That is the fail-open port's mirror one layer down — **a permanently non-empty falsehood**, served as a `200`, reddening no gate and failing no test, because format-validation and referential-validation **agree on every well-formed input and differ only on the defect**. The same shape the store already guards against elsewhere by ADDRESSING rather than by predicate. | Platform providers (the store) · ADR-0082 (the ruling) | **This is ADR-0082 §6 step 3's FIRST item, not a follow-up to it** — step 3 introduces the evidence event, and an evidence reference that cannot be joined to a real package derives nothing, which is the same defect arriving by the other door. The store must be given the ability to ask whether a package exists at a hash **within the caller's own partition**, and refuse the write when it does not. **Refuse, never warn and never store-then-reconcile:** a reconciliation pass would have to decide what to do with records already written, which is a second source of truth about what is outstanding. **And the test must prove the negative** — a well-formed hash binding to nothing is REFUSED — because a test over a hash that happens to exist passes under both implementations and would certify the gap closed while it is open. **— CORRECTED 2026-08-06 ON MEASUREMENT, AND THE CORRECTION MOVES THE SUBJECT RATHER THAN THE FINDING. THIS ENTRY NAMED THE WRONG MODULE.** It was filed against the run record store: that `onPackageAuthored` proves 64 hex and never proves resolution. **ADR-0082 P-82.5's subject is the EVIDENCE INGRESS ROUTE** — *"`POST /v1/evidence` … refuses a reference that cannot be bound to a known run"* — and §6 lists it as **step 1, to land BEFORE either store**. **THE STORE-SIDE VERSION COULD NOT HAVE BEEN BUILT AS WRITTEN.** `sealed-package-writer.ts` records the run **before** it publishes the package, deliberately, so a partial write fails toward the discoverable failure (P-81.1's reasoning). An existence check inside `onPackageAuthored` would therefore refuse **every authoring**, and "fixing" it means inverting an ordering chosen on stated grounds — trading a loud failure for a silent, permanent one. **At authoring the hash is not a reference to validate: it is DERIVED from the package being sealed in the same act, which is a stronger binding than a lookup** — a lookup can pass while the hash came from somewhere else. **AND THE REAL FINDING IS LARGER THAN THE ONE FILED: §6 STEP 1 WAS NEVER BUILT WHILE STEP 2 WAS BUILT ON TOP OF IT.** `evidence-ingress.ts` recorded its own deferral in its header — *"it cannot yet enforce that it names a package THIS PLANE AUTHORED, because no run record exists to resolve against — that is ADR-0082 §6 step 2"* — and **the deferral expired SILENTLY when step 2 landed.** A correctly-written, correctly-cited deferral becomes a stale disclaimer the moment its precondition is met, and nothing watches for that: the ADR even warned *"built after the stores, this is two stores and no derivation."* **Closed by `2eeafb9`**, which lands P-82.5 at the route with the refusal indistinguishable from an unowned hash (P-70.4). **The transferable rule: a deferral that names its own precondition SHALL be re-read when that precondition is met, or it becomes an understatement of what the code does.** |
| **D-140** | **A GATE THAT READS COMMENTS MEASURES WHAT A FILE SAYS, NOT WHAT IT DOES — AND THE FIRST ONE TO PROVE IT WENT RED ON ITS OWN SUBJECT'S EXPLANATION OF THE RULE IT ENFORCES.** Measured 2026-08-06 building `verify-run-record-write-surface.js` (ADR-0082 P-82.9). The gate forbids an event-discriminator field on the write path. Its first run reported **FAIL — a `kind: 'fetch'` field is a third event wearing a field's clothes**. There is no such field. `run-record-store.ts` explains **in prose**, at the site, why such a field would be the defect, and the scan matched the explanation. | **IT FAILS IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, AND THE SECOND IS THE DANGEROUS ONE.** A warning about a forbidden construct reads as the construct — a false RED, which at least announces itself. But a construct **discussed** in a comment can equally mask a real one: a gate that greps for a forbidden string finds it in the documentation and cannot tell whether the code also contains it. **In this repository the exposure is unusually large**: the reasoning is written at the site by standing convention and is routinely longer than the code it describes, so for most files the majority of the scanned bytes are prose. Every existing `verify-*.js` that reads source with a bare `readFileSync` is in scope; `verify-data-sovereignty-store.js` already stripped comments and says why, so the technique was known and not generalised. | Platform governance | **Strip comments before scanning, and never re-derive the stripper.** Both gates now share the same two-substitution form (`/\*…\*/` and `//…`). (1) **Sweep `governance/verification/` for gates that scan source text without stripping** and classify each: prose-vulnerable, or matching on a pattern prose cannot produce. (2) **Hoist ONE `code()` helper** to a shared module rather than a third copy — two copies is where a divergence starts. (3) **A gate's own fault proof should include a comment mentioning the forbidden construct**, so the stripping is proved rather than assumed: without it, a gate that stopped stripping would stay green. |
| **D-141** | **WITH ONE SUBJECT, "ANY MODULE THAT CALLS `purgeExpired`" IDENTIFIES THE PURGE DRIVER. WITH TWO, EITHER DRIVER SATISFIES BOTH SUBJECTS — AND A STORE WHOSE OWN DRIVER WAS DELETED BORROWS ITS NEIGHBOUR'S AND STAYS GREEN.** Measured 2026-08-06 extending `verify-data-sovereignty-store.js` to ADR-0082 P-82.8's second enumerated subject. The gate discovered its driver by scanning `src/storage/` for `.purgeExpired(` — correct and unambiguous while the sealed package store was the only subject. With the run record store added, the sweep returns **both** drivers for **both** subjects: each finds a scheduled, alerting driver started by a factory, and neither notices it is the other store's. | **THE PROPERTY SILENTLY WEAKENS FROM "THIS STORE'S RETENTION IS ENFORCED" TO "SOME STORE'S RETENTION IS ENFORCED"**, and it weakens at the moment a second subject is added — which is the moment the gate looks like it got stronger. Deleting `run-record-purge.ts` would leave the run record store with no driver, its C3 records never purged, and the gate **GREEN**, because `sealed-package-purge.ts` still answers the query. **This is the sibling-gate failure arriving by another door**: not two gates disagreeing about scope, but one gate whose per-subject question has stopped being per-subject. It generalises to every discovered-rather-than-declared input in a multi-subject control. | Platform governance | **Declared, per subject, plus a check that the two lists agree — and the second half is what closes it.** Each subject now names its own `driver`, so a missing driver goes RED for that subject alone. That alone is not sufficient: an unenumerated store would carry an ADR and a retention, satisfy the plane-wide sweep, and be measured by no subject at all. The final check compares the sweep against the enumeration — **every persisting module is an enumerated subject or one's declared driver** — and is fault-proved by a module that declares correctly and is not enumerated. **The transferable rule: in a multi-subject control, an input DISCOVERED by pattern is shared by every subject; only a DECLARED input is one subject's.** |
| **D-142** | **ONE EVIDENCE RECORD PER RUN: A RUN PRODUCING SEVERAL DISTINCT REFERENCES HAS ALL BUT THE FIRST DROPPED, SILENTLY — AND NOTHING DOWNSTREAM CAN TELL A ONE-REFERENCE RUN FROM A TRUNCATED ONE.** Raised 2026-08-06 implementing ADR-0082 §6 step 3. `onEvidenceArrived` is idempotent on `runId` and **first write wins**: a second arrival returns the stored record unchanged. That is correct for a RETRY — an Execution Plane whose `202` was lost will resend, and refusing would make a successful submission look unsent — **and it is indistinguishable, at the store, from a genuinely different second reference.** The response reports `alreadyRecorded: true` in both cases. | **THE GAP SURFACES ON THE FIRST REALISTIC RUN, NOT ON AN EDGE CASE.** A functional-testing run produces a **screenshot AND a trace AND a video** — which is what the Execution Plane already emits — so the *normal* shape of a completed run is several distinct evidence references, and this store keeps one. **The loss is silent in both directions:** the EP is told `202 accepted` for every reference it sends, and a reader of the record sees a well-formed evidence record with no field that could indicate anything was dropped. **It does NOT break the subtraction** — `/work` needs only *has any evidence*, so the run still leaves the collection correctly — which is precisely why nothing goes red: the property the platform measures is satisfied by the first reference, and the ones after it are lost against a green suite. **Stages 10-12 are the consumer that will find it**, holding one artefact where three were captured, with no way to know a fourth was never offered. | ADR-0082 (the ruling) · Platform providers (the store) | **OWED AS A DECISION, NOT A REPAIR, AND DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN INSIDE AN IMPLEMENTATION STEP.** P-82.6 enumerates *"the evidence reference handle"* — **singular** — and the enumeration IS the ruling. Multiplicity is genuinely undecided by ADR-0082: it is not obvious that a run should accept unbounded references (an accreting record is P-82.4's unauthorised C1 store one shape along), nor that it should accept exactly one. **Settling it here would decide an open question in a diff reviewed as plumbing** — the failure CHARTER §17.1.2 records at a different subject. **What the ADR amendment must answer, and none is a follow-up to the others:** (i) is the cardinality per run bounded, and by what? (ii) does a second DIFFERENT reference REFUSE (a caller defect) or ACCUMULATE (a normal run)? — these are opposite answers and the store cannot infer which; (iii) if it accumulates, what is the retention unit — the run, or each reference? **Until it is ruled, the limitation is recorded at the write site in source** so a reader meets it where the dropping happens, not only here. |

| **D-146** | **"WHICH TENANCIES HOLD THE CORRECTED RECEIVER?" HAS NO ANSWER, THE ACK CANNOT BECOME ONE, AND OPTION B DOES NOT CLOSE IT.** Recorded 2026-08-06 with [`D-145`](D-145_UPDATE_RECEIVER_DELIVERY_DEADLOCK_REPORT.md)'s ruling, **deliberately as its own entry**, because it is the one question that survives the decision that was just taken. **This plane cannot read what a tenancy HOLDS — only what it was SENT.** That asymmetry is load-bearing and correct: it is the same asymmetry `work-path-distribution.ts` is built on, and it is why `undistributed()` answers *"was this tenancy ever sent a path?"* and can never answer *"does it currently hold one?"*. | **THE ACK IS DISQUALIFIED, AND UNDER OPTION B THERE IS NO ACK AT ALL.** Even under A the ack would prove only that **`.update-available.json` was written** — never that an install happened, because `applySolutionUpdate` does not install. **Under B the update channel is not used, so this plane's record contains nothing whatsoever about who received the corrected package.** The consequence is precise: for every existing tenancy, *"does this EP still silently consume events it refuses?"* is **unanswerable from this plane**, and the population that does is the population that cannot tell you so — **the defect suppresses its own signal.** It is [`D-143`](TECHNICAL_DEBT.md)'s shape (a claim that cannot be falsified against the register) with a live safety property behind it rather than a numbering scheme. | Cross-plane contract · ADR-0035 · P-70.3 (why delivery state was removed) · D-145 (the ruling that leaves this open) | **OPEN, AND EXPLICITLY NOT CLOSED BY THE D-145 RULING — RECORDED SO THE RULING IS NOT MISREAD AS SETTLING IT.** The honest candidates all require the EP to SAY, because only it knows: the receiver **declaring its own version** on a channel this plane already receives, or the **integrity-report shape P-78.5 already established** (EP-initiated, diagnostic, authoritative about exactly one thing — that this EP said so) carrying a receiver version. **Both are cross-plane contract changes and neither is authorised here.** **WHAT MUST NOT HAPPEN IS THE CHEAP ANSWER:** this plane recording *"B was performed for tenancy X"* in its own register. That is a second record of a fact written by the party that never checked — **P-78.4's defect, and the exact defect OBL-006 just removed from the ack** — reintroduced one layer out, as the spreadsheet D-145 already names as B's cost. **A register of what we SENT is legitimate; a register of what they HOLD is a fabrication.** |

| **D-147** | **`publishWorkPaths` HAS NO CALLER OUTSIDE ITS OWN TESTS, SO THE ROTATION CARRIER CANNOT BE RUN AGAINST THE DEPLOYED STORE AT ALL.** Measured 2026-08-06 while attempting exactly that. A census across every `.ts`/`.js`/`.mjs` outside `node_modules` and `dist` returns: **the definition, the barrel re-export, and eleven call sites in `work-path-distribution.test.ts`. Zero production callers.** There is **no route, no controller, no CLI, no scheduled job** that invokes it. The engine can distribute a work path; **the deployed system has no way to ask it to.** | **THIS IS D-122'S SHAPE, RECURRING, AND THE PROGRAMME ALREADY WROTE THE LESSON DOWN.** D-122 was *a store with a writer that had zero non-test callers and looked wired for as long as nobody asked*; the P-82.9 gate was built specifically to census permitted callers **in both directions** — no unpermitted module writes, **and every permitted module still does.** That gate governs the run-record store and **does not cover this module**, so the identical defect reappeared in a sibling under a gate written for its neighbour. **The measured consequence is immediate and blocks a requested action:** the re-emit of `upd-1` against `join(config.state.dir, 'tenants')` **cannot be performed** — not because of credentials or environment, but because **nothing in the deployed image calls the function.** The sweep that "succeeded" earlier succeeded against the repo's `.gitignore`d `./tenants/` **because a script I wrote called the library directly**, which is not a capability the platform has. | `work-path-distribution.ts` (the definition) · `engine/index.ts:134` (the barrel) · `work-path-distribution.test.ts` (all eleven calls) · D-122 · ADR-0082 P-82.9 (the gate that would have caught it, on a different subject) | **CLOSED 2026-08-06 — `POST /api/work-paths` (sweep) and `GET /api/work-paths` (asks, writes nothing), platform-admin, mounted unconditionally by `WorkPathController`.** The route is SLUG-LESS because the subject of a sweep is the POPULATION: a per-slug route would inherit `authoriseTenantRequest` for free and answer a different question, leaving *"is any tenancy stranded?"* as unanswerable as before — and the stranded tenancy is why the module exists. **The cost of that shape is stated rather than skipped:** the authorisation is COMPOSED here from `authz.ts`'s own predicates (`can(tenant:configure)` + `isGlobalPrincipal`, called not restated), which P-80.2 permits only because this route carries no slug and therefore reaches none of the shared path — the position `/api/packages/{hash}` and `/v1/evidence` are already in. **`isGlobalPrincipal` is not redundant:** `tenant:configure` is held by tenant-admin, whom `mayAccessTenant` confines by slug, and THERE IS NO SLUG HERE TO CONFINE IT WITH. **The trigger is ON DEMAND, never scheduled** — the module separated the two so the trigger would stay a decision, and a schedule puts an unattended writer in the deployment. **The census went GREEN ON ITS OWN SUBJECT**, which is the proof rather than the claim; its reachability property is KEPT, not retired with the instance that motivated it (CHARTER §17.1.1). 9 new tests. *(the entry as raised, unedited)* **OPEN — AND IT IS A BUILD, NOT A RULING, BUT IT IS NOT THIS SESSION'S BUILD.** What is owed is a caller with an owner: **an operator-invoked route or an adoption-time hook**, plus the decision of **when a sweep runs** — at tenancy registration, on demand, or on a schedule — which is an operational question the module deliberately left open by separating `undistributed()` (asks, writes nothing) from `publishWorkPaths()` (writes). **THE GATE LESSON IS THE LARGER HALF:** P-82.9's caller census proved its worth and was scoped to one store. **Any module whose whole purpose is to WRITE on behalf of an operator needs the same both-directions census**, or the next one is found the same way — by someone on the far side noticing nothing ever arrived. |

| **D-148** | **THE TWO DEPLOYMENT DESCRIPTORS DISAGREE ABOUT WHERE READINESS POINTS, AND THE ONE THAT IS WRONG RE-CREATES THE EXACT CONDITION `HealthController` WAS WRITTEN TO REMOVE.** Recorded 2026-08-06, found while adding the `package-signing-key` reference to both files ([`18f8255`](DEPLOY_READINESS.md)) — **it is not that change's defect and was deliberately not repaired inside it.** `deploy/iac/main.bicep:110` points Readiness at **`/api/ready`**; `deploy/azure/containerapp.yaml:74` points it at **`/api/health`**. **The split is not cosmetic — the two endpoints were separated on purpose.** `health()` is O(1) and *touches nothing*, because a dependency failure must not kill the pod; `ready()` calls `repo.probe()` and returns **503**, because the readiness probe decides on the HTTP status ([`health.controller.ts:27-58`](../packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/health.controller.ts#L27-L58)). That separation closed **KNOWN_LIMITATIONS L-3**. | **A CONTAINER WITH A DETACHED STATE VOLUME STAYS IN ROTATION SERVING 500s** — the precise failure the controller's own header comment names, on the Azure Files (SMB) mount this deployment specifies, where volume loss is the realistic fault. **It is invisible while both descriptors are not applied together, and it is decided by WHICHEVER IS APPLIED LAST**, which is an ordering accident rather than a decision: `containerapp.yaml` applied after `main.bicep` silently reverts readiness to liveness and **nothing reports a change**, because both probes return `200` from a healthy process and the regression only appears during the fault. **THE CLASS IS THE REGISTER'S OWN "CONFORMANCE BY ACCIDENT" (§3)**: the deployment is correct exactly as long as nobody applies the other file. | `deploy/iac/main.bicep:109-111` · `deploy/azure/containerapp.yaml:67-78` · `packages/tenant-onboarding-engine/src/server/health.controller.ts:50-58` (`ready()`, the 503) · `DEPLOY_READINESS.md` §6 (flagged there; **this entry is the register record it needed**) · KNOWN_LIMITATIONS L-3 | **`containerapp.yaml` IS THE ONE THAT IS WRONG — `main.bicep` ALREADY ENCODES THE INTENT.** Point its Readiness probe at `/api/ready` and leave Liveness and Startup on `/api/health`; that is a three-line change to one file, and it makes the descriptors agree rather than making the deployment depend on apply order. **THE LARGER HALF IS THAT NOTHING COMPARES THEM.** Two descriptors describe one deployment, both are in this repository, and **no gate reads one against the other** — so probe paths, env mappings and secret references can diverge silently, and this one did. A check that parses both and fails on any disagreement in probe path, secret name or env var is the mechanism; **without it the next divergence is found the same way — by a fault that the probe was supposed to catch.** *(Owner: platform. Resolving milestone: the deployment milestone that applies either descriptor — it must not ship split.)* |

| **D-150** | **TWO ADAPTER SPI READS NOW HAVE NO CALLER, AND THE ONLY WAYS TO CLOSE IT ARE BOTH ARCHITECTURAL DECISIONS.** `TestManagementAdapter.discoverContainer` and `.discoverGrouping` are ADR-0085's reuse-or-create reads — the pair whose ABSENCE ADR-0085 records as having made the reuse-or-create decision *unrepresentable*, so that a create was unconditional and N runs of one story produced N containers. They were added to make the tenant's disposition **actionable rather than merely reportable**, and their only *caller* was the Functional Testing capability, removed under ADR-0087. They are therefore declared, implemented as journal recorders by capabilities 2 and 3, and invoked by nothing. **This is not dead weight that can simply be swept:** deleting them un-implements an accepted ADR-0085 mechanism and returns the platform to the state that ADR exists to prevent, while keeping them leaves `verify-devchange-conformance` and `verify-discovery-conformance` RED on 'every declared adapter SPI method is invoked by at least one capability'. **The gate is right and should not be weakened** — it is the control that caught this. The real question is which surviving capability, if any, owns the reuse-or-create decision now that capability 1 does not exist; that is an owner's decision, and it is recorded here rather than answered by whoever next finds the gate red. | `verify-devchange-conformance.js` / `verify-discovery-conformance.js`, on the first run after the ADR-0087 removal | **OPEN** |
| **D-149** | **THE WORKING TREE IS PERMANENTLY DIRTY FROM REGENERATOR OUTPUT, AND IN `git status` A TIMESTAMP-ONLY DIFF IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A CHANGED VERDICT.** Measured 2026-08-06 classifying **every** dirty path before a push, rather than carrying an earlier session's classification forward. All 24 are regenerator output and **not one carries a changed finding**: 20 files under `governance/platform-certification/reports/` differ **only** in their `_Generated <ts>_` stamp (`13:18:53.389Z` → `16:33:43.722Z`); `history.jsonl` gained **15 appended records whose verdicts are byte-identical** — `NOT CERTIFIED · 50% · 0/6`, same six capability states, only `at` differs; `docs/customer-success-package/{MANIFEST.json,README.md}` re-stamped `commit` and `onboardingDurationMs` with the content hash unchanged; `docs/production/PERFORMANCE-BENCHMARK-REPORT.md` re-measured the same 9 benchmarks at the same sample counts. `15-drift-report.md` reports **2 drifts · high-severity present** on both sides. **The diagnosis: another session was running the certification suite repeatedly, and changed nothing that suite measures.** | **A PERMANENTLY DIRTY TREE IS A DISABLED INSTRUMENT, AND IT IS THE CHEAPEST ONE THE PROGRAMME HAS.** `git status` is what answers *what have I changed* — and when its answer is 24 lines of churn on **every** read, a load-bearing file in that list costs nothing to miss. **That is the condition under which the two deployment descriptors went unread this morning:** `containerapp.yaml` and `main.bicep` both lacked the `package-signing-key` reference, the absence was in this repository the whole time, and it was diagnosed as *"blocked on an Azure operation"* until [`18f8255`](../deploy/azure/DEPLOY_READINESS.md). **The churn does not hide a file by covering it — it hides a file by training the reader to skip the list**, which is worse, because the reader is not aware of having stopped looking. **THIS IS D-127'S CLASS INVERTED.** There, generated evidence was correctly `.gitignore`d and a register cited it anyway; here, generated evidence is **tracked** and its noise floor is high enough to mask a real change. **Both are the same unanswered question — which artefacts are measurements and which are regenerations — and neither file says which it is.** | `governance/platform-certification/reports/*.md` (20 files, the `_Generated_` stamp) · `governance/platform-certification/history.jsonl` (append-per-run, verdict-invariant) · `docs/customer-success-package/{MANIFEST.json,README.md}` · `docs/production/PERFORMANCE-BENCHMARK-REPORT.md` · D-127 (the same question, opposite tracking decision) · D-144 (what the skipped list cost) | **OPEN — AND THE 24 ARE DELIBERATELY LEFT UNCOMMITTED, WHICH IS A DECISION AND NOT AN OMISSION.** Committing them writes timestamp churn into a governance history whose entire value is that **a change in it means something**; the next reader diffing `history.jsonl` to find when a verdict moved would page through 15 records that moved nothing. **AND THE OBVIOUS REPAIR — `.gitignore` the reports — IS ALSO WRONG UNEXAMINED**, because it silently makes a certification verdict unreviewable, which is what D-127 already costs on its own subject. **What is owed is that a regenerator whose inputs did not change SHALL NOT produce a changed artefact.** Three mechanisms, and the first is sufficient alone: (i) the run stamp moves **out of** the versioned report and into the run record, so a report diff is a verdict diff by construction; (ii) `history.jsonl` appends **only on a verdict change**, since a run that changes nothing is already recorded by the report it did not alter; (iii) benchmark figures carry a tolerance, and a re-measurement inside it does not rewrite the file. **Until one lands, `git status` in this repository is not a usable check and should not be relied on as one** — which is the honest statement of the current condition, rather than tolerating it a second time without naming it. |

| **D-144** | **EVERY "IS DONE" IN THIS PROGRAMME'S STATE FOR THE LAST 31 COMMITS IS TRUE OF THE COMMIT AND FALSE OF THE DEPLOYMENT, AND NOTHING ON EITHER SIDE SAYS SO.** Measured 2026-08-06 **only because the Execution Plane probed the live instance and disagreed with this plane's record.** `origin/main` is at **`42d30a3`**; local `HEAD` is **`c4b6874`**; `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...HEAD` = **`0 31`** — **31 commits ahead, 0 behind, nothing pushed.** `azure-pipelines/deploy-api.yml` triggers on **`main`**, so the deployed instance builds `42d30a3`. **`origin/main`'s `api.ts` contains no `/work` mount** (`isWorkPath`, `handleWorkRequest`: absent) and **does contain `package-retrieval.ts`** — which is exactly the EP's measurement: `/api/tenants/carlisle-homes/work` byte-identical to a deliberate absentee, `/api/packages/{hash}` answering in its own vocabulary. **Also undeployed: `POST /v1/evidence` (`2eeafb9`), the subtraction (`8d71755`), the work path in the grant (`cc7b063`), and the OBL-006 receiver repair (`c4b6874`).** | **THE TWO CANDIDATE EXPLANATIONS THE EP OFFERED ARE BOTH EXCLUDED BY MEASUREMENT, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS AN ENTRY AND NOT A SHRUG.** It is **not a different composition root** — both roots that mount the route (`server/tenant.controller.ts` and `api.ts`'s embedded server) carry it locally and neither exists at `42d30a3`. It is **not a mount landing somewhere unserved** — the pipeline's branch and the branch's content account for the observation completely. **The deployment is simply behind, and "behind" was invisible from inside this plane because every instrument this programme runs — build, suite, governance — reads the WORKING TREE.** `PROJECT_STATE.md` headlined ***"`/work` IS MOUNTED AND SUBTRACTS"***, `NEXT_ACTION.md` recorded ADR-0080 §6 **DONE**, and both were true of a commit that no deployed instance had ever seen. **CHARTER §3 step 3 says state files are claims and the filesystem and git history are facts — and this is the case that rule does not cover: git history WAS the fact, agreed with the claim, and both were about something other than what serves traffic.** A green suite over an unpushed commit is a measurement of a private artefact. | Deployment / this programme's state discipline | **OPEN — AND THE REPAIR IS AN INSTRUMENT, NOT A PUSH.** Pushing clears this instance and leaves the class untouched. **What is owed is that "done" cannot be written without a deployed subject:** (i) programme state SHALL distinguish **committed** from **deployed**, and a milestone claiming a live capability names the commit the deployment serves; (ii) a check comparing `origin/main` to `HEAD` — **a divergence is not automatically a fault** (work in progress is normal) **but an unqualified "IS MOUNTED" over a divergence is**; and (iii) **the deployed instance SHALL report the commit it was built from**, so this plane can measure the gap instead of inferring it from a branch name. **THE HONEST NOTE: this was found by the OTHER PLANE, from outside, using a probe with a negative control — which is not a mechanism this plane possesses**, and is the same shape as [`D-127`](#) (found only because a human read a diff). **NOT SILENTLY REPAIRED IN THE SESSION THAT FOUND IT:** whether the 31 commits deploy, in what order, and against which environment, is an operational decision with an owner. |

| **D-145** | **THE CORRECTED UPDATE RECEIVER CAN ONLY BE ANNOUNCED THROUGH THE RECEIVER IT CORRECTS, AND EVERY EXISTING TENANT HOLDS THE BROKEN ONE.** Raised 2026-08-06 by the Execution Plane, immediately after the OBL-006 repair. `EP_UPDATE_AGENT` is a **generator constant**, so the broken receiver is in **every package this plane has ever produced**. An ADR-0035 `solution-update` arrives on the queue the defect governs: if it is **malformed** — missing `signature` or `contentHash` — the deployed receiver refuses it, **acks the refusal**, and the announcement is consumed, reported applied, and gone from both planes with no retry, because acking is what removes it from `pending`. | **ONE CLAUSE NARROWS BY MEASUREMENT AND IT CHANGES THE OPTIONS, SO IT IS RECORDED HERE AND NOT BURIED IN ONE OF THEM: `applySolutionUpdate` NEVER INSTALLS. It writes `.update-available.json` and two log lines**, and the marker's own `install` field states the operator-approved plan. **So the broken receiver can consume the ANNOUNCEMENT, never the artefact** — and what is silently lost is the operator's only notification that a fix exists. **The exposure is also CONDITIONAL, which bounds it:** a well-formed signed update is handled correctly by the broken receiver; only a malformed one is swallowed. **AND OPTION C IS CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT LEFT OPEN:** the deployed receiver writes to exactly **four literal, non-executable path constants** — `config/{application,integrations,capabilities}.json` and `.update-available.json` — **none under `bin/`, none derived from event data**, and the one extension point (a new event type) is the catch-all that **silently acks**. There is no shape of event that makes the broken receiver install code. | `program/D-145_UPDATE_RECEIVER_DELIVERY_DEADLOCK_REPORT.md` (options and costs) · ADR-0035 · ADR-0007 / INV-3 · AD-016 (signing custody, open) · D-144 (nothing is deployed yet) | **STOPS FOR A RULING — REPORTED, NOT CHOSEN, AND THE REPORT DELIBERATELY MAKES NO RECOMMENDATION.** Three options, each with a cost the others do not have: **(A)** a signed `solution-update` — **blocked today**, `api.ts:433` returns **`501 package signing is not configured`** and custody is **AD-016, open** — accepting that a malformed announcement is lost un-retryably; **(B)** out-of-band delivery, whose authorisation is stronger than it first appears (**ADR-0035's install leg is ALREADY out of band by construction**) but which is per-tenancy, manual, and **leaves no record of who received it** — P-70.3's delivery state reintroduced as a spreadsheet; **(C)** measured impossible. **TRUE UNDER EVERY OPTION, AND THE REAL QUESTION:** the ack proves a marker was written, never that an install happened, so **"which tenancies hold the corrected receiver?" needs an answer that is not the ack** — and this plane cannot read what a tenancy holds, only what it was sent. **THE ORDER IS FORCED BY D-144:** nothing can be announced to a deployed tenant until the deployment serves the code. |

| **D-143** | **THE EXECUTION PLANE'S OBLIGATION REGISTER NUMBERS TWO DIFFERENT OBLIGATIONS `OBL-003` AND TWO DIFFERENT OBLIGATIONS `OBL-004`, AND THE AMENDMENT REQUEST CITES THAT RANGE AS THOUGH IT WERE UNAMBIGUOUS.** Observed 2026-08-06 while implementing **OBL-006**. The EP's `IP-OBLIGATIONS.md` carries **two `OBL-003` rows and two `OBL-004` rows, each pair naming a DIFFERENT obligation**, and `IP-AMENDMENT-REQUEST.md` consolidates *"OBL-003, OBL-004 and OBL-005"* into one request. **The citation therefore names four candidate obligations at two identifiers**, and nothing in either document says which pair the request means. | **AN IDENTIFIER THAT RESOLVES TO TWO THINGS CANNOT CARRY AN ACCEPTANCE.** The obligation register is the cross-plane record of what this plane owes the other;
