**its identifiers are the only handle either side has on a specific obligation.** The consequence is not confusion at read time — it is that **"OBL-004 is discharged" is unfalsifiable**: this plane can implement one of the two, report it closed, and the EP's register will show a closed obligation and an open one under the same number with no contradiction anywhere. **The same shape as the ack this session repaired**, one register out: a record whose value cannot be checked against the fact it claims. **NOT LOAD-BEARING FOR OBL-006**, which is singular and unambiguous, so nothing implemented this session depends on the resolution. | **THE EXECUTION PLANE — ITS REGISTER, ITS CALL.** Recorded here so the observation is not lost; **deliberately NOT renumbered from this plane.** | **FLAGGED BACK, NOT FIXED, AND THE RESTRAINT IS THE POINT.** Renumbering another plane's register from here would (i) author a change into a document this plane does not own — Doc 19's boundary — and (ii) silently invalidate every citation the EP holds against the old numbers, including the amendment request itself. **What is owed FROM this plane is the observation and nothing else.** What is owed BY the EP is a decision it alone can make: which pair keeps the number, and whether `IP-AMENDMENT-REQUEST.md`'s *"OBL-003, OBL-004 and OBL-005"* is restated against the disambiguated identifiers. **Until then this plane cites obligations by their TEXT as well as their number** when reporting discharge, so a report cannot be misread as closing the other member of a pair. |

| **D-127** | **EVERY EVIDENCE ARTEFACT BACKING A CERTIFIED VERDICT IN THE FINAL CERTIFICATION REGISTER IS UNTRACKED, THE REGISTER CITES EACH BY CONTENT HASH, AND ONE OF THOSE HASHES CHANGED WITH NO COMMIT AND NO GATE NOTICING.** Found 2026-08-06 **by reviewing the ADR-0081 re-baseline diff**, which is the only reason it was seen. `FINAL_CERTIFICATION_REGISTER.md` cites **seven** evidence artefacts and binds each verdict to a content hash. **All seven are `.gitignore`d** — `governance/{operational,customer-success,production,deployment,supply-chain,traceability}/evidence.json` and `packages/contracts/compat/evidence.json` (`.gitignore:146`). The re-baseline moved **M2.2 Consumer Compatibility**'s cited hash from `c759dcf5a96a91d1…` to `7808ddd567e5cf9a…` — **and `verify-programme-closure` was PASS on both sides**, because nothing compares the register's cited hash to the artefact it names. **The verdict itself is unchanged and correct** (`verificationStatus: verified`, `certificationStatus: certified`, 7 fixtures, v1.0.0), so this instance is benign — **which is the finding, not the mitigation: a benign change and a substantive one are indistinguishable here, and neither produces output.** | **GITIGNORING GENERATED EVIDENCE IS CORRECT AND IS NOT THE DEFECT.** R-14.2 requires proofs to be **regenerated, never copied**, and committing a generated artefact is how a stale proof survives as a fresh-looking one. **The defect is that a REGISTER ASSERTS `CERTIFIED` AND OFFERS A CONTENT HASH AS THE PROOF, WHEN THE ARTEFACT THAT HASH BINDS TO EXISTS IN NO CLONE.** A hash is a binding to a specific artefact; a binding to an artefact nobody else can obtain is a **citation shaped like evidence**. **R-14.4 is the rule it fails** — *"every piece of evidence carries immutable provenance binding it to its origin"* — and here the binding is published while the origin is local, single-replica and regenerated in place. **AND R-14.3 CANNOT BE APPLIED AT ALL:** *"a proof that fails to replay invalidates the confidence it contributed"* presupposes you can tell a failed replay from a regeneration, and with the prior artefact overwritten and untracked **you cannot.** **THIS IS D-107'S CLASS WITH A CERTIFICATION CONSEQUENCE RATHER THAN A DOCUMENTATION ONE** — a figure in a document that is a claim about another artefact, which nothing compares — and **D-114'S CLASS ON A SECOND SUBJECT**: untracked, unversioned, single-replica state that a register treats as authoritative. D-114 was found only after a record was destroyed; **this one was found only because a human read a re-baseline diff, which is not a mechanism.** | `program/FINAL_CERTIFICATION_REGISTER.md` (seven cited artefacts, each bound by content hash) · `.gitignore:146` (`packages/contracts/compat/evidence.json`) · `packages/contracts/compat/evidence.json` (self-recorded `contentHash 7808ddd5…`, `commit 42d30a3`, `timestamp 2026-08-06T04:37:09Z`) · `governance/closure/emit-closure-package.mjs` (recomputes the register at emit time, comparing nothing) · `governance/verification/verify-programme-closure.js` (PASS on both sides of the change) · R-14.2, R-14.3, R-14.4, R-13.2 · D-107 · D-114 | **OPEN — RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, AND THE OBVIOUS REPAIR IS THE WRONG ONE.** **Committing the seven evidence files would satisfy the hash and BREAK R-14.2**, turning regenerated proofs into copied ones — the failure the `.gitignore` entry exists to prevent. **What is actually owed is a property, not a file:** the register's cited hash SHALL be **recomputed from the artefact at emit time and compared against the artefact's own self-recorded `contentHash`**, with disagreement — or absence of the artefact — an **ERROR** rather than a silently refreshed value. That is D-107's prescribed repair (*one accessor, whose unparseable/absent case fails the emit*) applied to evidence instead of to ADR status, and it makes the citation mean *"this was measured here, from this artefact, and the emitter checked"* rather than *"a hash was typed."* **A second property is owed beside it and is cheaper:** the register states, per artefact, whether it is **version-controlled or regenerated**, so a reader can tell a reproducible citation from a local one **without reading `.gitignore`.** **NOT FIXED IN THE CHANGE THAT FOUND IT:** it touches the closure emitter, so it lands with a fault proof under R-13.4 / R-13.7 and CHARTER §18, and folding it into an ADR acceptance is the scope error D-087 counts. |

| **D-076** | **ADR-0061 §6 CONTRADICTS ITSELF ABOUT ONE FILE, AND THE CONTRADICTION IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE BLOCKAGE.** Ruled 2026-08-05 and separated from D-075, which recorded it as a reason to stop. Four clauses of an **ACCEPTED** ADR, read together: §6 **step 4** names `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness` among five gates to **MIGRATE**, and step 4 **precedes** step 6; §6 **step 7** puts *"routing the live gateway"* **out of scope** — *"repository canonicalization does not perform it"*; §6 **step 6** puts *"re-point `authoring-bridge.mjs`"* **in scope**; and **ADR-0049 §2 states the live path in its own words as `ip-execute-gateway.mjs → authoring-bridge.mjs`**, so for that one file steps 6 and 7 are **the same act**. The acceptance banner adds a fourth constraint the steps do not carry: it does **NOT** authorise deleting legacy before behavioural equivalence is verified. **One step includes what another excludes, and nothing in the ADR compares them.** | **D-035's SHAPE INSIDE AN ACCEPTED RETIREMENT PLAN: a decision whose two halves were written against different pictures of one file, with nothing comparing them.** Step 7 treats `authoring-bridge.mjs` as an operational artefact and step 6 treats it as a repository one; **it is both, and it is the gateway's only authoring path**, so the separation the ADR relies on does not exist for it. The consequence is not academic — it is that G, executed as §6 orders it, performs the operational cut-over §6 says it does not perform, **with no ADR authorising it and the control that would detect it scheduled for deletion by the same step**. **A second, ORDERING half of the same defect:** §6 step 6 reads *retire and delete … re-point `authoring-bridge.mjs`*, but the bridge imports all three modules the deletion removes, so the stated order is unexecutable; the executable order is **re-point first, because the re-point is what proves the delete is safe** — replace-before-remove, applied to the step written as remove-then-re-point. §6 states neither the coupling nor the order. | Programme governance · [ADR-0061](../docs/adr/ADR-0061-canonical-functional-capability-runtime-adoption.md) §6 steps 4/6/7 + acceptance banner · [ADR-0049](../docs/adr/ADR-0049-canonical-runtime-cutover.md) §2 | **OPEN — REPAIRED BY AMENDMENT, NOT BY SUPERSESSION, AND THE AMENDMENT IS RULING R1 OF [`ADR_0049_SUPERSESSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](ADR_0049_SUPERSESSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md).** ADR-0061 §4's Decision and its constitutional conditions 1–3 are **untouched and must stay untouched**; what is wrong is one scope boundary in the migration strategy. The amendment owes three sentences §6 does not have: that steps 6 and 7 are one act for `authoring-bridge.mjs`; that step 6 covers **both** bridge exports (`authorViaFTE` **and** `reflectViaFTE`, which drives the same `runFTE`); and that step 6 has an internal order. **Not closable by deleting the gate that detects it.** |

| **D-077** | **THE METHOD'S BOUNDARY, RECORDED BESIDE THE METHOD — A SYMBOL SCAN CANNOT SEE A FILE PATH OR THE ABSENCE OF A STRING, AND AT LEAST FIVE SURFACES SIT IN THAT BLIND SPOT.** Ruled 2026-08-05. Section G's shape report §3 measured *"governance files referencing FTE legacy symbols"* and found **seven**. `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` is not among them and could not have been: **it references no FTE symbol at all** — a file **path** (`fs.existsSync(src/capability.ts)`, `:38`/`:91`) and the **absence of a string** in another file (`:84–86`). **Measured this session, the same blindness has four more instances**, each importing `authoring-bridge.mjs` **by path** and then **executing** it: `run-intent-conservation.mjs:12,38` — *itself one of the five gates §6 step 4 names for migration* — `verify-execution-contract.js:84,111,228,249`; `verify-package-governance.js:95,99,457`; `verify-reasoning-registry.js:98,134,385,491`. Add `record-fault-proofs.js:1524`, which anchors the RC-3 probe **inside that file**, and `test/authoring-bridge.test.mjs`'s 9 tests. | **THIS IS NOT A MISS; IT IS THE METHOD'S BOUNDARY, AND IT BELONGS BESIDE THE METHOD.** A symbol inventory answers *what imports this name*. It cannot answer *what reads this path*, *what asserts this string is absent*, or *what dynamically imports this file and runs it* — and the three questions it cannot answer are exactly where a retirement's blast radius hides, because a retiring module's dependants are found by symbol while a **surviving** file's dependants are found by path. **D-007's declaration-versus-implementation seam, arriving in the measurement method of the report written to prevent D-056/D-069.** The practical consequence for G: **Part 3's blast radius is four executing gates, one fault proof and nine tests wider than the four re-pointing obligations §2 measured**, and none of that width is visible to `retirement-inventory.mjs`, which measures `packages/functional-testing-engine` and resolves imports by symbol. | Method · `governance/capability/retirement-inventory.mjs` · [`SECTION_G_SHAPE_REPORT.md`](SECTION_G_SHAPE_REPORT.md) §3 · five governance surfaces | **OPEN — RECORDED AS A STANDING LIMIT OF THE INVENTORY, NOT AS A ONE-OFF EXCEPTION.** What is owed is **not** a bigger scan: it is that any retirement report state its method's boundary in the same document that states its results, so a reader can tell *nothing else references this* from *nothing else references this **by symbol***. **The shape report's §§2–3 are correct within their method and incomplete outside it**, and the four bridge-executing gates are the concrete cost. Named here so the next inventory does not have to rediscover it, and so §2's *"FOUR, measured. Not ~29"* is read as *four **symbol** obligations*. |

| **D-078** | **THE CUT-OVER READINESS VERDICT HAS NEVER BEEN COMPUTED — *"NINE OF TEN UNMET"* IS A TEST FIXTURE, AND THE ONE TIME IT WAS MEASURED THE ANSWER WAS EIGHT.** Measured 2026-08-05. `assessCutoverReadiness` has **six call sites in the repository and all six are inside its own reference conformance test**, each passing a hand-written literal (`test/runtime-cutover-readiness-conformance.test.ts:45,46,52,64,72,79`). **No production code, no governance gate and no evidence generator ever calls it.** `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` does not: **RC-1 (`:71`) is a regex over the source text** asserting the function is exported, and **RC-2 (`:76`) spawns the built test** — so the gate certifies that the assessor behaves correctly *given* evidence, and nothing supplies it any. The *"nine of ten UNMET"* carried through the shape report §10.2, **D-075 and `NEXT_ACTION.md`** is the content of `currentEvidence()`, authored 2026-07-29 and unchanged since. **The only measurement the programme ever took — `FT-M5-CUTOVER-001`, same date, probing docker/podman/nerdctl/containerd/kubectl/finch and the two `FTE_*` env vars — recorded 8 of 10**, because it measured CU-7 (*external contracts unchanged*) as **met**. The fixture records CU-7 `false`. | **D-007's SEAM IN THE CONTROL THE PROGRAMME TREATS AS THE AUTHORITY THAT RESERVES THIS DECISION.** The consequence is precise and it is why the behavioural-equivalence question has stood open for a year of programme time: **a precondition nothing computes cannot be met or waived on evidence — only asserted.** `behaviouralEquivalenceDemonstrated: false` is not a measurement that equivalence is absent; it is a literal nobody has ever been in a position to change, because no code path writes it. **This does not make readiness better than believed** — CU-2 (a container runtime) is absent *by measurement* and stays absent, and the recommendation is unchanged. It makes the *number* wrong and the *class* worse: every prior statement of the form *"nine of ten preconditions are unmet"* was quoting a fixture. | [ADR-0049](../docs/adr/ADR-0049-canonical-runtime-cutover.md) §4 · `src/runtime-cutover-readiness.ts` · `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` RC-1/RC-2 · D-075 (number corrected) · `NEXT_ACTION.md` (number corrected) | **OPEN — RULING R5 of [`ADR_0049_SUPERSESSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](ADR_0049_SUPERSESSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md).** Two honest resolutions and both are the programme's to choose: **re-found the assessment on measured evidence** (give `assessCutoverReadiness` a caller that reads disk, as FT-M5-CUTOVER-001 did by hand), or **re-scope the ten preconditions to what can be measured** and record the rest as external declarations. **What it must not remain is implicit.** D-075's *"nine of ten"* and `NEXT_ACTION.md`'s heading are corrected to *"eight of ten, measured 2026-07-29; the nine is a fixture"* when the ruling lands. |

| **D-079** | **RC-3 DETECTS A NAME, NOT A ROUTING — SO THE REROUTE THAT WILL ACTUALLY HAPPEN LEAVES IT GREEN.** Measured 2026-08-05. `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js:84–86` tests `/(runtime-entry-point-bridge\|createRuntimeEntryPointBridge\|RuntimeEntryPointBridge)/` against the concatenated raw text of `ip-execute-gateway.mjs` and `authoring-bridge.mjs`. **Two consequences. (α) It fires on a comment** — its own recorded fault proof does exactly that, planting `// premature-cutover probe: createRuntimeEntryPointBridge` above an unrelated import (`record-fault-proofs.js:1521–1528`). **(β) It does not fire on the composition Part 3 must actually use.** `createRuntimeEntryPointBridge` is **not available** to the bridge — it returns a `RuntimeExecutionOutcome` rather than a package body (the gateway signs a body, INV-2), it **dispatches** through the runtime SPI (`:112`, needing the unbound CU-4/CU-5 real signer and transport), and it **throws** where the live path returns a typed `registryRefusal`. The available successor is **`createCanonicalRunnerCapability(…).runThroughRunner(…)`, which matches none of the three strings.** | **A VACUOUS GREEN AVAILABLE BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY DELETION — AND IT ARRIVES ON THE RECOMMENDED PATH, NOT THE DECLINED ONE.** §10.3 removed one route to it by declining the cross-package deletion of `ip-execute-gateway.mjs`; **this is a second route, and it is inside the change the ruling authorised.** A Part 3 built the only way it can be built reroutes the platform's sole live authoring path onto the canonical runtime **while the gate that exists to detect a premature reroute reports PASS.** Under the ruled severity ordering that is the worse of the two outcomes — a control that stopped measuring, with no person in the loop, versus a control correctly red that a person must decide to overrule. **The gate's green would then mean *the bridge does not contain three particular strings*, which is not the proposition RC-3 is registered to assert.** | `verify-runtime-cutover-readiness.js` RC-3 (`:84–86`) · `record-fault-proofs.js:1521–1528` · `authoring-bridge.mjs` · `src/runtime-entry-point-bridge.ts:64–76,103–112` | **OPEN — RULING R3 of [`ADR_0049_SUPERSESSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md`](ADR_0049_SUPERSESSION_DESIGN_REPORT.md), AND IT FORCES THE SEQUENCE IN R4.** Proposed migrated form **RC-3′**: (1) the live path **reaches** the canonical composition; (2) it does **not reach** `createFunctionalTestingEngine` / `FunctionalTestingOrchestrator` / `buildCatalogue`; (3) the authorising ADR is **ACCEPTED on disk** — (1)+(2) measured by the module graph (`retirement-inventory.mjs`, on a **rebuilt** tree per shape report §9.5), not by a regex. Re-anchored proofs: revert the bridge to legacy → RED; reroute with the ADR unaccepted → RED; **a comment naming the canonical bridge → GREEN**, which is the probe that proves α is gone. **Because of (β), the gate migration must land BEFORE Part 3** — declaration and enforcement as one atomic change, D-012. |

| **D-074** | **THE FOURTEENTH SCOPE CORRECTION — AN EVIDENCE ARTEFACT ASSERTING ITS OWN VALIDITY AGED SILENTLY, AND THE COMMIT THAT AGED IT WAS THE ONE DISCHARGING THE PLAN IT SERVED.** `governance/capability/retirement-inventory.json` carried `"VALIDITY": "measured"`, `"method": "… ORPHANS = RETIRING \ SURVIVING"`, and `orphanCount: 10` **with `src/agents/design-sync.ts` among them**. Every field was true when written. Provenance is exact: `eca53fb` (2026-08-04) generated it, correct on that tree; `4d79e59` (2026-08-05) — ***"P-69.2 — design synchronisation composed onto the canonical runtime"*** — entered `src/domains/synchronisation.ts` into the canonical composition and `agents/design-sync.ts` with it (`synchronisation.ts:41` imports `designSyncAgents`, **`:152` constructs a Map over them — a runtime use, not a type import**); **the JSON was never regenerated.** Section G's shape report then read its module table from the stale file and its summary block from a fresh run, and **printed `ORPHANED : 9` directly above a table listing ten modules.** The retirement instruction built on it named a deletion set of **25 = 16 + 9**, which treats `RETIRING` and `ORPHANED` as disjoint when **`RETIRING ⊃ ORPHANED`**: the other seven members of the retiring closure are also in the *surviving* closure — `model.ts`, `domains/observation-interpretation.ts`, `agents/design-sync.ts`, `design-sync-composition.ts`, and the three `emitters/*`. | **A STALE ARTEFACT THAT SAYS `VALIDITY: measured` IS STRONGER EVIDENCE THAN NO ARTEFACT AND WEAKER THAN AN UNSTAMPED ONE, BECAUSE IT SUPPLIES THE CONFIDENCE AND WITHHOLDS THE DATE.** `measured` is a claim about **a moment**; read without the tree state it was taken against, it is received as a claim about **now**. The cost here was not a wrong number in a report: acting on it would have deleted `agents/design-sync.ts` and broken `domains/synchronisation.ts:152` — **the canonical synchronisation domain, landed the day before G was scoped, by the very commit that invalidated the evidence.** `model.ts` and `domains/observation-interpretation.ts` (Section D's port, two commits old) sat in the same arithmetic. **This is the first correction in this programme whose uncorrected form would have deleted a capability landed the same week**, and the mechanism that would have caught it is one the register already owns: **D-008's stamp, applied to a JSON rather than to a test count.** D-011 recorded this instrument as *valid* on the strength of that run; validity is a property of an instrument **and a tree**, and only the first half was recorded. | Governance evidence · `governance/capability/retirement-inventory.mjs` (the generator) · every `*-evidence.json` under `governance/capability/` that carries a `VALIDITY` field | **THE STAMP IS THE REPAIR, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE D-008 ASKED FOR — WHICH IS THE FINDING, NOT A COINCIDENCE.** Every artefact that asserts its own validity SHALL carry the `git rev-parse HEAD` and working-tree-dirty state it was measured against, and a consumer reading it against a different tree SHALL report `NOT CURRENT` (R-14.5) rather than the value. **Do not close this by regenerating the file** — regeneration fixes the instance and leaves the class, and the class is that *nothing in the artefact can tell a reader it has expired*. **A second mechanism is owed and is cheaper than it looks:** the generator already walks the import graph, so it can emit the head it walked. **Recorded before the first deletion of Section G, deliberately** — the correction is worth more than the deletion it unblocks, and a register entry written after the delete would have been written by someone who no longer had the evidence. |

| **D-073** | **THE ELEVENTH SCOPE CORRECTION — THE OWNERSHIP TEST WAS STATED WITH ONE FAILURE MODE AND HAS TWO, AND THE MISSING ONE WAS ASSERTED AS THE REASON RULING 3 DIFFERED.** ADR-0076 §4.2.1 introduced the test — *before making a field's valid set unrepresentable, ask who owns that set* — and concluded from it that *"rulings 2 and 3 are no longer symmetric: `stageRef`'s valid set is `StageName`, owned by the same package as the field."* **The premise was false.** `stageRef` is in `@dbiz/contracts`; `StageName` is in `@dbiz/capability-framework`, which declares `"dependencies": { "@dbiz/contracts": "workspace:*" }`. **The test is amended in §4.2.1 itself, not carried as a D-072 detail**, because a test recorded with one of its two modes will be applied with one of its two modes. | **MODE 2 IS INVISIBLE TO ANY READING OF THE FIELD, AND THAT IS WHY IT SURVIVED BEING STATED.** Mode 1 — the valid set contributed by consumers — announces itself the moment you look for the set and find five owners. **Mode 2 — the valid set living downstream of the field — has no local symptom at all**: `readonly stageRef: string` in a contracts file carries nothing about the dependency graph, not in its type, its neighbours, or its file. It announces itself only when you try, which is exactly what ADR-0074 §6.1 already prescribes and why landing the type produced the answer in one line of compiler output. **The correction also reverses the conclusion it was used for: rulings 2 and 3 ARE symmetric, by different mechanisms — both keep `string`, both are enforced by a gate, and both narrowings are owed an ownership decision.** | Programme governance (the class) · ADR-0076 §4.2.1 (the test) | **RECORDED AS THE ELEVENTH, AND THE ORDER THAT FOUND IT IS THE REUSABLE PART.** The test was written one turn before, adopted, and **found the very next ruling failing it** — the same order that produced T5, the criterion-namespace collision and D-071: **apply the rule to the work done under it, soonest.** Found before adoption rather than after, which is the difference between one correction and a class of decisions taken on a wrong premise. **The nine prior corrections were in planning artefacts and D-069 was in an accepted ADR's scope; this one is in a TEST — reasoning that governs future decisions rather than describing a past one — which is why it is amended at its statement rather than annotated beside it.** |

| **D-072a** | **RULED 2026-08-05: `StageName` MOVES TO `@dbiz/contracts`. THE TWELVE-STAGE LIFECYCLE IS A PLATFORM CONTRACT, NOT A FRAMEWORK IMPLEMENTATION DETAIL.** An event's stage is part of **what crosses**; an agent is a **framework** concept; the vocabulary they share belongs in the lower package. `contracts` declares no workspace dependencies, so **there is nothing for it to cycle against**, and `capability-framework` already depends on it and re-exports — so the move is downhill in the dependency graph rather than against it. | **THIS RESOLVES MODE 2 AT ITS CAUSE RATHER THAN ROUTING AROUND IT.** The ownership test's second failure mode is *the valid set living downstream of the field*; moving the set **upstream** makes the field's own package the owner, after which §2.1's preferred form applies to `stageRef` and the narrowing is mechanical. **It is also the only route that does not violate something**: adding the framework to `contracts`' dependencies inverts the graph, and restating the twelve names is D-007's prohibition by restatement. | Platform — `@dbiz/contracts` (destination) · `@dbiz/capability-framework` (source) · all five capabilities (the vocabulary) | **ITS OWN ADR, NOT A STEP INSIDE ANYTHING — it touches every capability's lifecycle vocabulary, and the surface is COMPILER-MEASURED per ADR-0074 §6.1 (consumers PLUS implementors, fixtures included) rather than estimated.** **DOES NOT BLOCK G**: `stageRef` stays `string` until the ADR lands, and **`verify-domain-stage-ref.js` holds the property in the meantime** — the eight relabellings are already correct and the gate fails on any regression, so the narrowing adds structural impossibility to a property already enforced rather than establishing one. |

| **D-072** | **RULING 3's NARROWING FAILS THE SAME OWNERSHIP TEST AS RULING 2's — FOR A STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT REASON, AND THE COMPILER SAID SO.** ADR-0076 §4.3 item 11 rules `PlatformEvent.stageRef` narrowed from `string` to `StageName`, and the instruction sequencing it stated the preferred form applies *"because `StageName` is owned by the same package as the field"*. **Measured, it is not.** `stageRef` lives in `@dbiz/contracts`; `StageName` lives in `@dbiz/capability-framework`, whose `package.json` declares `"dependencies": { "@dbiz/contracts": "workspace:*" }`. **The dependency runs contracts ← framework**, so the import inverts it. Landed anyway per ADR-0074 §6.1 — *land the type and let the compiler enumerate* — and the compiler answered before any consumer or implementor was reached: **`src/events.ts(21,32): error TS2307: Cannot find module '@dbiz/capability-framework'`.** | **THE OWNERSHIP TEST HAS TWO FAILURE MODES, NOT ONE, AND ONLY THE FIRST WAS KNOWN.** *(i)* The valid set is contributed by CONSUMERS — ruling 2, where five capabilities own SPI names the framework must not enumerate. *(ii)* **The valid set lives DOWNSTREAM of the field** — ruling 3, where the stage vocabulary is in a package that depends on the field's own. **Both defeat *make it unrepresentable*; neither is a reason to leave the field unchecked.** The second is the sharper of the two because it is invisible to every reading of the field itself: `stageRef: string` in a contracts file gives no hint that its valid set is one package away in the wrong direction. **The test was stated one turn before this and adopted; applying it to the very next ruling found the ruling failing it.** A rule is worth most turned on the work done under it, and soonest — this register's own sentence, earning a second instance. | Platform — `@dbiz/contracts` · `@dbiz/capability-framework` (the dependency direction) | **§6's PARTIAL-OUTCOME CLAUSE INVOKED, NOT OVERRIDDEN: (a) and (c) LANDED, (b) IS OWED.** The clause is explicit that (b) must not be forced to keep the set complete, and forcing it here has only bad forms: adding the framework to `contracts`' dependencies creates a **cycle**, and restating the twelve stage names inside `contracts` is **D-007's exact prohibition** — two implementations of one governed term. **The real repair is a decision, not a narrowing: WHERE DOES THE CANONICAL TWELVE-STAGE VOCABULARY LIVE?** `contracts` is the lower package and doc 12 is constitutional, so defining `STAGES` there and having the framework derive `StageName` from it would make the valid set owned by the field's package and the preferred form would apply. **That MOVES the lifecycle's single source of truth between packages and is an architecture decision, not an implementation detail** — recorded as owed rather than taken in passing, on §4.4's precedent. **(c) closes D-062's enforcement gap regardless: `verify-domain-stage-ref.js` compares both sides read from source, and a `string` that is checked is not a `string` that is unchecked.** |

| **D-071** | **RULING 2's NAMED SOURCE IS NOT THE SPI REGISTRY IT WAS ASSUMED TO BE — THREE ENTRIES AGAINST ELEVEN NAMES IN LIVE USE.** ADR-0076 §4.2 item 9 rules that `toolContracts` SHALL be narrowed to *"a union of declared SPI names, sourced from `CONNECTOR_SPI_DESCRIPTORS`, which the framework already exports"*. **Measured before landing the type, per ADR-0074 §6.1: `CONNECTOR_SPI_DESCRIPTORS` contains THREE descriptors** — `AuthenticationAdapter`, `ApplicationStrategyAdapter`, `ReportingAdapter`. The framework exports **nine** adapter interfaces, and `toolContracts` declarations across the platform name **eleven distinct values**: five of the nine framework SPIs (`TestManagementAdapter` 10, `ExecutionAdapter` 10, `TestDesignSyncAdapter` 7, `WorkItemAdapter` 6, `ProjectAdapter` 4), **three capability-OWNED SPIs the framework does not and should not know** (`SecurityAdapter` 14, `LoadGeneratorAdapter` 3, `MonitoringAdapter` 1 — D-057 recorded three capabilities each defining their own), and the three phantoms. **Narrowing to the named source would make eight of eleven legitimate declarations a compile error.** | **THE DECISION IS SOUND AND ITS MECHANISM SENTENCE WAS NOT — D-069's shape at a smaller scale, in the same ADR, found the same way: by measuring a named source instead of trusting it.** The narrowing's PURPOSE stands untouched — *make a name that matches no SPI type unrepresentable* — and what the measurement exposes is a question the ADR did not settle and could not have settled in passing: **does the framework own a CLOSED SPI namespace, or do capabilities EXTEND it?** A closed framework union excludes `SecurityAdapter` and forces three capabilities to either mis-declare or amend the framework to add their own adapter — which is P-004's bespoke-capability-architecture pressure arriving through a type. An open union catches nothing. **Neither is implementable without deciding who owns the namespace.** | Platform — `@dbiz/capability-framework` · Penetration Testing, Security Testing, Performance (the capability-owned SPIs) | **RULED 2026-08-05: THE SPI NAMESPACE IS OPEN; THE FRAMEWORK DOES NOT OWN IT. ITEM 9 IS STRUCK AND REPLACED BY A GATE — ADR-0076 §4.2.1, A GOVERNED AMENDMENT TO A FROZEN ADR, because this finding changes the DECISION and that is the one case the freeze admits.** `verify-tool-contracts.js` checks **resolution, not membership**: *does this name resolve to an SPI interface that exists*, wherever it lives. It resolves both live declaration forms — a bare SPI name and `Spi.operation`, the latter checked against the interface's actual members and therefore **stricter than the narrowing would have been**. **LANDED RED AND ESCALATED (P-002, R-18.12): it names exactly the three phantoms at five sites and nothing else.** Two net-new governance reds, both this gate, both intended — a gate that cannot be made green is left red and escalated rather than weakened, and what each of the five sites should declare instead is the owning capability's decision (§4.4). **PROVED BY DIRECT FAULT INJECTION: a planted `ThisSpiDoesNotExistAnywhere` is named by name and site, and the gate returns to exactly the three real phantoms when it is removed. The registry records `proved: false` and that is HONEST — the clean-passes half cannot hold while the clean run is legitimately red.** **§2.1's FIRST LIMIT is recorded in the ADR itself: *"make an unenforced field unrepresentable" has no implementation where the valid set is not owned by the type's package* — and D-012 already names the fallback, so a gate here is the rule working rather than being bypassed. Rulings 2 and 3 are NOT symmetric: `stageRef`'s valid set is `StageName`, owned by the same package as the field, so the preferred form applies there and not here. The test is OWNERSHIP OF THE VALID SET, applied before reaching for a type.** ~~**ITEMS 6 AND 7 ARE DELIVERED; ITEM 9 IS OWED A CORRECTED SOURCE.**~~ The meaning is ruled and `agent.ts`'s definition-site wording now states it, so the field's THIRD reading is gone and the two live ones are resolved to one. **ADR-0076 is not amended** — the decision is unchanged and only its mechanism sentence was wrong, which is D-069's precedent applied to its own ruling 2. **The namespace-ownership question is the ruling owed**, and it is small, well-posed and independent: it needs one answer, after which the union is mechanical. **Do NOT close this by narrowing to the nine framework interfaces** — that silently rules the ownership question in the framework's favour and breaks eighteen live declarations, which is the same defect as narrowing to the three. |

| **D-069** | **THE TENTH SCOPE CORRECTION, AND THE FIRST INSIDE AN *ACCEPTED* ADR: §4.1.2 PRICED THE BOARD PORT AS A COMPOSITION STEP, AND IT IS A PROJECTION BETWEEN TWO TYPE FAMILIES THAT HAVE NEVER BEEN CONNECTED.** ADR-0076 §4.1.2 item 4 reads *"the independent review board SHALL be composed into the canonical runtime at `reporting`"*, and §6 A1 sequences it as one step alongside the aggregator repairs. **Measured at A1: `ReviewSnapshot` has fourteen fields in agent-path model types (`Story`, `Requirement`, `Scenario`, `TestCase`, `AutomationAsset`, `TestOutcome`, `HealingAction`, `Defect`, `QualityReport`, `SyncReport`, `AuthoringQuality`, `CoverageCertification`, …) while `CanonicalCapabilityResult` carries fourteen DOMAIN result types. No canonical→`ReviewSnapshot` projection exists, and no canonical→model projection exists anywhere in the repository** — `ReviewSnapshot` is referenced only from the agent path. **"Compose the board at `reporting`" named the smaller half; the fourteen unconnected mappings were never counted.** | **THE PREVIOUS NINE CORRECTIONS WERE ALL IN PLANNING ARTEFACTS — A COUNT, A TYPE, A VERB, A REGISTRY. THIS ONE IS IN A DECISION THAT WAS PROPOSED, REVIEWED, RULED AND ACCEPTED**, and it survived all four because the sentence describes the *destination* correctly and says nothing about the distance. **A composition step and a cross-family projection read identically when written as "compose X into Y".** The prior instances are the same shape at lower altitude: D-051 found it in a registry, D-056 in four planning figures, ADR-0074 §6.1 in counting consumers and omitting implementors. **The generalisation this earns, and it is the ninth's raised one level: an interface change's surface is consumers plus implementors (ADR-0074 §6.1); a COMPOSITION's surface is the units plus the TYPE DISTANCE BETWEEN THEM.** Where two units come from different type families, "compose" is the name of the destination and not of the work. | Programme governance (the class) · ADR-0076 §4.1.2 / §6 A1 (the instance) | **RECORDED, AND ADR-0076 IS DELIBERATELY NOT AMENDED.** It is FROZEN on acceptance, and its own rule is that it is amended *only if a finding changes the DECISION*. **The decision is unchanged**: option C, B-then-A, the board at `reporting`, the aggregator repaired in the port. What was wrong is the **cost and the boundary of one migration step**, which is exactly what the register exists to carry. **Amending an accepted ADR to correct a scope estimate would make the ADR a running log and destroy the property that makes a frozen decision worth reading.** The port becomes **its own change** (D-070), and A1 is complete as the aggregator repair. **THE SEQUENCING COST, RECORDED HERE AS THE SAME CONSEQUENCE AND NOT AS A SECOND FINDING (2026-08-05).** §6 A1 reads *"Port the board with its aggregator repaired"* and sequences repair and port as **one step**. This correction split them — the repairs landed, the port became D-070 — and the split has a cost that only Section G's shape report could measure: **the repairs were landed into `src/agents/review-board.ts`, which Section G deletes.** The four tests proving G-1/G-2/G-3 drive `governance.final-certification` through `catalogue()` → `buildCatalogue()` → `reviewBoardAgents`, all three inside G's deletion set, so **they cannot be preserved as executable tests — the unit under test IS the deleted aggregator.** RULED 2026-08-05: preserved as a SPECIFICATION — [`BOARD_AGGREGATOR_PORT_SPECIFICATION.md`](BOARD_AGGREGATOR_PORT_SPECIFICATION.md), each finding carrying the defect, the repair, and the assertion that failed on the unrepaired form, written as what the PORTED aggregator must satisfy. **This is ONE consequence with the correction above, not two.** The correction was right — a projection between two unconnected type families is not a wiring step, and attempting it inside A1 would have invented fourteen mappings. **What it could not know is that the step it left behind would be deleted before the step it deferred was taken**, and no measurement available at A1 would have shown that, because G's shape report is what produced it. **The generalisation: when a correction SPLITS one migration step into two, the register SHALL record whether either half is inside a pending deletion set.** A1's repair and G's deletion were both correctly sequenced against the artefact each was reasoning about, and neither was sequenced against the other. **The platform now holds three repairs it can describe and cannot verify, for the duration of the port** — the *"declared but not executed"* shape of §4's standing rule, entered deliberately and with the exit named rather than arrived at silently. |

| **D-070a** | **RULED 2026-08-05: THE BOARD PORT DOES NOT BLOCK G — AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS A KNOWN ABSENCE IN THE SURVIVOR, STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED.** §4.1.3 records that review at `reporting` **does not gate progression**: the board reads a run already certified and executed and can report a finding it cannot prevent. **A mechanism that cannot prevent anything cannot be a precondition for deletion.** G's blockers were capability blockers and F3 closed them; `governanceOrchestrator` is reachable from three files, none canonical, so **retirement removes a board the surviving runtime never had.** | **AFTER G THE PLATFORM HAS NO INDEPENDENT REVIEW MECHANISM AT ALL, AND THAT SENTENCE BELONGS IN G's CLOSURE — not inferred from an open debt entry.** The distinction is the one this register keeps making: **an absence recorded in a backlog is a thing someone might read; an absence named in a closure is a thing the closure asserts.** It joins `sharedSteps`, `businessGoal`, `automationReady` and `executionType` as a **recorded capability reduction** (ADR-0069 P-69.2's form), and it is the largest of them — the other four are fields, this is the only unit in the platform that reviews a run without being able to touch it. **D-019's third half is what survives G: the triad reviews artefacts the composition handed it, and after G there is nothing else.** | Functional Testing capability · Section G's closure record | **THE PORT IS OWED, NOT BLOCKING — AND IT CARRIES A CONDITION THAT MUST BE MET BEFORE IT IS ATTEMPTED.** **D-061a's trigger fires at TWO and this is the second**, so the missing architectural role is named **before** the port, not during it: attempted without it, the port either **invents fourteen mappings** — feeding the platform's only independent review a snapshot nobody owns — **or waits indefinitely** on an ownership question no one has been asked. **Naming the role is therefore a precondition of the port and not of G**, and the two must not be confused: G proceeds, the port does not, and the reason is different in each case. |

| **D-070** | **THE BOARD PORT — ITS OWN CHANGE, SCOPED AND OWED. NOT A1's REMAINDER.** A projection from `CanonicalCapabilityResult` (fourteen domain result types) to `ReviewSnapshot` (fourteen agent-path model types), between two families with **no existing projection in either direction**. It feeds the platform's only measurably independent review mechanism. | **THE REASON IT STOPPED, RECORDED VERBATIM SO NO LATER READER RE-DERIVES IT OR TALKS THEMSELVES PAST IT: *a mis-mapped snapshot yields a reviewer approving a run it has misread, which is worse than no reviewer because it manufactures independent-looking assurance.*** Every other mapping defect in this register produced a wrong number a mechanism could later disagree with. **This one produces a wrong number wearing the platform's own independence guarantee** — fourteen reviewers, `ai=0 tool=0`, `ReviewSnapshot` frozen by type, all of it intact and all of it reading the wrong run. The independence properties survive a bad mapping perfectly; that is precisely what makes it dangerous. | Functional Testing capability · the owner of the mapping question (D-061) | **NOT ATTEMPTED, on ADR-0075 P-75.5's reason repeated verbatim: deciding what a domain's output means to a consumer inside a port is how a decision arrives with nobody having taken it.** The aggregator repairs it depends on are landed and proven, so nothing about this is blocked on further discovery — it is blocked on an **ownership ruling**, which is D-061's question and is held by the programme rather than by this work. **Sequenced after rulings 2 and 3**, both of which are specified and need no owner ruling, so ADR-0076 keeps moving while the ownership question is decided where it belongs. |

| **D-061a** | **THE SECOND INSTANCE OF D-061's CLASS — A FIELD MAPPING WITH NO OWNER — AND TWO INSTANCES MAKE IT A CLASS RATHER THAN AN ECHO.** Recorded 2026-08-05 at ADR-0076 A1. **Two independent mappings now lack an owner:** *(i)* `dbiz.observation-set@1` → `ObservationInterpretationInput` — the declared input contract is not the type consumed, and the work-item field mapping has no owner (D-061, from ADR-0075); *(ii)* `CanonicalCapabilityResult` → `ReviewSnapshot` — fourteen fields across two type families never connected (D-070). **They are not the same deferral seen twice: the first is a cross-plane contract-to-type mapping, the second a cross-family result-to-snapshot projection, and neither was discovered by looking for the other.** | **BOTH SIT WHERE TOOL-SCHEMA OR CROSS-FAMILY KNOWLEDGE BELONGS TO NEITHER COMPOSITION NOR TENANCY, and that is the finding.** A composition knows what it produces; a tenancy knows what its tool holds; **neither owns the statement that THIS field means THAT field.** Every mechanism in the platform has an owner — domains, agents, adapters, gates, ADRs — and this kind of knowledge has none, so each instance gets deferred individually with a good local reason. **Two deferrals with good local reasons and no common owner is how a missing role stays invisible.** | Platform architecture (the role question) · Functional Testing (both instances) | **THE TRIGGER IS RECORDED SO THE THIRD INSTANCE IS NOT A THIRD DEFERRAL: if a third mapping appears with no owner, that is a MISSING ARCHITECTURAL ROLE rather than three separate deferrals, and it is answered by naming the role — not by wiring the third mapping.** Recorded now, at two, because the trigger is worth nothing written after the third. **Neither instance is wired here**; both are owed to the same ruling, and their being owed to *one* ruling rather than two is this entry's whole contribution. |

| **D-067a** | **WHAT ZERO MOVEMENT MEANS FOR THE INSTRUCTION THAT PRODUCED IT — recorded because the instruction was right and its precondition was absent.** B1 was executed under a binding rule: *treat every `progressedTo` movement as a finding to read, not a test to update.* **Not one assertion moved across five capabilities.** The rule is sound and it **only bites where something was positioned to move** — it detects a test that passed because a control was missing, and it is silent where no test ever exercised the control at all. | **ZERO MOVEMENT IS NOT A NULL RESULT. IT IS THE MEASUREMENT OF AN ABSENCE, AND THE ABSENCE HAS THE SAME CAUSE AS THE DEFECT.** No capability suite moved because no capability suite had ever placed a non-`ok` outcome at stage 4 (D-067) — the same missing case that let `architecture-review` go ungated for the platform's life. **So the instruction's silence and the defect's survival are one fact observed twice.** The consequence must be stated in the strongest available terms: **B1's green suites across all five capabilities are NOT evidence that the five are sound at stage 4.** They are evidence that none of them asks. A reader meeting *"zero net-new, all suites green"* would reasonably conclude the gating change was absorbed cleanly by five well-tested capabilities; **what actually happened is that five capabilities were gated on a stage none of their suites has ever exercised negatively.** | Platform governance (the reasoning) · all five capabilities (the owed cases) | **THE GENERAL FORM, worth more than the instance: a rule that reads CHANGE detects a wrong assertion and cannot detect a missing one.** Reading movement as a finding is correct and needs a companion — *did anything move, and if nothing did, was anything positioned to?* **A control newly connected to a suite that never exercised it produces the most reassuring possible signal and the least evidence.** The owed work is D-067's five cases, and **each is that capability's decision under ADR-0076 §4.4 (`UNDECIDED — <capability>`), so it is RECORDED AS OWED and deliberately not written here** — writing five refusal predicates to make five suites exercise stage 4 would be taking five capability decisions in order to improve a test signal, which is the inversion D-024 and D-057 both warn against. |

| **D-066** | **ARCHITECTURE REVIEW IS THE ONE GOVERNANCE-TRIAD STAGE WHOSE VERDICT `certify()` NEVER READS — IT IS CHECKED FOR PRESENCE AND FOR NOTHING ELSE. PROVED BY OBSERVATION, THROUGH THE REAL FRAMEWORK.** Found 2026-08-05 while measuring D-019's current state for the R-12.11/R-12.2 ADR. `certification.ts:76` refuses a run that did not traverse all three triad stages (R-12.2) — **presence only**. The verdict loop then iterates `CERTIFICATION_GATES`, and `GATE_STAGE` maps gates onto seven stages: `policy-review`, `guardrail-review`, `execution-planning`, `execution`, `reflection`, `reporting`, `certification`. **`architecture-review` is not among them.** No gate is a verdict about stage 4, so nothing that stage produces is ever read. **Measured by running a real capability through `runCapability` and refusing at one triad stage per run** — a `StageResult` carries a module-private seal and cannot be forged, so this is the framework's own path (R-12.11): `refuse` at `policy-review` → `certified=false, firstRefusal=story-certified`; at `guardrail-review` → `certified=false, firstRefusal=test-certified`; **at `architecture-review` → `outcome=refused`, `certified=TRUE`, `firstRefusal=null`.** The same run repeated with `notApplicable` gives the identical result, so **the gap predates ADR-0071 and was not created by it.** | **A GOVERNANCE-TRIAD STAGE CAN DO ITS WORK, SAY NO, SEAL THAT ANSWER, AND THE RUN CERTIFIES.** This is `@dbiz/capability-framework`, so it holds for **all five implemented capabilities** independently of what any of their architecture reviews compute — the same framework-level scope that made D-019 platform-wide. **It is the third distinct half of D-019 and the only one still fully open.** D-019 named two: the triad's stages could not *say* no (closed by ADR-0071's `refuse`), and `certify()` never read `value` (still true, and now partly moot for the two gated stages since `outcome` is read instead). **The third is that one of the three stages has no channel at all**, which no amount of vocabulary reaches. **ADR-0071 is the reason this is now sharp rather than theoretical:** before it, a stage's only decline was `notApplicable`, and stage 4's `notApplicable` was already unheard; after it, a capability can write a genuine, reasoned architectural refusal, seal it, and watch the platform certify over it. **The negative branch is live in the Functional Testing capability today** — `canonical-runner-capability.ts:156` emits `notApplicable` at `architecture-review` when no architecture components were authored, and `canonical-runner-capability.ts:255` calls `certify(outcome.results)` on the result. **D-019 recorded that branch as unreachable-by-sequencing; this entry adds that it would not be heard if it were reached.** | Platform — `@dbiz/capability-framework` (`GATE_STAGE`, `certify`) · all five implemented capabilities | **NOT REPAIRED, AND THE ONE-LINE REPAIR IS THE WRONG ONE TO TAKE UNRULED.** Adding `'architecture-certified': 'architecture-review'` to `GATE_STAGE` and the gate list is roughly two lines, and it changes what `progressedTo` means for every capability — the gates are an **ordered** list and the new gate lands first, so every existing progression check shifts. **More importantly it is a certification-surface change and this register's own rule (D-024, D-057) is that a negative-path decision belongs to the capability that owns it**: today five capabilities emit *something* at stage 4 that nothing has ever read, and turning that into a gate makes each of those emissions load-bearing at once, without any of the five having decided what its architecture review should refuse on. **Sequenced INTO the R-12.11/R-12.2 ADR rather than alongside it.** That ADR is already deciding what independent review means and where it lives; **an ungated triad stage is the same question with the answer already measured**, and ruling on the mechanism while leaving a third of the triad inaudible would produce a design that its own framework contradicts. **The probe is the evidence and it should be kept**: a gate that has never been observed to fail is indistinguishable from one that cannot (D-008), and this one was observed *not* failing when it should have. **CLOSED 2026-08-05 by ADR-0076 §6 phase B1.** `architecture-certified` is the FIRST member of the ordered `CERTIFICATION_GATES` and maps to `architecture-review` in `GATE_STAGE`. **The probe landed as a conformance test BEFORE the repair and was observed FAILING against the unmodified framework** — `no certification gate reads architecture-review — its verdict cannot affect certification` — then passing after it; **32 pass / 1 fail before, and the 1 was the probe.** It asserts the property over `GOVERNANCE_TRIAD` rather than over `architecture-review` alone, so a fourth triad stage or a reordering cannot reintroduce the gap, and it asserts end-to-end that a sealed refusal at **each** triad stage reaches `certified: false` with a matching `firstRefusal`. **Measured: capability-framework 76 → 77, `pnpm -r test` 0 failures across all 15 projects, governance 156 checks / 23 red with the failing-check set BYTE-IDENTICAL to a stashed clean tree rebuilt from source. `verify-programme-closure` PASS. ZERO NET-NEW.** **What closing it revealed is recorded separately as D-067**: the predicted `progressedTo` movement did not occur in any capability suite, because no test in the platform had ever placed a non-`ok` outcome at stage 4. |

| **D-064** | **THE COVERAGE DENOMINATOR'S PROVENANCE WAS STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE TO EVERY MECHANISM IN THE PLATFORM, BECAUSE A PRE-SPLIT ARRAY AND A SPLIT ARRAY ARE THE SAME TYPE.** Recorded 2026-08-05 as its own entry, having been established by ADR-0075 and carried until now only inside that port's narrative. Before the port, `RequirementInput.rawAcceptanceCriteria` arrived at the canonical composition as `readonly string[]` **already decomposed** — split by whatever implemented `fetchRequirement`, outside the composition, outside the Intelligence Plane's interpretation domain, and outside any certified unit. `repositoryIntelligence.coverageSummary.total` is derived from that array's length, so **the number the sovereignty audit calls the coverage denominator was set before the composition began.** The defect is not that the split happened elsewhere. It is that `string[]` carries no evidence of who produced it: **the array a certified interpreter would have produced and the array a caller had already split are indistinguishable at every point they can be observed** — by the type system, by a conformance test, by a gate, by a reviewer reading the code, and by the run itself. | **NOT A WRONG VALUE — AN UNVERIFIABLE ONE, AND THAT IS A DIFFERENT AND WORSE CLASS.** Every finding this register holds about a number is a finding that the number was wrong or unreachable: D-013's vacuous `published`, D-043's self-refuting reason, D-053's double-counted links. Each was findable because a mechanism could in principle disagree with it. **Here no mechanism could disagree, because none could tell what it was looking at.** The denominator could have been right on every run the platform has ever performed and the platform would have had no way to establish that — which means every coverage figure it has published rests on a provenance claim nothing verified. **This is D-018's rule read from the failure side:** where a rule can be made unviolatable it is, and the converse holds — where a distinction cannot be represented, no amount of testing recovers it. **The remedy that worked was structural and is the reference form: `RequirementInput` no longer carries acceptance criteria at all**, so the reasoner cannot re-derive a denominator from text it is structurally unable to reach. A test asserting *"the criteria came from the interpreter"* was available and would have been unfalsifiable for the same reason the defect was invisible. | Platform governance (the class) · Functional Testing capability (the instance, closed by ADR-0075) | **THE INSTANCE IS CLOSED; THE CLASS IS OPEN AND IS RECORDED HERE BECAUSE IT GENERALISES BEYOND ONE FIELD.** The question it earns as a pre-landing check: **does this type distinguish a value this composition produced from one it was handed?** Any collection typed as its element's plain array fails that check by construction, and this platform passes such arrays across the plane boundary and between domains routinely. **The class is not being swept here, deliberately** — a sweep that found instances would produce a list of types to widen, and widening a type is the cheap half; deciding which ones genuinely need provenance is the expensive half and belongs with an owner. **Recorded so the next reader meets the class rather than re-deriving it from one ADR's narrative**, which is the failure mode D-057 named about ADR-0072's unrecorded scope. |

| **D-065** | **THE ADR INDEX IN `DECISIONS.md` IS SIX ENTRIES BEHIND THE ADRs ON DISK — NOT FOUR — AND NOTHING COMPARES THEM.** Measured 2026-08-05 **by diffing the index's rows against `docs/adr/` rather than by reading either**: **67 ADR files on disk, 61 rows in the index, six absent** — `ADR-0060` (cloud-native provider platform), `ADR-0063` (SBOM build-artefact model), `ADR-0071` (stage refusal primitive), `ADR-0072` (publication outcome SPI), `ADR-0073` (design-sync publication outcome), `ADR-0074` (connector read reachability). **Nothing is indexed that does not exist**, so the drift is one-directional. **Re-diffed after ADR-0076 was authored and indexed the same day: 68 on disk, 62 rows, THE SAME SIX ABSENT** — a new decision indexed at the moment it is taken does not touch the backlog, which is the distinction D-054 draws between recording and reconstructing. The index's own header sentence still reads *"Thirty-five ADRs"*, and `verify-adr-completeness` checks the ADRs themselves — banner conformance, change control — while **no check anywhere compares the index to them.** | **THE CORRECTION IS THE FINDING, AND IT LANDED ON THE ENTRY THAT EXISTS TO RECORD THE DRIFT.** `DECISIONS.md`'s own drift note (added 2026-08-05 while writing ADR-0075) names exactly `ADR-0071` through `ADR-0074`, and the instruction to record this repeated that set. **Both are wrong by two, and both are wrong in the same direction, because both were written from what the recent work had touched rather than from a diff.** `ADR-0060` and `ADR-0063` are older than every ADR the note names — the drift predates the sessions that discovered it, and a reader taking the note at its word would conclude the index was accurate up to ADR-0070. **A gap measured from memory of recent work reproduces the boundary of that work**, which is D-057's finding — a scope over a session rather than over the artefact — arriving in the register entry written about a scope defect. **This is D-012's shape in a document rather than a type**: a declared control with no reader, and the second instance in `program/` itself after D-051's registry. | Programme governance — `program/DECISIONS.md` · the absent gate | **RECORDED AND DELIBERATELY NOT RECONSTRUCTED, for D-054's reason.** Six rows written from filenames — all a reconstruction could draw on without reading six ADRs and re-deriving what each closes — would **wear a record's clothes**, indistinguishable to the next reader from rows written when the decisions were taken. `PROJECT_STATE.md` §8 already records that a plausible-looking index is worse than an absent one. **`DECISIONS.md`'s drift note is corrected in place to the measured six**, so the index declares its own incompleteness accurately rather than under-stating it. **The repair is a gate and it is the cheapest one in this register: enumerate `docs/adr/ADR-*.md`, enumerate the index rows, diff, fail on either direction.** Six lines of `.mjs`. It is not performed here because this entry is a record and the ADR in front of it is a ruling, **but nothing about it is hard and it is the one mechanism that would have caught all six** — including the two that four separate readers, twice, wrote down as not existing. |

| **D-060** | **FOUR OF FIVE INTERPRETATION CAPABILITIES ARE NOW COMPOSED AND STILL UNREAD.** ADR-0075 composed `observation-interpretation` third and wired ONE consumer: `story-intelligence` derives its acceptance criteria from the decomposition, which is the coverage denominator (audit V-08). **Criterion Structure (V-09), Artefact Classification (V-11), Reference Extraction (V-11) and Story Completeness (V-10) are produced on every canonical run and read by no domain in the sequence.** `governance/capability/sovereignty-register.json` names their consumers: `story-review` — **which does not exist on the canonical runtime** — plus `test-design`, `coverage-measurement` and `executive-reporting`, which do. | **THIS IS §7's SHAPE CHOSEN DELIBERATELY, NOT INHERITED, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THAT IT IS NOW MEASURABLE.** Before the port these four ran nowhere: `describeCriterion`, `extractReferences` and `assessCompleteness` had **no caller in this repository at all**. They now execute on every run and their output is discarded, which is a worse-sounding state and a better-evidenced one — an unread output is a finding a census can produce, whereas an uncomposed capability is an argument. **Wiring them was refused for the reason ADR-0072's scope silence teaches (D-057): choosing what `executive-reporting` reports, or what `test-design-intelligence` does with an artefact kind, is designing those domains, and doing it inside a port is how a decision arrives with nobody having taken it.** | Functional Testing capability — `observation-interpretation` (producer) · `test-design-intelligence`, `executive-reporting` (the named consumers that exist) | **NOT WIRED, AND THE GAP IS NAMED WITH ITS CONSUMERS RATHER THAN LEFT AS A SILENCE.** Each is a separate decision about a certified domain's output, and each is owed the same treatment `sharedSteps` got in F3 — a design item with a ruling, not a side effect. **`story-review` is the more interesting half: the register names a consumer the canonical runtime does not have**, so either the runtime is incomplete or the register describes the agent path; that question belongs with F1's agent layer, where the sixteen review-board agents land. |

| **D-061** | **THE DOMAIN'S DECLARED INPUT CONTRACT IS NOT THE TYPE IT CONSUMES, AND THE MAPPING BETWEEN THEM HAS NO HOME.** `sovereignty-register.json` declares `observation-interpretation`'s `inputContract` as **`dbiz.observation-set@1`** — the frozen EP→IP payload with its `assertNoKnowledge` guard. What it actually consumes is `ObservationInterpretationInput`: a **flattened projection** in which someone has already decided which work-item field is the acceptance criteria, which comments count, and what an artefact is. ADR-0075 composed the domain and carried the facts; it did **not** close this. | **THE MAPPING IS THE PROBLEM, NOT THE PLUMBING.** Deciding that `Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria` is the acceptance-criteria field is **tool-schema knowledge**: it belongs neither in a composition (which would then name a provider, R-11.5) nor in a customer's tenancy (which is the sovereignty finding this domain exists to close). **So the projection is not a shortcut around a contract — it is a contract with an unowned half**, and the half is the same one `WorkItemObservation.fields: Record<string, unknown>` deliberately leaves open. Until it has an owner, the guard that refuses knowledge at the boundary (`parseObservationSet`) runs nowhere on the canonical path, because no canonical path ever holds an `ObservationSet`. | Platform — `@dbiz/contracts` (`observation-set.ts`) · Functional Testing capability (`observation-interpretation`) · the connector layer that does not yet exist | **RECORDED OPEN BY ADR-0075 P-75.8, and it is the next question after Section D rather than a defect in it.** It cannot be settled without deciding where tool-schema mapping lives, which is the same unowned space P-69.3 records for connector realisation (*"tool I/O belongs to the EP"*) — and a field-name map is not I/O, so it does not obviously go there either. **The cheapest first evidence is the one this port produced: the canonical composition now has a place to put an `ObservationSet` and nothing to put in it.** |

| **D-062** | **EIGHT OF THIRTEEN DOMAINS DECLARE A STAGE THEY DO NOT RUN IN, AND THREE NAME SOMETHING THAT IS NOT A STAGE.** Measured 2026-08-05 while placing the fourteenth. Each domain's completed-event declares a `stageRef`; the through-runner composition decides where the domain actually executes. **They agree in five cases and disagree in eight**: `test-design-intelligence` declares `guardrail-review` and runs in `discovery`; `test-management-intelligence`, `automation-intelligence` and `automation-architecture` declare `execution-planning` and run in `context`; `healing` declares `execution` and runs in `reflection`; and **`defect-management`, `synchronisation` and `executive-reporting` declare `defect-management`, `synchronisation` and `executive-reporting` — none of which is one of the twelve stages.** Nothing compares the two. | **D-007's CLASS, IN THE ONE FIELD THAT LOOKS LIKE IT MUST ALREADY BE CHECKED.** `F-7` asserts *every agent runs in the stage it declares* and passes over 144 agents; there is no equivalent for domains, so the same sentence is enforced for agents and unenforced for the domains those agents were meant to decompose. **The events are emitted by nobody today** — thirteen modules define a completed-event and no composition calls one — which is why the drift has cost nothing and why it will cost something exactly when observability starts consuming them. **The fourteenth domain deliberately did not match the runner** (it declares `context`, the architecturally correct EP→IP stage, and runs in `planning`): matching one of fourteen would have made it the exception and implied the other thirteen had been checked. | Functional Testing capability — all fourteen domain modules · the twelve-stage arrangement in `canonical-runner-capability.ts` | **NOT REPAIRED, AND THE REPAIR IS NOT OBVIOUSLY "MAKE THEM MATCH".** Two readings are live: `stageRef` as *where this domain executes* (which the runner owns and which would make three declarations invalid outright) and `stageRef` as *which lifecycle stage this event belongs to* (which is a projection and can legitimately differ from the arrangement). **Choosing without a ruling would relabel eight domains on a guess.** It belongs with the R-12.11/R-12.2 ADR alongside D-058's `toolContracts` ruling — the same question in a different field, and the same reason not to answer it in passing. **A gate is writable the moment the ruling exists and is not before**, which is why none is proposed here. **RULED AND CLOSED 2026-08-05 — ADR-0076 §4.3, ruling 3 steps (a) and (c).** `stageRef` means *where this domain executes*, the same meaning `AgentDefinition.stage` carries. **Nine relabelled against the measured runner arrangement**: `test-design-intelligence` guardrail-review→discovery, `test-management-intelligence`/`automation-intelligence`/`automation-architecture` execution-planning→context, `healing` execution→reflection, `defect-management`→reflection, `synchronisation`→**certification**, `executive-reporting`→**certification** (both stage 11, not `reporting`), and `observation-interpretation` context→planning. **Behaviour-neutral: no composition emits these events today.** **`observation-interpretation` is EIGHT DEFECTS AND ONE CORRECT DECLARATION OVERRULED** — its `context` was the architecturally right answer, declared deliberately so that matching one of fourteen would not imply the other thirteen had been checked; the ruling makes `stageRef` report the RUNNER rather than the architecture, and the record sits in the domain file rather than being relabelled silently with the other eight. **`verify-domain-stage-ref.js` closes the enforcement gap — `F-7`'s sentence for domains, with BOTH SIDES READ FROM SOURCE** (the declaration from each domain module, the arrangement from the runner's stage callbacks resolved through `canonical-domain-steps`), so neither side can be satisfied by a list the gate carries. **All fourteen agree. PROVED by two planted faults before it was trusted: a stage the domain does not run in fires check 3 alone; a value that is not a stage fires checks 2 and 3.** Registered in `run-all.js` and **PROVED in `proofs.json`**. **The type narrowing (§4.3 item 11) is NOT delivered and is recorded as D-072** — it fails the ownership test, the compiler said so, and §6's partial-outcome clause covers exactly this. |

| **D-063** | **`rawBusinessRules` AND `rawDependencies` STILL ARRIVE PRE-INTERPRETED — THE SHAPE ADR-0075 REMOVED FROM THE CRITERIA, PRESENT TWICE MORE IN THE SAME TYPE.** `RequirementInput` lost `rawAcceptanceCriteria: readonly string[]` because a split array means the splitter ran outside the composition. **The two fields beside it have exactly that shape and were left alone**: something upstream decided where one business rule ends and the next begins, and the sovereignty register declares `business-rule-extraction` an **Intelligence-Plane** capability produced by `agents/story-and-test.ts#story.business-rule-extraction`. | **THE SCOPE IS DELIBERATE AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE ENTRY.** V-08's finding is specifically that the criterion count is the **coverage denominator** — every coverage figure the platform publishes is a ratio over it — so the criteria were the load-bearing instance and the port fixed the one that decides a number. **Business rules and dependencies feed risk keywords, validation rules and cross-module scenarios; they change what is authored, not what coverage is measured against.** Left unrecorded, the next reader sees one field repaired beside two identical ones and cannot tell whether the other two were examined or missed. | Functional Testing capability — `RequirementInput` · `story-intelligence` · the agent that owns extraction | **NOT REPAIRED, AND IT IS F1's PORT RATHER THAN SECTION D's.** The declared producer is an **agent**, so the capability that would own the split does not exist on the canonical runtime until the agent layer does. **Repairing it here would mean either inventing a second interpretation domain or moving extraction into `story-intelligence` — the reviewer-inside-the-producer shape ADR-0075 §3 rejected for the criteria**, and rejecting it there while accepting it here would be the inconsistency this register exists to prevent. |

| **D-058** | **A `failureHandling` CAN DESCRIBE AN OPERATION THE AGENT DOES NOT PERFORM — A FOURTH ANSWER TO D-024's QUESTION, WHICH ADMITS ONLY TWO.** D-024 asks *could the platform have honoured this when it was written, and can it now*, and separates *never implementable* from *stopped being true*. **Three declarations are neither: they are about something the agent never does.** *(i)* `story.retrieval` (FTE) declares `toolContracts: ['ProjectAdapter']`, `responsibilities: ['fetch via adapter']` and *"a story that cannot be retrieved stops the run"* — **its handle receives an already-fetched `Story` and calls no adapter**; the fetch is at `capability.ts:405`. *(ii)* `security-testing sync.traceability` declares *"a traceability link that fails is recorded published:false with a reason"* and **calls `publishResult`, linking nothing.** *(iii)* `performance reporting.executive-pdf` declares *"a PDF that renders below a valid document size fails the reporting review"* and calls **a caller-supplied function**, inspecting no size. Measured across 624 production declarations; 21 more declare a `toolContracts` their handle never calls, of which **three name a contract that exists as no type anywhere in the repository** — `CustomerFindingStore`, `EvidenceCustody`, `TargetConnectivity`. | **THESE ARE THE ONES A TYPE-WIDENING PROGRAMME WILL REPORT AS CLOSED WITHOUT TOUCHING THEM.** `story.retrieval` is already marked keepable by any audit that asks *can its SPI fail* — `ProjectAdapter.fetchStory` gained `ReadOutcome` under ADR-0074 — **and the declaration will still be describing a call the agent does not make.** That is why it is a separate class rather than a subset: D-007's instances describe the agent's own work and have stopped being true; D-024's describe the agent's own work and never could be true; **these describe someone else's work, so no change to this agent can make them true or false.** A reader takes all three for a commitment, and for the first two there is no mechanism anywhere that could honour or violate them. | Platform governance (the class) · Functional Testing · Security Testing · Performance · Penetration Testing (the `toolContracts`) | **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, and the `toolContracts` half is NOT a defect until a ruling exists.** Two readings of `toolContracts` are both in live use — *"this handle calls that SPI"* and *"this run's data came through that SPI"* — and `repository.search.*` is correctly the second after D-045 moved its read into the composition. **Inventing an answer here would put a THIRD meaning in the tree**, which is the duplication CHARTER §4 forbids arriving as a definition rather than as a document. **It belongs with the R-12.11/R-12.2 ADR**, already the queue's naming-and-declaration item, and the three contracts that name no type are its cheapest first evidence. |

| **D-052** | **THE TWO SYNCHRONISATION PHASES ARE COLLAPSED ON THE CANONICAL RUNTIME, AND THE AGENT PATH'S OWN HEADER ARGUES AGAINST IT.** `syncOrchestrator` states the rule it was built to keep: *"TWO PHASES, AND THE SEPARATION IS THE POINT. Design synchronisation persists certified test assets and stops; result synchronisation publishes what an execution produced. Collapsing them would put a Test Run in reach of the authoring stage, and a phase that can create one eventually will — reporting an execution nobody performed."* On the agent path the separation is structural — two phases, two stages, two branches. **On the canonical runtime there is ONE synchronisation domain, and since `4d79e59` `synchronisation.execute` performs both: `execution.publishResult` and `composeDesignSync` run in the same function body.** | **THE COLLAPSE IS PRE-EXISTING; THE RE-COMPOSITION MADE IT CONSEQUENTIAL.** ADR-0039's domain 12 already published test cases, results, evidence and defects together — what it lacked was the design-time write, **so the design phase was not in the room to be endangered.** It is now. **The guarantee still holds, but it changed KIND:** on the agent path a design agent cannot create a Test Run because it runs in a phase that has no route to one; here it cannot because the nine touch only `TestDesignSyncAdapter`. **A structural guarantee became a reachability guarantee**, and reachability is the weaker of the two — it is one injected dependency away from being false, where a phase boundary is not. Measured negatively by `run-functional-completeness.mjs` C-4b, which is the closest thing to the old boundary that this shape admits. | Functional Testing capability — `domains/synchronisation.ts` · ADR-0039 domain 12 | **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, and the repair is not the obvious one.** Separating them means re-sequencing `CANONICAL_DOMAIN_SEQUENCE`, which is frozen and immutable by construction. **The honest question is whether the canonical runtime should have a FOURTEENTH domain — design synchronisation, before execution — rather than whether this composition should have been written differently**, and that is a capability decision of the same class as P-69.2 itself. **Naming it is what stops the next author reading the collapse as intended design**, which is the failure mode every entry in this register shares: not a wrong decision, but a decision nobody recorded taking. |

| **D-053** | **EVERY CANONICAL TEST CASE IS LINKED TWICE TO ONE WORK ITEM, BECAUSE THE STORY AND THE REQUIREMENT ARE THE SAME IDENTIFIER.** Measured at `4d79e59` from the ordered adapter call sequence: 8 `linkWorkItem` calls for 4 cases, 8 distinct (case, link) pairs, 0 duplicate submissions — and yet each case emits `linkWorkItem(tc, 'REQ-1', 'story')` **and** `linkWorkItem(tc, 'REQ-1', 'requirement')`. The composition passes `storyId: story.requirementId`; each case carries `requirementIds: [design.requirementId]`; **the two are one value.** The store does not de-duplicate them because the link STRINGS differ by type, and the link census counts both as successes. | **THIS IS D-046 ARRIVING AS BEHAVIOUR, AND IT IS ALSO ASSUMPTION #5 REACHED WITHOUT ASSUMPTION #5's CAUSE.** #5 recorded that duplicate links accrete when *a requirement is also a supplied work item*; that route is unreachable here, because `workItemIds` is empty. **The conclusion arrived anyway, by a mechanism neither the original entry nor its correction described** — which is the sharper finding: a recorded assumption was verified against the wrong cause and would have been marked HOLDS by anyone checking only the cause. D-046 recorded `RequirementInput` as *"the shape of a story, named as a requirement"* and 2a was closed on the measurement that `req.id` is the caller's own identifier passed through. **It is. The naming mismatch was not the defect; the CONSEQUENCE of the two being one value is, and 2a's closure did not look for one.** | Functional Testing capability — `canonical-domain-steps.ts` `stepSynchronisation` · `agents/design-sync.ts` traceability | **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED — and suppressing either link would be the wrong repair.** Both relationships are real and the agent path legitimately publishes both against genuinely different ids; dropping one here would lose a relationship on one runtime to tidy a coincidence on the other. **The question the customer's tool actually asks is whether two link TYPES to one work item is noise or information**, and that is answerable only against a real ADO or Jira instance — so it is deferred to the first live connector run rather than decided in-reference, where every adapter accepts everything. **RULED A LIVE DEFECT AND FIXED, 2026-08-05, in its own change — the deferral above is WITHDRAWN.** Deferring it to a live connector run was wrong for a reason the entry itself contains: these are *duplicate links written into a customer's tool*, and a defect that only becomes visible against a real instance is one that ships. **The repair de-duplicates by TARGET WORK ITEM in `sync.design-traceability`, not by link string** — the store already de-duplicates the string, and the two strings differ by type, which is why both were written. **COVERAGE WINS THE COLLISION:** where one item is both, the `requirement` link is kept, because it is what a traceability query reads; `story` carries provenance, which is inferable from the same item, where the coverage relationship is not inferable from anything. **The agent path is unaffected — story and requirement are different items there, nothing collides, every link it wrote before is still written.** `sync.design-validation` gained `storyId` and now checks the RELATIONSHIP rather than the LABEL: asserting a `story:` prefix was a check on which of two labels a collision resolved to, so a correctly-linked case could fail its own read-back. **MEASURED: canonical `linkWorkItem` calls 8 → 4 for 4 cases, one per case, zero duplicate pairs; agent path unchanged; suite 389 → 391 with a positive-and-negative guard on both runtimes (R-13.4).** |

| **D-046** | **A TYPE WHOSE NAME SAYS LESS THAN ITS SHAPE, AND IT COST F3 AN OPEN ITEM.** `RequirementInput` — the canonical runtime's entry value — carries `id`, `title`, `statement`, `rawAcceptanceCriteria`, `rawBusinessRules` and `rawDependencies`. **That is a STORY, named as a requirement.** `StoryIntelligenceResult` then emits `requirementId: req.id` (`story-intelligence.ts:142`), and `req.id` is **never modified anywhere in the domain** — a pure pass-through of the caller's identifier. | **THE NAME MADE A NON-GAP LOOK LIKE A GAP FOR THE LENGTH OF A SECTION.** D-036 recorded `storyId` as one of eight inputs the canonical runtime cannot supply to design-synchronisation. **It supplies it, and has all along** — under a name that says the value is a requirement identity the platform derived. Because a derived identifier published as a customer's own would be D-013's class at the publication boundary, **the item could not be closed without checking**, and checking meant reading the domain rather than the type. **A name that understates its shape does not mislead a compiler; it misleads the reading that decides whether work exists.** | Functional Testing capability — `RequirementInput`, `StoryIntelligenceResult.requirementId` | **RULED 2026-08-05: the reference is renamed to `storyId`, with the pass-through recorded as the reason, and D-036's list drops from eight to seven.** **Recorded rather than merely fixed, because the class recurs and is cheap to detect:** the register already holds `sharedSteps` (an absent capability read as an absent field) and `traceabilityReferences` (a synthesised reference that looks like a customer identifier). **All three are naming carrying a claim about provenance that the type does not check.** The narrow lesson is specific: **where a field's name asserts where a value CAME FROM, the assertion is unverified and must be read in the producer before it is trusted** — which is D-032's *a source before a field*, applied to a name rather than to a gap. |
